Both pairing roles were exercised on-device against the real relay in this ADR's Verification; the honest residual gaps are real two-device concurrency, a camera QR scan, and the outbox round trip — scheduled for Slice 11 (§12 matrix; emulator stands in earlier if needed).
**Consequences:** Plan §7 step 8 + §10 steps 1-5 + §6.1 + NFR4 satisfied on the single available device. ADR #22's SecretsRepository now stores the derived pairing key; the enrollment record (mirror, both sides) is persisted. Next per plan order: §8.5-adjacent foreground service (Slice 8).

**ADR \#24 Title: App-Block Foreground Service (Slice 8) — specialUse FGS Poll Loop, Time-Bounded Ticks, §8.2 Degraded States**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + on-device verified 2026-08-30 (plan §8.1–8.3; plan doc checked periodically throughout — no drift).
**Context:** The plan's §8.1–8.3 require app blocking that works WITHOUT the accessibility overlay (Enhanced Mode remains optional, doubling as the transient bridge while BlockerActivity starts). Detection must be poll-based — no push API exists for foreground changes. §8.2 requires permission/stop degradation to be a visible, explicit-re-enable state, checked on the service's OWN cycle, never only on MainActivity.onResume. §8.3's recovery state machine (RecoveryStateStore, RecoveryCountdownTracker) existed from Slice 2 with zero callers — BlockerActivity is its first consumer.
**Decision:** (1) **specialUse FGS** (`PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS`; `FOREGROUND_SERVICE` + `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_SPECIAL_USE` with specialUse property) polling `UsageStatsManager.queryEvents` every 1500ms for the latest ACTIVITY_RESUMED (same underlying value as the deprecated MOVE_TO_FOREGROUND; pre-29 package read reflectively via `mPackage`). First tick looks back 12h so the last RESUMED in the window IS the current foreground app — the §8.1 startup recompute, not stale-overlay detection. START_STICKY restart replays the same path with a null intent. (2) **BlockerActivity** (taskAffinity "", back-proof via OnBackPressedCallback no-op for legacy + predictive back, FLAG_EXCLUDE_FROM_RECENTS): §8.3 recovery countdown (friction 60s, override cooldown 600s), unlock via Rule 3 expiry (`blockExpiresAt`, strict `>` compare), self-dismisses on Stop (Home/keyguard) and clears the shared `activeBlockTarget` so relaunch re-blocks. (3) **§8.2 degraded states** — enum {APP_STOPPED, USAGE_ACCESS_REVOKED, NOTIFICATIONS_OFF} with the hard states outranking the soft, persistent alert notification + in-app banner, explicit re-enable required after APP_STOPPED; APP_STOPPED detected via `getHistoricalProcessExitReasons` `REASON_USER_REQUESTED` since a last-known-good watermark (API 30+; reinstall kills are not USER_REQUESTED — no false positive). (4) **THE WEDGE DEFECT + FIX (on-device 2026-08-30, XOS):** sync binder calls (appops `unsafeCheckOpNoThrow`, `checkSelfPermission`, `areNotificationsEnabled`, `queryEvents`) wedge indefinitely after a shell-side permission/appop mutation (`appops set ... deny`, `pm revoke`), silently freezing enforcement while "Protection active" still shows — a plan §9 silent-loss violation. Coroutine cancellation CANNOT interrupt a blocked binder call. Fix: EVERY binder-touching call runs inside a fresh-thread-per-submission executor with a 10s hard timeout (`future.get` → abandon the wedged thread; it un-wedges on the next mutation and exits); the coroutine loop itself is binder-free. The first fix (scan-only in the executor) was still wedged by the POST_NOTIFICATIONS revoke — `checkNotificationsPermission()` and the heartbeat's `hasUsageAccess()` were ALSO wedge points running in the coroutine; the second fix moved the whole tick (usage check, scan, permission check, heartbeat) into the executor. Detection is deliberately best-effort: latency measured per plan §11 on the §12 matrix, no number claimed until then.
**Verification:** On-device X6532 (pure FGS path, `--ez requireService false`): victim → "detected" at +477ms → BlockerActivity topResumed; Back ×2 no-op; Home self-dismisses; relaunch re-blocks. §8.3: countdown ticking (0:55 → 0:38), override reveals victim, Rule 3 expiry persists across clear-task relaunch. §8.2 matrix: force-stop → APP_STOPPED + notification + banner (re-enable verified); usage revoke → USAGE_ACCESS_REVOKED within ~6s, restore → recovered; POST_NOTIFICATIONS revoke/restore — the exact sequence that wedged the previous build — now degrades (NOTIFICATIONS_OFF at tick 30) and recovers with heartbeats continuous through every mutation (`tick 40/60/80/100/120`, degradation=null at the end). Unit suite + lint green.
**Trade-off Accepted:** Poll cadence caps detection latency at ~1.5s+ (plus tick cost) — no instant-block claim (plan §8.1 fixed-interval design; overlay mode, when also enabled, covers the transient). A wedged thread is abandoned per mutation — bounded thread churn, acceptable on the 10s timeout. Single-device (XOS) verification; the OEM matrix (Samsung/Xiaomi, Task-Manager Stop UI variations, wedge-prone builds) is the §12 run scheduled for Slice 11. API 30+ guard on the Stop detection means pre-30 devices have no Task-Manager Stop surface — no APP_STOPPED path there by design (API-gated per the all-OS mandate).
**Consequences:** Manifest gained `PACKAGE_USAGE_STATS` (tools:ignore), `FOREGROUND_SERVICE`, `FOREGROUND_SERVICE_SPECIAL_USE` + specialUse property; `ForegroundBlockerService` (poll loop, degraded states, alert notifications) + `BlockerActivity` (recovery/override/unlock) live in `blocking/`. V1 UI later surfaces the degraded banner + re-enable affordance already used by MainActivity's banner. Next per plan: Slice 9 (attempt log §6 + partner outbox).

**ADR \#25 Title: Attempt Log + Partner Outbox Wiring (Slice 9) — Attempt-First Ordering, Worker-Side Event Creation, Uid-Never-Persisted**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + on-device verified 2026-08-30 (plan §6 + §14; plan doc checked periodically throughout — no drift).
**Context:** Plan §6: every enforced block and forced override is recorded durably ("attempt log table"), and partner notification rides a transactional outbox with idempotency keys — "neither a missed notification nor a duplicate is silently possible by design" (plan line 246). Plan line 80: WorkManager only for ≥15-minute housekeeping, never inline on enforcement paths. The local source of truth is "the client-side attempt log (Room) plus the transactional outbox delivery-status field" (line 290). Logical-flow decision (user-driven review): only blocks and overrides reach the partner — degraded/protection-health states, countdown starts, and rule expiry are local states by design (§8.2 alerts are for the user's own protection status; a Task-Manager Stop is an accepted limitation per plan line 8, not a notified event).
**Decision:** (1) **attempt_log table in AppRulesStore** (Room v3→4; entity + DAO `insert/recent/pendingEventCreation/linkEvent/count`). The plan's "same transaction" (attempt row + outbox row) is impossible across two Room databases (attempt log and chain events live in different stores — both plain-FBE, SQLCipher only in ActivityLogStore/secrets), so ordering is attempt-first with a worker-side reconcile: `AttemptRecorder` (best-effort, never throws — enforcement must not fail because of telemetry) writes the row on the detection path; `PartnerSyncCoordinator` (15-min unique-KEEP WorkManager worker, `enqueuePartnerSync` in MyExodusApp, gated on `isUserUnlocked` like the health check) creates the chain event for each unlinked attempt (oldest first, batch 100) and links it via `linkEvent(id, sequence)`. The unique (pair_id, sender_key_id, sequence) chain index + deterministic Firestore doc id (SHA-256(pairId|senderKeyId|sequence)) remain the idempotency guarantee (line 246). (2) **Signing gate (NFR4):** event creation runs only while the 900s biometric auth window is warm — `beginSign` UserNotAuthenticatedException stops the reconcile pass (every remaining attempt would fail the same way), leaving the attempts unlinked for the next warm run; no prompt is ever shown from background. (3) **Per-run steps are independent:** reconcile → drain outbox (PermissionDenied never retried, Failed retried — §9 semantics) → mailbox pull → backfill response; a drain failure never skips the pull. Unpaired → the whole pass is local-only (the attempt log IS the record). (4) **Uids never persisted:** partnerUid comes from the pair doc's `members` map at runtime (PairUidResolver: App Check install → anonymous sign-in if needed → read pair doc). Event payload: `EventPayload(TYPE_ACTIVITY, "blocked:<package>" | "override:<package>")` — compact, decryptable by the partner, parseable for future partner UI. (5) **Escalation/refactor details:** `EventCreator.Created` now carries the sequence (link needs it; `is CreateResult.Failed` checks unaffected); `FirestoreRelay.DrainResult.Sent` carries the delivered count for the SyncReport; `AccountabilitySigner` opened (open class + open `beginSign`) for test fakes standing in for AndroidKeyStore. FGS tick records the attempt via `runBlocking` on the executor thread (the tick's 10s hard bound covers a wedged store).
**Verification:** Unit suite green (335 tests): `AttemptLogTest` (durable block+override recording, newest-first, unlinked-null, link writes sequence, pendingEventCreation oldest-first); `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest` with real crypto (plain EC keypairs, fake signer warm/cold, fake relay lambdas): unpaired → attempts stay local + no remote step; blocked → linked event decrypts to "blocked:com.instagram.android", drain called with resolved uids; override → "override:" payload; cold window → deferred but drain still runs; **second run → no duplicate event (plan line 278 idempotency)**; drain failure → pull/backfill still run. Migration chain 2→3→4 proven end to end from verbatim v2 DDL (Room post-migration schema validation passes; attempt_log usable; recovery_state intact). Lint zero (16 warnings cleared: FGS POST_NOTIFICATIONS pre-33 guard — a real spurious-NOTIFICATIONS_OFF bug on every pre-33 device, MainActivity pre-26 notification-settings intent guard — a real ActivityNotFoundException crash risk, plus deliberate-code suppressions for the documented pre-29 reflection, the API-30 constant, the 33+ predictive-back attribute, and the debug-only pairing harness). On-device X6532 (pure FGS path): victim detected → BlockerActivity topResumed → `MyExodusAttempts: attempt logged: outcome=BLOCKED rule=rule-… target=com.example.myexodus.test`; DB pull confirms the row + user_version=4 + integrity ok; event_sequence NULL (unpaired device → local-only, per design).
**Trade-off Accepted:** Cross-DB atomicity is impossible → a crash between event creation and the link write lets the next run create a second event for the same attempt (visible duplicate, never silent — documented deviation from the plan's same-transaction ideal). A cold auth window defers delivery up to 15 min (NFR4's gating is deliberate; the FGS tick's inline `runBlocking` insert adds a few ms to the enforcement path, bounded by the 10s tick timeout). Two-device outbox round trip (real partner mailbox receipt + backfill) is scheduled for Slice 11's §12 matrix with the pairing harness.
**Consequences:** Plan §6 (attempt log + partner notification), §14 (outbox housekeeping), line 80 (worker cadence), lines 246/278/290 satisfied. `attempt_log` + v4 migration land in AppRulesStore; `PartnerSyncCoordinator`/`PairUidResolver`/`PartnerSyncWorker` land in `accountability/sync/`. The event is the partner's future UI surface ("blocked:com.instagram.android" decrypts on their device). Next per plan order: Slice 10 (minSdk floor/flavors), then Slice 11 (§12 device matrix + two-device verification).

**ADR \#26 Title: Minimum SDK Floor API 26 (Slice 10) — Plan §3 Floor Declared, Pre-O Guards Pruned, Flavors Confirmed Absent**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + verified 2026-08-31 (plan §3 line 35, §12 lines 282-286; plan doc checked periodically throughout — no drift).
**Context:** Plan §3 declares the minimum supported Android version as API 26 (Android 8.0) — "the floor where UsageStatsManager and modern background-execution restrictions are both present and well-documented; devices below this are out of scope for V1" — and line 286 requires the floor declared in minSdk. Lines 282-283 define two build flavors (Play + APK) with the application ID replaced before the first signed build; ADR #15 already removed the flavor split (the project will NOT be published on Google Play — direct APK only), so no flavor structure exists or is added here.
**Decision:** (1) `minSdk = 24 → 26` in `app/build.gradle.kts`, with an inline comment citing plan §3 and the §16/§314 analytics-confirmation flag (the floor is provisional until confirmed against target-user-base device analytics — flagged to the user, not silently treated as final). (2) **Pre-26 dead paths pruned** (lint `ObsoleteSdkInt` surfaced 7 sites the bump made unreachable): BlockerAccessibilityService's two receiver-registration `when` blocks (O-branch is now the else of the 33+ check), EnhancedModeHealthMonitor + DnsSinkholeVpnService notification-channel guards (channels are mandatory since 26), OverlayController's TYPE_PHONE fallback (TYPE_APPLICATION_OVERLAY since 26), ProtectionNotifications' pre-O early return, MainActivity's pre-26 notification-settings intent fallback (added 2026-08-30 lint fix; ACTION_APP_NOTIFICATION_SETTINGS is always resolvable now), and the `mipmap-anydpi-v26` resource folder merged into `mipmap-anydpi`. All remaining SDK gates stay: pre-Q `mPackage` reflection (26-28 still need it), API 30 Task-Manager Stop gate, API 31 key-rotation path, API 33 NOTIFICATIONS/predictive-back handling.
**Verification:** Full unit suite green (335 tests, including Robolectric @Config(sdk=[34]) — unaffected by the floor), lint zero warnings/errors (the bump initially surfaced 7 ObsoleteSdkInt warnings; all pruned), APK installs on the X6532 (Android 14) and MainActivity opens with the FGS service running.
**Trade-off Accepted:** API 24/25 (Android 7.x, ~1-2% of active devices per current share) are now out of scope per plan §3 — the floor is a product decision pending the §16 analytics confirmation (flagged, not overridden). The `DnsSinkholeVpnService` spike keeps its `SDK_INT < 28` private-DNS branch (26/27 devices still need it).
**Consequences:** Plan §3 + line 286 satisfied; §314's confirmation item remains OPEN for the user's product call. compileSdk 37 / targetSdk 36 unchanged. Next per plan order: Slice 11 (§12 device matrix + full verification — two-device pairing, camera QR, FGS latency, Task-Manager Stop, OEM matrix).

**ADR \#27 Title: Slice 11 Device Matrix & Full Verification — Harness Root-Cause Fixes, Emulator Abandoned for One-Device Two-Role, Two Production Defects Caught On-Device**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + verified 2026-08-31 (plan §11 + §12; plan doc checked periodically throughout — no drift).
**Context:** Plan §11 requires p50/p95/p99 latency measured on a low-tier AND a mid-tier physical device before any number is claimed; §12 requires the device matrix (permission-state, recovery state-machine, VPN, overlay/Activity, Task-Manager Stop, reboot, migration, Keystore-loss, outbox/idempotency, two-device pair operation). The project has ONE physical device (Infinix X6532/XOS, budget tier). The standing directive: make do with one device while still proving multi-device operation. The Android emulator was attempted as the second instance and ABANDONED after 4+ hours: the guest (same low-tier host) crash-restarted system_server repeatedly (watchdog deaths, mount/activity/package services flapping, boot_completed never landed) — the user's warning that it was too weak proved accurate. The replacement: one-device two-role through the real Firestore relay (same semantic as ADR #23's accepted trade-off — the protocol's device-to-device interaction is only through the relay).
**Decision:** (1) **Emulator abandoned; one-device two-role adopted.** Pairing + sync round trip run both roles in one process on the physical device, with DISTINCT anonymous uids (the harness signs out between roles — a single uid would collapse the pair doc's `members` to one entry and PairUidResolver finds no partner, caught on-device). The emulator never runs a timing suite. (2) **Harness root-cause fixes (XOS background-activity-start gate).** XOS intermittently blocks a long-backgrounded app from launching OTHER apps' activities — the victim launch silently produced zero events (no WSC, no decide, overlay never shows), failing the latency suite at cycle 8 and the functional suite after the dialer took foreground. Fixes in `DeviceVerificationActivity`: the functional suite's phone-app scenarios moved LAST, and `launchAllowedApp()` became a self-launch (`FLAG_ACTIVITY_REORDER_TO_FRONT` of the suite itself — self-launches are exempt and refresh the background-start token between victim launches). A TelephonyManager call-state wait was tried and reverted (`getCallState()` is READ_PHONE_STATE-gated on API 34 — dangerous permission; the reorder made it unnecessary). (3) **Two PRODUCTION defects caught only by the device matrix — both fixed:** (a) `AccountabilitySigner.beginSign` used `Signature.getInstance(SIGNATURE_ALGORITHM, "AndroidKeyStore")`; on XOS the AndroidKeyStoreBCWorkaround shadow provider breaks provider-name lookup (`NoSuchAlgorithmException: no such algorithm ... for provider AndroidKeyStore`) while the default chain resolves it — partner sync could NEVER sign on this device class. Fixed by dropping the explicit provider (the pattern the pairing harness already used successfully; confirmed by an on-device probe: explicit fails, default succeeds, alias exists). (b) `FirestoreMailboxReceiver.pull` queried `accountability_events` filtering ONLY on pairId; the read rule is an OR over authorUid/partnerUid, which the rules engine's query-coverage check cannot prove for an unconstrained query → the whole mailbox pull was PERMISSION_DENIED. Fixed by adding `whereEqualTo("partnerUid", myUid)` — the receiver skips own-senderKeyId events anyway, so behavior is preserved (a sibling backfill query was audited and is coverable: its rule references only pairId, which the query constrains). (4) **One-device constraints documented, not hidden:** §11's mid-tier leg is unmeasured — the low-tier X6532 figures are the only shipped numbers, flagged provisional until a second physical device exists; the §12 OEM matrix (Samsung OneUI, Xiaomi MIUI) is deferred for the same reason; the overlay/Activity test's FLAG_HIDE_NON_SYSTEM_OVERLAY_WINDOWS leg was exercised against Chrome rather than a banking/gaming app (none installed). (5) **Play-policy self-check (§12) is moot by design** — ADR #15 removed Play distribution; direct-APK only.
**Verification:** All §12 items closed on the single physical device: functional suite (life-safety keyguard LOCK/WAKEUP/UNLOCK dance, tapjacking touch-injection assert, browser URL block/hide, dialer + emergency holders, in-call UI) = PASS 09:49; latency suite (30 cycles, engine-internal stages) = PASS 10:21 — detection p99 5ms / submission p99 43ms / presentation p99 80ms (total ≈128ms; detection far under the <1000ms §2 target, presentation under the <100ms target), provisional to a second physical device; fgs suite (pure-FGS block, no overlay) = PASS 10:25; Task-Manager Stop (force-stop → REASON_USER_REQUESTED → APP_STOPPED → banner → re-enable) = PASS 10:30; multiwindow split-screen = PASS 10:33 (skip-branch: XOS has no adb split-screen command — bounds logic covered by unit tests). **Two-device round trip, one-device two-role through the real Firestore relay = PASS 14:24** (pair 741421525772739a, SAS 41840, distinct member uids): fgs suite seeded a fresh attempt (rule-94873202922115, BLOCKED) → sync run 1: `enrolled=true, uidsResolved=true, eventsLinked=1, drained=true, drainedCount=1` (event seq 1, chain head written, mailbox doc server-confirmed — zero pending mutations — with authorUid/partnerUid set) → sync run 2: `eventsLinked=0, drainedCount=0` — idempotency, no duplicate partner notification (plan line 278 dedup by event ID); attempt row linked (event_sequence=1, verified by pulling db+wal together — a lone db pull reads stale and hides WAL-resident rows). A second round trip on pair e10bb21e0b7c5326 passed identically at 14:04 before the pull fix (minus the receipt leg). Mailbox pull now permitted with no PERMISSION_DENIED. WorkManager on XOS remains unusable (`cmd jobscheduler run -f` fails; periodic jobs START/STOP-canceled without running; no workdb ever) — the coordinator is driven in-process by the sync suite, with the worker path covered by unit tests. **[Amended 2026-10-01: WM DOES dispatch on XOS — a workdb now exists on both X6532s (periodic partner-sync on the exact 15-min cadence, A `period_count=127`; FCM wake one-time works SUCCEEDED `run_attempt=1` on both devices). The 2026-08-31 "no workdb" observation was real for that build/state but is not a platform law. The conclusion stands, re-grounded: WM dispatch on XOS cannot be depended on for latency (nor across OEMs), so user-visible "Check now" keeps running the production pass in-process and the worker remains a background supplement. Evidence: pairing plan §12.3 status block 2026-10-01 (2).]** Unit suite 335 green (including `AccountabilitySignerTest` and `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest` with the warm/cold signer fakes); lint zero.
**Trade-off Accepted:** One physical device bounds what §11/§12 can prove — mid-tier latency, OEM matrix, and true two-device concurrency wait for a second physical device; the ADR #19/#20 two-device gates stay re-applicable. The harness's self-launch token refresh, the warm-signing-window button, and the sync suite are debug-only scaffolding — production code was touched only by the two defect fixes above (signer provider chain, pull query shape). One-device two-role proves the protocol's relay semantics but not simultaneous peers; the emulator was never a credible second device on this host.
**Consequences:** Plan §11 (latency with real device data, one tier) + §12 (all automated items; two-device round trip via one-device two-role) + §17 AC items verified on-device. Remaining per plan: §12 Play self-check (moot — no Play distribution), OEM matrix (needs 2 more physical devices — user's call), §16/§314 minSdk analytics confirmation (user product call, flagged in ADR #26), App Check enforcement (§18, user console step). Next: Slice 11 closeout → V1 UI workstream (research + color scheme first).

**ADR \#28 Title: Full-Project Review Pass (2026-08-31) — Protocol Hardening, Blocking-Path Fixes, Rules Tombstone**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + verified 2026-08-31 (pre-V1-UI full-project code review; five-axis review per `code-review-and-quality`, cross-checked against every accepted ADR and the plan decision tables).
**Context:** Before the V1 UI workstream starts, the entire project was reviewed end to end (correctness/readability/architecture/security/performance) with the question "will this break live, or in 6 months". The review produced 13 fixes across three subsystems plus four flagged product decisions below. Every fix is a root-cause repair of a real defect or an accepted-plan invariant that the code had drifted from — none is speculative hardening.
**Decision:** (1) **CEK pair-domain separation (accountability).** `CekDerivation.deriveCek` now takes `pairId` and includes it in the HKDF info (`"StrictMode-CEK" || pairId`). Previously the CEK was derived from the ECDH shared secret alone; with the shared secret rotating only on re-pair, two pairs made with the same key material would share a CEK and, because destruction is device-level (§10), one pair's shred would free the other's ciphertext. Pair-binding keeps each pair's chain under its own key domain. Safe for existing pairs: re-pairing derives a fresh CEK, and old ciphertext is never pulled again. (2) **Bilateral deletion drains the FULL outbox, not just the request (accountability).** `initiate()` loops drain batches until `Sent(0)`, bounded by `MAX_DRAIN_BATCHES = 40` (2x the Req 27 chain cap of 1000 at batch size 50); the agreement key dies with everything encrypted under it, so shredding while older events still sit in the outbox would strand them forever. `PermissionDenied` and relay `Failed` keep their §9 semantics. (3) **Mailbox pull watermark (accountability, quota fix).** `FirestoreMailboxReceiver.pull` now advances a persisted per-pair watermark (`enrollments.mailbox_watermark_ts`, Room v4, MIGRATION_3_4) and queries `startAfter` it — the free-tier 50k-reads/day cap was being consumed re-reading the entire mailbox on every 15-min worker tick. Requires the composite index `(pairId, partnerUid, createdAt)` — deployed via `firestore.indexes.json`. (4) **Task→suspend bridge consolidated.** Four files each carried a private `await()`; now one canonical `FirestoreTasks.await` (withContext(IO) + suspendCancellableCoroutine), 11 call sites. (5) **Chain gap-recovery fork guard.** `ChainStateStore.fillGap` now checks the stored head's `previousEventHash` against the chain head and flags a fork instead of appending a mismatched sequence (previously a malicious/errant gap-fill could attach to the wrong head and silently produce a fork in store state). (6) **Chain-head optimization.** `EventCreator` reads the local head via a new `localHead(pairId, senderKeyId)` query instead of loading the full chain and filtering — same semantics, no O(n) load per event. (7) **Tapjacking (blocking, Security).** `BlockerActivity` sets `window.decorView.filterTouchesWhenObscured = true` — the blocked app could previously draw over the block screen and capture taps on override/escape. The Compose `ui-security` artifact's filter is NOT a dependency (verified in sources); the View API covers the whole window. (8) **Overlay-permission revocation detection (blocking).** New `ProtectionDegradation.OVERLAY_REVOKED`: on Transsion/XOS the blocking overlay needs the `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW` appop, which can be revoked; the FGS health tick now checks it, the FGS notification reports the degradation, and the MainActivity banner offers the fix intent. (9) **Call-UI title spoof fix (blocking).** Window-title markers ("Incoming call", "In-Call UI") are now trusted ONLY on non-application windows: an app fully controls its own window title, so a hostile app could previously exempt itself by faking an incoming-call title. App-hosted call UI stays protected via the live dialer query + fallback package list. (10) **Binder round-trip pruned (blocking, performance).** `getWindows()` is only called for window-state-changed events; content-changed events resolve their display from the event (API 33+) or default display. (11) **Notification-ID collision.** Health alert moved 1001→1003 — 1001 is the FGS "Protection active" notification; a shared id would have replaced it with the health alert. (12) **Migration DEK zeroing (data).** `DatabaseMigration` zeroes the legacy DEK after the pass (`Arrays.fill` in `finally`), same contract as the activity-log key. (13) **Revocation tombstone (accountability, Security).** After §10 deletion removes the pair doc, either former member could re-create it (members is self-asserted) and reopen the mailbox. `BilateralDeletion` now writes an append-only `deleted_pairs/{pairId}` doc BEFORE deleting the pair doc (the rules gate the tombstone's create on the pair doc existing + membership), and the pair create rule denies re-creation when a tombstone exists. Tombstone write is best-effort (`runCatching` + warn): the bilateral contract (transmit, shred, wipe) must not hinge on a hardening write. Deployed to production `myexodus-53804` (rules + composite index) after the full suite passed. **(Corrected 2026-09-13, ADR #33:** the composite index was not actually live — `firebase.json` carried no `indexes` key, so the index deploy was a silent no-op; genuinely deployed + built 2026-09-13. Also, the production Hilt builder omitted `MIGRATION_3_4` — defined here and registered in the test path, but not in production — so any existing v3 DB failed every open until 2026-09-13.**)**
**Verification:** Full unit suite green — 335 tests (new/updated: `BilateralDeletionTest` drain-loop order + `tombstoneFailure_doesNotBlockDeletion`, `CekDerivationTest` pair-domain, migration-chain tests now register MIGRATION_3_4, `PairingFlowTest` fake DAO gains the watermark method, `EmergencyExclusionManagerTest` title-spoof cases flipped); lint zero (this pass also surfaced + fixed three regressions: `AccessibilityRecord.getDisplayId` is API 33 not 24 — the earlier comment was wrong, gate added; two `Uri.parse`→`toUri` KTX; one `mutableStateOf(0L)`→`mutableLongStateOf`); `assembleDebug` builds; Firestore rules compiled + released server-side.
**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) Tombstone write is best-effort — a relay failure at that instant leaves the pre-tombstone gap until the next deletion attempt; accepted because the gap is only exploitable by a former member deliberately re-creating the doc (the app never does). (b) Below API 33, non-WSC events assume the default display (correct on every single-display device, which is the whole supported fleet). (c) `attempt_log` (ADR #25) remains plain-FBE in `myexodus_rules.db` — **FLAGGED for the user**: V1 Epic 2 Step 10 mandates SQLCipher for behavioral data, and `ActivityLogStore` (the only SQLCipher behavioral store) is currently dead code; encrypting attempt_log or routing attempts through ActivityLogStore is a product decision (flagged, not silently changed).
**Consequences:** The protocol's §8.4/§9/§10 invariants (pair-domain key separation, outbox-drained shredding, quota-bounded pulls, revocation-proof mailbox) now match the plan's intent under hostile conditions. Remaining flagged product decisions from the review (not fixed, user's call): clock roll-forward allows a bypass via daily-limit reset (device clock is user-controlled — mitigation would need server time or TrustedClock, out of V1 scope), partner is not notified on key rotation (rotation is local-recovery only, per §10), App Check enforcement is still a user console step (§18), and the multi-pair coordinator is designed but single-pair per device (§8.4). Next: V1 UI workstream (research award-winning UIs + ask color scheme first, per standing rule).

**ADR \#29 Title: Blocking Overlay as an Interactive Pause Card — Interception Escape Hatch Realised, Attempt Logging on URL Blocks, NFR-S10 Touch Filter**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete 2026-09-13 (user-directed; V1-doc conformance on the shipped URL block path).
**Context:** ADR #7 accepted a visible "Open Recovery Tools" button on block overlays as a deliberate trade-off and asserted that "the recovery dashboard remains reachable from every block overlay via the Interception Escape Hatch (V1 spec Req 25)". The shipped overlay did not have it: `OverlayController` drew a full-screen opaque scrim with a single message line and zero interactive elements. A user who landed on a blocked URL was therefore in a dead end — the page behind was unreadable and untouched, and the only ways out were home/back out of the browser or disabling the blocker in Settings. The V1 doc's glossary defines the Interception Escape Hatch as "a dedicated link embedded directly into the active blocking overlays allowing instant, single-tap navigation back to the local MyExodus recovery dashboard", State 2 Path A names the button "Open MyExodus Recovery", and the NFRs require "a maximum of 1 tap for the user to access recovery resources from the active block screen" and `setFilterTouchesWhenObscured(true)` on all block overlays (NFR-S10). Two further gaps surfaced in the same pass: URL blocks never recorded an attempt, though State 2 requires `access_attempts_count` to increment on intercept; and the scrim's alpha (0xEE) left the blocked page faintly readable. A user question drove the fix — "what if the user comes back to the browser and genuinely wants a different site?" — and the user chose, from presented options, an **interactive overlay card** over relaunching the block Activity.
**Decision:** The overlay renders content-driven UI instead of a fixed message. (1) **`BlockOverlayView` + `OverlayContent`** — a `sealed interface` with `AppBlocked` (current behaviour: title + message, every action `GONE`) and `UrlBlocked(host, ruleId, browserPackage)`, which names the blocked site and shows two buttons. The action-free app cover is deliberate: a non-browser window under an overlay has no "different site" to offer and no rule-bound recovery session to start, so the card stays a wall. (**Superseded 2026-09-13 by ADR #32** — user-directed one block language: the app cover now names the app and carries the rule's single recovery action; only the different-site action stays URL-only.) (2) **Action 1 — "Open MyExodus Recovery".** `RecoveryStateStore.startRecovery(ruleId)` runs first (the same call `BlockerActivity` makes, so the Req 13 friction timer and the override cooldown are identical, not a parallel path), then `MainActivity` is started with `EXTRA_START_TAB = TAB_RECOVERY`, which `AppShell` consumes into the Recovery tab. The ruleId comes from a new `BlockDecisionEngine.matchingUrlRule(canonical)`, which `decideUrl` also now calls — one matcher, so the rule the overlay names is by construction the rule that blocked. (3) **Action 2 — "Go to a different site".** An `ACTION_VIEW` intent for `about:blank` targeted at the blocked browser's own package: one tap lands on a blank page in the same browser, and because the blocked tab remains in history a single back-step re-blocks it. This is the escape the dead end lacked, and it is honest — it does not lift the block, it leaves the blocked page. (4) **Attempt logging on URL blocks.** The intercept records to `attempt_log` via the shipped `AttemptRecorder.recordBlocked(ruleId, host)`, gated on `firstShow` so a re-render or a duplicate event does not inflate the count (State 2 increments once per intercept). Before this, only app blocks were counted, so the Rules screen showed "No attempts yet" for a website rule that had blocked dozens of times — a dishonest status surface. (5) **NFR-S10 + opacity.** `filterTouchesWhenObscured = true` on the view and the background raised to fully opaque. (6) **Mechanism unchanged.** The URL intercept still uses the accessibility overlay already chosen by ADR #17/#21 — `TYPE_ACCESSIBILITY_OVERLAY` via `Dialog`, or bare `TYPE_APPLICATION_OVERLAY` on Transsion/XOS where the accessibility overlay type is rejected. No `attachAccessibilityOverlayToDisplay` re-architecture, no new service, no schema change; `BlockerActivity` remains the app-block surface. The overlay is per-display cached and re-`bind`s its content on every show, so one instance serves every block type.
**Verification:** Unit: `BlockOverlayViewTest` (new — URL block exposes exactly the two labels, each carrying the right payload to its callback; rebinding to `AppBlocked` hides them all) and the updated `OverlayControllerTest`/`BrowserUrlScannerTest` for the widened callback. Full suite green (349 tests, 0 failures), lint zero on the touched files, `assembleDebug` green. Device (Infinix X6532/XOS) — all PASS: (1) blocked site renders the pause card naming the site, with both buttons and the hint; (2) "Go to a different site" → `about:blank` opens in the same browser, the overlay dismisses, the address bar is usable; (3) `duckduckgo.com` scans → Allow, browsable; (4) returning to the blocked tab re-blocks (log shows `'example.com' -> Block -> shown`); (5) "Open MyExodus Recovery" → Recovery tab opens with the live friction timer (observed 0:47 of the seeded 60s) and "Blocked: example.com"; (6) countdown completes → rule expires + recovery session consumed (db: `recovery_state` 0 rows, `block_expires_at` stamped), site scans Allow; (7) URL blocks now record to `attempt_log` (5 BLOCKED rows for the rule where the baseline was 0) and Home renders them — **attempt counts render on the Home screen's rule rows, not the Rules screen** (the Rules rows show schedule + state only; the lint-"unused" `rules_attempts_*` strings (deleted 2026-09-13 as dead resources) confirm Rules never rendered counts — this corrects the earlier claim in this ADR); (8) app blocks unchanged — AnkiDroid routes to `BlockerActivity`; (9) the scrim is opaque — the blocked page is not readable through it. **Follow-up closed same day (ADR #30):** the ~2-of-5 non-persistence caveat this ADR flagged was root-caused in a device-testing sweep — a browser window-state-changed event (page load, tab switch) carries no URL evidence, decided Allow, and hid the card with no re-scan (device-reproduced 8/8 before the fix). The Allow path now re-asks the scanner whenever a URL card is up in the same browser package. Two adjacent defects were fixed in the same sweep (the scanner turning non-page omnibox text into Allow; a full-display card swallowing navigation-bar touches). Re-verified on X6532: 12 consecutive fresh loads, card persistent, zero hidden-after-shown events.
**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) "Go to a different site" is a one-tap exit from the blocked page rather than a wall the user must back out of — it trades some friction for the dead-end removal the user asked for, and it does not weaken enforcement (the block still applies to the site; only the page changes). (b) Recovery is reachable in one tap, which is exactly what ADR #7's accepted trade-off ("a tempting distraction") already priced in. (c) The card's buttons are a third place that starts a recovery session (block screen, Recovery tab, overlay) — accepted because all three call the same `RecoveryStateStore`, so there is still one session and one set of rules. (d) Copy is English-only, matching the rest of the app; no duration is printed, keeping the 60s-vs-"3-to-5-minute" discrepancy unexposed to users.
**Consequences:** ADR #7's escape-hatch reachability is now real on the URL path rather than assumed; the app-window path is exempt by design and that exemption is stated in the ADR rather than left implicit — **superseded the same day by ADR #32** (the app cover now shows the same card, so no surface is exempt). Req 9's attempt data is now complete across both block types, which is what the Home screen's per-rule attempt counts surface — and what the partner alert thresholds (Req 6) will read. V1 doc State 2 carries an implementation-status note mirroring this; plan v2 records the work as deviation 5 (UI Phase 1.5, explicitly not part of its three pages). Device verification complete on X6532 (2026-09-13). Next: the remaining V1 UI Phase 1 pages' follow-ups (streaks, partner UI) per the vault workstream note.

**ADR \#30 Title: Device-Test Sweep on the URL Pause Card — Scan-Decides Allow Path, Page-Like Omnibox Rule, Bar-Free Card Bounds, Display-Sized Cover, Runtime Browser-Toolbar Discovery**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + device-verified 2026-09-13 (same-day sweep following ADR #29's device testing; every fix is a root-cause repair of a device-caught defect, none speculative).
**Context:** ADR #29 shipped the URL pause card with a ~2-of-5 non-persistence caveat. The same-day device-testing sweep on the X6532 root-caused it and surfaced four adjacent defects, all caught with the card live. (1) **The non-persistence:** a browser window-state-changed event (page load, tab switch) carries no URL evidence, decided Allow, and hid the card with no re-scan — device-reproduced 8/8 before the fix. (2) **Omnibox false Allows:** the scanner decided on non-page address-bar text — mid-typing fragments ("ex") and the omnibox's own hint ("Search or type URL") canonicalize to a dotless host that matches nothing, yet Allow lifted the card over a still-blocked page. (3) **Nav-bar touch loss:** a full-display card drew above the navigation bar and swallowed its touches, so Home/Back stopped working with the card up — directly contradicting the card's own hint ("Home and back always work"). (4) **Sub-window-sized cover:** a cold launch can fire the blocked app's sub-window (loading/transient dialog) before its main window; the already-shown gate then froze the cover at that sub-window's size — AnkiDroid rendered readable around a 684×563 card. (5) **Package allowlist (user-raised the same day):** real devices carry arbitrary browsers; an allowlist can only ever cover browsers someone listed.
**Decision:** (1) **The scan is the single decider for URL cards.** The Allow path no longer hides a URL card on a browser's window-state event: when a URL card is up and the event's package is that browser, the scanner is re-asked for the address bar and only its verdict decides — evidence decides, not arrival order. Non-browser Allows (app block lifted, plain app switch) still hide; the browser probe sits behind the content check so the common case never probes. (2) **Page-like text only.** Address-bar text decides only when it is page-like — an explicit `scheme:` prefix or a dotted host — unless a rule matches it outright; everything else is a logged no-op (`BrowserUrlScanner`). (3) **Card bounds stop at the system bars.** The cover is sized to the display area minus the system bars (API 30+ `WindowInsets`; full display below R), so the navigation bar stays visible and its touches never reach the card. (4) **The cover sizes to the display, not the deciding window.** A block transition shows display bounds — the sub-window freeze is fixed by construction — while split-screen pane coverage for other restricted windows stays with `blockOtherRestrictedWindows`. (5) **Runtime browser-toolbar discovery replaces the package map.** `BrowserUrlScanner.viewIdFor` probes a small set of conventional toolbar IDs (`url_bar`, `url_bar_text`, `location_bar_text`) inside the event package's own tree, memoizes per package, and retries after 10 s — any browser exposing a conventional toolbar id is scanned with no code change. Brave was first added to the map (address-bar id verified from the installed APK's resources), then the map itself was retired. The enhanced-mode plan's Step 7 carries the revision and the maintenance point (a browser with none of the candidate IDs still needs one list entry).
**Verification:** Unit suite green; lint zero. Device (Infinix X6532/XOS, 2026-09-13) — **12 consecutive fresh blocked-page loads: the card stayed up throughout, exactly one recorded attempt each, zero hidden-after-shown events; the phone's own Home/Back buttons were visible and functional with the card up (real taps); the AnkiDroid cold launch renders the cover at display size (the 684×563 freeze gone); toolbar discovery exercised against the installed Brave browser (toolbar located in its own tree, no map entry).** The scan-decides Allow path is what makes each of the 12 loads one attempt instead of one hidden-card incident.
**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) The re-ask costs one probe per browser WSC while a card is up (memoized; failed probes retried every 10 s) — accepted so evidence, not arrival order, decides. (b) A dotless, schemeless real address is not scanned — the omnibox adds the scheme on navigation and real web addresses carry a dot, so actual page loads stay page-like; a logged no-op, never a hidden card. (c) The card does not cover the system bars — that is the point (their touches never route through the cover, and the State 2 Path B guarantee holds). (d) A browser whose toolbar is absent at first probe (fullscreen video, first paint) is unscanned for up to the 10 s retry window — bounded, and it cannot stay invisible for the process lifetime.
**Consequences:** ADR #29's persistence caveat is closed with a root cause rather than a retry; URL blocking has one decider (the scan) end to end; the card can no longer contradict its own hint; the per-browser maintenance burden is retired for conventional toolbars. V1 doc's pause-card bullet mirrors this; plan v2 deviation 5's amendment records it; enhanced-mode plan Step 7 carries the code-side revision.

**ADR \#31 Title: Block Screen Parity — BlockerActivity Is the URL Pause Card's Twin; the Recovery Tab Owns Every Unlock**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + device-verified 2026-09-13 (user-directed: one block language across app and URL blocks).
**Context:** The app block screen (blocking-core plan §8.1) carried its own block design, including its own countdown/override flow on the screen, while the URL path had just shipped the pause card (ADR #29/#30). Two surfaces, two languages for the same event; the user directed one: the block screen becomes the card's twin and the Recovery tab owns the friction UI for both block types. A device-caught bypass rode the same pass: a completed recovery session row was never consumed, so after a rule re-arm the next blocker launch found the spent session (`remaining == 0`) and auto-unlocked the rule instantly — zero friction (the blocker flashed and vanished; the rule expired again, device-reproduced).
**Decision:** (1) **BlockerActivity = the card twin.** The screen shows the blocked app as its hero (`blocked_target_title`), the same "Blocked by your MyExodus rules" line, the same single "Open MyExodus Recovery" button (`startRecovery` + `MainActivity` `TAB_RECOVERY`), and its own honest hint ("You can always press Home to leave. Blocking resumes when you come back."). The friction flow is not re-implemented here: since this ADR the Recovery tab (`RecoveryScreen`) holds the only countdown UI, and the cooldown-gated override for both block types lives there too. (2) **Back cannot dismiss into the target.** The legacy `onBackPressed` path and the predictive-back callback both no-op; Home always works (`onStop` → finish; the screen never lingers in the background, and the FGS relaunches it when the target returns). (3) **Startup recompute, no stale-overlay detection.** `onCreate` re-decides from current state and finishes immediately if the rule no longer blocks. (4) **Tapjacking** (from ADR #28) stays: `filterTouchesWhenObscured` on the whole window. (5) **Unlock paths collapse to one call site.** The spent-session bypass is fixed by consuming the session in every unlock path — `RecoveryStateStore.clear()` called from `ui/recovery.unlockRule`; since the Recovery tab owns every unlock, that single call site is the whole class of paths (regression guard `RecoveryUnlockTest` fails if `unlockRule` stops clearing). The legitimate crash-resume case is unaffected: only a session whose unlock actually fired is cleared, never one mid-countdown.
**Verification:** Unit suite green (including `RecoveryUnlockTest`); lint zero. Device (X6532, 2026-09-13): the block screen renders the card content — app named, single recovery action, hint; Back is swallowed (focus unchanged); Home exits and the activity finishes; the recovery action lands on the Recovery tab with the live session ("Blocked: AnkiDroid"); the spent-session bypass no longer reproduces (the session is consumed by the unlock that fired). Re-observed with ADR #32's verification the same day.
**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) The block screen loses its in-screen countdown — seeing the timer costs a tab it no longer opens by itself — accepted: one recovery flow, one implementation, and the screen's single action lands exactly where the timer lives (user-directed). (b) Back is swallowed while Home is free — the exit is the keyguard-safe route, matching the blocking-core plan's §10 escape-hatch invariant, and the hint copy states it rather than hiding it.
**Consequences:** The app-block surface now speaks the URL card's language (title, message, single escape); the countdown has one home, so the "two timers" divergence class cannot exist; every unlock runs through one `unlockRule` call site, making the consumed-session invariant auditable in one place. V1 doc's Req 13 status + recovery bullets mirror this.

**ADR \#32 Title: App-Window Cover Becomes the Card — Same Block Language on the Instant Bridge, Recovery Action Without the FGS**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + device-verified 2026-09-13 (user-directed the same day as ADR #31: one block language across app and URL blocks, on every surface).
**Context:** ADR #31 made the app block screen the URL pause card's twin, but the accessibility cover — the instant overlay shown the moment a restricted app's window appears, and the whole wall while the foreground service is not running — still rendered ADR #29's action-free message ("the card stays a wall"). That exemption predated the one-language direction and failed exactly where it mattered most: when the FGS is down (cold start before the poll arms, killed service), the cover is the only surface the user ever sees — AnkiDroid's rule was live and the FGS-down wall named nothing and offered nothing, while the block screen one poll later spoke the card language.
**Decision:** `OverlayContent.AppBlocked` gains `target` and `ruleId` (`String?`), and the cover renders the card: the app named as the hero (`blocked_target_title`, label via `appLabel()` with the raw target as fallback), the same "Blocked by your MyExodus rules" line, the same single "Open MyExodus Recovery" action — `RecoveryStateStore.startRecovery(ruleId)` then `MainActivity` `TAB_RECOVERY`, the identical call every other surface makes — and no different-site action (browser-only; a rebind to an app block hides any stale one, unit-tested). The rule id comes from the published snapshot (`publisher.snapshot.appRules[packageName]?.ruleId`); its only absent case is the decide/show race with a rule deletion, and the card then states the block without an action (unit-tested) rather than offering a session that cannot exist. The URL-scan race that previously fell back to a plain message (`handleUrlResult`) now renders this card with the host as target and a null rule id. Attempt recording is unchanged (`firstShow && rule != null`). The cover remains touch-consuming, opaque, and NFR-S10-filtered (ADR #29), remains `FLAG_NOT_FOCUSABLE` (no focus overrides), and still yields to `BlockerActivity` when the blocker's own window becomes the active foreground (the a11y Allow path hides it) — the handover is cover → activity, same title/message/action each side; each surface's hint names its own escape mechanics (the cover passes Back through to the app beneath; the activity swallows Back and says so).
**Verification:** Unit: `BlockOverlayViewTest` (3 tests — URL card still exactly two payload-carrying labels; an app block shows the named target with recovery-only and a rebind hides the stale different-site action; a null rule id yields no action while the block is still stated). Full suite 360 green, zero lint, `assembleDebug` green. Device (Infinix X6532/XOS, 2026-09-13, AnkiDroid the restricted app): (1) the cover renders the full card the instant the blocked app's window appears (captured at +0 s; still up at +2.7 s, before the activity handover) — app named, single recovery action; (2) handover confirmed — once BlockerActivity's window lands the cover hides (the activity's Compose card visible with its own hint; window list clean after Home, no lingering overlay); (3) Back on the block screen is a no-op (focus unchanged); (4) Home escapes and the activity finishes; (5) returning to the blocked app re-blocks; (6) the cover's "Open MyExodus Recovery" was tapped during the transient cover window (logcat shows no `BlockerActivity` start in the entire window) → the Recovery tab opened with the live friction session "Blocked: AnkiDroid" (0:44 observed) — the cover's own wiring, not the activity's. **FGS-down wall staged + verified (same day, follow-up run):** `am stopservice` is refused for the non-exported service, so a new debug-harness suite (`fgs-down`) stages the state from inside the app — stops the FGS, then exits via the HOME intent (device-caught pitfall, twice: plain `finish()` and `finishAndRemoveTask()` both left `MainActivity` the shared task's top, whose `onResume` restarted the FGS within ~0.5 s; the HOME-intent exit keeps the task backgrounded so nothing resumes). With the service down: a restricted app's launch produced the cover alone — full card, app named, zero `START … BlockerActivity` in the window, service count 0 throughout, cover still up 20 s later (through the Hiber `float_window` important-scene event). Tapping the cover's "Open MyExodus Recovery" opened the Recovery tab with the live 60 s friction session ("Blocked: com.example.myexodus.test", 0:57 observed) and the FGS restarted ~65 ms after the MainActivity start (`Background started FGS: Allowed … uidState: TOP`); the timer completing stamped the rule Expired and scans returned Allow — the full cover-only → recovery → restore cycle. One earlier cover-vanish observation (2026-09-13, ~20 s in) did not reproduce under the same Hiber event in this run; its only correlate was a `uiautomator dump` executed in the window before it, noted as a harness-side hazard rather than a product defect.
**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) The cover offers the recovery action on a surface that can appear over any restricted app — ADR #7's accepted "tempting distraction" trade, applied uniformly by user direction. (b) A rule deleted between decide and show leaves the cover actionless rather than stale-actioned — the card states truth without a dead button. (c) The two surfaces keep different hint copy by design — each names its real escape (Back passes through on the cover, is swallowed on the activity); one language, honest mechanics.
**Consequences:** The app-block language is identical on the instant cover and the block screen, and the FGS-down case — previously a message-only wall with no way to recovery — now carries the escape hatch; ADR #29's action-free exemption is fully superseded (ADR #29's Decision point 1 and Consequences carry the annotation). V1 doc's pause-card bullet updated; plan v2 deviation 5's amendment records the change. The cover's card is the first surface where the recovery action is reachable while the FGS is down, closing the last "no path to recovery from a live block" gap.

**ADR \#33 Title: Second Full-Project Review Pass (2026-09-13) — Gate 2.5 Tool Sweep, Production Room-Builder Migration Registration, Firestore Index Wiring, Full Device Re-Run Under Stress**

**Status:** Accepted; two production fixes implemented + device-verified 2026-09-13 (second full-project pass after ADR #28; same review axis "will this break live, or in 6 months", plus the full Gate 2.5 tool sweep and a complete device matrix re-run with stress).
**Context:** Standing directive: re-read all docs/plans, review the entire project, run every verification tool the workflow doc names, then device-test everything on the X6532/XOS including stress runs, and cross-check the docs for what remains. Both production defects found were divergences between a test/config path and the production path — structurally invisible to the unit suite because the tests exercised the half that was correct.
**Decision:** (1) **Production Room builder omitted `MIGRATION_3_4` (accountability, HIGH — fixed).** `AccountabilityDatabase` declares `version = 4` with `MIGRATION_3_4` (the mailbox watermark column, ADR #28 point 3) defined and registered in the in-file open helper — but the production Hilt provider (`di/AccountabilityModule.kt`) built the database with only `MIGRATION_1_2` and `MIGRATION_2_3`. Room's contract: an on-disk DB at v3 opened by v4 code with no migration path throws `IllegalStateException: A migration from 3 to 4 was required but not found` on first access — the X6532's DB was exactly that (read at user_version 3), and the sync path dies before it can run. Fresh installs create v4 directly and never migrate, which is why every verification since the bump passed. Fixed by registering all three migrations on the production builder. (2) **`firebase.json` carried no `indexes` key (accountability, config — fixed).** The file wired only `"rules": "firestore.rules"`, so `firebase deploy --only firestore:indexes` reported "Deploy complete" while creating nothing — the server had zero indexes. ADR #28's claim that the mailbox composite index `(pairId, partnerUid, createdAt)` was "deployed via `firestore.indexes.json`" was therefore never true in production; the gap surfaced as the sync suite failing `FAILED_PRECONDITION` (missing index, then "index is currently building"). Fixed by wiring the key, redeploying; the index built (~4 min) and the on-device sync round trip passed. (3) **Gate 2.5 sweep — all four tools re-run clean (2026-09-13):** gitleaks commit-history scan 0 leaks (dir-scan hits are gitignored tool artifacts only); opengrep custom ruleset 7 rules over 139 files, 0 findings; Trivy lockfile scan unchanged posture (all findings build/test tooling, none reachable in the APK); SonarQube 26.8.0 full scan — 0 vulnerabilities, 0 security hotspots, 0 security-relevant issues; 58 issues total, every one triaged non-blocking (17 MINOR delete-result-ignored false positives on Compose code, Compose composable complexity, long explicit param lists). (4) **On-device matrix re-run (X6532/XOS):** functional, latency, browser, fgs, fgs-down, and sync (after fix 2) suites all PASS; multiwindow remains the ADR #27 skip-branch (XOS exposes no adb split-screen command — a real split needs a manual gesture; bounds logic is unit-covered). Stress pass: 30 rapid block/allow cycles + a 10 s freezer pinch → 0 FATAL, 0 ANR, PSS 191→166 MB, exactly one live `BlockerActivity` record, no overlay leak after Home. VPN spike: all 7 gates PASS (routes, dns_blocked, dns_allowed, https_200, ipv6_sinkhole, private_dns opportunistic — DoT bypass disclosed by design, stop_restores). Task-Manager Stop cycle re-verified end to end: harness force-stop correctly classified `REASON_USER_REQUESTED` → APP_STOPPED banner + notification → Re-enable clears both; enforcement itself is unaffected by design (fail-safe — the engine keeps blocking while degraded). (5) **One review finding falsified by device evidence:** the MEDIUM "exact-alarm scheduling may silently degrade" concern was wrong — `canScheduleExactAlarms()` returns true for apps that declare NEITHER exact-alarm permission (deny-by-default applies only to apps declaring `SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM`), and the device Settings row reads "Exact alarms — Allowed". No code change; recorded so the finding is not re-raised. (6) **LOW findings flagged, not fixed this pass** (none production-behavioral — see Trade-off d).
**Verification:** Unit suite 360 green; lint zero; `assembleDebug` green. Migration fix on-device: pre-fix DB read `user_version = 3` (SQLite header offset 60, big-endian), first DAO access on the fixed build migrated it to 4 (same read). Index fix on-device: sync run 1 `eventsLinked=1, drainedCount=1`; run 2 `eventsLinked=0, drainedCount=0` — idempotent, no duplicate partner event. SonarQube dashboard (local instance): ncloc 10797; coverage shows 0 because JaCoCo is not wired into the scan — test coverage is proven separately by the unit suite, not by Sonar. Device left clean: rotation state default (`accelerometer_rotation=0`, `user_rotation=0`), Home shows "Protecting" with the harness-seeded rule state.
**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) Coverage is invisible in SonarQube until JaCoCo is wired into the scan — accepted this pass; wiring it is tooling work, and the unit suite is the coverage evidence meanwhile. (b) The delete-result-ignored Sonar findings are false positives (Compose `remember`/`derivedStateOf` result-discard shapes) — triaged individually rather than silenced via rule exclusions, so the scan keeps reporting them. (c) multiwindow stays a skip-branch until a manual split-screen session is run. (d) LOW findings left as-is: unused `keystore` param on `provideIdentityMigration`, stacked KDoc in `FirestoreSchema`, hardcoded strings in `EnhancedModeScreen`, residual `LatencyProfiler`, an unused local at `VpnPacket.kt:242`, `RecoveryPolicyDao` declared abstract class rather than interface. (e) The VPN `private_dns` gate accepts the disclosed DoT bypass (policy enforces on DNS; an app using its own DoT resolver behaves as with any VPN-less network) — unchanged from the earlier spike, restated here so the acceptance is on record.
**Consequences:** Both real defects were invisible to tests by construction — the test path registered the migration and the config path never ran; the standing lessons: when a Room schema version bumps, verify every builder that opens that database (test AND production), and when a Firestore index is required, verify it on the server, not just in the JSON file. `firebase.json` and `di/AccountabilityModule.kt` are the entire production diff of this pass. Remaining known items unchanged: OEM matrix (needs more physical devices — user's call), App Check enforcement (§18, user console step), V1 Phase 2 (M3 Habit Tracking & Streaks) plan pending.

**ADR \#34 Title: Emulator Sweep (Medium_Phone, stock API 36) — Cover Hand-Off Root-Cause, One-Screen Cover Restyle, Dialog Decor Fix**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete + emulator-verified 2026-09-13 (user-directed restyle: one visible pause screen).

**Context:** First stock-Android emulator pass (sdk_gphone64_x86_64, API 36, Medium_Phone AVD) alongside the X6532/XOS device. The user caught three defects: (a) the URL/app cover rendered as an inset dimmed floating panel instead of a full-bleed card, alongside visibly wrong padding; (b) one block showed TWO pause screens — the dark cover never hid once `BlockerActivity` landed, leaving the cover above the activity's light card; (c) even when sequenced correctly, the dark cover swapping to the light card read as two pause screens ("a black and white one… one is sufficient"). Root-cause of (b), from the a11y window dump: the activity's delivered WSC carried the **starting window's** id (dead moments later — `windowFor(event.windowId)` null forever), and the real window (e.g. a11y id 135) emitted **only own-package content-changed events**, which the pipeline skipped outright ("our app is never a restricted target") — so nothing ever re-decided and the cover stayed up. This never reproduced on X6532 because its transitions complete inside the deferred-recheck window and its WSC carries the settled window.

**Decision:** (1) **Dialog theme decor dropped.** `dialogFor()` now removes `FLAG_DIM_BEHIND`/`dimAmount`, zeroes `windowAnimations`, and swaps the theme background for a transparent `ColorDrawable` — the stock-Android path fills the window exactly like the XOS `addView` path (the inset rounded panel + dim was the Dialog's theme decor, not the bounds; the bar-free bounds themselves were correct). Overlay padding switched from raw px to dp. (2) **Own-activity content draw is the hand-off signal.** In the `TYPE_WINDOW_CONTENT_CHANGED` branch, own-package events are no longer skipped unconditionally: if a card is shown on that display and the event's window is `TYPE_APPLICATION` and `isActiveForeground` (per-display `isActive`, root fallback), the cover hides — the durable surface (or a recovery screen) is drawing, so the bridge's job is done. The overlay's own churn cannot match: its window is never `TYPE_APPLICATION` (same invariant as the existing WSC own-cover guard). No generation advance, no decide — a pure retract. The bounded deferred WSC re-checks (6×400ms) stay as the window-list-lag belt for other packages. (3) **One-screen cover (user-directed, light style chosen).** `BlockOverlayView` now paints the `BlockerActivity` card's own theme values: `SandLight` background, `TextLight` 22sp title, `MutedLight` 14/12sp message/hint; buttons are teal (`TealLight` / `surfaceVariant` secondary) M3-metric pills (24/8dp content padding, 40dp min height, ripple, `stateListAnimator = null` — the framework button's theme elevation shadow and drawable content insets are gone with the replaced background). The cover→activity hand-off is now visually seamless; the per-surface hint strings stay honestly different by design (the cover permits Back — the activity swallows it, ADR #31/#32).

**Verification:** Emulator (Medium_Phone, API 36). Decor fix: cover fills `Rect(0,63-1080,2337)` edge-to-edge, no inset/dim, on the app-block path. Hand-off: `decide … -> Block` → cover shown (submission 473ms) → `BlockerActivity` draws → its own-package content-changed events → cover hidden; final dumpsys shows no on-screen `CREATE_ACCESSIBILITY_OVERLAY` window and the screenshot shows exactly one light surface (the silent Dialog-path hide is the only no-log hide path, and no `decide -> Allow` preceded it, so the content-draw rule is provably the actor). FGS-down whole-wall: the cover alone rendered the restyled light card (screenshot), holding over a restricted app with the service count at 0. FGS-launched-only cycles (a11y chain loses the race) render the activity card alone. APP_STOPPED degradation banner (from an earlier adb force-stop) cleared via "Re-enable"; pill "Protecting". Build/lint/install green; full block language now visually one on stock Android. Known emulator quirk (not a product defect, re-observed 3×): the **first** block transition after an a11y re-bind (post-`adb install -r` + `enabled_accessibility_services` re-set) delivers no WSC for the already-topmost app — a second Home→App transition decides normally; same family as the XOS dead-event-routing quirk the §12 harness recycles for.

**Consequences:** The cover hand-off no longer depends on the activity's WSC arriving with a live window id; the emulator joins the X6532 as a verified platform for this path. Outstanding from this sweep (user-visible, scheduled): **split-screen test** — abandoned on this emulator (the host is a 2015 dual-core Mac: guest transitions take seconds and `am start --windowingMode` cannot force a live pairing; user will test on another emulator); **website/URL blocking test on the emulator** — not started (Chrome `com.android.chrome` is present; still needs a URL rule + browser-toolbar discovery exercise on this platform). **Both outstanding items were subsequently resolved or moved — see ADR #35 (URL test DONE; split-screen moved to Lean_API34).** Emulator environment notes for future sessions: `-no-snapshot-save` was passed (cold boot ~15–20 min on this host; drop the flag to keep snapshots); reinstall drops the a11y binding (re-set `enabled_accessibility_services`); system ANR storms at boot need `settings put global hide_error_dialogs 1`; latency on this host: detection ~1.6s / submission ~0.4s / presentation ~2–3s, BlockerActivity "Displayed" ~+4s.

**ADR \#35 Title: Emulator URL Test Complete + Two Defects Fixed — Dialog Window-Animation Ordering (Invisible Card), Room 3.0.2 → 3.0.3 (Process Death at Friction-Timer Completion)**

**Status:** Accepted; both fixes implemented + emulator-verified 2026-09-13 (same session as ADR #34's sweep, Medium_Phone/API 36).

**Context:** The ADR #34 sweep's remaining item — websites (URL) blocking on the emulator — ran end to end and caught two defects, one pre-existing and one new. (a) The URL pause card **decided but rendered zero pixels**: `dumpsys` showed the overlay window attached, topmost, `HAS_DRAWN`, `isVisible`, alpha 1.0, yet screenshots showed Chrome unreadable-on. Killing animation scales globally unstuck it — the window was stalled at the start frame of the Dialog theme's entrance animation. Root cause, from the attrs dump (`wanim=0x103030f` still present despite the code writing `windowAnimations = 0`): `dialogFor()` wrote window attributes **before** `dialog.setContentView()`, and `setContentView` triggers `installDecor`, which re-applies the theme's `windowAnimationStyle` (and dim/background) over anything written earlier. The XOS `addView` path has no decor, which is why X6532 never hit this — the stock-Android Dialog path was first exercised in the ADR #34 sweep, and the defect surfaced only when the card had to appear under animation scale 1 (the sweep's verification ran after animations were disabled). (b) At the moment the 60 s friction timer completed, the app **died**: `FATAL EXCEPTION` with zero app frames — `java.lang.IllegalMonitorStateException` at `androidx.room3.TriggerBasedInvalidationTracker$syncTriggers$2$1.invokeSuspend(InvalidationTracker.kt:658)`, then `Force finishing activity MainActivity`, WIN DEATH, and AM restarting both services. The completion path (`unlockRule`) calls `RestrictionRuleDao.updateAndBump` (a suspend `@Transaction` wrapper) + `RecoveryStateStore.clear()`; Room 3.0.2 invokes `syncTriggers()` before starting the wrapper's transaction, and its `ObservedTableStates.onSync` held a `ReentrantLock` across the suspending `connection.withTransaction(IMMEDIATE)` — when the transaction contended and the coroutine resumed on a different pool thread, `unlock()` ran on a non-owner thread. The crash skipped both writes: `recovery_state` kept the spent session row and the rule stayed unexpired.

**Decision:** (1) **Reorder `OverlayController.dialogFor()`: decor install first, attribute writes after.** `setContentView` now runs before the `window.attributes` reads/writes, so the dim/background/animation zeroing actually sticks (the file carries the invariant as a comment at the ordering point). No other change — bounds, type, cutout mode, profiler attach unchanged. (2) **Bump Room `3.0.2 → 3.0.3`** (`gradle/libs.versions.toml`) — the release note names exactly this failure: "Fix a regression that would cause Room to throw an `IllegalMonitorStateException` when using `@Transaction` wrapper DAO functions" (b/543285472, b/553140228); the 3.0.3 source diff confirms the mechanism fix (`onSyncLock: ReentrantLock` → `onSyncMutex: Mutex`, action now `suspend crossinline`). Room 3.0.3 released 2026-09-09; same minor line, no API/schema change. Dependency locking is enforced (`lockAllConfigurations()`), so `:app:dependencies --write-locks` regenerated `app/gradle.lockfile` in the same change — **any dependency bump in this repo must regenerate the lockfile or the build fails at configuration time.**

**Verification:** Emulator (Medium_Phone, API 36, animations at default scale 1 throughout). URL test, all legs: rule for `example.com` (always-on, WEBSITE) → Chrome navigation → `url scan 'example.com' -> Block` → card visible naming the host with both actions and the hint (screenshot) → `attempt_log` rows BLOCKED → "Go to a different site" → `about:blank`, card hidden → re-entry → re-blocked → "Open MyExodus Recovery" → Recovery tab, live countdown "Blocked: example.com". Dialog fix: fresh window dump shows `ty=ACCESSIBILITY_OVERLAY` with **no `wanim` field** (zeroed; previously `0x103030f`), `isVisible=true`, `mDrawState=HAS_DRAWN`, `presentation=1822ms frame=1786ms` logged by the FrameMetrics profiler — the card painted immediately. Crash fix: full flow repeated on the 3.0.3 build — tap recovery on the card → 60 s countdown → completion: **no crash** (same pid before/after, crash buffer empty, no FATAL/`has died`), `recovery_state` rows 0, rule `block_expires_at` stamped (`1789333118889`), UI back to "No recovery session". Killable-by-crash writes verified as committed. Unit suite 360 green with 3.0.3; lint zero; APK rebuilt + installed.

**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) The crash was fixed by dependency bump rather than an app-side workaround — no workaround exists in app code (the lock lives inside Room's invalidation tracker on a background coroutine), and 3.0.3 is a same-day patch on the same minor line. (b) The ordering invariant in `dialogFor()` is kept as an in-file comment + this ADR; `setContentView` re-applying theme decor is a framework behavior that will silently return if the writes move back above it. (c) The emulator's spent-session leftover from the crash (rule unexpired + lingering `recovery_state` row) was consumed by the post-fix verification run itself; no manual DB cleanup needed.

**Consequences:** The URL blocking path is now emulator-verified end to end, including the recovery escape from the URL card and friction-timer completion — the two legs that ran only on X6532 before. ADR #34's outstanding list is down to **split-screen, moved to Lean_API34 (landscape; user-directed, Medium_Phone shut down first)**. Pattern for future dependency bumps: Room patch releases can carry concurrency fixes the unit suite cannot surface (Robolectric rarely reproduces connection-pool contention), so the emulator/device completion flows are the real regression net for timer/completion write paths.

**ADR \#36 Title: Emulator Split-Screen Pass (emulator-5554, stock API 36, portrait) — Pane-Bounded Cover, Allow-Branch Bypass, Harness Passive Mode**

**Status:** Implemented + unit-verified; on-device end-to-end harness run **PASS** (2026-09-14, emulator-5554, portrait) including the allowed-pane-poke regression. The landscape path never became usable on this image (user-directed to portrait).

**Context:** ADR #34/#35's outstanding item — the multiwindow device-suite suite (`DeviceVerificationActivity`, `--es suite multiwindow`) must prove plan §8 Step 5 / Decision 10 multi-window coverage: the cover covers ONLY the restricted pane (victim pane center) and not the allowed pane (display center). On this AOSP fallback launcher: swipe-up-hold → Overview → long-press the card's icon → "Split screen" → pick the second app. Resulting geometry (1080×1920): Settings pane [0,0,1080,947], divider ~[0,897,1080,1023], victim pane [0,973,1080,1920] — display center (540,960) sits 13px ABOVE the victim pane top, so a pane-bounded cover [0,973,1080,1857] satisfies both assertions.

**Decision:** (1) **Already-shown `show()` re-bounds instead of no-op'ing.** `OverlayController.show()` gated an already-shown display to a silent no-op, swallowing the service's per-window re-bound: the cover froze at whole-display size and swallowed the allowed pane (first live split, 2026-09-14). Now an already-shown display re-applies bounds (`refreshBounds`: `updateViewLayout` on the XOS addView path, window-attribute re-apply on the Dialog path); content stays first-wins, so the label lookup still runs only on block transitions. Unit: `OverlayControllerTest.secondShowOnSameDisplay_reBoundsInsteadOfSkipping`. (2) **Per-display re-bound to the restricted pane's LARGEST window**, clamped to the bar-free `displayBounds` — `blockOtherRestrictedWindows` now selects the largest-area restricted window per display (the AnkiDroid 684×563 sub-window freeze guard) and intersects with the bar-free display (the nav-bar Home/Back swallow guard). (3) **Allow-branch bypass fixed (reproduced live).** An allowed pane's WSC (scrolling Settings in the split) decided Allow and the unconditional `overlay.hide(generation, displayId)` tore the cover off the still-blocked victim pane — the victim app was fully interactive again (log: `decide com.android.settings gen=8 -> Allow` → cover gone, 2026-09-14). Now the hide is gated on `restrictedPaneBeside(displayId, allowed)`: hide only when NO restricted window on the display is DISJOINT from the window being allowed. Disjoint = partition partner (keep the cover); overlap = occluded fullscreen still fading out or a PiP sliver (hide must proceed, or the cover would strand over the launcher — the "Home always works" promise). No visibility test: the android-37 stubs drop `AccessibilityWindowInfo.isVisible`, and overlap already separates occluded windows from partitions. The guard costs a window-list walk, so callers short-circuit on a not-shown overlay. (4) **Harness passive mode (`--ez preSplit true`).** This WM dismantles a split container on ANY programmatic bring-to-front of a pane task (both CLEAR_TASK relaunch and plain NEW_TASK re-parented the victim to fullscreen; container children sz=0). The suite seeds, logs `MARKER_MULTIWINDOW_SEEDED`, and waits while the HOST forms the split from Recents. Engagement is detected on the victim's a11y window shrinking below the display (`waitForSplitEngaged`, real metrics, 50px slack) — NOT on the overlay: the fullscreen victim blocks the moment it is brought up, which satisfied `waitForOverlay` before any split existed (three runs skipped as fullscreen, 2026-09-14). Budgets: 180 s engagement + 60 s overlay. Formation rules learned on-device: (a) the app picked in the split picker is the ADDED pane and receives input focus — form the split FROM the allowed app and ADD the victim, so the victim's resume WSC passes the active-foreground gate and `decideApp` → Block fires; (b) restoring a pre-existing pair from Recents instead lands focus on the OTHER pane and the victim's WSC is dropped after deferral (`deferred WSC dropped ... winType=1`); (c) never tap the victim pane to transfer focus — the standalone test APK crashes on any touch (missing Kotlin stdlib; the same signal the tapjacking suite counts as a leak), which kills the pane and collapses the split. `awaitMultiWindowWindows()` polls the a11y window list (it lags the overlay's shown-state by a frame). (5) **Harness `seed()` crash fixed.** `resetAndReopen` closed the cached DB under the backgrounded MainActivity's dashboard collectors → `FATAL EXCEPTION: attempt to re-open an already-closed object: myexodus_rules.db` (`AttemptLogDao.observeBlockedCounts`). `seed()` now wipes rules via the live DAOs and reuses/creates the active profile — the same store instance also keeps the blocking engine's publisher snapshot live (the reset swapped the store out from under it).

**Verification:** Unit green (`OverlayControllerTest` re-bound test; full suite + lint zero on the fixes). On-device (emulator-5554) **PASS, 2026-09-14** — preSplit run end-to-end: harness seeded → host formed the split (Settings fullscreen → Recents → its card menu → Split screen → victim added as the bottom pane) → `decide com.example.myexodus.test gen=14/15 -> Block` ×3 → `multi-window: overlay bounds Rect(0, 973 - 1080, 1857) restricted to victim pane Rect(0, 973 - 1080, 1920)` → `RESULT: multiwindow=PASS`. Screenshot evidence: the light cover card sits exactly over the victim pane (nav-bar strip below untouched, so Home/Back work) while the Settings pane above stays fully interactive. Allowed-pane-poke regression **PASS**: swipe inside the Settings pane → `decide com.android.settings gen=16 -> Allow` → the cover SURVIVED (pre-fix it vanished instantly) — the `restrictedPaneBeside` fix verified on-device.

**Consequences:** Multi-window app-block coverage is implemented and end-to-end verified on the emulator. Known gap, not yet addressed: URL cards still show at `displayBounds` (whole display) in a split — the pane-clamp was applied to the app-block path only; URL-card multi-window coverage is a separate follow-up. The WM dismantle behavior means every bring-to-front of a pane task in future suites must go through the Recents formation path (or a manual host dance), and the harness host dance rules in Decision (4) are emulator-specific — re-check on other OEMs.

**ADR \#37 Title: Habit Tracking & Streaks (V1 Phase 2 / Milestone 3) — Wall-Clock-First Streak Engine, Reboot-Consistent Milestone Alarms, Encrypted-Store Seam, Streaks UI**

**Status:** Accepted; implementation complete (plan Steps 0–5, `app/plans/habit-streaks-implementation-plan.md`), JVM suite 418 green + lint zero, and **device-verified on both devices** 2026-09-14 (emulator-5554 stock API 34 + X6532/XOS 14) including the +7-day forward-time milestone leg. No product defects were found by the device pass — its findings were all environment mechanics (below), a first for this project's device sweeps.
