(c) **The emulator/CI path depends on a registered debug token** — a secret that must be passed per run; if revoked or absent, every Firestore-touching instrumented test fails closed (403), which is the honest failure mode and is documented in `AppCheckTestSupport`'s recipe.

**Consequences:** Plan v2 §13 line 560's monitor→enforce arc is complete; §17 line 620 is re-read as: rejection half verified on the emulator (no-token run), acceptance half realized as the registered debug-token path (the AD-03b no-GMS acceptance half is void, annotated in the plan). The deployment record is amended in `MyExodus Build & Dependencies.md`. Invariant for future work: any new instrumented test that touches Firestore must call `AppCheckTestSupport.useDebugProviderIfRequested(...)` and be run with the debug secret on non-attesting devices.

**ADR \#58 Title: Approval-Flow Device Leg (Task #32 — Extending the ADR #46 Harness, and the DI Migration-List Gap the Leg Caught: 9→10)**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented + device-verified 2026-09-19 on the X6532 (task #32). Authority: ADR #48 (Consequences: "The ADR #46 harness is the template to extend with an approval leg when a second attesting identity is available"), plan v2 §7 item 13 (line 260 "Approval UI + epoch-bound state machine"), §8 Step 12 (lines 422-425), §6 line 240 (badge-before-respond).

**Context:** The approval state machine shipped with JVM coverage for the whole transition table (`ApprovalFlowTest`) and a UI card whose respond gate is a unit-tested one-liner, but nothing had ever carried an `approval_request`/`approval_response` across the real wire — rules, relay, mailbox server timestamps, real attestation, real biometric window. Plan §17 line 626 accepts the JVM trio only, so this leg is additive evidence, and it is the first leg that could test the responder's AD-16 *server-anchored* deadline against a real Firestore `request.time` rather than an injected clock. The leg's first run found a production defect before it found a pass (below).

**Decision:** (1) **The ADR #46 harness gained an approval leg** — `RoundTripTestEntryPoint.approvalFlow()` exposes A's DI-wired responder half; `PartnerTestIdentity` gained `approvalFlow()` (requester half on B's own DB) and, new for this leg, `receiver()` — B's *full production receive pipeline* (`FirestoreMailboxReceiver` + `MailboxMessageHandler` + `MailboxEventProcessor`) wired to B's own database and the secondary app, with the DI graph's exact handler ordering. (2) **One seam was added to production code: `AccountabilityKeyStore` and its `agreementPrivateKey()` are now `open`** — B's CEK is ECDH over the *virtual partner's* agreement key, and this mirrors precisely the seam `AccountabilitySigner.beginSign()` already provides for signing (the object-subclass pattern used since ADR #46). No other production file changed for the leg. (3) **The leg asserts the full §8 Step 12 contract on the wire**: canonical `requestEventId` derived from B's chain position (`<senderKeyId>:<sequence>`, never self-reported); A's RESPONDER row with `expiresAt − createdAt == 72h` anchored on the mailbox document's server timestamp; the request visible through `PartnerDashboardSource` — the exact read model the §6 line 240 card consumes — with `canRespondToApprovals(...) == true` (the badge gate that enables the buttons); the decision chained, drained, pulled by B and applied by `applyResponse` (epoch triple-check passed, since both sides' pairing epochs must agree for the append to land); APPROVED → CONSUMED exactly once through `casConsume`; and AD-18b (B's pipeline acked what it consumed). (4) **Production defect found and fixed at the root: `provideAccountabilityDatabase` registered migrations only up to `MIGRATION_8_9` while the schema is v10** — every installed device with a v9 accountability DB would fail to open it after upgrading to this build ("A migration from 9 to 10 was required but not found"), taking the whole partner/accountability layer down on those devices; the app's DB-health gate is the only thing that would have surfaced it to a user. Fix: `AccountabilityDatabase.ALL_MIGRATIONS` is now the single list both builders (DI and the companion `get()`) register, and `AccountabilityDatabaseMigrationTest#allMigrations_coverEveryVersionStepToTheDeclaredSchemaVersion` asserts the list covers every step to the version Room actually creates for a fresh install — the class of gap that has now recurred twice (7→8 in ADR #50, 9→10 here) cannot ship silently again.

**Verification:** On the X6532 (real attestation, one warm-window tap covering both identities): `PartnerApprovalInstrumentedTest#requestTravelsResponderDecidesResponseApplies` **OK (1 test)** in 30 s — `A pull: … pulledCount=1`, `A decision drain: … drainedCount=1`, `B pull: PullResult(appended=1, …)`, `RESULT approval=PASS request=dcee1e38…:1 serverClockDelta=-1208ms`. The production approval notification fired live on the device (HIGH channel) from A's DI-wired `notifyRequest` — the user saw it. **The migration defect was caught by this leg's first run** and verified fixed on-device against the real v9 file: the DB header read `user_version = 9` before the fix (open failed) and `user_version = 10` after it — i.e. the upgrade path this bug broke is now exercised end-to-end on the one device that had the broken state. Regression: full JVM suite **671/0/0/0 across 90 classes** (+1 over ADR #57's 670 — exactly the new guard test); `:app:assembleDebug` BUILD SUCCESSFUL.

**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) **The AD-16 rollback guard is device-clock-dependent** — the responder refuses a decision while `now < row.createdAtEpochMillis`, and `createdAt` is the mailbox document's *server* timestamp; a device clock running behind Google's by Δ simply cannot decide a fresh request until it catches up (it is a delay, never a false expiry — the direction is conservative). Measured on the X6532: **−1208 ms** (device ahead). The leg waits out a positive Δ rather than bypassing the guard; if a real user's clock is badly behind, the responder sees the refusal and the card stays actionable once the clock passes the anchor. (b) **The §6 badge is asserted through its data path, not by driving Compose** — the leg proves the pending row reaches the dashboard read model and that the badge gate reads verified; it does not screenshot the card (the card's own gate function is covered by the Step 13 tests). (c) **The harness's B-side database is wiped at construction, not migrated** — a stale pre-bump file there would fail the same way the production gap did; that is acceptable for a debug-only harness, and it is what made the production gap visible.

**Consequences:** The ADR #46 harness now has a *receive* half for the virtual partner (`PartnerTestIdentity.receiver()`), so future device legs that need B to ingest A's traffic (unpair's terminal ack, rotation-epoch traffic, bilateral deletion) no longer need hand-wired transport. Migration registration is single-sourced; any future schema bump must extend `ALL_MIGRATIONS`, and the JVM guard fails loudly if it does not. Recorded honestly: the approval flow's *JVM* acceptance (plan §17 line 626) was already met — this leg adds wire evidence and is the reason a **real upgrade-blocking defect** was found before shipping.

**ADR \#59 Title: UI/UX Audit Pass (Plan `ui-ux-improvement-session.md`, 2026-09-19) — One Token System Across Every Surface, the Authority Signal as the Single Status Source, and the Four Root Causes the Screenshot Pass Uncovered**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented + device-verified 2026-09-19 on the X6532 (task #39). Authority: plan `app/plans/ui-ux-improvement-session.md` findings 1–22 (+23/#24 found during the pass); user mandate 2026-09-19 (all five scope additions, incl. color consistency and logical coherence); ADR #47 (nav labels), ADR #48 (partner semantics untouched).

**Context:** A screenshot audit of every surface × state (light + dark, on-device) was run against a locked persona (restricted user, mid-urge, low patience) plus the first-time-user lens. The audit found 22 issues; four had code-level root causes beyond styling, and several findings were recurrences of one systemic gap: color roles that had never been defined (`secondaryContainer`/`onSecondaryContainer` were absent from both schemes, so M3 baseline purple leaked into the nav pill, tonal buttons, and the partner card), and status surfaces reading *different* signals for the same instant (`monitor.enabled` vs `ProtectionDegradation`).

**Decision:** (1) **Token completion, single source** — `secondaryContainer`/`onSecondaryContainer` defined in both schemes as warm taupe (distinct from `errorContainer`, which the partner card needs); the block surfaces (a plain `View` outside Composition) read the *same* Kotlin palette constants via `.toArgb()` (`OnPrimaryLight/Dark`, `SurfaceVariantLight/Dark` added as named constants used by both `Theme.kt` and `BlockOverlayView`). Gold stays streak/milestone-only (widget compact status word moved to neutral ink; recovery timer numerals moved off tertiary). (2) **One authority signal for status** — Home's chip renders the same tri-state `ProtectionAuthority` the degradation banner reads: Protecting (teal) / Limited (taupe, detection-without-enforcement) / Blocker off (error); block rows render "Not running" whenever detection is impossible, so chip, banner, and rows cannot contradict in one frame. (3) **Notification reconciliation lives in the change-gated publisher** — the stale "Protection was stopped" alert survived Re-enable because `ProtectionStatusMonitor.publish()` early-returns on unchanged state and `reenable()` pre-cleared the flag it would have observed; `reenable()` now only clears `requiresExplicitReEnable` so the next publish reconciles notification 1002 in the same pass that clears the banner. (4) **Block-surface theming follows system night mode** — deliberate deviation from finding #16's literal "force dark Compose theme": the defect was a light card at night; forcing dark in day mode would contradict the app's light Desert Dawn surfaces. `BlockerActivity` inherits the DayNight window theme; `BlockOverlayView` re-resolves night at every `bind()`. (5) **Deep links re-deliver** — `MainActivity` is `singleTop` and `onNewIntent` re-reads the extras (finding #8's foreground no-op); tab values are the capitalized `TAB_*` constants, lowercase silently no-ops. (6) **Widget multiplicities made visible, minimally** — the snapshot carries the displayed habit's label (cleared when that habit is removed, unit-tested) and the per-widget switcher stays deferred with the deferral recorded here, not only in a code comment; `SizeMode.Responsive` gains a narrow-tall preset so 2×2 maps to the expanded face. (7) **Destructive confirms wear the error role everywhere** — delete-rule (finding #18) and the recovery override (finding #24, irreversible Rule-3 retirement) both render their confirm action in `colorScheme.error`. (8) **Tappable rows route to the exact editor** — a Home block row opens *that rule's* editor (AppShell carries the rule id; RulesScreen consumes it), not merely the Rules tab. (9) **Cover-settle loop** — the a11y service re-checks the restricted window's bounds over up to 6 samples (~4.2 s) instead of one, stopping early on two equal samples, aborting on generation change or a gone overlay.

**Verification:** Full suite **673/0/0/0 across 90 classes** + `:app:assembleDebug` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. Device-verified on the X6532 light + dark for every tab, the block overlay and BlockerActivity, Recovery + override dialog, the launcher widget (compact neutral ink; resize 2×1→2×2 committed with bounds `[32,136][360,516]` and the expanded face incl. habit label), the three chip states live (Protecting / Limited / Blocker off) with logcat `authority=DEGRADED_NO_DETECTION`, the degraded banner + "Not running" rows in one frame, F14 end-to-end (`dumpsys` shows 1002 gone after the Re-enable action), and the foreground deep link. Close-out pass on the fresh install (2026-09-19): Home row → exact rule editor, two-line Rules row with the full date, error-role override confirm through a live block→recovery flow, cold-start themed splash, and the #2/#3 empty states + first-run wizard on the data-disposable emulator (two cold-start ANRs there were host CPU contention, cured by `cmd package compile -m speed -f`). Evidence: `Obsidian/MyExodus/ui-audit-2026-09-19/` (`fix-*.png`, `leg-*.png`). Known limitation: API 24/25 legacy `ic_launcher.webp` rasters still show the template robot (adaptive icon covers 26+).

**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) **The Rules row and Home row now wrap to two lines** — a long schedule ("Always on · until Sep 19, 2026 · 3 attempts") is fully readable but taller; the alternative (shorter template) would drop information the persona needs mid-urge. (b) **"Limited" is deliberately not an error state** — with a11y off but usage access on, detection still works; the taupe chip says so and the tap-through to Settings is the fix path, rather than crying wolf with red. (c) **Widget multi-habit remains per-app, not per-widget** — the label makes the tracked habit visible; a per-widget configuration would be a new feature, out of this pass's scope and recorded here so it is not silently dropped. (d) **Testing side effect:** the friction timer was let run to zero while observing Recovery (twice across the session, incl. the close-out override-flow verification), retiring the test AnkiDroid rule via the documented Rule-3 path — the exact mechanism finding #17 asked to disclose; the rule was re-armed via the "Clear" expiry path each time and the final state reads "Expired", as the session found it.

**Consequences:** A visual pass can no longer leak baseline M3 colors — every role the surfaces use is defined in one palette, and the block surfaces (outside Composition) read the same constants. Status surfaces have exactly one source of truth per instant, so the chip/banner/row triad cannot contradict. The two infinite-recurrence classes (undefined color slots; change-gated publishers skipping reconciliation) now have named root causes and devices-level evidence. Future UI passes inherit the audit's method (screenshot every surface × state, both themes, on-device) and the plan's findings table as the checklist.

**ADR \#60 Title: Leaf Logo Brand Identity (2026-09-19) — Keyed-Alpha Icon Pipeline, White-Field Adaptive Icon, and a Brand Plate for Night Mode**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented 2026-09-19. Authority: user-supplied brand masters (`assets/brand/myexodus-logo-master.jpeg` 1024² white-field, `myexodus-logo-on-black.jpeg` 500²); user decision 2026-09-19 (white icon field, "use them anywhere that applies — be critical"). Supersedes the Desert Dawn vector mark (`ic_launcher_foreground.xml`'s sun/horizon) as the app's identity.

**Context:** The app shipped with a vector "Desert Dawn" sun mark as its launcher icon. The user supplied the real logo — a three-leaf gradient mark plus a `MyExodus` wordmark — as JPEGs, i.e. with no alpha channel: "no background" was a black field, not transparency. Every icon surface (adaptive icon, legacy rasters, Android 12+ splash, notification silhouette) and both in-app identity surfaces needed the new mark.

**Decision:** (1) **Alpha is derived, not assumed** — `scripts/GenerateIcons.java` keys the white field out of the master on a Chebyshev distance-to-white ramp (30 → transparent, 80 → opaque) and un-premultiplies the surviving colour off white so antialiased edges carry no pale halo. A *global* threshold, not a border flood-fill: the design has white **inside** it (the separation gap where the stem crosses the leaves is background, and it must key out too). (2) **The mark is split from the wordmark by measurement, not by eye** — the row-ink profile's widest empty band in the middle 40–92% of the ink extent is the gap; the script throws rather than emit a mis-split asset. (3) **White field, per the user's pick** — the adaptive icon is `@android:color/white` with the mark as a per-density 108dp PNG foreground, sized so the bbox diagonal ≤ 60% of the canvas, i.e. inside the 66/108dp safe circle: no OEM mask crops the leaf tips (the app targets every skin, so the *guaranteed* zone is the only safe budget). Legacy rasters (48–192px) are the mark on a white square / white circle. (4) **In-app art sits on a white plate** — the wordmark and stem are dark green, which the near-black `SandDark` surface swallows entirely; `ui/BrandMark.kt` therefore draws both the lockup and the mark on a white chip (invisible in light mode where the surface is already white, an app-icon-style chip at night). The plate is brand, not palette, and stays white in both modes. (5) **Placement, deliberately sparse** — launcher icon + splash (automatic), the lockup on the wizard's **first** step only (a repeat on all five permission steps would be chrome), and the mark beside the Settings title (the app's only about-ish surface). Notification small icons need no change: `minSdk = 26` means the system derives the silhouette from the adaptive foreground's alpha, so they now read as a leaf. (6) **The retired mark is deleted, not kept** — the two vectors and the ten legacy `webp` rasters are gone; git is the archive.

**Verification:** `:app:assembleDebug` BUILD SUCCESSFUL. X6532: app-info page renders the white-field leaf (adaptive icon live); Settings header renders the mark chip in **dark mode** (the case the plate exists for); the app-installed launcher entry resolves to the new mipmaps. Emulator (API 36): Android 12+ splash renders the leaf on the sand window background. `logo_mark.png` composited over `SurfaceDark` by an independent checker — 53.1% fully transparent / 45.0% fully opaque, no white box. Honest gap: the wizard's lockup was **not** observed on screen — the physical device has already completed first-run (its flag was left untouched) and the emulator could not complete a cold start under host CPU contention (the same condition ADR #59 recorded); the component itself is the one verified in Settings, composed the same way. **(Corrected 2026-09-19, lint close-out:** the plated legacy rasters (`mipmap-*/ic_launcher{,_round}.png`, 48–192px) are deleted, not kept — with `minSdk 26` (ADR #26) the `mipmap-anydpi` adaptive XML out-ranks every density PNG in resource resolution, so the rasters could never render and lint's `IconLauncherShape` ×5 flagged dead files. The folder was also renamed `mipmap-anydpi-v26` → `mipmap-anydpi` (restoring the ADR #26 end state) and `scripts/GenerateIcons.java` no longer emits rasters (`plate()` + the round/square diagonal constants removed). Behaviour-neutral: `R.mipmap.ic_launcher{,_round}` (manifest icon, notification small icons) resolve to the same adaptive XML as before, and `mipmap-*/ic_launcher_foreground.png` is untouched as the only density-split layer)**

**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) **The in-app lockup shifts the wizard layout on step 0→1** when it disappears; a permanent header would be stabler but repetitive on a permission screen. (b) **The adaptive mark is conservative (~63% of the visible area)** — a tighter fit would look fuller at large sizes but risks shaving the pointy leaf tips under a circular mask on some OEM skins. (c) **Assets are regenerated by running a JDK single-file program from the repo root**, not by a Gradle task — a build-plugin would be machinery for a step that changes when the brand does. (d) **`drawable-nodpi` bitmaps, capped at 256/320px**, rather than density buckets: they are scaled by Compose, and the cap keeps the decoded bitmaps off the heap's large-object path.

**Consequences:** The brand has one source of truth (`assets/brand/` masters + `scripts/GenerateIcons.java`) and every surface derives from it, so a future brand tweak is one run, not eleven hand-edited files. The ADR #59 "known limitation" about API 24/25 rasters still showing the template robot is retired by this change. Icon geometry decisions (safe-circle diagonal, plate rationale) are recorded here rather than inferred from PNGs.

**ADR \#61 Title: Recovery Resource UI (2026-09-19, plan v3) — Extend-the-Tab Intervention, Effort-Tiered Offline Catalog, Log ≠ Bypass, and the v9 Recovery Rail**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented + device-verified 2026-09-19 (task #35; JVM 721/0/0/0, `:app:assembleDebug` PASS, instrumented migration 8→9, X6532 + emulator-5554 device legs). Authority: `app/plans/myexodus-recovery-resource-ui-implementation-plan-v3.md` (W1–W8; v2 archived, audited line-by-line in v3 §6); V1 doc Req 8/10/13/19/20 + Task 3.1, State 3. Retires the Phase-1 note "the vault workstream note's 'recovery resources' has no spec anywhere and is not built" and the Phase-1 deviation (3) (composed-surface `FLAG_SECURE`).

**Context:** The friction/override rail, the partner transport, the widget, and the block surfaces all shipped (`blocking/recovery/*`, ADR #21/#29–#33; partner v2, ADR #45–#58), but the recovery screen itself was a countdown plus an override — no suggested techniques, no breathing pacer, no coaching steps, no crisis link, no urge log, and Req 8/19/20 + Task 3.1 were unbuilt. The user's v2 plan mixed already-shipped infrastructure with the actual delta; v3 isolated the delta and this build executed it. During the build, the V1 doc's State-3 prose ("Transition: Friction timer expires AND user submits their encrypted urge log") was found to contradict the shipped engine (log ≠ unlock) — corrected in the V1 doc the same day.

**Decision:**
(1) **Intervention stays the Recovery tab (OPEN-1 default)** — no `InterventionActivity`: the 1-tap path already existed (block-screen button + widget "Manage urge" → tab), and a dedicated surface is added only if a recorded latency measurement demands it. The tab gains: effort-ordered suggested techniques, the breathing pacer (4 s in / 6 s out, prompts rotate every 30 s, optional haptic tick default-off, system "remove animations" honored as static text pacing — `BreathingPacer.kt`), inline coaching steps, the crisis link, and the urge-log action. The timer remains `RecoveryTimer`/`RecoveryCountdownTracker` — no new countdown math. OPEN-5 taken (no audio in V1); OPEN-7 taken (shipped vocabulary, not v2's "Pause").
(2) **Willpower windows are a pure domain on a persisted table (W4)** — `recovery/RiskWindowEvaluator` (no Android imports): `[start,end)` minutes-of-day, overnight wrap where the after-midnight part uses the *previous* day's mask bit, day mask bit0 = Monday, ≤3 windows, default one 20:00–06:00 all days created on first read (`RecoveryPlanRepository`), persisted in the new `risk_window` table; recomputed from `LocalDateTime.now()` + `ZoneId.systemDefault()` at every use, with no skew detector to go stale (timezone/DST honored at the next evaluation). The catalog (`recovery/RecoveryResourceCatalog`) holds four techniques with LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH effort tiers entirely in code (no table): `ordered(inRiskWindow)` presents low-effort grounding first while a window is active; `recommended(rating, inRiskWindow)` picks urge-surfing for intensity ≥ 7 or an active window. The evaluator recompute caught and killed a real drift during the build: `promptRotationSeconds` existed on the catalog but the pacer hardcoded 30 — the data is now the single source.
(3) **Coaching plan: ≤5 items per kind, ≤200 chars (W3)** — reusing the `CoachingStep` entity + DAO (schema v8, unused until now), ordered add/edit/delete/reorder in a gate-protected Settings screen. OPEN-4 taken: all items render grouped by kind during interception, and the item whose `associated_trigger_window` matches the active window gets a "now" marker — display all + highlight, not filter.
(4) **A log is not a bypass (W2, Req 10)** — the urge log persists (intensity 1–10 skippable, technique, outcome — OPEN-2) and routes home via `Intent.ACTION_MAIN` + `CATEGORY_HOME` (`MainActivity.onRouteHome`, `runCatching` + logged fallback); the restriction stays active: `ui/recovery.unlockRule` has exactly two call sites (countdown expiry, cooldown-gated override) and the log path touches neither. The V1 doc's State 3 and Milestone 2 prose were corrected to match the shipped engine (seeded 60 s friction, documented at Req 13; "Persistent Partner Messages" was never part of State 3 as shipped).
(5) **The partner half rides the shipped transport as coarse text (W5)** — `recovery:<technique>:<outcome>` emitted at session end through the existing `TYPE_ACTIVITY` rails (`RecoveryMirror`; drained by `PartnerSyncCoordinator` under the signing window when a peer is active), decoded by `PartnerPresentation.parseActivityText` into a sealed `PartnerAlert.Recovery` and rendered in the dashboard "Recent activity". Technique + outcome + time only — never intensity or notes (INV-13 analog); unpaired/paused/kill-switch-off leaves the row unlinked with no error. No rules change, no deploy gate. OPEN-3 taken: text prefix, not a new `EventPayload` type.
(6) **Privacy gate extracted, per-surface (W6)** — `ui/recovery/PrivacyGate.kt`: `BIOMETRIC_WEAK or DEVICE_CREDENTIAL`, no CryptoObject, 60 s memory-only unlock window, bypass + one-time explanation banner when no secure lock screen exists (never a dead end). Applied to the coaching editor, the risk-window editor, and the session history — not the intervention flow or the crisis screen (those must never be blocked mid-urge). `FLAG_SECURE` + `setRecentsScreenshotEnabled(false)` (API 33+) on the recovery surfaces; the Phase-1 "composed-surface count" note is superseded by this real gate + flag set.
(7) **Data rail v8 → v9 (W7)** — `recovery_session` (technique, start/end intensity, `STARTED → COMPLETED | ABANDONED | INTERRUPTED`, epoch timestamps, zone, entry, false-alarm, `event_sequence` mirror link) and `risk_window` in the SQLCipher store (encrypted at rest by construction); indices on `started_at_epoch_ms` and `event_sequence`; explicit `MIGRATION_8_9` registered in `AppRulesStoreFactory.build()` **and every migration-test list** (the recurring trap that killed the accountability DB on-device once — the guard test mirrors ADR #58's). Session recovery: `STARTED` rows past the 15-minute bound (`RecoverySessionDao.closeStaleAsInterrupted`, `endedAt = startedAt + 15 min` — the v2 M2.4 shape) close as `INTERRUPTED` once per launch after the DB is ready; when the start already mirrored, a reconciling end event is enqueued in the same pass. History screen: paged newest-first, behind the gate.
(8) **Open defaults taken and recorded, not silently** — OPEN-1/2/3/4/5/7/8 per above; OPEN-6 stays separate (Req 15 has no sender UI); OPEN-9 stays deferred (pairing UI is open product decision #16 — the mirror is harness-verifiable only); OPEN-10 (clinical review of technique copy) stays an owner task — the build ships neutral copy with no efficacy claims, and the Settings→About "self-help tool, not medical treatment" footer stands. *(Corrected 2026-09-23: at the time this line was written the footer did **not** stand — no About surface and no such string existed. It was built under ADR #82; the claim is true now, it was not then.)* Req 20's literal partner happy path (partner views the *steps*) is the partner-visible-summary decision (vault #12), not silently dropped.

**Verification:** JVM **721/0/0/0** (new: `RiskWindowEvaluator` overnight/day-mask/boundary, catalog ordering + recommendation, plan limits ≤5/≤200, session state machine, migration-list guard, mirror round-trip + drain-link + unpaired no-op, crisis-list release guard, editor/plan-display tests); `:app:assembleDebug` PASS; instrumented migration 8→9 against a real encrypted file — `OK (5 tests)`, emulator-5554, run via `am instrument` after pre-compiling (the Gradle-connected wrapper crashed the process on the degraded emulator; the known host-contention pattern, cured the same way as ADR #59's). **That W8 leg caught a real break: W5's sealed `PartnerAlert` left `PartnerDashboardInstrumentedTest` uncompilable** (it still read `.target`/`.isOverride` off the now-sealed list) — invisible to the JVM suite, which never compiles `androidTest/`; fixed with `filterIsInstance<PartnerAlert.AppActivity>()`, and the androidTest source set compiles again. Emulator-5554: all W1 content renders; urge log driven end-to-end (routing home observed — top = launcher); coaching editor round-trip across all three kinds; risk-window editor + live in-window state; pacer with system animations off (ticks, phase words, prompt rotation); completed session → history row with persisted intensity; gate prompt + lock-less banner; `FLAG_SECURE` live (screencap black); block-screen → Recovery entry (1 tap). X6532 (`install -r` only): tab renders, widget entry routes, session survived backgrounding (0:48 → 0:14 after 20 s away — the dual-clock countdown kept ticking), gate screens reachable. Req 10 legs (emulator, scripted, timestamped): rule armed → Chrome blocked → recovery entered → urge logged → Chrome relaunched inside the 60 s window → `decide com.android.chrome -> Block` and the block screen held (relaunch at 52 s and at 28 s in two runs). Honest gaps: (a) the final Req 10 run's log taps were blind (fixed coordinates) — the non-unlock claim rests on the enumerated unlock call sites (JVM-tested) plus the relaunch evidence, not on re-reading the row from History in that run; (b) the X6532 biometric-unlock leg needs the owner's finger/credential and was not completed there — the gate's full path is emulator-verified; (c) the live partner receive leg awaits a pairing UI (decision #16).

**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) **The tab, not an activity** — the intervention inherits app startup and shares the Recovery tab's existing idle/countdown states; a dedicated activity would trim a few hundred ms but adds a second surface to keep in the same block language. (b) **Technique contents live in code**, not the DB — no per-user editing in V1; a table would add a migration path for content that only changes with the build. (c) **The mirror is coarse by design** — intensity and notes never leave the device; the partner sees *that* a technique was used and how it ended, which is the minimum that supports the "did you walk through your steps?" conversation without leaking the urge's content. (d) **A window's "now" marker is display-time only** — the active window is not stored on the session row; it recomputes per view, which is what makes timezone/DST behavior free.

**Consequences:** Req 8/19/20 + Task 3.1 are built and the recovery surface now matches the V1 doc's State 3 (corrected to the shipped engine the same day). Every willpower decision is derived at read time from one pure evaluator — there is no cached window state to invalidate, and the persistence layer only stores the windows themselves. The unlock invariant is unchanged and now explicitly documented: two call sites, and an urge log is not one of them. The mirror extended the activity vocabulary without touching rules or the payload format, so the partner stack's deployment surface stayed closed. The v9 rail follows the same migration-list contract as every previous step, with a guard test so a future migration cannot repeat the ADR #58 class of defect.

**ADR \#62 Title: Third Full-Project Review Pass (2026-09-19) — Gate 2.5 Sweep on a Fresh SonarQube, Opengrep False-Positive Fix, and the Full Test Re-Run After Recovery UI**

**Status:** Accepted 2026-09-19. Review + tooling pass, no production-code behavior changes (one script fixed: the opengrep ruleset). Verification in progress at write time — updated below as each leg completed.

**Context:** Task #9 after the Recovery Resource UI workstream (ADR #61): a project-wide review pass checking the new work against the CLAUDE.md Code Review Mode checklist, then the full Gate 2.5 tool sweep and a complete test re-run (JVM + instrumented + device). Codebase grew from 139 tracked files (ADR #33, 2026-09-13) to 230 Kotlin files / 21,458 ncloc.

**Decision:** (1) **Code review of the new workstream — no defects found.** `RecoveryScreen.kt` (728 lines, the new largest file) is one screen plus nine private sub-composables and four pure helpers — justified under the >400-line rule by cohesion, matching the `PartnerScreen` precedent (792). The unlock invariant holds: `unlockRule(...)` has exactly two call sites (countdown expiry `RecoveryScreen.kt:176`, cooldown-gated override `:232`); `sessions.logOnly(...)` touches neither rule nor recovery state. No `!!`, no `GlobalScope`, no empty catches, no `runBlocking` in the recovery packages; every domain piece (evaluator, catalog, plan/session repositories, privacy gate) has JVM tests. `MIGRATION_8_9` is registered in the AppRulesStore factory list and covered by the instrumented migration test (the ADR #58 "registered in one place only" defect class has a guard). (2) **Opengrep false positives fixed at the rule, not silenced.** The `myexodus-no-secret-logging` rule flagged two log *messages* that merely name the word "token" (`PushInstallationRegistrar.kt` — "no FCM token", "FCM token rotated") while logging no value. The rule now carries `pattern-not` exclusions for string-literal arguments (`Log.$LEVEL($TAG, "...")` and friends) so only expressions that can carry a value are flagged; documented in-file next to the existing LocalContext exclusion. Re-run: 7 rules over 230 files, 0 findings. (3) **Trivy lockfile scan — posture unchanged, none reach the APK.** 38 HIGH/CRITICAL findings, all in build-tooling configurations: `io.netty:*` 4.1.93/4.1.110 arrive only via AGP's `unified-test-platform-*` (22 netty entries, zero on any runtime/compile classpath); `org.bouncycastle:bcprov-jdk18on:1.79` arrives only via `androidLintTool` + test platform. The shipped runtime/compile classpaths carry `bcprov-jdk18on:1.85.2`, ≥ every fix version these CVEs list (1.84 / 1.85). (4) **Gitleaks — clean.** Commit-history scan over 10 commits: 0 leaks. Dir scan: the two known gitignored `.cache/web-search-pro` cache-hash FPs only. (5) **SonarQube 26.8 full scan on a fresh DB (local instance, reinstalled after `/private/tmp` was wiped):** 0 vulnerabilities, 0 security hotspots. 96 issues: 75 code smells, 21 bugs — every one triaged non-blocking: S3776 cognitive complexity ×24 (Compose screens/services, accepted class since ADR #33), S899 ignored `Boolean` return ×21 (all `delete()` on best-effort purge paths — RecoveryManager file cleanup, stale-hash row clears — where failure is non-fatal by design; same FP class as ADR #33's 17), S107 long parameter lists ×21, S108 empty blocks ×7 + S1313 hardcoded IPs ×4 (all in `DnsSinkholeVpnService` — packet-discard loops and sinkhole addresses are the design), S1192 duplicated literals ×5, S1066/S1871/S6511 ×2 each (deliberate semantic branches / style), S6526/S6532/S6517 ×1 each (known LOWs from ADR #33). One new-code hit, S6619 on `RiskWindowsScreen.kt:141`: "useless non-null check, always succeeds" is a false positive — removing the check does not compile, because `dialogWindow` is `RiskWindow?` when `adding=false && editing=null`. Triaged individually, not silenced, per the ADR #33 trade-off (b) standing decision. Metrics: ncloc 21,458, 230 files, comment density 19%, cognitive complexity 2,398. (6) **Tooling trap recorded (SonarQube boot):** `sonar.sh` ignores `JAVA_HOME` and uses PATH java unless `SONAR_JAVA_PATH` is set, and does not quote paths; the app needs Java 21+ (class-file 65) while ES needs a full JDK (`jdk.attach` missing from the bundled JRE tarballs). Working recipe: symlink Android Studio's JBR 21 to a space-free path and start with `SONAR_JAVA_PATH=<symlink>`. (7) **Test sweep:** JVM suite re-run with `--rerun` (true execution, not up-to-date): **721 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped, across 97 classes** (~9 min). The first emulator instrumentation attempt was ANR-stuck on app startup under host contention from the concurrent SonarQube analysis (scan + ES + Gradle on one machine); re-run after the scan finished. Instrumented + device legs: see Verification.

**Verification:** JVM 721/0/0/0 (97 classes, forced re-run 2026-09-19). Opengrep exit 0 (0 findings / 230 files / 7 rules). SonarQube quality gate: 0 vulnerabilities, 0 hotspots (dashboard: ncloc 21,458). Trivy posture: all findings non-shipped tooling configs, evidenced by lockfile configuration tags. Gitleaks: history 0 leaks. Emulator (emulator-5554) full androidTest run: 41 tests, 29 pass, 12 fail — every failure triaged as an environment precondition, not a regression: 5 partner-suite tests need a signed-in identity and a warm biometric signing window (1 × explicit `no signed-in identity` message; 4 × `beginSign` NPE on the null PrivateKey — the harness probe runs before its own try/catch, so the cold case reports raw NPE instead of the friendly message; harness wart noted for a message-only fix — the emulator has no way to warm the window); 7 blocking-suite tests (EnhancedModeDeviceTest ×5, EnhancedModeMultiWindowTest ×1, LatencyDeviceTest ×1) fail on `BlockerAccessibilityService never connected … (settings readback [null])` — the emulator never granted the accessibility binding these device-only suites require, which is why they were written for the X6532. X6532 (physical, XOS) full androidTest run: 41 tests, 31 pass, 10 fail — same two precondition classes plus one device-specific: 3 partner-suite tests fail `UserNotAuthenticatedException: User not authenticated` (the signing key's biometric window is cold; only the owner's finger re-warms it — precondition, and these three passed on this device on 2026-09-19 earlier under ADR #58); 7 blocking-suite tests fail `BlockerAccessibilityService never connected` — the documented XOS rule ("a process started by `am instrument` NEVER completes a bind", memory/ADR path since 2026-08-27), which is exactly why these suites' device verification runs in-process via `DeviceVerificationActivity` + `scripts/enhanced-mode-device-test.sh`; that script is the device leg for them. PushInstallation ×2 and DailyMaintenance ×1 pass on the device (auth + FCM + WorkManager real rails available there, not on the emulator).

**Trade-off Accepted:** (a) The S899/S6619 false positives keep being reported rather than silenced via rule exclusions — same standing call as ADR #33 (b): they stay visible so a real ignored result can never hide behind them. (b) The VPN sinkhole's hardcoded IPs and empty discard blocks are the feature, not a defect — S1313/S108 stay triaged rather than suppressed. (c) Cognitive complexity in Compose screen files is inherent to the screen-per-file organization; extraction would trade readability for a metric. (d) Trivy findings in AGP test-tooling dependencies are accepted (they never enter the APK; a future AGP bump refreshes them).

**Consequences:** The Gate 2.5 baseline is re-established on a fresh SonarQube DB (the previous instance's H2 data lives in `/private/tmp` and does not survive cleanup — the scan is reproducible from the recipe, the trend line is not). The opengrep ruleset is stricter now (literals excluded, expressions still flagged), so any future `Log.*(TAG, token)` call is still an error. No code changes were needed by the review; the pass's output is evidence plus one ruleset fix and the recorded tooling recipe.

**ADR \#63 Title: Main-Thread Database Access (2026-09-19) — a Plain App Resume Could ANR on the Store Lock; Every UI Path Now Dispatches DB Work Off `Dispatchers.Main`**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented, compiled, and device-re-verified 2026-09-19 (task #9). Production-code behavior change (dispatchers only — no logic, schema, or contract change).

**Context:** The task-#9 device sweep on the X6532 failed the latency suite intermittently with `overlay shown=false after 8000ms`. Logcat showed the a11y manager dropping the victim's window events (`AccessibilityManagerService: wait for adding window timeout: 1136`) and an "Application Not Responding: com.example.myexodus" dialog. The ANR trace pinned the main thread parked in `MainActivity.onResume → StreaksViewModel.refresh → HabitRepository.refreshActive → AppRulesStoreFactory.open()` — waiting on the factory's `synchronized` lock, held by a `DefaultDispatcher-worker` inside `sqlite3_prepare16_v2`. Root cause: `AppRulesStoreFactory.open()` is a synchronous, lock-held SQLCipher open (key derivation, migrations, recovery classification). UI code reached it from Main-immediate scopes — `viewModelScope` in `StreaksViewModel`, `rememberCoroutineScope()` in the recovery/rules/partner screens — so any concurrent opener (widget snapshot writer, milestone alarms, the blocking engine) that held the lock blocked the main thread for seconds. When the main thread stalls, the a11y framework marks the client unresponsive and drops its window events (AOSP delivery timeout), which is exactly the no-overlay symptom the device harness reported: the flake was a real production defect wearing harness noise.

**Decision:** Every UI coroutine that reaches a repository dispatches to `Dispatchers.IO`: `viewModelScope.launch(Dispatchers.IO)` for all five `StreaksViewModel` operations (refresh/select/onboard/ack/relapse), `scope.launch(Dispatchers.IO)` for every direct DB tap action (recovery override + urge log + session finish, coaching add/move/delete, risk-window add/edit/toggle/delete, history paging, rules enable/disable, rule save/delete, partner check/dismiss/respond/unpair), and `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)` inside the shared per-screen helpers (`RecoveryScreen.reload/reloadPlan`, `unlockRule`, `RiskWindowsScreen.reload`, `CoachingPlanScreen.reload`, `SessionHistoryScreen.loadMore`) so their `LaunchedEffect` and lifecycle-observer callers are covered by one wrap each. Standing rule: `Dispatchers.Main` publishes state, never waits on the store lock. The measure was deliberately *not* applied by making `open()` suspend — that would ripple through every non-UI caller, including the blocking engine's hot path. **Second pass (same day, whole-class sweep of every `open()` caller before the re-run) found and fixed three more Main-thread sites in the app shell and the block wall:** (1) `BlockerActivity`'s "Open MyExodus Recovery" escape button ran `RecoveryStateStore.startRecovery` (a store write via `open()`) from `lifecycleScope` — the one tap a walled user makes, now `launch(Dispatchers.IO)`; (2) `MainActivity`'s composition resolved the shell's store handle with `remember { rulesStoreFactory.open() }` — an unconditional main-thread open on every cold start, now a health-keyed `LaunchedEffect` that resolves the handle off Main and re-resolves it on every health transition (which also fixes two latent shell bugs: the old `remember` cached a *closed* handle across a user-confirmed reset and cached `null` forever across a successful retry — both now re-resolve); (3) the profile-bootstrap `LaunchedEffect`'s `ensureActiveProfile()` got the same `withContext(Dispatchers.IO)` wrap. Repositories/stores were left as-is — their callers are the dispatch boundary.

**Verification:** `:app:compileDebugKotlin` PASS; JVM 721/0/0/0 (forced re-run, dispatchers only — no test asserted the previous main-thread behavior); device script re-run on the X6532 with the rebuilt APK (2026-09-19, run 7): a11y bound on the first probe, freezer-survival PASS, functional **PASS** (life-safety ×3 incl. user-assisted keyguard unlock, tapjacking PASS, browser-URL cycle), latency — **detection p50/p95/p99 = 2/8/10 ms** (the same suite measured p99 1699 ms pre-fix, which was the ANR symptom), submission 34/44/46 ms, presentation 40/110/131 ms. The presentation p99 misses the <100 ms §2 sub-target for the second consecutive run (was 113 ms); plan §12's recorded finding holds (XOS first-frame property, documented rather than gamed) — and the XOS stage is `addView` → first `OnPreDraw`, i.e. a Main-thread traversal measure, so the tail is either WMS transition deferral or Main-thread scheduling under the suite's own launch storm; the staged measure cannot distinguish the two. Total detection→overlay p99 ≈ 179 ms vs the <1000 ms §2 budget (met by ~5.6×). Multi-window was not re-run on this device (XOS has no split-screen; AOSP split-screen already verified on the emulator 2026-09-14).

**Trade-off Accepted:** Compose snapshot-state writes now happen from IO threads (`scope.launch(Dispatchers.IO)` bodies set `mutableStateOf` values). Snapshot state is thread-safe by design and this is already the app's pattern (`SettingsScreen`, `SetupScreen`, `AppPicker`, `MainActivity`); the alternative — a suspend store API — would touch the enforcement hot path for no user-visible gain. Non-UI callers (alarm receivers, widget writer, blocking engine, FGS) already launched on IO scopes and were left untouched.

**Consequences:** The resume path can no longer ANR on the store lock, and neither can any screen-level tap. This also removes a category of a11y event loss under load, which matters beyond the harness: a stalled main thread silently degrades blocking responsiveness on the device. The harness lesson is recorded with it — an intermittent "no overlay" on device is a symptom that must be traced through the ANR/a11y logs before being called flaky.

**ADR \#64 Title: Path A Liveness Is the In-Process Service Instance, Not the Platform's Client-Side Enabled List (2026-09-19) — Input Hardening, and the Measurement Trap That Mimicked a Frozen-Status Defect**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented 2026-09-19 (task #10 audit; landed in task #9's scope). Production-code behavior change (input derivation only). JVM-verified and device-verified post-fix — but the defect that triggered the investigation is **not confirmed**, and the change is recorded as hardening, not as a shipped-defect fix (see Verification).

**Context:** The task-#10 audit read the Home chip as **"Limited"** while every other signal said Path A was alive: `dumpsys accessibility` listed the blocker as Enabled **and** Bound, `settings get secure enabled_accessibility_services` held it, window events were flowing (`MyExodusBlocker: event pkg=…`), the harness probe read `BlockerAccessibilityService.connected == true` (`MyExodusVerification: service connected`), and the FGS heartbeat — logging `statusMonitor.authority.value`, the *same* StateFlow the chip collects — printed `tick 40/60: usage=true authority=FULL degradation=null`. The chip maps to "Limited" one way only (`authority == DEGRADED_POLLING_ONLY`), which `authorityFor` yields only for `!a11yAlive && usage && overlay`; `a11yAlive` was `health.isServiceEnabled() && BlockerAccessibilityService.connected.get()`. With `connected` proven true, the chain appeared to prove the client-side `AccessibilityManager.getEnabledAccessibilityServiceList` read returned false while the service was bound and delivering. That inference **collapsed under a controlled test** (Verification): the reading itself was unreliable.

**Decision (the change stands on code reasoning; its trigger did not survive scrutiny):** (1) `ProtectionStatusMonitor.refresh()` derives Path A liveness from `BlockerAccessibilityService.connected` **alone** — the liveness source the enforcement plan names for Path A ("its process reports itself alive"). The removed term was redundant next to it by the code's own semantics: `connected` is true only while a bound instance lives in this process, and the client-side list can only ever add a false negative (`listed=false` with a live instance is not a liveness fact) or a false positive (`listed=true` with no instance — the XOS fresh-process case — is not Path A either). Nothing in the authority model needed the second term, and the plan never asked for it. (2) `EnhancedModeHealthMonitor.isServiceEnabled()` counts a live in-process instance as enabled (`connected || listed`): the same reasoning applied to its other consumers — the Settings/Home/Setup enablement rows, the setup gate, and the enabled→disabled alert, which a list read disagreeing with a live instance could otherwise fire as a false "blocker is off" notification. (3) The now-unused `health` dependency was dropped from `ProtectionStatusMonitor`. (4) Regression coverage: new `ProtectionStatusMonitorAuthorityTest` (live instance + empty platform list ⇒ `FULL`; listed-but-dead ⇒ not `FULL`) and two new `EnhancedModeHealthMonitorTest` cases (live instance counts as enabled with an empty list; a flaky list drop does not alert while a genuine unbind does, exactly once).

**Verification:** JVM — full `:app:testDebugUnitTest` **725 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped / 98 classes** (4 new across the two classes above). Device (X6532, post-fix APK, a11y re-bound with the documented recycle; 2026-09-19 19:25–19:27): `protection APP_STOPPED -> OK (authority=FULL cause=path_a oem=INFINIX api=34)`, `MyExodusBlockerFgs: tick 60: usage=true authority=FULL degradation=null`, and the Home chip renders **"Protecting"**. **The original defect did not survive verification:** in the same seconds, `uiautomator dump` on the fixed build still reported `text="Limited"` while `screencap` showed "Protecting" — the dump serves a stale accessibility node tree on this device while the app's own a11y service is active. Every pre-fix "Limited" reading in this audit came from `uiautomator dump`; no pre-fix screenshot ever showed it. Whether the pre-fix build genuinely rendered "Limited" for a sustained period is therefore **unproven**, and this ADR makes no claim that it did. What is proven: the input derivation is now exactly the plan's, and the fixed build's rendered state agrees with the authority flow (screenshot + heartbeat + transition log, all matching).

**Trade-off Accepted:** Dropping the list term means a service that is *listed* but not bound (the XOS "a fresh process with the setting present does not bind" rule) reads as not-alive. That is the honest state — no instance, no Path A detection — and the Settings row still shows what the platform believes.

**Consequences:** The authority input now matches §8 Step 6's own wording; the enabled→disabled alert can no longer fire off a list read that contradicts a live instance; the widget (`ProtectionSnapshotWriter`) derives from the same flow and inherits the same input. Recorded measurement trap, now standing device knowledge: **on XOS, `uiautomator dump` is not a trustworthy read of the current screen while an accessibility service is active — verify UI state with `screencap` and read the image.** A large share of this audit's investigation followed a "frozen chip" that existed only in the dump's stale node tree. Also recorded: an OEM's client-side accessibility list is not ground truth for liveness in this app — the in-process instance is — worth applying to any future consumer of `getEnabledAccessibilityServiceList`.

**ADR \#65 Title: Task-#10 Scenario-Audit Fix Batch (2026-09-19) — the FGS Start-Foreground Contract, Rotation Recreation, the Website-Field IME, the Cold-Start Blocks Flash, and an Honest Check-Now Gate**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented, JVM-green, and device-re-verified 2026-09-19 (task #10 scenario audit, legs #12–#17). One production-crash-class fix (item 1), one platform-behavior fix (item 2), three smaller correctness/presentation fixes (items 3–5). Three audit observations are recorded as notes, not fixes (below).

**Context:** The task-#10 mandate walked every feature as a real user across the scenario matrix on the X6532 (unusual inputs; mid-run interruptions — rotation, screen-off, app-switch, keyguard, biometric gate; loading/error/offline states; feature transitions — block-during-recovery; soak). Five defects surfaced; each is fixed at its single shared site.

**Decision:**
1. **The FGS start-foreground contract is re-armed per start (crash class).** Every `startForegroundService()` call — including one landing on an already-created service — re-arms the platform's requirement to post a foreground notification within seconds; `onCreate` alone satisfied the first start and missed every subsequent one. On-device repro: background the app, return to it (ON_START → `refreshProtectionChecks` → `EnforcementForegroundService.start()`) while the service still lived → dumpsys showed `isForeground=false` on a live `ServiceRecord` (notification and foregroundId still present) → ~30.02 s later the process was killed with `ForegroundServiceDidNotStartInTimeException` ("Bringing down service while still waiting for start foreground"), and the death presented as "the friction timer was lost after HOME" until the logcat death line was read. Fix: `enterForeground()` (the idempotent `startForeground(NOTIF_FGS, ongoing(this))`) is called from **both** `onCreate` and `onStartCommand` (after the ACTION_STOP branch); re-asserting per start also repairs any lost-foreground state. Red → green on device: 0 crashes over the post-fix 40 s watch, `lastStartId=2`, `isForeground=true`; a backgrounded friction timer then survived intact (9:55 → 9:27 observed).
2. **`MainActivity` takes the Compose-template `configChanges` set** (`orientation|screenSize|screenLayout|smallestScreenSize|keyboardHidden|uiMode`). Rotation was recreating the Activity: the delay timer and pacer reset, and a running recovery session logged an ABANDONED row plus a fresh STARTED row per rotation (reproduced; the rule editor's plain-`remember` validation banner vanished while its `rememberSaveable` text survived, proving destruction). Process death is still covered by the existing app-start sweep (stale STARTED rows relabel INTERRUPTED) and the tint controls' prefs re-read.
3. **The website-target field declares a URI keyboard** (`KeyboardType.Uri`, `KeyboardCapitalization.None`, `autoCorrectEnabled=false`): the XOS IME sentence-capitalized "github.com" to "GitHub.com" on-device — the field's own comment says a hostname is not prose.
4. **A cold start must not flash "No blocks yet" at a user who has blocks:** `HomeScreen`'s rules flow seeds `null` (not `emptyList()`), and `BlocksCard` renders a skeleton bar for `null` and the real empty state only for an observed-empty list — the streak card's own loading rule, applied to the block list. `BlocksHeader` counts `rules?.size ?: 0` so the header never claims a count the list has not yet read.
5. **"Check now" is gated on the radio** (`ConnectivityManager.activeNetwork` capability check, `MainActivity.isOnline()`). Rationale, from Firestore's documented offline-write-queue semantics: offline, queued writes complete locally and the sync pass returns a clean report, so the button would claim a sync that never left the device; the radio is the only truthful pre-check and the failure copy already tells the user to check the connection. The code comment records explicitly that no air-gapped device run backs this line (the earlier comment's "seen on-airplane-mode" claim was not true and was corrected to match the evidence).

Also fixed with a JVM pin (adb cannot type emoji — `input text` is ASCII-only): `RuleTargetsTest.websiteTarget_emojiAndOverlongHosts_surviveCanonicalization` — emoji hosts (`😀.com`), 80-char labels, and 70-char labels must stay valid targets and survive `targetKey` without throwing (the IDN transform's reject path must never crash the editor).

**Verification:** JVM — full `:app:testDebugUnitTest` **728 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped** with all five fixes in (one new test class member: the emoji/overlong pin). Device (X6532, post-fix APK): item 1 red→green as above; item 2 device-verified — the editor's banner now survives portrait↔landscape; item 3 device-verified through the app picker + URL field flow; item 4 seen at cold start ("Reading your streak…" placeholder overhead, no "No blocks yet" flash); item 5's gate observed returning the honest failure state offline. Scenario legs closed in the same audit: rule-on-Shazam → block screen → "Open MyExodus Recovery" → 60 s friction session lands on the Recovery tab with the technique intact (9:56→9:33) → countdown completes → rule unlocked → **Shazam opens** — the full transition cycle, test rule deleted afterwards; mid-run matrix (rotation, screen-off during timer/pacer, app-switch, keyguard) all state-accurate; the privacy gate fires on History with no stored window, cancelling it renders "Your history is locked." + retry (device-confirmed twice on a force-stopped process), and the retry re-raises the prompt.

**Verification addendum (force-stop leg + close-out, 2026-09-19 late):** `:app:lintDebug` BUILD SUCCESSFUL, report reads **No issues found**. The force-stop scenario was walked end-to-end on the X6532: `am force-stop` → the honest degraded surface in three places — notification "Protection was stopped" (with its action), Home banner "Blocker off / MyExodus was stopped from the Task Manager. Time limits stay paused until you re-enable." + Re-enable, Settings → Protection "Accessibility service — Off — blocked apps open normally" + Fix — while the FGS kept ticking `authority=DEGRADED_POLLING_ONLY degradation=APP_STOPPED`. The Re-enable action is acknowledge-and-rederive (`ProtectionStatusStore.reenable()` + refresh, `MainActivity`); tapping it cleared the flag, the degraded notification (id 1002) disappeared, and the chip correctly flipped to **"Limited"** (Path B still live) rather than claiming "Protecting" — the two-tier authority/degradation model behaving exactly as designed. The full user-facing restore then ran: Settings Fix → `AccessibilitySettingsActivity` (deep link verified) → MyExodus blocker → toggle → system "full control" dialog → Allow → `enabled_accessibility_services` set, `authority=FULL degradation=null` on the next permission cycle, Settings row "On — MyExodus sees when a blocked app opens", Home chip **"Protecting"** (screencap-verified). Two observations: (a) **the stale-dump trap re-confirmed** — after the a11y re-bind, `uiautomator dump` once more served `text="Limited"` while `screencap` showed "Protecting", the second independent on-device reproduction of ADR #64's finding; UI state in this audit is screenshot-verified. (b) **XOS kills the FGS-hosting process as `cached idle & background restricted` and the START_STICKY restart is not prompt** — the process (FGS `isForeground=true` moments earlier) was killed, and the FGS did not return for ~17 minutes until the app was manually launched; no FATAL/ANR in the app at any point (all three session kills were system-initiated). Battery non-exemption is already surfaced by the app (Settings row + Fix), so this is the predicted consequence of the user's current device setting, not a new defect; recorded for the §8.2 continuity story and for anyone reading soak results. Soak: PSS stable ~203–231 MB across restarts, 200+ FGS ticks observed, no app-caused crash.

**Recorded, not fixed (audit observations):**
- **The X6532's face sensor auto-satisfies `BIOMETRIC_WEAK` prompts.** `dumpsys biometric` lists a modality-8 (face) sensor at max strength; a prompt left untouched resolves to success in ~1–2 s, which is what opened History without an explicit credential and briefly looked like a gate bypass. It is not one: `PrivacyGate.markUnlocked` has exactly one caller (the auth callback — grep-verified) and the cancel path was device-confirmed to render Locked. The plan's W6 design ("shoulder-surfing lock, not a key gate") already accepts this class; recorded so no future audit re-chases it.
- **`partner_sync_delayed` ("Sync is taking longer than usual…") can read as fault when the partner is merely quiet past 24 h** — the freshness buckets derive from the mailbox watermark (last *received* event), which is the plan's own R-01 mechanism; candidate copy clarification, not a defect.
- **28 hardcoded `Text("…")` literals remain across `ui/`** (HomeScreen's "No blocks yet"/"Your blocks" among them) while the rest of the app reads `strings.xml`; single-locale today, pre-existing debt, flagged not swept (out of the audit's scope, would touch many screens' copy).

**Trade-off Accepted:** `configChanges` means configuration changes no longer rebuild the Activity — state that *should* reset on a config change must reset itself (the app's in-flight surfaces are all session-scoped and revert or re-derive on their own ticks/observers); the alternative (restoring every timer through saved state) is more code for less truth. The Check-now gate can return the failure copy on a captive portal that reports INTERNET capability — the same copy ("check your connection") remains the right user-facing answer there.

**Consequences:** The FGS crash class is closed at the single shared start site (MainActivity resume, EnhancedModeScreen toggle, BootReceiver all route through `EnforcementForegroundService.start()`); the app can no longer be killed by merely foregrounding it while protection is on. Recorded for the plan-fidelity sweep: the enforcement plan v2 §8.2's startup/recompute story is unchanged — this fix is contract plumbing beneath it, and no plan text needed amendment. Remaining audit legs: unpair-mid-session is testable — a live test pair (`dcee1e380ead…`, Since 2026-09-19) exists on the device — but unpairing mutates shared state (the pair is a test artifact; re-pairing needs the second device), so it awaits a user ruling; two-device both-roles stays blocked on a second biometric device; the device-clock flip for date rollover stays unrun (no root) with the rollover math JVM-covered instead (streak days are duration-based — midnight/DST-immune by construction; daily-limit day anchors roll on local-date/timezone change, `UsageAccountingEngineTest`).

**ADR \#66 Title: App Check Debug Seam + the Emulator Two-Device Rig (Task #18, 2026-09-20) — the `-testing` Artifact That Broke Every Debug Run, the AVD That Failed Because Its GPU Was Off, and the Attestation Boundary Confirmed by Two Emulators**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented and verified on both AVDs 2026-09-20 (task #18). One production seam added (`AppCheckInstaller.factoryOverride`), one debug-only provider, one dependency correction, one AVD config fix. The pairing leg of task #14 is closed as **structurally blocked**, with device evidence (below).

**Context:** Task #18 needed a second finger-driveable device for the two-device scenario legs; task #14's pairing leg needed a second *identity*. The X6532 (`DEVICE-SERIAL-REDACTED`) stays the primary; `emulator-5554` (AVD `Lean_API34`) and `emulator-5556` (AVD `Lean_API34_B`) are the pair. Two independent problems blocked it, and a third was assumed to be a problem and is not.

**Decision:**

1. **`firebase-appcheck-debug-testing` is removed from every classpath — it crashed every ordinary debug run.** The artifact exists to supply a *runtime-passed* debug secret, and its registrar installs a provider whose `DebugSecretProvider` calls `InstrumentationRegistry.getArguments()` unguarded. That call only has an arguments bundle under `am instrument`; in a normal (non-instrumented) debug process it crashes provider creation, i.e. app startup. The plain `firebase-appcheck-debug` artifact takes the same secret provider as an OPTIONAL component, so with `-testing` absent it falls back to a persisted, auto-generated secret — which is exactly what a manual device run wants. Task #21's instrumented path is unaffected: `AppCheckTestSupport` writes the `-e firebaseAppCheckDebugSecret` value straight into the provider's own `StorageHelper` and installs `DebugAppCheckProviderFactory` itself.

2. **Manual emulator runs get their own substitution point, mirroring the instrumented one.** `AppCheckInstaller.factoryOverride` is a `@Volatile var` on the production object, set by `DebugAppCheckProvider` — a `ContentProvider` in the **debug source set only**, scoped to emulator builds by a `Build.FINGERPRINT`/`HARDWARE`/`PRODUCT` check, so the X6532 keeps real Play Integrity. A provider (rather than an `Application` hook) because providers are constructed before `Application.onCreate`, which is where `AppCheckInstaller.install` reads the override. Release builds cannot reach the seam: the assigning class is not on the release classpath. Each emulator install generates its own secret; it is read from `shared_prefs/com.google.firebase.appcheck.debug.store.<persistenceKey>.xml` (`DEBUG_SECRET`) and registered with the console — never written to a repo file (ADR #57).

3. **AVD `Lean_API34_B`'s instability was a GPU-config defect, not resource exhaustion.** The AVD was created with `hw.gpu.enabled=no`, which forces software GLES (swangle) regardless of the `-gpu` flag; on a guest the emulator clamps to **1 vCPU** (it requires ≥6 host logical cores to allow more), SwiftShader/ANGLE rendering plus the resulting GC churn wedged SystemUI (ANRs, a zombie SystemUI, a keyguard that could not be swiped). Fixed at the source in `config.ini` — `hw.gpu.enabled=yes`, `hw.gpu.mode=host`, `hw.ramSize=3072` — after which the boot log reports `gles_mode_selected:host` and the device is stable. Launch line: `emulator -avd Lean_API34_B -no-snapshot -no-boot-anim`. `-no-snapshot` also avoids a pending-snapshot `FATAL`.

4. **The emulator can never be the pairing partner — now proven across two emulators, not inferred.** With A as INITIATOR (uid `OFu7cp67hwXzzgnF8ZvreWtWRQe2`, session `dbcdbd726c4f4146`) and B as PARTNER (uid `I3XChsYbLZQZZe9fZwfWPXtX3y23`), the full ceremony ran: token generated, QR exchanged, B's biometric prompt satisfied (fingerprint `accept=1`), B signed the transcript and assessed A's payload — and rejected it:

   ```
   I MyExodusPairing: partner outcome: Rejected(reason=attestation: key security level is SOFTWARE)
   I MyExodusPairing: partner material rejected or timed out — pair aborted
   ```

   Note what did *not* fail: `PairingFlow.ownPayload` succeeded and logged `QR_TEXT`, which means the emulator's software keymaster **does** emit a well-formed `KeyDescription` extension carrying an embedded challenge — the rejection is precisely the two deliberate policy checks in `AttestationVerifier.verify`: `keySecurityLevelOf(leaf) == SOFTWARE` (the emulator reports level 0) and `validateChain` PKIX-pinned to the Google Hardware Attestation roots (the emulator's chain roots at a test root). **No waiver was added.** The obvious debug seam — skip both checks in debug builds — was considered and rejected: `PartnerTestKeys` documents in the same repo that the ADR #46 virtual partner works *because* its chain is "a genuine TEE chain rooted in the pinned Google attestation roots", so an emulator waiver would create a second, permanently-unguarded path around the protocol's central trust assumption, in exchange for covering one property (distinct physical keystores) that the virtual-partner harness already covers in every dimension except that one. Attestation policy is an open product question (plan §16 item 2), not an implementation detail to settle with a debug flag.

**Verification:** Both AVDs at 1080x1920 / density 420, PIN 1234, fingerprint enrolled (`dumpsys fingerprint` `count=1`), animations off, stay-awake on, `locksettings set-disabled true` where the keyguard could not be driven, and App Check live under Firestore enforcement — each emulator's own generated secret console-registered. **A↔B pairing attempt:** logs above (`TokenExpired`/`rejected or timed out` on the initiator, the explicit `SOFTWARE` reason on the responder). **Fresh-install end-to-end on B** (the rig's real payoff — a device with zero prior state): all five onboarding steps walked to *Granted* → a Camera block created under Rules ("Always on", live status "Blocked now") → Camera launched → the a11y overlay fired with the correct copy and the "Open MyExodus Recovery" CTA. **Two platform behaviours re-confirmed on the emulator, both matching the X6532:** `am force-stop` unenables the accessibility service (re-assert `enabled_accessibility_services` after any force-stop), and the force-stop surfaces the app's own honest degradation — "Blocker off" chip plus the "MyExodus was stopped from the Task Manager" banner, cleared by Re-enable.

**Trade-off Accepted:** Debug builds on emulators ship a real, persisted App Check debug secret and a factory override that a release build cannot reference. That is the same trade already accepted for the instrumented path (ADR #57) and is bounded by the emulator check in the provider; the alternative — no manual emulator runs at all under enforcement — would leave the whole emulator matrix unable to reach Firestore.

**Consequences:** The two-device emulator rig is usable for every scenario leg that does not require a *pair*; the pairing leg stays on the ADR #46 virtual-partner harness (a second TEE identity on the X6532), which remains the only route to a real cross-identity pairing round-trip. Task #14 is therefore blocked by design, not by setup, and the residual coverage gap is stated precisely: distinct physical keystores. **Do not add an attestation waiver without a product ruling on plan §16 item 2** — the code now reads as if this were settled, and it is not.

**ADR \#67 Title: Unpair and Approval Respond Could Never Raise the Biometric Prompt (Task #19, 2026-09-20) — a Main-Thread Contract Violated on a Background Dispatcher, Hidden by a Swallowed Exception**

**Status:** Accepted; fixed and device-verified on the X6532 2026-09-20 (task #19). Three code changes: the prompt's thread affinity, a tri-state unpair outcome, and logging on the previously silent failure paths. No plan requirement was contradicted — one plan line was corrected (below).

**Context:** Task #10's scenario walk (feature-transitions leg, mid-run-biometric branch) tapped *End partnership* on the Partner tab of the X6532 — a live test pair (`dcee1e380ead…`) from the Step 14 work. The screen answered instantly with **"Couldn't end the partnership. Check your connection and try again."** and no biometric prompt ever appeared. The failure was 100% reproducible and independent of network state. Nothing in logcat named a cause: the screen wraps the callback in `runCatching { onUnpair(...) }.getOrDefault(false)` — a swallow with no `onFailure` logging — and the resolver beneath it returned `null` through three more silent `runCatching{}.getOrNull()` branches.

**Decision:**

1. **The prompt is raised on the main thread by the function that needs it, not by its callers.** Both Partner-tab actions (`onUnpair`, `onRespondToApproval`) are launched from `Dispatchers.IO` — correct for the Firestore and journal work they do — and both funnel into `MainActivity.signWithBiometric`, which constructed `BiometricPrompt` and called `authenticate()` on that worker thread. `BiometricPrompt` manipulates the host `FragmentActivity`'s `FragmentManager`, which asserts main-thread affinity. `signWithBiometric` now does its keystore work (`Signature.getInstance` + the warm-window `initSign`) on the caller's dispatcher, then raises the prompt inside `withContext(Dispatchers.Main.immediate)`. Fixing the shared function rather than each call site is deliberate: the *symptom* is unpair, the *defect* is a suspend function whose contract silently depended on its caller's dispatcher — the same shape would break again at the next call site.

   Observed cause, verbatim (logcat, X6532, reproducible):

   ```
   W MyExodusPartnerTab: unpair initiation threw
   W MyExodusPartnerTab: java.lang.IllegalStateException: Must be called from main thread of fragment host
   W MyExodusPartnerTab:  at androidx.fragment.app.FragmentManager.ensureExecReady(FragmentManager.java:1698)
   W MyExodusPartnerTab:  at androidx.biometric.BiometricPrompt.authenticate(BiometricPrompt.java:972)
   W MyExodusPartnerTab:  at com.example.myexodus.MainActivity.signWithBiometric(MainActivity.kt:454)
   W MyExodusPartnerTab:  at com.example.myexodus.MainActivity.endPartnership(MainActivity.kt:404)
   W MyExodusPartnerTab:  at kotlinx.coroutines.scheduling.CoroutineScheduler$Worker.run(CoroutineScheduler.kt:717)
   ```

2. **A cancelled prompt is not a failure.** `UnpairOutcome { UNPAIRED, CANCELLED, FAILED }` replaces the Boolean that conflated "the user chose to stop" with "the operation failed", threaded `MainActivity.endPartnership` → `AppShell` → `PartnerScreen`. `CANCELLED` renders nothing at all: nothing was journaled, the pair is untouched, and there is nothing to report. The approval-respond path keeps its existing copy, which was already truthful for a cancel ("Couldn't record your decision. The request is still open — try again.").

3. **The swallows that hid this now log.** `PairUidResolver.resolve` logs a distinct reason for each null it can return (anonymous sign-in failed / no uid / pairing document unreadable / no other member), and both screen callbacks log a thrown exception via `onFailure` before defaulting to failure. This is not cosmetic: the swallow is what turned a deterministic code defect into a plausible-looking network problem, and is why it survived review.

**Verification:** `:app:testDebugUnitTest` **728 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**; `:app:assembleDebug` green. X6532 walk after reinstall: Partner tab → *End partnership* → confirm → **the prompt appears** (`dumpsys window` shows `Window{81f078e u0 BiometricPrompt}` full-screen, and `screencap` returns an empty file — the FLAG_SECURE signature of a live secure prompt, the same trap ADR #64 records, used here as positive evidence) → BACK cancels → logcat `MyExodus: approval biometric prompt: Fingerprint operation canceled by user.` → **no error text rendered**, pair card still "Paired with dcee1e380ead… · Last synced 18h ago", pair intact. Also settled by this run: `resolve()` **succeeded** (the prompt is only reached after it returns non-null), so the "Check your connection" copy was wrong twice over — it was neither the network nor a server rejection, and App Check enforcement on the physical device was not implicated.

**Trade-off Accepted:** The prompt's keystore work stays on the caller's dispatcher while the prompt itself runs on Main — two dispatches per prompt instead of one. The alternative (running `Signature.getInstance`/`initSign` on Main for a simpler flow) would violate NFR3's "cryptographic operations must execute on background threads". The warm-window `initSign` inside `onAuthenticationSucceeded` remains on the main executor exactly as before — pre-existing, and recorded rather than silently swept.

**Consequences:** Unpair initiation is reachable for the first time on a shipped build — every prior device attempt failed before the prompt, so **task #15(b)'s earlier note ("verified up to the biometric gate … cancel → pair intact") was a misread and is corrected here**, not carried forward: the gate had never been reached from that screen (the pattern is committed code, not a working-tree change). The approval-respond path shares the fixed function and is fixed with it, but it is *not* separately device-exercised — that needs a live pending request from the virtual-partner harness, and is named as a residual gap rather than implied. The completion leg (a real fingerprint on the prompt → `Deleted` → tombstone) still needs the user's finger. New pattern entry: `app/docs/engineering-patterns-reference.md` §5, "Thread affinity of UI objects called from coroutines". Companion finding worth keeping: a `suspend` UI-raiser must own its thread affinity, because the caller's dispatcher is an implementation detail of the caller.

---

**ADR \#68 Title: The Block-Mode Matrix and Rule-Level Day Scope (2026-09-20) — the Editor Could Not Express "Daily Limit Only", and Days Now Scope the Whole Rule**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented + JVM-verified + emulator-verified 2026-09-20. No plan requirement was contradicted; the change implements a gap the plan left open (the editor's mechanism composition) and records the semantics the user settled by ruling.

**Context:** User defect report: "i tried putting 120min daily usage but always on was toggled on so the block was always active yet thats not what i want and if i toggle it off i have to select time range". Two real defects, one root: the editor's `isAlwaysOn` defaulted to true and silently subordinated the daily limit (the engine's `evaluateRule` returns Block on always-on regardless of the quota), and untoggling it forced a start≠end window before save — so a plain daily limit was **unreachable from the UI entirely**, even though the engine has supported it since ADR #37 ("tracked" = enabled + non-expired + `dailyLimitMs > 0`). Secondary UX defect: the limit field was minutes-only, so "8 hours" required the user to do arithmetic. The user then ruled the composition semantics by decision ("run all the scenarios … the scenarios are finite"): three **independent** switches, at least one required, nothing pre-selected; and **days scope the whole rule** — a rule's `activeDaysOfWeek` bounds always-on, the window, and the quota alike, not the window alone.

**Decision:**

1. **The editor is three independent switches** (RuleEditor.kt): *Block all day* (`isAlwaysOn`), *On a schedule* (window), *Limit daily use* (hours + minutes, app-only). `validateBlockMode` rejects the empty selection (`ModeError.NoneSelected`), an equal start/end pair while the window is live (`SameTime`), and a zero/blank limit while the limit switch is on (`LimitInvalid`) — and accepts each mechanism alone and window+limit together. `composeRuleFields` stores exactly what the switches say: always-on saves `isAlwaysOn=true` with window/limit **zeroed** (the mechanisms it overrides are not stored — the row must not claim what the engine will not do), window+limit store both under one day scope. Defaults are all-off for a new rule; an existing rule's switches derive from its stored fields. Legacy rows that carry always-on + a limit keep their stored shape and stay engine-equivalent to always-on (A+Q ≡ A), which the matrix below pins rather than silently normalizing.

2. **The limit input is Hours + Minutes**, parsed by `parseLimitTotalMinutes` into one total (blank/zero/non-numeric = no limit), with a live `= blocked after 8h a day` preview. The old minutes-only field is gone.

3. **Days scope the whole rule** (user decision 2026-09-20). `BlockDecisionEngine.evaluateRule` computes `active` from the rule's days in the rule's timezone and gates **all three** mechanisms on it; `UsageAccountingEngine.processUsageObservation` gained a day gate as step (b2) — after rollover, before accumulate — so an inactive day accrues nothing, warns nothing, blocks nothing, and can never eat into an active day's budget (a stale row still rolls over). Both enforcement paths' `isLimitTracked` (Path A accessibility, Path B FGS) now requires `dayScopeCoversToday(rule)` alongside `dailyLimitMs > 0`, so an inactive day produces no `observe` line at all. `"1111111"` keeps every pre-existing row behavior byte-identical (proved by the unchanged 728-test baseline before the new tests landed). Day evaluation zones differ deliberately and are recorded: the decision engine judges days off the caller-supplied `nowEpochMillis` in the rule's zone (reproducible for tests/dashboards), the accounting/services judge "today" in the rule's zone via `dayScopeCoversToday`, and the tracker's own day anchor stays device-local (Decision 20).

4. **The editor's day control moved from the window block to a rule-level "Active on" control** (DayChips, presets Every day / Weekdays / Weekends + per-day), and `scheduleSummary` prints the day scope **once** per row, only when it narrows the rule — "Always on · weekends", "8h daily limit · weekdays", "05:00–12:00 · 2h daily limit · weekends". A limit beside always-on is never claimed (it is inert under this ADR's semantics).
