**Deviations from the reference, stated rather than silently taken:** (a) **one card, not a bare list** — the reference puts rows directly on the background, but the app has one card convention and Settings was already using it; a bare list here would make Settings the only screen in the app whose sections are not surfaces. (b) **No back-arrow header** — the reference's subpages carry one, but Settings is a tab root reached from the nav bar, and every other tab root (Home, Rules, Recovery, Streaks, Partner) renders a header without a back affordance; adding one would imply a route out that does not exist. (c) The reference's grouped sub-headers were dropped: with eight rows in one card, the dividers carry the grouping, and the section headers had already been deleted from `strings.xml` for exactly this reason.

**Trade-off Accepted:** The index is one tap away from the six rows that used to be visible on arrival. This is the same bargain ADR #77–#79 made and it is the same answer: the six rows are *actions and warnings*, not glanceable state — the index states the verdict ("everything is on" / "N need attention") in words, so a user who does not open the subpage is still told the thing that matters.

**Consequences:** Settings reads as an index rather than a scroll, and every control on it is now named for a screen reader. The Protection summary is the screen's one live claim, and it is backed by a test instead of a `remember`ed expression. What is **not** covered: the pane routing is string-keyed `remember` state with no Compose UI-test harness on this screen (the same ceiling ADR #77–#79 recorded), so the device pass is its only verification; and the About screen's disclaimer is a string-resource assertion at review time, not a test. Device verification (X6532, 360dp COMPACT, wireless adb): the index in **both themes**, the Protection subpage, the Coaching plan editor (add → item overflow menu with Move up/Move down correctly disabled for a single item and Delete enabled → delete → confirm), the Risk windows editor (dialog), and About. The two recovery panes — Coaching plan and Risk windows — both returned **0 bytes** to `screencap`, which is ADR #80's INV-11 fix confirmed on device from the route that was previously exposed.

---

**ADR \#83 Title: The Rules Sweep — Every Switch Names Its Own Block, the Editor Takes the Shared Header, and a Rule Row Shows the App's Real Icon (2026-09-23)**

**Status:** Accepted; user-approved 2026-09-23 ("All 7 findings + real icons", "Yes, use BackRow"), built, JVM suite green (**1052 / 0 / 0 / 0** across 136 classes — the +1 class is the new guard), device-verified on the X6532 at 360dp COMPACT.

**Context:** The Rules sweep of the UI/UX audit (finding #25 was filed against this screen and left open through the Settings pass). The audit's finding was narrow — six switches announcing identical words — and the sweep found that the same defect class existed one file over, worse, plus five unrelated defects.

The reference pick was `9edc905b` (a transaction list: section header → rows sharing one surface → row = leading entity mark · title + muted subtitle · right-aligned trailing value). Rules already matched that shape on every element but the leading mark, which was a single generic `Icons.Default.Apps` glyph for every app row. Four reference structures were refused with reasons recorded in the session plan: the date-group headers (Rules already groups by target type; a second grouping axis has no data behind it), the *Show all* pill (nothing overflows), removing the row dividers (the reference's rows carry no control; Rules rows carry a trailing switch and need the boundary), and the 3-up action row (two target types, not three).

**Decision:**

1. **Each row's switch names its own block.** `rules_pause`/`rules_resume` become `%1$s`-parameterised and the row passes its label, so the list accounts for itself: *"Pause Instagram"*, *"Pause a.192.0.2.100.nip.io"*. The old strings announced *"Pause this block"* six times over. This is finding #25's Rules half (task #18).
2. **The editor's three mechanism switches are named from their title.** They had **no** accessible name at all — worse than the list's defect, because silence reads as nothing to say rather than as a repeated sentence. This is the same class ADR #82 fixed on Settings.
3. **The editor's header becomes the shared `BackRow`.** It was a bare `TextButton("Back")` next to a `titleLarge`, while `BackRow`'s own KDoc promises *"the way out is the same shape on every screen."* Recovery, Partner, Streaks, Protection and About all use it; the Rules editor was the only screen contradicting the shared component's contract. `RuleEditorScreen` now has no bespoke header.
4. **The row states what tapping it does.** The whole row is `clickable(onClick = onEdit)` and announced as clickable without ever saying what a click would do; it now carries `onClickLabel`.
5. **The add buttons are labelled with actions, not nouns.** *"App"* and *"Website address"* became *"Add app"* / *"Add website"*. They could **not** be fixed by editing the existing strings: `editor_target_app` / `editor_target_website` are shared with the editor's target-type picker (`RuleEditor.kt`), where they name a *type* and must stay nouns — the sweep adds `rules_add_app` / `rules_add_website` rather than corrupting the picker.
6. **The failed-write banner says what its dismiss button does.** It read *"not saved"* alongside a button labelled **"Cancel"** (the editor's `rules_cancel`, reused) — which reads as cancelling an operation that has already failed. It now says *"Dismiss"*.
7. **A rule row shows the app's own icon.** The leading mark now comes from the installed package via the same `PackageManager.getApplicationIcon(...).toBitmap(px, px).asImageBitmap()` technique the app picker already used — so this is a generic glyph deleted in favour of machinery that existed three files over, not new machinery. Uninstalled packages and website rules fall back to the glyph (globe for websites). The shared composable is `AppMark` in `ui/AppLabels.kt`, beside `rememberAppLabel`, because "how a blocked target presents itself" is one responsibility; `AppPicker`'s private duplicate is deleted. `APP_MARK_SIZE` is the single source for the mark's 36dp and the divider's text inset, so the two cannot drift apart.
8. **A source-scanning guard makes the switch defect unrepeatable.** `NamedSwitchTest` fails the suite if any `Switch(`/`Checkbox(`/`RadioButton(` in `ui/` renders without a `contentDescription` in its own argument list, with one carved-out exception: `onClick = null` marks a control presentational (the row above owns the tap and carries the label). The rule is mechanical because the defect is invisible to sighted testing — the screen looks and behaves correctly the whole time. `BreathingPacer`'s unnamed switch was fixed in the same pass; leaving it would have shipped a failing guard, and deferring a one-line accessibility fix to a later sweep would have been a dangling thread by choice.

**Deviations from the reference, stated rather than silently taken:** (a) **Desert Dawn, not the reference's near-black** — row grouping keeps the app-wide `surfaceContainerLow` + `shapes.medium` card. (b) **The reference's right-aligned value maps to the existing `StatePill`**, not to a number; the trailing element on a rule row is a control. (c) **The reference's marks are brand logos** (Amazon, McDonald's); ours are the device's *own* installed app icons, read from the PackageManager — the same species of mark without reproducing anyone's brand asset. (d) **Section headers stay `titleMedium`**: the reference's muted small header works because its rows shout, and Rules' rows are quieter than that. (e) The row is *not* hidden behind a chevron and the pill *is* tappable-to-edit — recorded here so neither gets "fixed" later.

**Trade-off Accepted:** `Modifier.semantics` on the switch means a linear screen-reader pass announces the row's title text and then the switch, so *"Block all day"* is heard twice. That is the standard Compose shape for a switch that is a sibling of its label rather than its child (which is every row in this app), it matches `RuleRow`, and the alternative — `clearAndSetSemantics` on each title — costs a per-row opt-out to save one repeated word.

**Consequences:** The Rules list and its editor account for themselves to a screen reader, the editor's way out matches every other subpage, and every app row carries its real mark. What is **not** covered, stated plainly: (i) the banner's *"Dismiss"* string could not be device-verified — triggering a failed rule write on the device needs a write that fails for a reason the UI can reach, and it is a literal string change with no branch behind it; (ii) `getApplicationIcon` is main-thread work done once per rule and is **not** cached across scroll recycling — the same ceiling `AppPicker` has always had, and a list long enough for it to matter wants a shared bitmap cache, not a bigger mark; (iii) the mark's decorative `contentDescription = null` is deliberate (the target's name is always text beside it) and `AppPicker`'s previous `contentDescription = label` was removed with it — that is a change to the picker's announcement, taken as a fix rather than a regression, since it double-announced the label that sits beside it. Device evidence (X6532, 360dp COMPACT, wireless adb): the list with real icons for `Instagram`/`Bolt`/the test fixture and globe glyphs for three website rules, dividers inset to the text, add buttons reading **Add app** / **Add website**; the a11y dump showing six distinct switch names where there had been six identical ones; the editor opening on the `BackRow` header (*"Edit block"*) with all three mechanism switches named in the dump as *"Block all day"* / *"On a schedule"* / *"Limit daily use"*.

**ADR \#84 Title: The Partner Sweep — the Hub Names the Partner, the Cards Recover Their Hierarchy, and Every Tile Carries Its Own Answer (2026-09-23)**

**Status:** Implemented and device-verified. Owner sign-off 2026-09-23: **"All 5 findings"**, **"Yes — live second line"**, and the reference choice delegated (*"pick whichever u think is the better option ui ux wise"*). JVM suite **1052 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped across 136 classes**. Verified on the X6532 (360dp COMPACT, wireless adb) in **both themes**.

**Context:** Fifth application of the owner's dense-page ruling, and the second sweep where the reference folder was read *after* the findings rather than before — the five findings came off the running device, and the reference was then picked against them.

The reference pick is **`13.14.48`** (a sports-booking app), chosen because it is the **only file in the folder built around another party**: a two-line identity header with a leading mark, cards that carry their own state pill and an inline CTA, and entity rows with a leading mark, a meta line and a trailing action. Every other candidate is fitness, finance, auth, or nav-bar chrome. **`204def2b`** (a dark routine app) was the runner-up and was refused on a code fact rather than taste: its distinctive structure is a 2-column grid of *category* cards, and the Partner model has no categories. **`13.14.07`** (a reminder app, the strongest card anatomy in the folder) was refused for the same reason the Rules sweep refused several structures: the day-strip, month switcher, segmented tabs and search field would all be invented data.

**What the device showed before any change.** The X6532 is in a paired-and-healthy state with a 4-day-stale sync, which is the hub's most informative case because `ChainStatusCard` early-returns on `HEALTHY` and `ApprovalRequestCard` renders only when a request is pending — so the hub was drawing **the sync warning and three tiles, and nothing else**. Five findings:

1. **The hub never named the partner.** The key fragment lived exclusively on the Pairing subpage. The tab labelled *Partner* never answered *"which partner?"* on screen — one tap to find out.
2. **The healthy hub rendered its own answer as blank space.** `PartnerScreen`'s KDoc states the hub's job is to answer *"does anything need me?"*; a quiet "no" was drawn as an empty region rather than as a sentence.
3. **No hierarchy — the identity, a transient warning, and the destructive unpair entry were the same card.** Eight occurrences of `surfaceContainerLow` + `shapes.medium` + 16dp in one file. The Pairing page rendered three identical blocks in a row.
4. **The loudest control served the least important action.** `Check now` was the page's only filled `Button` and rendered first, on a page about a person.
5. **Tiles carried no state, and their subpages then spent a tap saying nothing.** Activity was one 155px card reading *"0 · 0 · No shared activity"* above 950px of dead space.

**Decision:**

1. **The hub's first row is the partner's identity** (`PartnerIdentityCard`): the chain verdict as a leading mark, *"Paired with dcee1e380ead…"*, *"Since Sep 19, 2026"*.
2. **The leading mark is the chain verdict, not a portrait.** The pairing model has no partner name and no photo — the key fragment is the only identity that exists. A monogram, an avatar, or an initial would be inventing an identity the app does not have. The mark changes **glyph** with the state (`VerifiedUser` / `Sync` / `History` / `GppBad`) and carries the words as its `contentDescription`, so the verdict never depends on colour alone; the tint only reinforces it.
3. **A healthy chain still draws.** `HEALTHY` previously produced no card at all. It now produces a quiet grey disc holding the verified glyph — because *"checked and fine"* is a different statement from *"nothing to say"*, and finding 2 was exactly that conflation.
4. **One card shape with three tones replaces eight copies of the same `Surface`.** `PartnerSurface(tone)` — `CALM` (`surfaceContainerLow`), `NOTICE` (`secondaryContainer`), `ALERT` (`errorContainer`) — now backs every card on the tab, `ChainStatusCard` included. Escalation is reserved: `CALM` is the default, and the hub's sync card takes `NOTICE` only when it is actually there because something needs attention.
5. **`Check now` is emphasis-matched to its page.** Filled on the Pairing subpage, where the user went deliberately to manage sync; a `TextButton` on the hub, where it is a routine resync and the page's real subject is the partner. This is a deliberate **inversion** of `13.14.48`, whose hero-card CTA is filled precisely because *"Join Game"* is that page's primary goal.
6. **Each tile carries the datum its subpage would otherwise cost a tap to show** (`NavTile` gains an optional `status`): *Requests · "None waiting" / "N waiting"*, *Activity · "N this week"*, *Pairing · "Synced 3d ago" / "Never synced"*. Finding 5 is answered by the tile, not by hiding it — a tile that appears and disappears is a discoverability defect of its own.
7. **Activity rows gained a leading mark**, reusing the `AppMark` built in ADR #83: the app's real launcher icon when this device has it, the generic glyph when it does not (the partner's phone may block apps this one has never seen), and a `SelfImprovement` glyph for a recovery row. The label is now `weight(1f)` + ellipsized, so a long app name cannot push the trailing timestamp off the row.
8. **The approval badge keeps its whole sentence and gains a leading glyph** instead of becoming a pill. Its copy is a trust explanation; truncating it to fit a shape would trade the reason for the ornament. The trailing `HorizontalDivider` after the last request was dropped.

**Deviations from the reference, stated rather than implied:** no day-strip (Partner has no time axis), no search field (nothing to search), no `View More` link and no per-row overflow `⋮` (nothing overflows; there are no row-level actions beyond Approve/Reject), and no gradient hero (Desert Dawn has no gradient — emphasis comes from the container slots, which the codebase already used). The reference's date-group *section header + trailing link* pattern was also refused: the hub's subpages are already reached by tiles.

**Supersedes part of ADR \#78 (signed off 2026-09-22).** That ruling filed identity as *"reference material, one tile away"*, which is what produced finding 1. The correct reading of ADR #78 is narrower than how it was implemented: **the sync card** should stay quiet on the hub when healthy — that part stands untouched and the card still renders only when `syncNeedsAttention`. What was wrong was treating *the partner's identity* as reference material. The two are now separated: the hub states **who** and **is it verified**; the Pairing page carries **what the code means**, the sync mechanism, and the way out.

**Trade-off Accepted:** The hub is now longer than the lean 3-tile version and re-states the pairing date that the Pairing page also shows. Accepted because the duplication is *identity*, not *verdict* — the two surfaces cannot disagree about who the partner is, whereas a duplicated verdict could. The chain verdict itself appears exactly once per surface.

**Consequences:** The tab named "Partner" now names the partner, the verdict is legible before any tap, and the destructive entry is no longer the same visual weight as the identity. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) the remaining dead space below the tiles is unchanged and honest — a healthy paired state has exactly four things to say, and padding it with anything else would be inventing content; (ii) the chain-verdict mark's `HEALTHY` disc uses `surfaceContainerHighest`, which is a deliberately quiet tone and is the least prominent element on the hub — if it proves invisible in use, the fix is a stronger container and not a new card; (iii) `PartnerScreen.kt` is still ~1000 lines and is still one file for hub, three subpages, and six card composables; the tones and `PartnerSurface` removed the duplication that mattered, but a further split is available if it grows again; (iv) light-theme confirmation covers the hub only — the three subpages were verified in dark, and the two surfaces they share with the hub (`PartnerSurface`, `NavTile`) are the ones the light pass exercised. Device evidence (X6532, wireless adb): the hub in dark and light showing the identity row plus `Shared activity is verified` in the a11y tree, and tiles reading `None waiting` / `0 this week` / `Synced 4d ago`; the Pairing page keeping its filled `Check now` while the hub's is a text button; the Requests and Activity subpages rendering through the shared card. The first device pass caught one defect the source could not: `Nothing waiting` **ellipsized to "Nothing waiti…"** at 360dp in an 88dp text box, fixed to `None waiting` and re-verified.

---

**ADR \#85 Title: The Streaks Sweep — the Ladder Folds Into the Hero, the Label Row Stops Being Two Copies, and the Tab Fits One Screen (2026-09-23)**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented 2026-09-23, device-verified on the X6532 (dark + light). Signed off by the owner before any code changed: ladder handling **"Fold it into the hero"**, string conversion **"Composable chrome only"**. Reference pick delegated ("pick whichever u think is the better option ui ux wise" was the Partner ruling; this pass applied the same latitude). JVM **1054/0/0/0 across 137 classes** (1052 + the new 2-test guard).

**Context:** Audit findings #27 and #28 had been open since the 2026-09-22 cognitive-load pass and were re-measured live before this change, at 360dp on the X6532:

- **#28** — `StreakCards.kt` rendered `"Days free from ${state.targetLabel}"` with no `maxLines` and no `overflow`. The live habit is labelled `qqqq…` (95 characters), and the label wrapped to **four lines**: bounds `[72,274]–[608,467]`, 193px, against 41px for one line.
- **#27** — the "Had a slip?" card's title sat at `y=1289–1320`, cut exactly by the scroll viewport edge at 1320. The page's only action was not merely below the fold; it was *amputated*, which reads as broken content rather than as "scroll for more".

Two source facts reframed both findings. First, **Home's `StreakHeroCard` is a near-copy of the Streaks `StreakSummaryCard`** — same Surface, same padding, same numeral row — and Home's copy *had* the cap (`maxLines = 1`, `TextOverflow.Ellipsis`). The defect was not "someone forgot a cap"; it was that the row exists twice and one copy drifted. Second, **the milestone ladder's first row said what the hero already said**: the hero's caption read *"Next milestone: Day 7 — 4 days to go."* and the ladder's first row read *"Day 7 · 4 days away"* — same fact, different words, one card apart. The 293px `MilestoneLadderCard` was spending the page's most expensive real estate to say one thing twice.

**Decision:**

1. **The label row is one composable, not two copies.** `HabitLabelRow(text, onSwitch)` in `ui/streaks/StreakCards.kt` renders the label for both Home and Streaks, capped to one line with ellipsis in **both** of its branches (with and without the switch affordance). This is the root-cause fix for #28: capping only the Streaks copy would have left the drift mechanism in place.
2. **The milestone ladder folds into the hero as a rung strip.** `MilestoneLadderCard` is deleted. The three rungs (`MilestoneEvent.MILESTONE_DAYS`, the same constant Home's grid uses) render as three equal columns inside the hero: filled `CheckCircle` in gold for a reached rung, `Schedule` for one ahead, and the **next** rung's distance in gold — the same gold the numeral uses, and the same statement the deleted caption made, now said once. The distance is always words ("4 days away", "Reached"), never a bare glyph, so state never rests on colour alone.
3. **The result is a page that fits one screen.** Measured after the change at 360dp: the hero ends ~676, the `What to expect` row occupies 740–855, and the relapse card runs 951–1250 — **"Log a relapse" is fully above the viewport edge at 1320**, and the layout needs no scrolling. Finding #27 is closed by making the page smaller, not by moving the destructive action above live state it does not belong above.
4. **The timeline spoke stopped repeating its own header.** `WithdrawalTimelineCard` renders only beneath the spoke's `BackRow`, which already names the page "What to expect"; the card's internal title was a second copy of the same words three lines below. The card now opens with its subtitle ("Your four-week curve, week by week."), which is not a restatement — it says *which* curve and whether the live streak is still inside it. The disclaimer (Decision 12) is untouched.
5. **The Streaks copy moved to `strings.xml`.** The surface was the last swept page still built entirely from Kotlin literals — and `"What to expect"` existed as **three independent literals across two files**. Converting the composables' chrome collapses them to one resource and lets the spoke's header and the hub's link row be edited in one place. Scope stops at the composables, deliberately (see Deviations).
6. **The new guard test is the actual deliverable for #28.** `HabitLabelGuardTest` fails the suite on two conditions: (a) any mention of `R.string.streaks_days_free` / `streaks_days_free_from` whose nearest enclosing call open is not `HabitLabelRow(` — i.e. a surface rendering the label itself, which is how the cap was lost; and (b) `HabitLabelRow` failing to cap in both branches. It is enforced mechanically because the defect is **invisible until a habit name is long enough to wrap**, which no reviewer types by hand.

**Deviations, stated rather than implied:**

- **`StreaksPresentation.kt` stays pure Kotlin, and so does `StreaksViewModel.kt`.** The presentation file is the tested copy layer (`StreaksPresentationTest`), and its sentences are parameterised with plural branching — converting them would either break the tests or push a `Resources` parameter into a pure function. The ViewModel already maps failures to user-facing copy at its own layer; moving that to resources is a layering decision, not a UI sweep, and is not claimed here.
- **`13.02.04` is the reference pick**, and only its **week strip** was taken: a horizontal row of labelled units with exactly one marked current. Its greeting/avatar header has no model behind it (the app holds no user identity beyond `BrandMark`), its 2-up stat tiles belong to Home's `StatTrio`, its "See All" is a bare word where ours must name the destination, and its day-strip *data* is per-day history this app does not keep (Decision 17 — no calendar math). Also refused: `13.01.43`'s day strip for the same data reason, and `e8c8e77c`'s chip action row (our one action is the relapse card and it belongs last).
- **The relapse card's placement is unchanged.** It ends the page on purpose: `RelapseCard`'s own KDoc records that "you lost your streak" framing drives churn, and its confirmation dialog is a deliberate extra step. Moving it above live state would make relapsing the most prominent action on the tab. The finding was about the *clipping*, and the clipping is what was fixed.

**ADR #79 is superseded in part:** its rule — the hub keeps everything that changes while the user watches, and the timeline stays behind a link — is untouched, and the rungs still live on the hub with live countdowns. What does not survive is its ladder *as a card of its own*: the same three facts now render inside the hero.

**Trade-off Accepted:** The hero now carries four things (label, numeral, rung strip, best-run line) where it carried three, and a reached rung's checkmark is smaller (18dp) than the ladder's was (20dp). Accepted because the alternative — keeping a 293px card so a checkmark can be 2dp larger — is what put the page's only action off the screen. The rung strip also duplicates Home's `MilestoneGridCard` in *subject*; the two remain different in kind (a 90-day visual progress window vs. three named rungs with distances) and derive from the same `state.milestones`, so they cannot disagree.

**Consequences:** The Streaks tab is now one screen at 360dp, its action is reachable without a scroll, and neither its label nor its copy can silently drift again — the first is guarded by a test, the second by a resource. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) the strip's three columns were verified at COMPACT/360dp with a 95-character habit label and the system font scale at default; a large font scale plus a long label could still tighten the "N days away" line, and the mitigation would be `labelSmall` sizing rather than a layout change; (ii) `HabitLabelGuardTest` enforces the *call shape*, not the rendered result — it cannot catch a layout that caps the text but still leaves the row tall, which is why the device measurement (33px, from 193px) is the evidence that the fix landed; (iii) the Home hero's "Reading your streak…" skeleton was observed again during this pass and cleared within ~3s of launch, so it is the designed skeleton resolving rather than a stuck state — recorded here as the evidence the earlier candidate-defect note was waiting on, with the caveat that it is one launch's observation, not a proof. Device evidence (X6532, wireless adb, 360dp): the hub in dark and light with the label on one line, three rungs reading `Day 7 / 4 days away` (gold), `Day 30 / 27 days away`, `Day 90 / 87 days away`, and the relapse button fully visible; the picker sheet, the "Add another habit" setup card with all five duration and three severity options, and the timeline spoke all rendering from resources; the relapse confirmation reading *"Your current streak of 3 days ends and a new one starts at Day 0."* with both substitutions correct.

---

**ADR \#86 Title: The Warning Banner and the Block Walls Get One Language, and the Overlay Learns the Window Class (2026-09-27)**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented and device-verified on the X6532 (dark + light + the 800dp MEDIUM class). UI sweep task #4 — the limit-warning and block-overlay surfaces (`BlockOverlayView`'s four states: `AppBlocked`, `UrlBlocked`, `DailyLimitBlocked`, `Warning` L1/L2 — plus `BlockerActivity`, the activity-hosted block screen). §8 Step 10's model is untouched: capped top strip, generic copy, 5 s auto-dismiss, no package painted on the banner.

**Context.** Five defects, all device-caught, none visible to code review:

1. **The banner was a bare centered line** — the wall's 22sp title view re-parented into the strip on every bind, no glyph, no surface of its own.
2. **The walls had no visual anchor** in either host, while every in-app Compose surface anchors on the target's real icon (ADR #83's `AppMark`).
3. **The overlay never took the adaptive ramp.** At 800dp it rendered pixel-identical to 360dp. Root cause: `OverlayController.viewFor(displayId)` builds the view from `createDisplayContext(display)`, whose `Configuration.screenWidthDp` lags a display-size change — device-caught reading `360dp` while the display was `1600px / 320dpi`, with the display **metrics in the same read live**. (The accessibility service's own `onConfigurationChanged` did receive the change — the staleness is the display context's Configuration, not the event.)
4. **The banner died ~5 ms after attach, twice over.** Its own appearance makes the app below re-emit window-state changes; the service decided Allow on them and hid the banner — which the attach-confirm had already marked delivered, so it never retried (the user saw a flash). Separately, Chrome's omnibox content-change fired a URL-scan Allow that hid the banner 270 ms after it was shown.
5. **After any banner, a reused view's wall lost its title.** The warning path's per-part `GONE` toggles were one-way — the title stayed hidden through a later wall bind.

**Decision:**

1. **The banner is an inset surface card.** Rounded 20dp, 1dp `outlineVariant` stroke, `surface` background, 16sp copy on its own `TextView` (the wall's 22sp title is a different role; one view across both meant re-parenting on every bind), with a leading `ic_timer` glyph — **teal at level 1, error tone at level 2** (the words already say the fact; colour only reinforces). The strip itself stays transparent: a warning is advisory, the app below remains visible around the card. Walls stay opaque Sand — covered content must not be readable.
2. **Walls anchor on the target's real launcher icon in an 88dp soft-teal (`primaryContainer`) disc.** The View overlay resolves `packageManager.getApplicationIcon` at bind (fallback `ic_block` for app walls, `ic_globe` for URL walls, tinted `onPrimaryContainer`); `BlockerActivity` paints the same composition in Compose via `AppMark` — one disc language for both hosts. The disc is decorative; the title still names the target as text.
3. **The overlay joins the adaptive ramp (`ui/Adaptive.kt`).** `applyTypeScale()` runs at every bind and scales the walls' title/message/hint, the warning copy, and the disc/icon sizes. The width class is computed from **live `resources.displayMetrics`** (`widthPixels / density`), never the display context's `Configuration`. `BlockerActivity` uses `rememberTypeScaleFactor()` — window-class based, which is also what made its behaviour correct under XOS's auto-split (see Consequences).
4. **The banner owns its 5 s window against the app's own churn.** `decideApp`'s Allow branch skips the hide when the current content is that package's `Warning` (the same guard class as the daily-wall one); the URL-scan Allow branch skips the hide for the browser's own `Warning` **and** the daily wall — URL evidence is not a foreground transition. A different package's Allow still yields the banner, and the banner still follows the foreground when the user switches apps. `OverlayContent.Warning` gained `packageName` for exactly this guard; it is not painted.
5. **Every per-part visibility toggle in `bind()` is two-way.** The a11y tree and the view tests read child visibility directly, so container-level visibility alone is not enough.
6. **No entrance animation.** An alpha-0 + `post { animate }` reveal shipped invisible on the X6532 (window `HAS_DRAWN`, delivery confirmed, zero pixels painted). A 5 s transient gets a hard appearance — the honest default.

**Trade-off Accepted:** The banner card is inset 12dp from the strip edges instead of edge-to-edge — less surface, more affordance language. Level 2's glyph tone is error-coloured on a transient surface; accepted because the copy states the fact and the tone matches the level-2 language the user has already learned elsewhere.

**Consequences:** One visual language across the cover, the activity, and the banner; the overlay scales with the window class it is displayed on; two classes of self-inflicted banner death are closed, and the rebind regression is pinned by `BlockOverlayViewTest#warning_usesTheInsetCard_notTheWallColumn` (asserts the wall title's visibility on the bind *after* a banner). **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) the EXPANDED class on the overlay is unverified — the shared ramp formula is MEDIUM-verified here and EXPANDED-unverified everywhere, tracked with the sweep's final adaptive pass (task #10); (ii) the ramp's metrics-vs-Configuration choice cannot be pinned by a JVM test — under Robolectric both agree — so the device log line plus pixel measurement are its evidence; (iii) a mid-flight display resize can make XOS auto-split the running stack — observed when `wm size` changed under a live victim + `BlockerActivity` (left pane ≈225dp) and the activity correctly took the COMPACT factor for its pane, which is also evidence the window-class choice (not display-class) is right for the activity host; (iv) the disc resolves the icon at bind — a package updated while a wall is up shows the stale icon until the next bind, accepted (the title is the truth carrier).

Device evidence (X6532, wireless adb): level-1 banner dark + light + 800dp (card width 574px → 640px, ×1.125); level-2 banner dark with the error-toned glyph; banner survival — shown 13:40:10.908, Chrome WSC Allow at 11.066 and URL-scan Allow at 11.729 with **no hide logged**, visible at +1 s and +3.5 s, auto-dismiss logged at +5.0 s; daily wall dark + light + 800dp (`MyExodusOverlay: overlay typeScale widthDp=800 factor=1.125`, disc icon bbox 96px → 108px, ×1.125 exactly); wall title present after a banner→wall rebind on the reused view; four consecutive show→evict→hide cycles at ~1.06 s each (attach-confirmed `GLOBAL_ACTION_HOME`); the `AppBlocked` cover and `BlockerActivity` rendering the identical card across the handover (`START u0 …BlockerActivity` logged between two same-content frames), `BlockerActivity` verified dark, light, and at 800dp (icon face bbox 58px → 66px, ×1.125). Device state restored inside every command chain: display size/density back to physical 720×1600/320, night mode back to `yes` (its original state), usage seeds cleared.

---

**ADR \#87 Title: The Widget Becomes Four Compositions and the Store Becomes a Cache With a Proof — the Preset a Launcher Matches Is the Size It Reports, the Flagged Void Becomes a Band, and a Process That Has Not Read Yet Carries Instead of Answering (2026-09-27)**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented and device-verified on the X6532 (dark) and the AOSP emulator (light + dark). UI sweep task #1 — the widget's render variants plus the owner-reported "No streak yet" flicker. The snapshot/writer/freshness triad (ADR #40) stands; this ADR changes what a cell renders and what the writer publishes when the repository has not read yet.

**Context.** Six defects, owner-flagged or device-caught:

1. **"No streak yet" flashed over a good streak at every process start** (owner report). `habits.state` is null until the first durable read lands; the publish triggered by that transient null treated it as the answer "no habit" and wrote it over a known day count.
2. **Every process start re-opened the durable store** (owner-flagged; XOS's app freezer makes process starts frequent). The stored day count was re-read at each start even when the accumulator's 1:1 advance with `elapsedRealtime` (Decision 17) proves it cannot have crossed a day.
3. **The resized card rendered a hero over a void** (owner-flagged). `TALL_SIZE` was declared at the size the card is *drawn*; launchers match presets on the nominal AOSP grid (74dp/row) — a preset at the visual size is never matched, so the card stretched the 110dp preset instead.
4. **The 2×2 cell re-voided at the bottom**: the action was pinned to the bottom edge, and both tested launchers draw this cell taller than the preset they match (~187dp drawn for a reported ~193dp on AOSP), so the slack landed as a void.
5. **Two copy misstatements**: "Day 30.23d" (owner: *"confusing, replace the ."*) read as a decimal; the action "Open ›" promised the app's main page while landing on Recovery (owner: *"someone may assume it opens the main page… think of ux"*).
6. **The number had no destination** — the day count was not a tap target, and the milestone line (the number's next-rung story) did not exist.

Measured while fixing #3/#4, and the reason both are sizing facts and not layout taste: **the host picks the largest declared preset that fits within the cell's actual bounds.** AOSP: 2×1 → COMPACT; 2×2 → NARROW (120×110 inside its 230.86×126.5 max). XOS: 1-row → COMPACT (~312×89); 2-row → TALL (312×174, pinned min==max). **EXPANDED (260×110) composes clean every session and is unreachable on both test launchers** (AOSP caps width at 230.86dp; XOS reports either ~312×89 or 312×174).

**Decision:**

1. **A resolved repository is an answer; an unresolved one is not a reading.** `streakFieldsFor(state, resolved, existing, sample)`: state present → publish it; resolved with null state → honest clear; unresolved → carry the stored fields. The carry is what kills the flicker, and it is a carry, not a re-read — the store already holds the truth. Pinned by `ProtectionSnapshotWriterTest#unresolvedState_carriesTheStoredStreak`, `#resolvedWithoutAHabit_clearsTheStreak`, `#unresolvedFirstRun_publishesNothing`.
2. **The snapshot carries its own proof of freshness** (`StreakSample`: accumulator + verified clocks, persisted with the record). `needsStreakRefresh` decides purely from the stored pair whether the day count can still be proven current — only a day boundary or a reboot invalidates it. Fail-closed on every unreadable input (absent pair, differing boot count, elapsed regression all read as "refresh"). Consequence: a process start with a young sample performs **no** durable read; the read happens exactly when it is owed.
3. **The honest caption when freshness expires**: when the predicate fires, the stored age renders as "Checked 2h ago" in the footnote slot, ahead of the milestone line whose arithmetic the stale count would make equally stale. This closes spec §16 Q5 (visible "last confirmed" vs silent UNVERIFIED) in favour of visible, on the number the user is deciding from; the status word itself stays silent-UNVERIFIED per the original ruling.
4. **Four presets, each a whole composition** — COMPACT (1-row), NARROW (2×2), EXPANDED (4×2), TALL (resized full-height, 312×174). Routing is on the reported height/width (100dp/140dp/200dp), so any size a launcher reports picks a variant by containment; TALL is declared at the size launchers *report* because that is the number they match on. The tall composition is bands (hero + milestone band + hairline + data strip), not a headline: the band is filled with the number's own story — label, "Day 30 in 23d", and the library's `LinearProgressIndicator` (gold on `surfaceVariant`). Reference port of the gallery's `11.54.56` band card.
5. **The narrow stack centers instead of pinning**: slack from a cell drawn taller than its preset lands as margin, not as a void.
6. **Copy says what the tap does**: "Tools ›" (discreet) / "Manage urge ›" (detailed) both land on Recovery; the day count is a tap target into the Streaks tab — the same `TAB_STREAKS` extra the milestone notifications use; the milestone line reads "Day 30 in 23d" (strip) / "Day 30 in 23 days" (sentence).

**Trade-off Accepted:** the compact non-discreet card shows the status word *instead of* the day count — the measured 2×1 budget (124dp) cannot carry "Day 12 · Not protected" at readable sizes, and a clipped status word is an incomplete claim (FR3a). Status wins; the day count waits for the bigger variant.

**Consequences:** the widget shows the same number the app shows and can now say why it believes it; process starts no longer touch the encrypted store when nothing could have moved; every launcher-reported size maps to a composition built for it. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) EXPANDED is composed but has never been rendered by a host — unreachable on both test launchers; the preset stays because it is the correct 4×2 shape for a launcher that reports it, and this sentence is the honest gap; (ii) the TALL band was seen dark (X6532) and the NARROW stack light + dark (emulator); TALL in light and EXPANDED in either theme remain unrendered; (iii) the launcher does not always rebind on a resize (observed: no new options, no composition — the host re-measures held RemoteViews), so a resize can briefly leave the previous composition stretched; no app-side fix exists for that, and the change gate keeps the next publish from making it worse.

Device evidence (X6532 dark; emulator AOSP light + dark): TALL 2-row "Day 7" + "NEXT MILESTONE · Day 30 in 23d" + gold band at 7/30 + "Tools ›", with `widget publish: … carried=true days=7 refresh=false write=false` proving the carry across a process start; 1-row COMPACT sentence form "Day 30 in 23 days"; NARROW discreet ("No streak yet" + "Tools ›") and non-discreet ("Not protected" chip + "Manage urge ›") in light and dark, centered. Emulator widget prefs restored to their original `discreet_mode=true` and night mode back to light in the same command chains.

**ADR \#88 Title: The Recovery Spokes Read Like the Reference — the Sheet Opens Whole, the History Row Stamps Its Day, and the Walkthrough Walks a Rail (2026-09-27)**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented and device-verified (emulator forced to 360×640dp, then re-verified at its native 411×731dp; X6532 360×800dp for the sheet). UI sweep task #27 — the four spokes the Recovery hub routes to (`TechniqueRunner`, `SessionHistoryScreen`, `UrgeLogSheet`, plus the two sheets' anchor defect found on the way). The hub itself was swept under ADR #77; this ADR covers what the tiles land on. The log ≠ bypass ruling (ADR #61) and the privacy invariant (ADR #80) are untouched.

**Context.** Seven defects; the plan's one unconfirmed candidate (the urge log's scale) was the first thing the mandated pre-touch dump settled — wrong in mechanism, right in symptom:

1. **The History header was the app's last bare `TextButton("Back")`** (recorded with task #27; the same class ADR #83 retired elsewhere).
2. **The History rows were an uncomposed list**: title, then a same-weight line of facts, no day anchor — while the reference (`11.56.04`, Today's tasks) stamps each row against now.
3. **The urge log's 1–10 scale was a `LazyRow`** ≈472dp of chips in ~312dp of content. Dump (X6532): chips 1–6 visible, 7–10 off-screen with the scroll affordance a bare edge. Not "the top of the scale scrolls off" as guessed — the *bottom* of the scale was cut, which is worse: 1–6 read as the whole scale.
4. **Two literal `"• "` bullets** baked into strings (`TechniqueRunner`, `RecoveryScreen`) — the glyph rode inside the accessible text and the layout.
5. **SoberWalkthrough was a text list, not a walkthrough.** Five phases with no position, no progress, no done-state; the reference (`12.01.19`, order tracking) is a vertical numbered rail where the filled run is the progress.
6. **The urge sheet opened at M3's partially-expanded anchor** (50% of screen). At 360×640dp the 315dp content was clipped at the halfway line — "Log it" sat flush at the display edge with its lower half under the fold, reading as overflow. The first hypothesis (content taller than a full sheet) was wrong: dropping the sheet's height showed the whole content *fit*; the anchor was the defect. The same dump-first rule from the plan caught it.
7. **The habit picker had the same shape** one screen over: a non-scrollable `Column` in a partially-expanded sheet — with enough habits every row under the halfway line is unreachable, not merely clipped.

**Decision:**

1. **History rows follow the reference's task-row anatomy**: a 4dp rounded accent bar down the left (teal only for a finished run or a bare log — the app marks what was practised, not what was not), the title with its **day stamp trailing**, and one muted details line built from the non-null parts (`Completed/Stopped` · `Intensity N/10` · `Nm Ns`); a bare log omits outcome (its row is born COMPLETED — one row claiming both "Logged, no technique" and "Completed" would be two answers to one question).
2. **`historyTimestampLabel` is pure and injected** (epoch, now, zone, day words — locale defaulted): "Today · 3:42 PM" / "Yesterday · 3:42 PM" while the day is current, else "Sep 25 · …" / "Dec 30, 2025 · …" once the year differs. The day is spoken only while it is still current; midnight and New Year are ordinary inputs to a test, not clock races. The zone is **the device's at read time** — the row stores where it started, the list reads where the user is now.
3. **The intensity scale is a `FlowRow`**: all ten chips on two rows, no scroll, no hidden end; a second tap clears the selection (skippable at any point), and each chip carries "Intensity N of 10" — ten bare numerals read as nothing to a screen reader.
4. **`BulletLine` is one component** shared by the technique runner's if-then/value items and the plan spoke: the glyph is decoration with its own column, the text is the accessible content.
5. **SoberWalkthrough is a rail**: numbered 26dp circles (Done = filled + check, Current = filled + number, Upcoming = outline + number), connectors that fill teal behind the user and stay toneless ahead, body text under the current phase only, eyebrow "Phase N of M". The circles are `clearAndSetSemantics` — position is spoken by the eyebrow; a screen reader hears the walkthrough without five bare numerals.
6. **Both sheets pass `skipPartiallyExpanded = true`** and keep a scrolling column as insurance: the sheet opens whole (content ≈315dp fits every phone), and content that someday outgrows the screen scrolls instead of hiding. The picker's list scrolls at any habit count.

**Trade-off Accepted:** the rail's circle states are conveyed by fill and glyph — decoration, deliberately silent to a11y (the eyebrow carries position; the titles carry identity). On this surface the a11y tree is also the only verification channel that exists (FLAG_SECURE), so the circle *colors* rest on code, recorded as a gap rather than claimed.

**Consequences:** History reads as a log the user can scan by day; the urge log's whole scale is visible and each chip is named; a walkthrough user can see where they are without reading prose; and both sheets open showing everything they contained. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) `HabitPickerSheet`'s fix (same two modifiers) is code-verified only — its device sight with a long habit list is deferred to the next Home/Streaks pass, and this sentence is the honest gap; (ii) the rail's Done/Current/Upcoming circle treatment is invisible to `uiautomator` (decorative, `clearAndSetSemantics`) and uncapturable (FLAG_SECURE), so its device evidence is structural (eyebrow, node titles, body-under-current) plus code; (iii) ~~the coaching-plan editor's three add buttons and one delete button were found during this pass with **no accessible name** (`content-desc=""` on all four) — same class as findings #25/#26, recorded here, fix scheduled with the editors sweep (task #3), not silently dropped.~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-27 (task #3, ADR #89): the buttons have carried accessible names since 2026-09-22 ("Add — Steps" / "Add — If-then plans" / "Add — Values" / "Actions for “…”"); this item quoted the raw dump's *parent* clickable node while the named child sat one line away in the same XML. The defect did not exist; what exists is the dump-reading hazard already noted at the end of this ADR. Recorded here so the false finding is retired, not quietly absent.**

Also recorded, so the next audit does not re-open it: `uiautomator`'s `NAF="true"` and empty `text`/`content-desc` on Compose clickables are **raw-node artifacts** — merged `mergeDescendants` semantics (the label a TalkBack user actually hears) do not appear in the dump, exactly as they do not for stock Material buttons. The hub's three `NavTile`s dump this way and announce correctly.

**Verification:** JVM — `HistoryStampTest` (7 cases: same day + time; yesterday; 23:59 vs 00:01 boundary; "Sep 25" two days back; year-qualified "Dec 30, 2025"; New Year's Eve still "Yesterday" on Jan 1; zone honoured — 22:30 UTC is next-day 00:30 at UTC+02:00). Full unit suite **1082/0/0/0**. Device (emulator 360×640dp + native; X6532 360×800dp, a11y tree — `screencap` is 0 bytes by design on this tab): the urge sheet whole on **both** devices — all ten chips on-chip-first dump (rows at y1134/y1302), buttons at y1608–1752 with the bottom inset clear, chips 7 and 10 independently confirmed on the phone; a bare log written ("Logged, no technique · Today · 6:35 PM · Intensity 7/10"), then a full walkthrough completed (eyebrow "Phase 1..5 of 5", body under the current phase only, empty if-then state rendering) and logged with intensity skipped ("Completed · 1m 6s", no intensity line), then a second run stopped ("Stopped · 0m 10s") — History then read Urges logged **3** with buckets **1 / 1 / 1**, and the one-time no-lock banner consumed itself after the first visit; `BulletLine` rendered live from a plan item (glyph node x42–63, text x84); the walkthrough's Done path opened the log sheet and the Stop path returned to the hub with no sheet. The emulator's forced density was reset to 420 in the last chain that touched it; test-created emulator data (3 recovery rows, 1 plan item) is left on the disposable emulator deliberately.

**ADR \#89 Title: The Settings Spokes Read Like the Index — Two Dead-Grey Switches Get Their Reason, and the Editors' "Unnamed" Buttons Were Named All Along (a Raw-Dump Misread, Corrected) (2026-09-27)**

**Status:** Accepted; implemented and device-verified (X6532). UI sweep task #3 — the spokes of the Settings index: the Protection subpage, the Enhanced Mode and Strict Mode switches, About, and the two editors the index routes to (Coaching plan, Risk windows). ADR #82 designed the index; ADR #80 put the editors behind `SecureSurface()`. This ADR covers what the rows open, and corrects one of its predecessor's findings.

**Context.** The sweep opened with a claim to re-check and found two genuine defects of one class.

1. **The ADR #88 (iii) claim was a misread and is corrected here.** It recorded the coaching-plan editor's three add buttons and one delete button as having **no accessible name** (`content-desc=""` on all four). The pre-touch dump on the X6532 disproves it: the editor's add buttons announce **"Add — Steps" / "Add — If-then plans" / "Add — Values"** (named child nodes at [616,353], [616,514], [616,675] on the same dump pass), the row overflow announces **"Actions for “{item}”"**, and the strings behind them (`recovery_plan_add`, `recovery_plan_item_actions`) have shipped since 2026-09-22 — the same day `CoachingPlanScreen.kt` and `RiskWindowsScreen.kt` were last modified, a day before the #7 pass that reported the defect. What #7 quoted was the **parent** clickable node of the `IconButton` — `content-desc=""` is how a raw `uiautomator` dump renders a Compose clickable whose description sits on its child; the named child was in the same XML, one line away. The lesson is the one already recorded at the end of ADR #88: in a raw dump, read the node that carries the description before writing a finding about its absence.
2. **Enhanced Mode's switch dies silently for 24 hours.** The switch is gated by Strict Mode's cooldown (`enabled = !advancedProtection && strictMode.canDisable()`), and when the cooldown is active the row greys out with no explanation — for the full 24 hours. This is the *common* path: every user who enables Strict Mode meets it. The Strict row above it carried its countdown line; this row did not.
3. **Strict Mode's switch greys out silently under Advanced Protection.** The Enhanced row swaps in the unsupported reason ("Enhanced Mode isn't supported with Advanced Protection enabled on this device."); the Strict row kept its generic subtitle under the same condition — the mirror image of the fix ADR #82 made one row up.

**Decision:**

1. **The Enhanced row renders the same countdown line the Strict row does** — "Strict Mode active — in-app controls unlock in {h/m}" — computed from `cooldownRemainingMillis`, with the row's own 60-second ticker. The tick read deliberately stays inside each row's composable: the tick is what forces `enabled = …canDisable()` to re-evaluate, so the switch **frees itself** the moment the cooldown expires. Extracting one shared ticking child would have looked tidier and broken exactly that — the parent would not recompose when the child ticked.
2. **New string `enhanced_mode_strict_unsupported`** ("Strict Mode is locked while Advanced Protection is on this device."); the Strict row swaps its subtitle under Advanced Protection, mirroring the Enhanced row.
3. **No code change for the editors.** Their controls were already named; the record was corrected, not the code.

**Trade-off Accepted:** the Enhanced row's switch still *looks* like a switch that could be flipped — the countdown line explains the state but the control is not restyled into a status. Accepted because the lock is temporary by design (24h) and the switch becomes the live control again at expiry; a permanently restyled variant would misdescribe the state it returns to.

**Consequences:** no control in the Settings spokes now goes grey without a stated reason; the two-editors finding is retired as false rather than "fixed", and ADR #88's text carries the correction. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) the expiry leg was verified by moving the staged cooldown into the past and restarting the process — the 60-second self-freeing tick was not watched live at a real boundary (it is the same ticker the Strict row has shipped with, and the recomposition dependency is code-evident); (ii) About carries the brand mark (decorative), name, version and disclaimer and nothing else — no store links by design (direct-APK distribution, ADR #15); its Back row is the only control and is named.

**Verification:** device on the X6532 (physical; `uiautomator` a11y tree — the Settings index is capturable but the a11y tree is the same-channel check the rest of this sweep uses). Cooldown leg: staged `strict_mode.xml` (`enabled=true`, cooldown ≈23h ahead), process restarted — both switches' **toggleable nodes read `enabled=false`** (`[560,497][664,593]` and `[560,754][664,850]`), Strict `checked=true`, and the Enhanced row's new line rendered **"Strict Mode active — in-app controls unlock in 22h 59m"** with the row height visibly grown to carry it. Expiry leg: cooldown timestamp moved into the past, process restarted — both toggleable nodes `enabled=true focusable=true`, both warning lines gone, Strict still `checked=true` (the mode stays on; only the lock lifts). Restore: the prefs file **did not exist** before staging, so the restore is its deletion — Strict back to `checked=false`, Enhanced still `checked=true`, blocking status still "Limited", dumps removed, app left on Home. Editors, same device: Coaching plan — all three add buttons named as above, Back named, three empty states rendered ("Nothing added yet."); Risk windows — a real overnight window ("8:00 PM–6:00 AM · every day") with named toggle ("On for this window — …"), named overflow ("Actions for “…“"), named "Add a window"; About — Back named, "MyExodus", "Version 1.0", the self-help disclaimer. JVM full suite **1082/0/0/0** on the build carrying the switch fixes.

---

**ADR \#90 Title: The Subpage Rule's Mechanical Half Becomes a Test — One Straggler Fixed, One Was Dead Code, and the Guards That Already Stood Are Named (2026-09-27)**

**Context:** plan task #29 asks for cross-cutting guard tests over three rules introduced during the UI sweep. Two of the three already exist from earlier steps of this same session (working tree, not yet committed — "exists and runs green" is the claim, not "released"): `NoLiteralTypeSizeTest` (a literal `fontSize`/`lineHeight` outside `ui/theme/` opts a screen out of the window-size type step — ADR #75) and `ScaledTypographyTest` (every role scales, stays `sp`, 1.0f is identity — the same ADR); `SecureSurfacesTest`'s source scan with the exemption-liveness assert (every recovery screen declares its own `SecureSurface` — ADR #80). The third rule — the owner's 2026-09-22 subpage ruling ("dense pages get subpages, never a page for one trivial control") — had only its judgement half unenforceable; its mechanical half ("a pane you can enter offers the same way out") was convention-only. Enumerating `onBack:` declarations against `BackRow(` renderers found exactly two offenders: `RulesScreen.EditorUnavailable` (a bare `TextButton` with the string `editor_back`) and `ComingSoonScreen`. Writing the guard then exposed that the second offender does not exist at runtime: `AppShell` is composed only with a non-null store (`MainActivity` gates on `usable && rulesStore != null`) and all six tabs are enumerated, so `ComingSoonScreen`'s two call sites — `store == null` and the `when`'s `else` — were both dead branches.

**Decision:**

1. **`PaneBackAffordanceTest`** (JVM source scan, house style of `SecureSurfacesTest`): (a) every file under `ui/` other than `BackRow.kt` that declares an `onBack:` parameter must render `BackRow(`; (b) no file under `ui/` other than `BackRow.kt` may use `ArrowBack`, `R.string.ui_back`, or a bare `"Back"` literal. Granularity is per-file, matching the existing guard's precedent.
2. **`EditorUnavailable` renders the shared `BackRow`** with the editor's own label ("Edit block"); the string `editor_back` is deleted with it (nothing else referenced it).
3. **`ComingSoonScreen` is deleted, not polished** — the file, its `onBack` parameter, and its copy. `AppShell` takes `store: AppRulesStore` non-null, the content `when` enumerates the six tabs with no fallback, and `val contentTab = tab.coerceIn(0, Tabs.lastIndex)` covers a restored out-of-range index (the only reachable source of one). `MainActivity` reads `val openStore = rulesStore` before the composition branch because a delegated property cannot be smart-cast.
4. **Both dead surfaces are named in the guard's KDoc**, so the fix list and the deletion are recorded where the next reader of the rule will look.

**Trade-off Accepted:** per-file granularity — a future file with two panes where only one renders a `BackRow` would pass. The per-composable parse that would catch it is a brittle source-scanner for a failure mode with no known instance; stated in the test's KDoc rather than paid for in advance.

**Consequences:** the pane rule is now enforced for files that do not exist yet, which is the point of a cross-cutting guard; the enumeration it forced removed a dead screen, two dead branches and a dead string rather than adding a third placeholder. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) the design half of the subpage rule — whether a split is worth making — is judgement and stays unenforceable by any test; (ii) `NotchedNavBar`'s `LabelSize = 11.sp` still escapes `NoLiteralTypeSizeTest`: the regex keys on `fontSize`/`lineHeight` assignments, and a size hoisted into a named `val` is not one (ADR #75 accepted this hole when the measured nav-bar rule was set — the window-size step does not apply to the nav bar's own label sizing); (iii) the widget's type literals remain outside `ui/` entirely by design (per-composition sizing, ADR #87).

**Verification:** JVM full suite **1084/0/0/0** (1082 before this ADR; the two new tests included), `PaneBackAffordanceTest` run standalone green first. Device on the X6532 (physical; `uiautomator` a11y tree): the `EditorUnavailable` pane was reached deterministically — the editor was opened from the Rules list, the app was backgrounded via `am start -a android.settings.SETTINGS`, the process was killed with `run-as … kill -9 <pid>` (NOT force-stop), and the task was brought back; the pane rendered the shared `BackRow` (arrow `content-desc="Back"` at `[40,112][88,160]`, label "Edit block" at `[112,110][704,162]`, body at `[32,216][688,289]`), self-healed into the real editor once the rules Flow emitted (~2–3s — the pane is the load-window state, not a stuck one), and its back arrow landed on the Rules list. Shell leg: tab switching Home→Rules→Home with the clamped index intact; the deleted placeholder had no visible effect by construction (unreachable). Restore, same chain: an earlier cold-start used `am force-stop`, which on XOS clears `enabled_accessibility_services` — the grant was re-cycled dummy→real with the app alive (`dumpsys accessibility`: service **Bound** again), the Task-Manager-stop banner that force-stop raised was cleared through the app's own Re-enable (chip back to "Limited", its pre-existing state), 141 stale `/sdcard/*.xml` dumps from earlier sessions were removed along with this pass's own dumps, and the app was left on Home.

**ADR \#91 Title: The Shell Stops Speaking in Literals — the Guard Finds the Copy Class Alive in Six Files the Per-Page Audits Had Passed, the Wizard's Two Backs Agree, and the Once-Per-Install Screen Gets Its First Device Sight (2026-09-27)**

**Context:** the shell + onboarding sweep (session task #6; no reference pick — the deliverable is mechanical, the same standing as ADR #90). Three threads converged:

1. **The literal-copy class was assumed dead and was not.** ADR #85 stated that Streaks was "the last surface whose copy lived as Kotlin literals". A tree-wide source scan falsifies it: six files still rendered user-visible English straight from Kotlin — `MainActivity`'s `DatabaseHealthScreen` (the whole pre-shell database gate: opening/retry/upgrade/recoverable/issue/key/storage/reset/dialog copy), `HomeScreen` (the streak CTA, the blocks empty card + its CTA, the encryption-pending title/body), `ProtectionNotifications` (both channel names, the ongoing title/text, and the two `DegradedCopy` triples), `DnsSinkholeVpnService` (channel name + title + text), `StreakNotifications` (channel name + milestone title/text), and `PartnerNotifications` (three channel names, one of them passed inline to its own `post()` helper). Each earlier per-page audit had looked at *its* page and found the class absent; no pass had looked at the class itself.
2. **Tab labels doubled as the deep-link protocol.** `AppShell`'s `NavTab.label` was a Kotlin literal matched against `MainActivity.TAB_*` by value — the same string was display copy and a wire value, so translating a label would silently break `--es extra_start_tab`.
3. **The wizard's back affordances disagreed.** `SetupScreen` rendered an on-screen Back from step 1 onward but left system BACK to the platform default, which finished the activity — the two backs did different things on the same screen. And the wizard is gated once-per-install by design, so the physical test phone (never uninstalled, data never cleared) could never see it again to verify anything about it.

**Decision:**

1. **`LiteralCopyGuardTest` (JVM source scan, house style of `SecureSurfacesTest`/`PaneBackAffordanceTest`; the deliverable of this pass).** Four patterns over `src/main/java` as raw file text (so a literal on the line after `Text(` is caught): `Text("A…`, `setContentTitle|setContentText|setSubText("A…`, any `*Copy("A…` constructor, and `NotificationChannel(id, "A…` — the channel **name** specifically, because the first argument is the channel id, a protocol value (the first guard run flagged `"vpn_spike"` as a false positive; the pattern now keys on the second argument, whatever surrounds the call). `DomainUsageDebugSection.kt` is exempted by filename with a comment: it renders only under `BuildConfig.DEBUG` and never ships, so its label stays a literal rather than minting a string for copy no user sees.
2. **The six files' copy moves to `strings.xml`** (42 new resource names total across `home_*`, `db_health_*`, `encryption_pending_*`, `protection_*`, `vpn_revoked_*`, `streaks_milestone_notification_*`, `partner_channel_*`, `enhanced_mode_health_channel_name`). Notification **ids** (`"vpn_spike"`, `partner_alerts`, …) stay literals: they are protocol, not copy.
3. **Display copy and wire value separate in the shell.** A public `ui.NavKeys` object owns the six protocol values (`"Home"`, `"Rules"`, …), `MainActivity.TAB_*` delegates to it, `NavTab` carries `@StringRes labelRes` (the six existing title strings — the rendered labels are unchanged today), and the deep-link matcher keeps its `equals(ignoreCase = true)` contract. The rail's `contentDescription` and `Text` both resolve through `stringResource`, and the fit test measures the resolved labels.
4. **`SetupScreen` gains `BackHandler(enabled = index > 0) { index-- }`** — system back is now the on-screen Back's twin; at step 0 the platform default stands (back leaves the app, as the absent on-screen Back implies).
5. **`SetupHarnessActivity` (debug variant only, registered exported in `src/debug/AndroidManifest.xml`) renders the production `SetupScreen` against the real `EnhancedModeHealthMonitor`**, mirroring the four existing debug harnesses. It exists because of the once-per-install gate: without it, the wizard would be permanently unverifiable on the never-uninstalled test phone.
6. **Two refusals, recorded rather than silently dropped.** (a) The suspected "unresolved-state defect" (a not-yet-read `checks == null` offering the wrong action while status is unknown) is unreachable: the gate needs `!setupSeen` **and** a genuinely missing permission, which only co-occur on a fresh install where "not granted" is the truth; the monitor's `init { refresh() }` populates `enabled` at process start and one IO read resolves `checks`. No change made. (b) No step-overview rail in the wizard: a done/current/upcoming triad would lie about skipped steps (every step is skippable), a five-name row crowds 360dp the way the nav bar's own six do (task #11's problem, not to be duplicated), and "Step N of 5" plus the card title already name the position.

**Trade-off Accepted:** the guard is syntactic. A literal routed through a named `val` (`val t = "Hi"; Text(t)`) escapes it, and the pattern requires an ASCII letter after the quote so glyph/format literals (`"•"`, `"${n}/${m}"`) deliberately never match. Both are stated in the test's KDoc; the parse that would close the first hole is a brittle scanner for a failure mode that review also catches, because a hoisted literal still has to be read once.

**Consequences:** the literal-copy class is now enforced for files that do not exist yet — the same argument as `PaneBackAffordanceTest` — and the enforcement immediately found six files where four consecutive per-page audits had each concluded the class was gone, which is exactly the evidence for why per-page review did not suffice. Deep links can no longer be broken by translation. The wizard's backs agree, and its future changes have a device-checkable surface. **V1 doc staleness corrected in the same pass:** the Settings bullet's "display over other apps (Transsion-only, 'not needed' elsewhere)" described the accessibility-overlay path; the FGS Path B fallback that shipped later is bare `addView` + `TYPE_APPLICATION_OVERLAY` on every device, so the permission is required on all OEMs and both the Settings row and the wizard's overlay step already say so. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) the six extracted notifications carry no device sight in this pass beyond the wizard/shell legs — their strings are compile-verified and unit-guarded, but no notification was watched rendering on the phone this pass (task #14, the milestone-notification bug, will be their device leg); (ii) `NoLiteralTypeSizeTest`'s known hole (`NotchedNavBar`'s hoisted `LabelSize`) is untouched by this ADR — task #11 owns the nav bar; (iii) the harness is debug-variant only by design, so the release build's wizard is covered by the same composable plus the gate code, not by the harness itself.

**Verification:** JVM — guard run standalone green after the fix round, then full suite **1085/0/0/0** (1084 before this ADR; `LiteralCopyGuardTest` is the +1). Device, X6532 (physical; `uiautomator` a11y tree — the wizard and shell are not FLAG_SECURE surfaces): `install -r` (app data preserved), then the harness walked end to end. Step 1 rendered "Set up protection / Step 1 of 5 / Usage access / Granted / Next" with **no** Back at step 0 and **no** Skip when granted (both correct by the step's own conditions); step 2 "What MyExodus sees" with Back + Continue and no Skip (disclosure is the one unskippable step); steps 3–5 ("Accessibility service", "Notifications", "Display over other apps") each "Granted" on the fully-configured phone — **system BACK from step 2 landed on step 1 with the harness still resumed** (`topResumedActivity` = `SetupHarnessActivity`, the new `BackHandler`); system BACK at step 0 finished the harness (platform default, `topResumedActivity` = the under-app); the on-screen Back tap also landed on step 1; Done on step 5 finished the harness. `dumpsys accessibility` after the whole walk: the blocker service still **Bound** (the install did not disturb the grant). Shell leg: all six tab labels render from resources on Home; `am start … --es extra_start_tab Recovery` (delivered to the top-most instance) switched to the Recovery hub — the protocol values survive the `NavKeys` refactor. Restore, same chain: this pass's `/sdcard/*.xml` dumps removed (`ls` confirms none remain), app left on Home.

**ADR \#92 Title: The Nav Bar's Icons Catch Up With Its Height — 24→28dp for the Ring the Bubble Was Always Sized For, a Refusal of a Bigger Bubble, and the Overdue ADR #74 Revision Note (2026-09-27)**

**Context:** task #11 ("larger icons + labels, no crowding"), the last of the sweep's per-page passes. Measured live on the X6532 before any code changed (360dp, fontScale 1.0, dark): item pitch **105.5px = 52.7dp**; the widest label, "Recovery" in the guard's active (semibold 11sp, zero-tracking) style, renders **97px = 48.5dp** — so all six labels clear the 4dp guard by **4.2dp**. Icons were 24dp in the 36dp bubble: a 6dp ring, in a bar that has grown taller than ADR #74's shipped geometry, so the icon floated in a disc with more air than Material ever gives it. The label half of the ask is measured shut: at 12sp "Recovery" measures ≈52.9dp against the 52.7dp pitch, which flips the bar to selected-only labels; buying the width back would take dropping the semibold-active treatment *and* shaving the slab margin — a treatment change the owner declined in the sign-off question (2026-09-27: chose "Icons only → 28dp"). Growing the bubble instead is refused by ADR #74's own arithmetic: radius 18→21dp pushes `notchSpan` 24.2→27.2dp, hence the end inset to 17.4dp and pitch down to 51.5dp — the six labels fall off the bar to pay for it. One debt is coincident: **ADR #74's text no longer matched the code it governs** — it declares a 70dp slab, 12dp margins and 10sp labels; today's file reads 78dp, 8dp and 11sp (the user's 2026-09-27 "the nav labels read too small" was answered in code, and later passes moved the geometry). No revision marker was ever recorded, which the doc's own change-control rule forbids; this ADR supplies it.

**Decision:**

1. **`IconSize` 24→28dp — the one constant that changes.** 28dp in the 36dp bubble is a 4dp ring, the same proportion Material itself uses (24dp icon in its 32dp indicator). 30dp+ starts crowding the disc edge; the bar's other constants (heights, corners, notch, lift, motion) do not move.
2. **Labels stay 11sp, zero tracking, semibold-active, all six on** — they are at the measured ceiling, not under-tuned; the measured fallback (`pitch − widest ≥ 4dp`) is the designed degradation on narrow bars or large font scales, untouched.
3. **ADR #74 carries the revision markers inline** (decisions 1 and 3 there) — slab 78dp, side margins 8dp, labels 11sp, as of 2026-09-27. The narrower margin is what pays for the extra label point: at ADR #74's 336dp bar the same 11sp pair would leave 2.9dp, below the guard.
4. **The bar keeps its own window-class ramp** (`rememberTypeScaleFactor` scales label, geometry and icon together, so a MEDIUM/EXPANDED window enlarges the whole bar as one unit); the label size stays a hoisted constant, which `NoLiteralTypeSizeTest` reads as a hole in ADR #90 (ii) but is by design: the rule targets screens opting out of the theme step, and the bar's type is a measured, bar-local rule.
5. **The expanded-width `NavigationRail` is out of scope** — M3 defaults, full labels, room to spare.

**Trade-off Accepted:** the visual delta is one step of icon weight (24→28dp); the intended gain is the ring, where 6dp of air becomes 4dp and the icon stops floating in its disc. The bubble itself stays 36dp — the reference's 0.73 bubble-to-bar ratio remains unaffordable at six always-on labels, which is ADR #74's trade restated, not a new one.

**Consequences:** the bar's icon-to-container proportion now matches Material's own at the same time as it keeps the notched-bubble signature; six labels still show at 360dp with the same 4.2dp of headroom; nothing about tab identity, order, deep links, motion or semantics changes. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) no light-theme capture this pass — the change is tint-independent geometry and the device sat in night mode (the user's current setting); ADR #74's both-theme evidence stands for the color mapping, but the 28dp ring in light is code-verified only; (ii) the vertical air above the labels (the 78dp slab exists so the label row clears the icons at `LabelBottomPadding 13dp`) is untouched — the bar remains proportionally taller than the reference, which is the ADR #74 trade; (iii) task #10 will re-check the bar's scale ramp with the rest of the app.

**Verification:** JVM full suite **1085/0/0/0** (no test change; the guard set is untouched). Device, X6532 (physical, dark theme — the device's current setting): `install -r`, app relaunched, then both extreme tabs captured by screencap and reviewed as cropped close-ups. Settings (rightmost, the notch's worst corner case): six icons at even pitch, the bubble overhanging the top-right with its notch fully inside the straight top edge, all six labels rendered, active "Settings" semibold; Home (leftmost): same on the left. The a11y dump reconfirmed the six label nodes after the change. This pass's `/sdcard` dumps removed in the same chain; app left on Home.

**ADR \#93 Title: Covering Is Not Stopping — Every Block Now Closes the App, the Wall Waits for the User's Own Close, and the Plan's Touch-Modal Clause Is Corrected by Measurement (2026-09-27)**

**Context:** two user reports against the daily-limit wall, both reproduced on the X6532 before any code changed:

1. *"audio is still playing well logicaly just block the app and close it."* The wall renders correctly and the app is evicted to Home — but covering is not stopping. Device evidence: with the wall up for Instagram, `dumpsys activity activities` still read `topResumedActivity=com.instagram.android` and `dumpsys audio` showed a focus-stack entry with `GAIN_TRANSIENT_EXCLUSIVE, notified: true` — the app sat resumed and audible *under* the wall.
2. *"if i press close button u added it still opens to instagram the blocks again but somehow exists itself."* The Close action (added earlier the same day) lifted the wall with the blocked app still resumed behind it, handing the user a flash of the app before the next tick re-blocked it and the app exited.

A third defect surfaced during the same work: the *retirement path*. `evictBehindDailyWall` was gated on the window-state-change event passing the deferred re-check — and on XOS, while our focusable wall covers an app, the app's WSC arrives with a window id that is not in `getWindows()`, so the re-check logged `deferred WSC dropped … window=false` and no re-eviction ever ran. Re-entry under a live wall therefore did nothing.

**Decision:**

1. **Blocking now closes the app (user ruling, FR2 completed).** `closeBlockedApp(packageName)` = `performGlobalAction(GLOBAL_ACTION_HOME)` then `ActivityManager.killBackgroundProcesses(packageName)`, with one retry at `BLOCK_CLOSE_RETRY_MS = 700` because the kill only lands once the HOME transition has actually backgrounded the target. It runs from the wall's own attach callback, from the app/URL cover's attach callback, and from the pre-window re-eviction below — the same three seams, one helper. `KILL_BACKGROUND_PROCESSES` is a normal permission granted at install (manifest comment records the ruling).
2. **The wall persists until the user's own Close — and only the host that can evict carries that Close.** `OverlayController.dismissWall` hides a shown `DailyLimitBlocked` only; `canEvict = !applicationOverlayHost` decides whether `BlockOverlayView` renders the Close action (Path A's a11y host: yes; Path B's bare-addView host: no — an in-place dismiss there would hand the app straight back, so its wall keeps following the foreground instead, and Path B's half of the close is `killWalledProcess` invoked once the user's own Home press has backgrounded the app). Close therefore lands on the launcher with the app already gone — no flash, no re-block loop.
3. **Re-eviction moved in front of the window gate.** `reevictIfWalled(packageName, displayId)` runs on `TYPE_WINDOW_STATE_CHANGED` *before* normalization and the deferred-window check: if the live wall's package is the one coming forward, `closeBlockedApp` runs again and the event returns early. This is the fix for the XOS dropped-WSC evidence above; re-entry under a wall now re-evicts deterministically rather than depending on a window list that this device does not populate for the covered app.
4. **Every card is `FLAG_NOT_TOUCH_MODAL` (plan §5 Decision 1 and §8 Step 4 corrected).** The plan required the block overlay to be touch-modal (`FLAG_NOT_TOUCH_MODAL` *not* set). Measured on the X6532, that is wrong for this app: XOS sized the touch-modal window's input region to 3x the display, so the physical Home/Back/Recents bar was swallowed whole with no dismiss affordance on the wall (the user's own report: "i cant press my phones tab, home or back button to dismiss the block screen"). Not-touch-modal keeps touches inside the card's bounds with the card and hands the bar strips back to SystemUI; with the wall's bar-free `displayBounds()` this is what makes the bars work over a live wall — re-verified live: the wall reads `frame=[0,80,720,1520]`, `touchableRegion=SkRegion((0,80,720,1520))`, NavigationBar0 sits at `[1520,1600]` outside it, a swipe-down opened the NotificationShade and a tap on Home dismissed it. The app/URL cover carries the same flag; the warning banner's flags are unchanged.
5. **The 2026-09-15 "hide 1s after eviction" note is superseded, not deleted by accident:** the wall no longer hides itself after eviction (`BLOCK_EVICT_HIDE_MS` and its gen-guarded job are gone) — hiding is the user's Close, and Path B's foreground-following model. The plan note is corrected in place under §8 Step 4.
