**Trade-off Accepted:** `killBackgroundProcesses` is the strongest close a third-party app can perform, and it is a *background* kill — an app holding foreground services survives it (Instagram: `HeroKeepAliveService`, `IgBgFetchTaskLifeService`, `TaskLifeDetectingService`, `startRequested=true`; `pidof` still returned the process after the kill). What the user asked for is nonetheless achieved and device-verified: nothing is resumed (top = launcher), nothing is audible (audio focus stack empty), and the next foreground attempt re-runs the close. The alternative — `am force-stop` semantics — is not available to a third-party app without device-owner privileges; recorded as the ceiling rather than claimed as a fix.

**Consequences:** a blocked app is now closed on every path that can close it, so the "still playing under the wall" class is dead by construction rather than by timing; the wall's persistence plus the Close action means the user always has exactly one affordance, and it always lands somewhere safe. The touch-modal correction removes the last place where the plan's text and device reality disagreed about input handling. **What is not covered, stated plainly:** (i) Path B's wall still cannot evict (no accessibility token, and a background Activity launch is the reliability risk Decision 16 exists to avoid) — its close happens on the user's own Home press, which is the strongest thing that path can do; (ii) the kill is a no-op for service-holding apps, as above; (iii) a `BAL`-blocked or OEM-frozen kill on another skin is untested — the X6532 is the only physical device in the loop.

**Verification:** JVM full suite **1093/0/0/0** (1085 before this ADR; the eight additions include `OverlayControllerTest.wall_dismissesFromItsOwnCloseAction_onTheEvictingHostOnly`, the flipped `FLAG_NOT_TOUCH_MODAL` assertions in `flagsForContent_perModel`, and `BlockOverlayViewTest.dailyWall_closeAction_dismissesAndNeverSurvivesARebind` / `dailyLimitBlocked_withoutAnEvictingHost_hasNoActions_showsTheHintOnly`). Device, X6532 (physical): the wall rendered and persisted beyond 40 s; the physical Home/Back/Recents bar stayed live over it (NotificationShade swipe + Home tap, both above); Instagram re-launched under the live wall logged `block close pkg=com.instagram.android evicted=true` and landed on the launcher; the Close tap produced 0 MyExodus windows, top = launcher, no app flash; `dumpsys audio` focus stack empty and no resumed app after the close. **Path B leg, same device, same day** (the a11y component removed from `enabled_accessibility_services` so Path B held authority — XOS zeroed the companion `accessibility_enabled` flag on its own when the list went invalid; both restored afterward through the dummy→real re-grant cycle): with a 60 s Instagram limit seeded, the bare-addView wall rendered full-screen over the still-resumed Instagram (`topResumedActivity=com.instagram.android`, screencap reviewed), carrying **no** Close action and a live nav bar; one `KEYCODE_HOME` — the user's own close — produced `MyExodusOverlay: hidden display=0` followed immediately by `MyExodusBlockerFgs: wall close pkg=com.instagram.android`, `topResumedActivity` back to the launcher, no MyExodus overlay window left, and `dumpsys audio` focus stack empty. Instagram's `pidof` survived (the service-holding ceiling above, identical on both paths). Device state restored in the same chain: tracker back to `used=731716, limit=7200000, w1/w2=NOT_DUE, blocked=false`, rule back to `limit=7200000 days=1111111 tz=Africa/Nairobi`, a11y component rebound with `accessibility_enabled=1`, app left on Home.

**ADR \#94 Title: The Journal Behind the Number — Schema v11 Lands, the Outage It Reports Is One the User Can Actually Fix, and a 3.8 GB Phone Says 225 Bytes a Row (2026-09-28)**

**Context:** the daily-limit undercount complaint class ("Instagram 3h42m, our number lower, the warning late") has one shared root: under schema v10 the only usage record was a per-day *scalar* on `daily_usage_tracker`, rewritten in place. There is no history, no interval, no way to audit a disagreement with the platform's own number, no record of when observation was dead — a scalar can only be believed or doubted. The usage-ledger plan (`app/plans/usage-ledger-implementation-plan.md.html`) replaces it with a durable append-only journal (§8), a projection that is a *derived view* of that journal (§8.3), gap diagnostics with a user-facing repair path (§8.7), and retention (§8.8). Steps 1–15 are implemented; this ADR is the close-out, and it carries the §11 numbers measured on the low-end device rather than deferring the DoD's performance line.

**Decision:**

1. **Schema v11 — the journal is real.** `usage_ledger` (unique `event_id`; covering `(package_name, day_key, kind, credited_ms)`; a `wall_clock_ts_ms` index for the age sweep) with kinds ACCOUNTING / HEARTBEAT / GAP_DETECTED / RECONCILIATION / CORRECTION / MIGRATION_CARRYOVER. Identity is deterministic — truncated SHA-256 over `(source, package, elapsedStart, elapsedEnd, bootCount)` — so a retried delivery (WorkManager retry, callback replay, service restart) reproduces the same id and the unique index turns it into a no-op, not a double credit; a *different* payload behind an existing id is a collision: rejected, logged, reported as nonFatal, never silently dropped. Corrections are keyed on `(targetEventId, instant)` so two identical corrections at different instants both land (re-keying on the instant is what makes redelivery of one correction idempotent without collapsing two real corrections). `Migration(10, 11)` uses plain DDL (AD-11 — an unexpected pre-existing table fails the migration loudly rather than being masked by IF NOT EXISTS — a deliberate departure from the file's other migrations, which AD-11 overrides), seeds carryover only for tracker rows whose `(local_date_anchor, timezone_id_at_anchor)` match the migration's own day+zone (§8.10, AD-12: a stale scalar is *yesterday's* value that no write reset — carrying it forward would hand today a budget it never used; those seed 0 and leave a local audit note of the raw legacy value), with deterministic `migration_seed:<pkg>` ids. **Operational note recorded for future device work:** the `od -j 58` user_version inspection recipe does not work on device — SQLCipher encrypts the whole file, header included; version inspection must go through a debug read or a pulled-and-decrypted copy.

2. **One entry point, `UsageLedgerRepository`.** In-transaction `append` (the caller owns the transaction — the journal row and the projection update commit or roll back together, §8.3), `appendStandalone` for writers with no companion projection update (heartbeats, gap rows, reconciliation), `correct` (AD-17: compensating rows, the referenced row is never touched), `dayTotal` (§8.13 audit read), `rebuildProjection` (§8.3 authoritative recovery — recomputes each current tracker row from the journal and re-derives `blockedToday` against the rule's limit), `prune` (§8.8). The day split happens at ingestion: an interval crossing local midnight becomes two rows with frozen `day_key`s, and the append outcome returns the *closing-day share* so the caller credits the projection with exactly what the journal accepted — the projection can never drift from the journal by attributing a midnight-crossing interval to the wrong day, and a zero-delta tick is never journaled (it is not an observation). The old direct scalar write path is gone.

3. **The temporal baseline is the journal itself** (§8.4/AD-15): the gap detector's last-seen frame is read from the latest row its own source wrote (`latestForSource`) — no separate baseline store exists, so the baseline can never disagree with the ledger by construction. §16's repository-fact open questions were answered from the actual repo at Step 1 (task record): cadence, WAL (already WAL: `JournalMode.WRITE_AHEAD_LOGGING` is set explicitly), SQLCipher/Room integration (Room 3 driver), crash-reporting content (counts/labels only, no ledger content or query text), and the v10 scalar's anchor fields — none required a new store.

4. **Gap diagnostics and escalation use the confirmed cadence.** Confirmed from the repo: 1.5 s poll tick, usage accounting every 30 s default / 5 s tight near a limit, heartbeat journal row every 30 s (20 ticks). Implemented threshold `MEASUREMENT_GAP_THRESHOLD_MS = 120_000` — the plan's initial "cadence × 3" default was revised upward after on-device measurement *as the plan anticipated*: at the real 30 s cadence with XOS tick jitter, 120 s is the first threshold that never fires on healthy operation; `ESCALATION_THRESHOLD_MS = 5 min` gates prompt eligibility on top of §8.7's corroboration (boot-count unchanged, no recoverable UsageStats activity). **Zero-length reboot-boundary convention:** a reboot writes a GAP_DETECTED row with a 0 ms span carrying the frame reset; `AccountingDiagnostics.isMeasuredOutage` excludes it from D1's count and largest-window — a reboot is not an interruption to prompt anyone about. AD-13 is honored in copy, logs, and code: labels are prompt-eligibility states only; no string anywhere asserts an OEM or Android cause.

5. **D1's report is one the user can fix.** The outage summary is derived from journal rows (last 24 h): count + largest measured window. The Home banner renders the report *even when there is no offer* ("a report with no offer is still worth showing" — the user should know tracking was interrupted regardless), while the **notification is the offer surface**: it posts only while a prompt route exists (a notice the user can act on, cancelled when the route is gone), and the Settings → Protection subpage carries the Fix row (deep link with `EXTRA_START_TAB=Settings`). Gating: `promptEligible` (recorded dead window ≤ 24 h fresh; 24 h prompt cooldown; 7 d decline cooldown; accepting the offer consumes eligibility) × `BatteryPromptGate.route(alreadyExempt)` → null / DIRECT_REQUEST / SETTINGS_GUIDANCE. `DIRECT_REQUEST_PERMITTED = true` — §16's open question 5 answered by the distribution ruling (ADR #15: direct APK, no Play listing; the policy-eligibility *check* is the gate, not a channel guess). **Device-caught defect fixed this session:** the `home_outage_body` plural's `quantity="one"` item read `%1$s` (the count argument) while every other item read `%2$s` (the duration) — the banner rendered "for up to 1 at one stretch" on a real device. Fixed to `%2$s` with a trap comment on the string; re-verified on the X6532 ("…for up to 9m at one stretch").

6. **§8.11 enforcement under uncertainty, exactly as specified.** Enforcement reads only the committed projection; an unresolved gap is never estimated and never backdated (AD-18/R12). Reconciliation credits land at completion, clipped to today's local midnight (`UsageGapRepair.repairWindow` start = `max(exit, localMidnight)`) — a credit that completes today can never hand today a budget that yesterday's usage should have consumed, and a gap spanning midnight credits each day only its own share. Both recovery paths (service-start process-death repair driven by `getHistoricalProcessExitReasons`; heartbeat `diagnosticsPass`) credit only windows no journal row already covers (`uncoveredMs`, interval-overlap dedupe per §8.9) and log their arithmetic. Consequence for the original complaint: any disagreement with the platform's number is now auditable row by row (§8.13 `dayTotal` plus the per-row journal) — the tool the open device bug (task #13) needs.

7. **AD-16 deviation, deliberate and recorded.** The plan's AD-16 dropped reconciliation watermarking/overlap logic as disproportionate for a single accounting source. The shipped design keeps both, because AD-16's premise is about *cross-source* overlap while the load-bearing case here is a *same-source* re-read: the exit-reason recovery path re-reads `getHistoricalProcessExitReasons` on every service start, which inherently re-offers windows already credited. `latestExitAfter(exits, watermark)` filters by the ACCOUNTING source's own last committed frame (a self-watermark — still not a second source), and `uncoveredMs` dedupes at interval granularity because a process death can land mid-interval. Shipping the plan's text literally would have double-credited on every service restart.

8. **Maintenance is one pass with two triggers.** `DailyMaintenancePass` (bounded journal prune; clock-jump gate) runs from WorkManager's daily job *and* from the service's own heartbeat cycle behind a 20 h `MIN_INTERVAL` guard — XOS never dispatches WorkManager's scheduled work reliably (ADR #27), so the in-process trigger is the real one on the test device, and the guard keeps the two triggers from double-running. Prune: 500 rows per transaction, 20 batches per run cap, idempotent, partial runs leave the journal consistent; `auto_vacuum` untouched. Clock-jump gate: a forward wall-clock disparity > 6 h suspends pruning for 24 h — a false "everything is old now" read would delete exactly the recent evidence an investigation needs — with the frame comparison one-sided (a backwards move cannot make old rows look new) and a reboot/monotonic reset treated as non-comparable (conservative: one skipped sweep, never a deleted-recently mistake). Both paths were device-exercised (see Verification).

9. **Backup exclusion (AD-14) verified:** `allowBackup=false` plus `data_extraction_rules.xml` and `backup_rules.xml` excluding `database`, `sharedpref`, `rules_snapshot.json`, and `datastore/` on **both** cloud-backup and device-transfer — the journal, the rules, and the wrapped key material exist only on the device.

10. **Failure behavior (open question 4 answered, not deferred):** a corrupt or unreadable rules database never crashes the app and never silently wipes: the store open classifies the failure (Keystore-key unavailability vs data issue vs transient storage), and the app surfaces `DatabaseHealth.KeyUnavailable` / `DatabaseIssue` / `Transient` with an explicit user-initiated Reset — fail-safe, user-in-the-loop recovery. Restore-from-backup is not an option by design (backups are excluded, #9). A failed migration rolls back loudly and leaves the prior schema intact. Key-unavailable and corruption states both route to the same explicit Reset action; nothing destructive happens without the user pressing it.

11. **§11 measured on the representative low-end device.** Device: Infinix X6532 (XOS), Android 14/API 34, 3.8 GB RAM — the low-end class the DoD asks for. Bench seam: `ledger-bench` (debug-only, bounded, self-cleaning — 800 rows dated 100 days back under a synthetic package so the *ordinary* prune is the cleanup path; the one bench tracker row deleted; `pruneEligibleRemaining=0` and pages-back-to-baseline verified after). Two runs, enforcement foreground service live, both agreeing:

- **single append** (own transaction, SQLCipher included): p50 **7.9–9.3 ms**, p90 16.7–18.4 ms
- **day-split append** (two rows + both index sets): p50 **9.4–10.8 ms**, p90 21.2–23.8 ms
- **accounting transaction incl. projection update** (production shape: tracker read + in-transaction append + insert/update): p50 **7.9–9.3 ms**, p90 14.0–18.4 ms
- **batched** (20 appends per transaction): 32–39 ms per transaction ≈ **1.6–2.0 ms/row** — ~5× cheaper than one-row transactions, the write-amplification-per-transaction effect
- **WAL write traffic** ≈ 3.7–3.9 KB/row transient; bounded by SQLite's default 1,000-page autocheckpoint (~4 MB), confirmed by a 3.0 MB WAL after an 800-row burst
- **retained storage = 225 B/row exactly** in both runs (pages in use measured via `page_count − freelist_count`; the free-page reuse is why the main file's length does not move — file size is a high-water mark, not a measure of what the rows added)
- **reads (p50)**: current-day projection read 0.43–0.51 ms; §8.13 per-package day total 0.66–0.69 ms; all-package day totals 0.94–1.08 ms; prune-candidate count 0.37–0.40 ms
- **prune**: 800 rows in 32–41 ms (two batches of ≤500), WAL grew ~309 KB during the sweep
- **SQLCipher/Room footprint**: process native PSS rises ~19.5 MB on first cold open of the accounting path, steady ~40–44 MB native thereafter (second run adds ~5 MB — arena high-water already reached); no OOM signal at the 3.8 GB device class
- **capacity model validated**: 30 s cadence → 2,880 heartbeat rows/day (one series, not per package) + 120 rows/foreground-hour/package; example 10 packages × 2 h/day = 5,280 rows/day → **475,200 rows at 90 days ≈ 101 MB** at the measured 225 B/row — inside the retention decision (R9: 90 days)

All numbers include SQLCipher — the store is always encrypted, so these are the production costs, not a plaintext baseline. Under the plan's §11 wording: single-event insert latency including day-split ✓, batched insert latency ✓, transaction latency including projection update ✓, WAL growth under realistic event rates ✓, all four read latencies ✓, capacity model ✓, prune-transaction duration + pruning WAL ✓, SQLCipher footprint on a low-RAM class ✓. (The §8.8 batch-size choice is validated by the prune numbers; "CPU cost of SQLCipher encryption" is measured end-to-end in the latencies, which is the only meaningful form for an always-encrypted store.)

12. **16 KB page-size (Step 15) verified now, not deferred.** All native libraries in the APK — including `libsqlcipher.so` — across armeabi-v7a / arm64-v8a / x86 / x86_64 carry ELF LOAD segments aligned to `0x4000`, and `zipalign -c -P 16 -v 4` reports **"Verification successful"** — exactly the alignment a 16 KB-page device's loader enforces. Runtime boot on an actual 16 KB device remains a release-stage item (no such device in the loop; the emulator is ruled out for device testing).

**Trade-off Accepted:** heartbeats write ~2,880 rows/day unconditionally — bought for 30 s-resolution gap accounting and predictable prune cost. Reconciliation credits land at completion, never backdated, so the projection can lag the platform's number until the credit lands — the alternative (estimating during an unresolved gap) is what §8.11/R12 forbid. The 90-day journal is a rolling window with no per-package hold: an older investigation has the projection but not the rows. The write traffic (~3.8 KB/row transient on a 30 s cadence) is real cost on eMMC-class storage; it buys the audit trail that makes any disagreement with the platform answerable row by row. The AD-16 deviation (#7) is kept because re-reading exit reasons on every service start makes the self-watermark and overlap check load-bearing, not gratuitous.

**Consequences:** the tracker is now a derived view; enforcement authority is unchanged (it reads only the committed projection). The feature's surfaces: Home banner (report always, Fix when offered), notification 1003 (offer-gated, auto-cancelling), Settings → Protection Fix row. Task #13 (the open undercount bug) gains its audit tool. §18's DoD is closed item by item except the two release-stage lines — runtime 16 KB boot (static verification done, #12) and staged-rollout monitoring, which is the Play-console form of a control that does not exist for a direct-APK distribution (ADR #15); crash telemetry is already live. The device's current ledger (read after the bench) holds 90 rows, `pruneEligible=0`, `carryover=0` — the latter expected: the DB was created fresh at v11 during later device work, and migration-seed rows exist only for a real v10 upgrade (that leg's evidence is the migration run itself, not today's DB).

**Verification:** full JVM unit suite green — 1,197 tests across 148 classes, 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped (`:app:testDebugUnitTest`, results at `app/build/test-results/testDebugUnitTest/`); the +11/+1 over the pre-adversarial count is `UsageLedgerAdversarialTest` (#95 below). Ledger-specific classes: `UsageLedgerMapperTest` (15), `UsageLedgerRepositoryTest` (16), `UsageGapRepairTest` (20), `AccountingDiagnosticsTest` (13), `BatteryPromptGateTest` (4), `LedgerMaintenanceTest` (14), `UsageAccountingEngineTest` (33, includes the ledger-coupled verdict paths). Migration: JVM `AppRulesStoreMigrationTest` (3) green; the v10→v11 leg additionally ran on device (instrumented, below). Device, X6532 (physical): live in-place v10→v11 upgrade; a real 564 s gap row plus heartbeats at the 30 s cadence; outage read model verified both ways (`promptRoute=null` while exempt → `DIRECT_REQUEST` unexempt); notification 1003 posts on an unexempt window and cancels on re-exemption; prune removed a seeded past-retention row; the seeded clock-jump suspended pruning with `ledger pruning suspended: §8.8 clock-anomaly cool-down` and reset clean; process-death repair credited 20,196 ms as RECONCILIATION with the tracker arithmetic reconciled (190 s accounting + 20.2 s reconciliation = 210,647 ms) and no escalation; the `home_outage_body` fix re-verified ("up to 9m at one stretch"); the §11 bench runs and their cleanup (#11); the 16 KB static verification (#12). Device left clean: whitelist restored, a11y bound with `accessibility_enabled=1`, APP_STOPPED cleared via the app's own Re-enable, seeded rows pruned.

## #95 — The Adversarial Pass Found One Real Defect: an Interval Closing Exactly at Midnight Was Credited to the Next Day (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Task #47 (user ask 2026-09-27: "stress test, try breaking, think of edge cases as many as possible"). The ledger's existing suites already pinned midnight crossings, DST both ways, remainder handling, clock jumps, reboot boundaries, idempotency, collisions, retention boundaries, and the rebuild invariant — so the adversarial pass was scoped to the cells those suites did *not* cover: boundary **equalities** (not near-boundaries), input-domain edges, a timezone move mid-day, a correction racing retention, two writers racing one observation, a rule deleted mid-day, a 50-day span, and a seeded randomized stream asserting the tracker↔journal invariant end-state.

**Defect found (real, fixed).** `UsageLedgerMapper.splitAtDayBoundary` guarded the split with `firstSpan <= 0 || firstSpan >= span`. Given `startDay != endDay`, the boundary (start of the day after `wallStart`) always satisfies `wallStart < boundary <= wallClockTs`, so `0 < firstSpan <= span` — the guard's only reachable firing is the **equality** `firstSpan == span`, i.e. an interval whose wall-clock close lands exactly on local midnight. That case was attributed 100% to the **new** day. But the interval is half-open `[start, close)`: an observation closing at 00:00:00.000 contains no instant of the new day, so its entire credit belongs to the day it began. Impact: the new day's budget absorbed up to a full interval of the previous day's usage (worst case, a user is blocked early the next day); the journal's day attribution was wrong for that row. Reachable whenever an observation close is exactly midnight `.000` — rare (millisecond resolution) but real across months of use, and deterministic once hit.

**Fix.** The guard is now `firstSpan <= 0L || firstSpan > span` (the `> span` half is retained as zone-skip/zone-repeat defense — some zones have skipped a calendar day outright). The equality case falls through to the proportional path, which yields **two rows**: the opening day carrying every millisecond, and a zero-length, zero-credit row on the closing day. That second row matters beyond bookkeeping: `AppendOutcome.Written` reports the *closing* slice as the projection's credit, so the caller (which has already rolled the tracker to the new day) credits exactly 0 — the new day is no longer docked, and Σ credit is still conserved by construction.

**Matrix — each row is one executed check (all green; names are test methods, `UsageLedgerAdversarialTest` unless noted).**

| Scenario | Check |
|---|---|
| Interval closing exactly at midnight | `matrix_intervalEndingExactlyAtMidnight_belongsToTheDayItBegan` — **failed before the fix, green after** |
| Interval opening exactly at midnight | `matrix_intervalStartingExactlyAtMidnight_belongsToTheNewDay` |
| Absurd span (50 days) — conservation + no negative credit | `matrix_fiftyDaySpan_conservesEveryMillisecond_andNeverGoesNegative` |
| Package name at the 255-byte limit / one over | `matrix_packageNameBoundary_255Accepted_oneByteMoreRejected` |
| credited == span accepted; credited > span rejected | `matrix_creditedAtTheSpanBoundary_accepted_onePastRejected` |
| Timezone move mid-day: day keys frozen, no credit lost | `matrix_timezoneMove_keepsWrittenDayKeys_andLosesNoCredit` |
| Correction whose target was pruned | `matrix_correctionAfterItsTargetWasPruned_isRejected_withTheReason` |
| Two writers racing one observation | `matrix_twoWritersRacingTheSameObservation_creditExactlyOnce` (one Written, one Duplicate, total unchanged) |
| Rule deleted mid-day, recreated same day | `matrix_ruleDeletedMidDay_recreatedSameDay_keepsAccruedUsage` |
| 300-op seeded random stream (observations, re-deliveries, rejected drafts, rule flips) | `matrix_randomizedStream_trackerStaysEqualToTheJournalSum` — tracker == Σ journal, `rebuildProjection()` a no-op |
| Seeded stream chosen to cross real local midnight (starts 23:58) | `matrix_streamCrossingRealMidnight_rollsTheTracker_andTheJournalHoldsBothDays` — anchor rolled to the new day, tracker == new day's Σ journal, previous day > 0, both days' rows survive the roll |
| Midnight crossing, DST both directions, remainder | already pinned in `UsageLedgerMapperTest` / `UsageLedgerRepositoryTest` (pre-existing) |
| Clock jump fwd/back, reboot boundary, cool-down | already pinned in `LedgerMaintenanceTest` / `AccountingDiagnosticsTest` / `UsageGapRepairTest` |
| Duplicate / collision / correction / retention boundary | already pinned in `UsageLedgerRepositoryTest` |
| WAL growth, prune cost, retained bytes | device-measured, §11 (#11 above) |

**Consequences.** The mapper's KDoc states the half-open reading explicitly so the guard is not "simplified" back. The adversarial class is the regression net for this exact boundary; every row above runs in the standard suite. No device leg: reproducing an observation close at exactly midnight `.000` on demand is not feasible, and the defect is pure arithmetic already pinned at the mapper, repository, and engine levels.

**Verification:** full JVM suite green at **1,197 tests / 148 classes, 0 failures** after the fix (usage package: mapper 15, repository 16, adversarial 11, engine 33, gap repair 20, diagnostics 13, maintenance 14, prompt gate 4, session machine 12, recorder 15).

---

## #96 — Server-Side Android Key Attestation: `registerPairing` Now Verifies the Proof's Attestation Chain (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner directive 2026-09-27/28: Android Key Attestation **is** in V1 scope, verified **server-side** — an explicit override of AD#7 / plan §5.7 / §10.7 / §16 Q3 ("the server does not attempt to remotely prove the key is hardware-backed"). Plan §19.4 item 2 (binding): "the proof carries the device's attestation chain; registerPairing validates PKIX to the pinned Google roots + KeyDescription (origin GENERATED, purpose SIGN, securityLevel ≥ TEE, challenge bound to the session) before accepting a proof. Port of the client logic; pinned test vectors." The unchanged half of the old decision still holds: the key is biometric-gated locally; the new claim is that its chain provably terminates at a Google hardware root.

**What shipped.**
- **`functions/attestation.js`** — a pure `node:crypto` port of the client `AttestationVerifier`: minimal DER reader, KeyDescription extraction (OID 1.3.6.1.4.1.11129.2.1.17, including the Keymaster `[1] EXPLICIT SET OF INTEGER` purpose encoding caught on the X6532 2026-08-30), Keymaster tag 702 origin, and a manual PKIX walk (`checkIssued` + `verify` + issuer CA flag + validity windows) to the two pinned Google roots. Same reason codes as the Kotlin verifier: `MALFORMED`, `ATTESTATION_MISSING`, `KEY_SOFTWARE`, `KEY_LEVEL_UNKNOWN`, `CHALLENGE_MISMATCH`, `ORIGIN_NOT_GENERATED`, `PURPOSE_MISSING_SIGN`, `KEY_CHAIN_MISMATCH`, `CHAIN_UNTRUSTED`.
- **Wire contract (breaking):** `PairingProof` gains `attestationChain: [b64 DER, leaf-first]` and `attestationChallenge: b64` — **both required**; there is no legacy-client fallback (§19's "no back-compat shims" reading; the pairing surface has no production users yet). Not part of the signed canonical message — they are evidence *about* `signingPublicKey`, which the signature already binds.
- **`registerPairing`** runs `verifyAttestation(proof)` after `verifyProof`; a failure throws `invalid-argument` with the stable reason and logs it.

**"Challenge bound to the session" — the honest reading (recorded deviation).** The keystore key is long-term: it is generated once, before any pairing session exists, so no fresh session value can be embedded in its certificate. Session binding is therefore realized as: (a) challenge *equality* — the proof's `attestationChallenge` must equal the challenge embedded in the leaf (ties the transported chain to the transported value), and (b) the proof signature itself — made by the attested key over the session transcript hash. Net property: *the session transcript was signed by a key whose chain terminates at a Google hardware root and whose origin is GENERATED with purpose SIGN.* That is the strongest form available with a long-term attested key.
- **Leaf-key binding (server addition beyond the client):** the leaf certificate's public key must equal the proof's `signingPublicKey` byte-for-byte (`KEY_CHAIN_MISMATCH`) — mirrors the client's `chain[0].publicKey` use in `EnrollmentFlow`, and closes the substitution hole where a valid chain and a valid signature belong to different keys.

**PKIX subset — decision.** Node 22 has `crypto.X509Certificate` but no path validator; the alternatives were a PKIX dependency (rejected: disproportionate for a ≤4-cert chain with pinned anchors) or a manual walk (shipped). Validity checks: every non-anchor cert must be within `validFromDate`/`validToDate`; the **anchor is validity-exempt** — Google's Root 1 expired 2026-05-24 and must keep validating (same exemption the client's JDK-PKIX path gets for free). Chain length capped at 4.

**Test vectors (cross-language pin).** Two families:
1. **Synthetic PKI** — `functions/test/helpers/der-pki.js` hand-builds a root→int→leaf PKI and the KeyDescription extension on `node:crypto` primitives (the inverse of the parser), so every check has a purpose-built vector: both purpose encodings, both acceptable levels (TEE/StrongBox), software and unknown levels, challenge mismatch, origin 2, missing extension, non-CA intermediate, expired leaf, expired anchor, tampered leaf byte, foreign root, leaf≠proof key, malformed inputs.
2. **The repo's openssl PEM fixtures** — byte-identical copies of `app/src/test/resources/attestation/` under `functions/test/fixtures/attestation/`, so one fixture set pins the Kotlin and Node implementations to the same verdicts. Pinned serials `E8FA196314D2FA18` / `3505A0A2FDCC51E4` asserted on both sides.
- Client-side mirror: `PairingFlowTest`'s `FakeRegistration` now enforces the server's new contract (chain parses, challenge equality, leaf key == signing key) on every positive-path run — a `buildProof` regression that drops the fields fails the JVM suite, not just production.

**Consequences.** Old clients cannot register pairings (intended, pre-production). The §10.7 threat-table row ("signature verification cannot remotely prove hardware-backing") is retired; the server-side check is strict — a software-level or non-Google-root chain fails registration, which is a **live risk for the user-authorized emulator-as-acceptor device test (task #39/#48)**: the emulator's chain provenance must be captured empirically before the ceremony; if the emulator image emits a software-level chain (or a non-Google root), registration will reject it and that leg needs an owner decision. Cost: one extra callable check on a rare path (both proofs once per device pair); no new Firestore reads/writes, no new collections.

**Verification:** functions suite **58/58** (`node --test`, 19 of them `attestation.test.js`); full JVM suite **1,197 tests / 148 classes, 0 failures** (Gradle XML, includes `PairingFlowTest` 10/10 under the strengthened fake + `PairingProofTest` 2/2); `index.js`/`attestation.js` syntax-checked. Deploy leg: task #40.

## #97 — Typed Invitation Code: Crockford Base32 Rendering of the Pairing Token (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Plan §19.4 item 1 (binding): *"Typed invitation code — Crockford Base32 encoding of the existing token (§8.2 rules: 12 data chars + mod-32 check char, `XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-C`, alias normalization), accepted alongside the existing QR on both roles. Unit-tested against §12.1."* Plan §8.2 (lines 248-253) pins the shape and check formula; §17 decision 17 pins 60 bits of entropy; decision 18 pins the check character to the alphanumeric alphabet (dropping Crockford's mod-37 symbols). Two plan-internal conflicts needed rulings to ship this: §8.2's original prose describes a server-mediated `createPairingSession`/`joinPairingSession` + HMAC-pepper flow, while §19.4's delta table (§8.2 line 546) says *"Same `PairingToken`, new human-readable encoding, both renderings accepted by the same exchange"* — **the §19.4 binding ruling governs**: the code is a second rendering of the shipped token, not a second protocol, and no callable-function surface is introduced.

**What shipped.**
- **`PairingCode`** (new, enrollment package): `encode(token)`/`encode(bytes)` render `XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-C`; `parse(raw)` returns `Valid(tokenBytes)` or `Invalid(reason ∈ LENGTH, CHARACTER, CHECK)`; `normalize` = plan §8.2 verbatim (uppercase, `I`/`L`→`1`, `O`→`0`, hyphens stripped) **plus whitespace stripped anywhere** (paste hygiene — spaces between groups are accepted; plan silence read per the robustness principle); `randomTokenBytes()` draws the 60-bit domain.
- **Token generation narrowed to the typed domain** — `PairingToken.generate` now draws 8 random bytes with the top nibble of byte 0 masked off (60 bits, big-endian, right-aligned) and 24 zero bytes of padding. The 32-byte `token` field, the SHA-256 document id, the CBOR codec, and every exchange field are byte-identical to before; only the entropy source changed. 12 Crockford chars cannot carry a 256-bit token, so generation moved to the domain every invitation must be typeable in. §17 decision 17 priced 60 bits adequate against a hypothetical Firestore exposure (single-use + 10-minute TTL); the per-session "typed vs QR" mode split was rejected — two token domains would make doc-id collision and exchange semantics mode-dependent for no security gain.
- **`FirestoreSchema.pairingTokenDocumentId(tokenBytes)`** overload — the raw-value → SHA-256 hex id, which is the entire addressing contract the typed path needs (a token document is unaddressable without the token; possession + expiry are the whole gate).
- **`PairingExchange.readTokenByValue(tokenBytes)`** delegating to the shared `readDocument`; `TokenReadResult.Found` gained `expiresAtEpochMillis` — the typed code carries neither session id nor expiry (it is 13 characters), so the acceptor hydrates `PairingToken(tokenBytes, payload.sessionId, doc.expiresAt)` from the document. `EnrollmentFlow`'s session-id cross-check then proceeds unchanged.
- **`PairingFlow.completeWithCode(code, myUid, signature)`** — parse (refuses invalid codes locally, zero network), then the identical read → verify → respond → register path `complete` uses, converging at the first Firestore read. `Outcome.TokenExpired`/`TokenAlreadyUsed` session ids became nullable: before the token document is read, this side genuinely does not know the session id (typed entry has no QR payload) — `null` states that, rather than a `""` sentinel.
- **Debug harness** (`PairingTestActivity`): initiator logs `CODE_TEXT: XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-C` alongside `QR_TEXT`; the partner's redeem button accepts either (code parsed first — exact 13-char length makes confusion with a base64 QR payload impossible) and routes to `completeWithCode`. This is the on-device entry for task #39.

**Check-character weights — implementation choice.** The plan pins the formula `(Σ weight_i × value_i) mod 32` but not the weights. Shipped: weights are the 1-based character positions 1..12, digits taken MSB-first 5 bits each. Deterministic vector pinned in tests: `1234-5678-9ABC-A` (digits 1..12, Σ i² = 650 ≡ 10 mod 32 → 'A'). Consequence, accepted by plan decision 18: a single-character corruption whose weighted delta is ≡ 0 mod 32 is undetected (e.g. `+16` at position 2 — pinned as a test so the trade-off is visible); adjacent transpositions are always detected. The check is a typo catcher, not a MAC — token possession and single-use are the actual security properties.

**Consequences.** Every invitation is now typeable by construction (`generatedTokens_areAlwaysInTheTypedDomain` pins it); hand-crafted/foreign 32-byte tokens outside the domain are refused by `encode` (IllegalArgumentException — a programming error, never user input). The QR path is untouched (same bytes, same doc id). Older debug builds that generated full-entropy tokens cannot have theirs typed — irrelevant pre-production, and their QR path still works. UI wiring (invite/join screens) lands in #36; the ceremony §19.5 multi-user work (#49) is unaffected — pair identity is the durable pairId, not the token.

**Verification:** full JVM suite green at **1,215 tests / 148 classes, 0 failures** (Gradle XML; +18 over #96's 1,197: `PairingCodeTest` 13/13 — round trip 200×, display shape, deterministic vector, alias/whitespace normalization, corrupt check/data char, transposition, blindness pin, wrong lengths, non-alphabet, encode-domain refusals, generated-token domain, doc-id equality with the token — and `PairingFlowTest` 15/15, +5 typed-entry: full round trip through the code path with aliased/noisy input, bad check rejected locally, unknown token → `TokenAlreadyUsed(null)`, expired → `TokenExpired(null)`, already-responded → `TokenAlreadyUsed(null)`). Device leg: task #39 (emulator acceptor can type the code; both roles also readable from `adb logcat -s MyExodusPairing`).

## #98 — Pairing-Path Rate Limits + Idempotency on the Shipped Surface: `registerPairing` Learns the §9 Ordering (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Plan §19.4 item 3 (binding): *"**Pairing-path rate limits** (§10.5) + **idempotency** (§9 ordering) on the pairing callables."* §19.3 (line 547) fixes the target: the five session callables are *superseded by shipped design* — "Their *security requirements* (§9 idempotency ordering, §10.5 rate limits) are real and are implemented against the shipped surface" — i.e. `registerPairing` + the rules-mediated token doc, not a revived callable set. §19.1 recorded that `/rate-limits` existed for the relay only. The §9 corrected ordering (plan lines 329–345) is exact: authenticate → hash → idempotency lookup (match ⇒ return cached, mismatch ⇒ reject) → *only then* expiry/logic → execute → cache `{functionName, requestHash, result, createdAt, expiresAt: now+24h}` → FCM only on fresh executions. §10.5: per-UID limits, mismatch abuse signal as telemetry, never an auto-lock. §10.12/§7 step 11: idempotency records live 24h, deleted by a scheduled job. §10.11/§13: explicit `maxInstances`, per-function timeout 30s, bounded request payload.

**What shipped.**
- **`functions/pairing-guards.js`** (new, pure — node-tested without an emulator): `isValidRequestId` (`/^[A-Za-z0-9_-]{8,64}$/` — the requestId becomes a Firestore document id, so `/`, `.` and length bounds are rejected before anything touches a path); `requestHashOf` = SHA-256 of `"registerPairing\n" + canonicalJson(proof − requestId)` (sorted object keys, array order preserved — so wire-map field order never changes the hash and the attestation-chain order does); `advancePairingWindow` (§10.5 counter over the shared fixed window); `isWithinRequestSizeLimit` (§13 ceiling, below).
- **`functions/rate-window.js`** (new): the fixed-window `windowState` math extracted from `relay-logic.js`, now shared by both rate-limited paths; `relay-logic.js` re-exports it so its test suite's import surface is unchanged.
- **`registerPairing` ordering** (index.js): auth → §13 size ceiling → requestId shape → **idempotency read** (hash match ⇒ return the committed result with no further processing; mismatch ⇒ `REQUEST_ID_REUSED`) → **rate-limit transaction** (`/rate-limits/pairing_{uid}`, one hour fixed window, 10 executions) → `verifyProof` → `verifyAttestation` → uid check → registration transaction → **best-effort cache write**. Replays never consume budget, and a genuine new execution consumes the budget before any crypto so a flood costs reads, not verifications. The rate-limit doc id cannot collide with the relay's `/rate-limits/{pairId}` docs: a pairId is two uids joined by a single `_`, and uids never contain `_`.
- **Cache write is outside the transaction and best-effort** — a failed cache write must not fail a registration that already committed; the transaction's own proof-equality path still returns the committed state on a retry (the two idempotency layers of §9). Only *committed* executions are cached; rejections are not.
- **`cleanupIdempotencyRecords`** (cleanup.js): daily `37 3 * * *` UTC, `expiresAt < now`, bounded batch — §10.12's 24h retention. `cleanupMailboxes` unchanged.
- **Rules:** `match /idempotency/{document=**} { allow read, write: if false; }` beside the relay/rate-limit denies — the Admin SDK is the only writer, same model as §19.1 records.
- **Client contract (breaking, pre-production — same reading as #96):** `PairingProof` gains `requestId` — a §9 envelope field, **not** part of the signed canonical message (the signature binds transcript/keys; the requestId is transport metadata). `PairingFlow.buildProof` generates UUID v4 per attempt; `PairingRegistration.wireMapOf` sends it.
- **Client retry (§9 client-side failure handling):** `PairingRegistration.submit` builds the wire map **once** and, on a transport-ambiguous failure only (`UNAVAILABLE` / `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED` — the request may have committed with only the response lost), retries **once with the identical payload** so the server replays the committed result. A second ambiguous failure surfaces as `SubmitResult.Failed` (transient, not a verdict); server verdicts (`INVALID_ARGUMENT`, `FAILED_PRECONDITION`, `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`…) stay terminal `Rejected` — previously every `FirebaseFunctionsException` mapped to `Rejected`, which mislabelled a dropped connection as the function's verdict.

**The hazard this actually closes.** Once a pairing exists, `registerPairing` has a re-pair path: a *terminated* record resets `proofs` on the next submission (epoch increments). A stale in-flight proof retried after an unpair could therefore re-enter the pending path — and if both devices' stale retries landed, they could re-activate a pairing with **no human ceremony**. The replay cache returns the committed result for an already-committed requestId *before* any state logic, so a lost-response retry cannot resurrect a dead pairing. The plan's own §9 example (a retry after the session expired must still return the cached success) is the same property.

**Rulings and deviations (recorded, not silent).**
1. **5 create + 10 join ⇒ one 10/hour per-UID cap** — the shipped surface cannot tell initiator submissions from partner submissions; both sides send one identical-shape proof. §19.3's reconciliation mandates adapting the requirement to the shipped design; inventing role flags to preserve two counters would add wire surface for no security gain.
2. **Per-session `confirmSas(false)` cap (max 3): N/A.** There is no shipped confirmSas channel — a human rejection in the shipped ceremony *is* not signing, and there is nothing to count or lock.
3. **Cross-session mismatch counter: telemetry only, and never an auto-lock** (plan §10.5's own words). Served minimally: the `TRANSCRIPT_MISMATCH` branch logs `{reason, pairId}`; the §14 metric/dashboard wiring is task #37.
4. **Fixed window, not rolling** — same math as shipped AD-23 relay limits, now literally shared code. Worst case is a boundary burst of up to 2× across a window edge; the cap's purpose (cost/blast-radius bound) is preserved, and the §13 `maxInstances` ceiling is the hard backstop.
5. **§13 payload ceiling = 16 KB, measured against the parsed request.** The plan's "a few KB is more than sufficient" predates the owner's attestation directive, which added the certificate chain to the proof: up to 4 leaf-first DER certs, ~2.5 KB each in the wild ≈ 13.4 KB as base64, plus fields. The check runs before the first Firestore read or crypto verification. (The v2 callable transport's own request ceiling is not configurable per function; the parsed-request bound is what "reject before business logic" can mean here.) Task #39 records the device-measured chain size.
6. **Ordering proof split.** The pure guards (idempotency-key validity + hash stability + window math + size ceiling) are node-tested here. The *handler* ordering — replay-before-expiry, replay-with-different-payload rejected, limits never locking an innocent counterpart — is the emulator suite's assignment (§12.2, task #42), matching the relay's precedent where pure decisions are unit-tested and the SDK wiring is verified under the emulator. Not duplicated with injected-deps fakes.

**Consequences.** Pairing now has a per-UID cost ceiling and a replay-safe commit path; the §9 guarantee holds end-to-end including the client retry. Adding a **required** `requestId` breaks any older client (pre-production; no back-compat shim — §19's standing reading). Idempotency records cost one extra read per genuine execution and one document per execution retained for 24h — bounded by the rate limit itself (≤10/UID/hour). Deploy joins #40; emulator scenarios #42; device leg #39 (the typed-code run re-verifies the wire contract under a real client).

**Verification:** functions suite **70/70** (`node --test`; +12 over #96's 58 — `pairing-guards.test.js`: requestId accept/reject incl. path-injection shapes, hash determinism + key-order independence + requestId exclusion + payload sensitivity + array-order sensitivity, canonical JSON, window allow-10/reject-11th/no-extend-on-reject/roll-over/future-start-reset, 24h retention constant, worst-case-chain fits / oversized rejected); all eight function modules `node --check` clean (index.js/cleanup.js are not imported by any test, so the syntax check is load-bearing until #42). Full JVM suite **1,219 tests / 150 classes, 0 failures** (Gradle XML; +4 over #97's 1,215 — `PairingRegistrationTest` 4/4: ambiguous failure retried once with an identical wire map and a doc-id-safe requestId, server verdict not retried, ambiguous-twice → bounded `Failed`, RATE_LIMITED terminal — plus `PairingFlowTest` 15/15 under the strengthened fake, which now rejects any malformed requestId the way the function does). A Java test shim (`FunctionErrors.java`) constructs the SDK's exception type, whose constructor is `internal` in Kotlin metadata though public in bytecode.

## #99 — The Ceremony UI + Multi-User Partner: §19.4 Item 4 (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Plan §19.4 item 4 (the headline deliverable): *"Production ceremony UI — invite/join/SAS/biometric/terminal screens over the shipped `PairingFlow`, wired from `PartnerScreen`, built from `/Users/musila/Documents/ReferenceInspirationUI` via the component-reverse-engineer skill (owner directive: no online UI research)."* §19.5 (owner ruling, binding on this item): one partner account supports **multiple users at once** — a roster + switcher, every stream scoped to the selected pair, a label per supported user, unpair-one-leaves-the-rest. The shipped gap this closes was recorded in §19.2: *"`PartnerScreen` — post-pairing dashboard + unpair. No enrollment entry point exists in production"* (OPD #25).

**What shipped.**
- **`PairingCeremonyScreen`** (new, `ui/partner/`) — the full ceremony as a human-speed state machine over the shipped flows, entered from `PartnerScreen` in both modes (`CeremonyMode.INVITE`/`JOIN`): name field → invite (typed code displayed + copy + QR-equivalent value) or join (OTP-style code boxes over a transparent `BasicTextField` that owns focus/IME/TalkBack) → SAS compare (hero digits, explicit match/mismatch choice) → one biometric prompt → terminal (paired / failed / expired / offline). Copy comes from mapped resources — a raw protocol failure never reaches the screen; the diagnostic reason goes to logcat under `MyExodusPairing`. Every network step runs off the main thread; cancel is offered at each non-terminal step.
- **Two-phase join** (`PairingFlow.prepareJoin`/`prepareJoinWithCode` → `finishJoin`): the acceptor's verification (material check, transcript, SAS computation) happens with **no signature and no commit**, the human compares digits, and only then does `finishJoin` sign + register. `complete`/`completeWithCode` are now literally the two phases composed, so the QR and typed paths share one implementation. This closes the asymmetry the old single-shot partner path had: previously the acceptor's proof was produced in the same call that revealed the SAS, making the human check a formality on that role. Token expiry during the human check → `TokenExpired` (token not consumed); `cancel` deletes the token so a late joiner reads `TokenAlreadyUsed`; cancel after the SAS was seen is asserted to block the partner from binding.
- **Label exchange (§19.5 item 3)** — `PartnerLabel.sanitize`: whitespace-class characters (tab/newline) become plain spaces *before* control/format characters are dropped (a dropped newline would fuse "Ana\nMaria" into one word in someone else's switcher), bidi overrides and NUL-class controls are dropped, length is capped at **32 code points** (an emoji is one character, never a split surrogate). The label rides the enrollment payload as CBOR key `"l"` and is **omitted when null**, so label-less payloads stay byte-identical to the pre-label format; decode **re-sanitizes** (the wire is a trust boundary — the label arrives from the other device and is rendered on this one). Absent/blank falls back to the key-id fragment the dashboard already shows, never a fabricated name. The self-chosen-name decision remains the one §19.5 flagged for veto; the owner has not vetoed it.
- **Multi-user (§19.5 items 1/2/4)** — `PartnerDashboardSource.load(selectedPairId)` builds every stream (chain head, envelopes → alerts, approvals, mailbox watermark, relay-rejection read, journal) from the selected enrollment, defaulting to the most recently activated when the selection is null or stale (the picked user was unpaired); `PartnerSelection` persists the pick in plain `SharedPreferences` (lazy accessor — the Hilt graph can construct pre-unlock at direct boot, where CE storage throws) — UI state, never protocol state; `PartnerScreen` renders the roster newest-first (label or key fragment, paired date) with radio selection. Unpairing one of N leaves the survivor on the dashboard: the journal row for the gone pair is not mistaken for "the pairing ended", and the dead selection degrades to the survivor rather than blanking the screen.
- **Schema v11** — `MIGRATION_10_11` adds `partner_label TEXT` to `enrollments` (nullable, additive), registered in `ALL_MIGRATIONS`; the migration test seeds a real v10 DB and asserts identity material survives, `partnerLabel` is null, an upsert with a label round-trips, and the Step-18 outbox columns are untouched.

**Rulings and deviations (recorded, not silent).**
1. **Task #39's "emulator acceptor" wording is not viable** under the owner's server-side attestation directive (#96): emulator software keys cannot produce a chain rooted in the pinned Google roots, so `registerPairing` would reject them by design. The device leg runs the shipped virtual-partner round trip on the X6532 — a second *attested* TEE keypair under test-only aliases (`PartnerTestKeys`) driving production flows as the partner uid. This is the same device path ADR #55/#58 already established; cross-device distinctness remained unexercised at the time (no second physical attestor existed). **[Amended 2026-10-01: closed — a second physical attestor (Infinix X6532C) paired with the X6532 through the production ceremony (same transcript hash, distinct hardware-attested key hashes; pair `mXyGJ…_xutCN…`); evidence in the pairing plan §12.3 status block.]**
2. **Deploy surfaced a real gap, fixed here:** `cleanup.js` exported `cleanupIdempotencyRecords` but `index.js` never re-exported it, so the function could never deploy ("No function matches the filter"). Fixed and deployed — the daily idempotency-record retention (#98's §10.12 claim) is now actually live, not just written.
3. **Known open defect recorded, not hidden (task #52):** with N enrollments, this device's *own* activity links to only one chain — `attempt_log.event_sequence` / `recovery_session.event_sequence` are single-valued, so the first pair processed by syncing wins and every other supported user sees "no activity". The new multi-pair coordinator tests deliberately do **not** assert the fan-out, because the correct behavior (broadcast vs. per-relationship) is a product call; the fix needs a per-pair link table + AppRulesStore schema v12.
4. **Device-found ceremony defect, fixed in the same pass:** on the X6532, creating an invitation and tapping **Cancel invitation** rendered the *expired* terminal ("The invitation ran out") instead of returning to the invite entry. Root cause: the `LaunchedEffect` waiting on the partner wrapped `awaitPartner` in Kotlin's `runCatching`, which also catches `CancellationException` — the cancel handler flips the step back to `Entry`, cancelling the effect, and the cancelled coroutine still resumed through its `await == null` branch and overwrote the live step with a terminal. `PairingCeremonyScreen` now routes both state-writing waits (the invite wait and the submit) through a local `runCatchingCancellable` that rethrows cancellation — the repo's existing convention (`UsagePersistence`). Pinned by `RunCatchingCancellableTest`, which also asserts the platform behaviour the defect exploited (`runCatching` *does* capture the cancellation).

**Consequences.** Production finally has an enrollment entry point; both roles now gate their cryptographic proof on the human SAS check; every supported user is visible and switchable with their streams isolated. Older enrollments (pre-label) render key fragments until re-paired — accepted, the fallback is exactly what the dashboard already displayed. The ceremony reuses `MainActivity`'s existing biometric prompt seam (`sign: suspend (String) -> Signature?`), so no new keystore surface was added.

**Verification:** full JVM suite **1,247 tests / 152 classes, 0 failures** (Gradle XML; +28 over #98's 1,219). The final three are `RunCatchingCancellableTest`: success wraps, ordinary failure is captured like `runCatching`, and cancellation propagates *unlike* `runCatching` — the control assertion pins the platform behaviour the cancel defect exploited. The other +25 are — `PartnerLabelTest` 6/6: null/blank/naked-bidi → null, bidi+NUL+format dropped, whitespace collapse incl. CRLF, cap exact/over, emoji code-point counting, no unpaired surrogate; `EnrollmentPayloadTest` +4: `"l"` omitted when null so label-less payloads stay byte-identical, label round-trip, hostile wire label sanitized on decode, blank wire label → null; `PairingFlowTest` +6: SAS exposed before any write, labels land on both records, null labels stay null, token expiring during the human check → `TokenExpired` with the token unconsumed, cancel → `TokenAlreadyUsed` for a late joiner, cancel-after-SAS blocks binding; `PartnerDashboardSourceTest` +6: every stream scoped to the pick (recorded pairIds per stream), switching changes the scoped data, null/stale selection falls back to newest, roster newest-first with per-user labels, unpair-one-of-two leaves the survivor, failed streams on a selected pair still name that pair; `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest` +2: every remote step runs per enrollment with its own resolved uids and the counts add up, an unresolvable pair is skipped and reported; migration test +1: 10→11 preserves the enrollment). Device (X6532, fresh APK, live project): the roster renders **8 real enrollments** with selection state; switching re-scopes the streams on device (header + "Last synced Sep 19" ↔ "Never synced yet"); the selection **survives process death** (cold start re-renders the previously picked user, not the newest). The cancel-defect fix was verified on the same device with the live backend: typed code `CSVW-7932-EP6R-S` created → tap "Cancel invitation" → the screen returned to the invite entry (name field + "Create invitation code"), no expired terminal (screenshot `/tmp/ceremony_cancel_fixed.png`). **Blocked on one warm-window biometric tap (the user's finger):** the fresh labeled 2-user pairing run and unpair-one-of-N — the probe run failed at Stage 0 with `UserNotAuthenticatedException`, exactly as designed when the 900s window is cold.

## #100 — The §12.2 Emulator Suite for `registerPairing` — and the Two Live Defects It Caught (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Plan §19.4 item 5: *"Tests (§12.1 unit on the new encoders + attestation; instrumented full-ceremony on both roles; device runs) and deployment."* The unit half lives in #96–#98; the deployment half in #40. The gap #98's own Verification paragraph named was the **integration half**: *"index.js is not imported by any test, so the syntax check is load-bearing until #42"* — nothing exercised the `registerPairing` handler against a real Firestore, and the relay's precedent (pure decisions unit-tested in-process, SDK wiring verified under the emulator) had no pairing-side counterpart. Task #42 was that leg: the §12.2 ordering/integration scenarios against the Functions + Firestore emulator.

**What shipped.**
- **`emulator-test/`** (new workspace; `npm test`): `register-pairing.test.js` (**19 handler tests + 4 HTTP-wire tests**) plus `helpers/proofs.js` (real ECDSA P-256 identities signing the server's own `canonicalProofMessage` — deliberately the real function, not a duplicated literal that would rot — and `makeAttestedIdentity` driving `functions/test/helpers/der-pki.js` so the *real* attestation verifier reaches `CHAIN_UNTRUSTED` over the wire). Two invocation levels: in-process `pairingFn.run({data, auth})` for ordering/transaction/idempotency semantics (stubbed attestation — the emulator cannot mint Google-rooted chains, ADR #96), and the HTTP wire via the emulator's callable transport (real App Check/auth token checks, real attestation verifier) via unsigned mock JWTs.
- **`firebase.json`**: `functions: { port: 5001 }` added beside the Firestore emulator. The suite discovers the functions port from the emulator hub (`GET http://127.0.0.1:4400/emulators`) — the CLI exports `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` but **not** `FUNCTIONS_EMULATOR_HOST`, and v2 callables live at `http://{host}:{port}/{project}/{region}/{name}`.
- **§12.2 mapping** (header of the test file records each): happy path; concurrent initiator/partner writes → one `active` doc, epoch 2; same-requestId concurrent double-submit → one idempotency doc; concurrent distinct requestIds; **replay after termination returns the committed result verbatim and the doc stays `terminated`** (the #98 hazard, now proven end-to-end); re-pair epoch strictly increases; TRANSCRIPT_MISMATCH (peer still pairs with a third uid); SAME_KEY; stale half-proof dropped; expired pending restarts clean; 10-then-`RATE_LIMITED` with an innocent second uid unaffected; `REQUEST_ID_REUSED`; oversized → `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE` before the requestId check; requestId shape table + non-object payloads; identity from `auth`, never the payload; a 9-row malformed/hostile proof table; unknown extras tolerated; attestation rejection precedes every write; retention ≈ 24h; and over the wire: no App Check token → `UNAUTHENTICATED` with no writes, no auth → `'anonymous sign-in required'`, tampered proof → `SIGNATURE_INVALID`, synthetic chain → `CHAIN_UNTRUSTED`.

**The two production defects this suite caught (both were live on `myexodus-53804` since #40).**
1. **The §10.5 pairing rate limit was inert.** `advancePairingWindow(snap.data(), now)` received the `/rate-limits/pairing_{uid}` document `{window: {...}}` but read `prev.start`/`prev.count` off the top level, so every execution started a fresh window — the counter was *written but never read back*, and the 10/hour cap never engaged. The unit tests had encoded the same wrong (bare-window) contract, which is exactly why 70 green tests shipped a dead control. **Root fix** (in `pairing-guards.js`, shared-function style): the unwrapping lives *inside* `advancePairingWindow`, mirroring the working sibling `incrementWindows(data, …)` in `relay-logic.js`, so a caller cannot pass a bare window and silently restart the count; the unit tests were converted to the document shape with a comment recording the incident, and a new pin test (`the persisted count is the count that is read back`) locks it.
2. **`createdAt` was destroyed by the activation write.** The registration `tx.set(ref, doc, {merge: false})` replaced the document, and `createdAt` was only set `if (!existing)` — so the write that flips `pending → active` (or any re-pair write) erased a field the §19.1 schema lists. **Fix:** the field is re-sent on every write (`createdAt: (existing && existing.createdAt) || FieldValue.serverTimestamp()`); it belongs to the record, not the epoch. Recorded consequence: pairing docs already live in production without `createdAt` gain a fresh (not original) timestamp on their next write — not recoverable, and no reader depends on it.

**Rulings and deviations (recorded, not silent).**
1. **Unknown extra fields are tolerated, not rejected.** An additive client field does not fail registration; it is bound by `requestHash` all the same, so a replay of a mutated payload is still `REQUEST_ID_REUSED`. Strict rejection would make every client addition a breaking wire change — robustness principle at the trust boundary that already has one.
2. **Attestation is stubbed in the in-process level and real over the wire.** The emulator cannot produce chains rooted in the pinned Google roots (ADR #96), so in-process runs inject `{ok:true}` to test *this handler's* ordering; the wire tests exercise the real verifier to its genuine rejection paths. The verifier's own accept paths are #96's unit suite plus the real-device leg.
3. **`firebase-admin` 14.4.0 is pinned in the emulator workspace** — v14 dropped the namespaced API, so the suite imports `firebase-admin/app` / `firebase-admin/firestore` modularly; same major as the functions' own dependency.
4. **Deploy joined this task, not #40.** The fixes are load-bearing on the money path; redeployed via `firebase deploy --only functions:registerPairing --project myexodus-53804` ("Successful update operation" — the emulator suite is what made the redeploy mandatory rather than merely pending).

**Consequences.** The §12.2 integration leg the plan demanded now exists and immediately repaid its cost by finding two live defects that 70 unit tests could not (both contract-shaped, invisible without a real Firestore and a real client). Future handler changes have a runnable regression net (`cd emulator-test && npm test`; needs the Firebase CLI and Java 11+ for the Firestore emulator).

**Verification:** emulator suite **23/23** (`npm test` via `firebase emulators:exec --only firestore,functions --project demo-myexodus`); functions unit suite **71/71** (`node --test`; was 70 — the new read-back pin). Deploy verified live: `firebase functions:list` shows all five functions including `cleanupIdempotencyRecords`; the post-deploy smoke probe of the callable endpoint returns `{"error":{"message":"Unauthenticated","status":"UNAUTHENTICATED"}}` — the new revision serves and App Check is enforced. JVM suite unchanged: **1,247 tests / 152 classes, 0 failures**.

## #101 — Pairing-Ceremony Telemetry: §7.16 + §14 Wired on the Shipped Surface (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Plan §7 step 16: *"Telemetry (§14), wired last, once the functional flow is verified end-to-end in the emulator suite."* The emulator verification landed in #100, which unblocked this step. §14 lists six requirements; the plan's own vocabulary predates the shipped ceremony (its session model — single active session, cancel reasons `user_cancelled` / `superseded` / `process_restart_required` — is not what #39/#97/#98 shipped: independent single-use token docs, and an in-memory-only pending payload that dies with the process).

**What shipped.**
- **Client — one event per ceremony terminal.** `PairingCeremonyTelemetry` (accountability/enrollment) reports `pairing_ceremony_outcome outcome={paired, sas_mismatch, expired, token_used, cancelled, failed}` through the existing `CrashReporting.nonFatal` rail (same one issue class, metadata-only per Decision 12 — no pairId, label, uid, SAS, or protocol material). Every terminal path in `PairingCeremonyScreen` routes through one `fail()`/`reportOnce()` seam: pair success, SAS mismatch, await timeout, expired/used at join or submit, user cancel (back row with a live token, or "Cancel invitation"), and any other terminal as `failed`.
- **Client — exactly-once discipline.** `reportOnce()` is an `AtomicBoolean` CAS (the screen works from IO dispatchers), reset at each attempt start (`startInvite`/`startJoin`). Without it, a double-tap on Cancel — or a back-row tap while the cancel coroutine is in flight — reports two events for one attempt and inflates the very rate §14 reads as a security signal. One event per attempt terminal is now a property, not an aspiration.
- **Server rails (deployed).** `registerPairing` logs `registerPairing idempotent replay` on the cache-hit path (§14 bullet 4's numerator — native metrics count invocations; this line is the replay count) and adds `uid: auth.uid` to the existing `TRANSCRIPT_MISMATCH` warn line (§14 bullet 2's per-reporting-UID label; the pairId already implies both members, so the uid adds no new exposure).
- **GCP artifacts (live on `myexodus-53804`).** Two log-based metrics: `pairing_transcript_mismatch_count` (labels `uid`, `pair_id` — manual review, never an auto-lock) and `pairing_idempotency_replay_count`. One new alert policy: *registerPairing throttled at maxInstances (429)* (WARNING, `run.googleapis.com/request_count` filtered to `response_code="429"` on the `registerpairing` service, ALIGN_SUM 300 s > 0, wired to the owner email channel). Four new dashboard tiles: requests by response code (invocations / errors / throttle), cold-start p50/p95, mismatches per submitting uid, idempotency replays; the dashboard's text map tile was updated to route every §14 bullet to its rail.

**Rulings and deviations (recorded, not silent).**
1. **§14 bullet 1's reason vocabulary reconciles as:** `user_cancelled` — the only shipped cancel reason (explicit cancel and back-out against a live token are both exactly that); `superseded` — no shipped counterpart (the token-doc design removed the single-active-session lock that could supersede); `process_restart_required` — no shipped counterpart (the pending payload is in-memory; a restart drops the attempt and the token dies with its TTL — nothing to require). Recorded in the telemetry class's KDoc; the wire strings are pinned by `PairingCeremonyTelemetryTest` so a silent rename cannot split a metric's history.
2. **Cold start uses the platform metric, not a new log line.** `run.googleapis.com/container/startup_latencies` (DELTA distribution) is emitted by Cloud Run for the `registerpairing` service and was verified carrying live data before the tile was built. A hand-rolled `bootMs` log line would measure the same thing less honestly (process init, not container startup) and add a deployment for no gain.
3. **The per-UID mismatch rail is a log-based counter, not a Firestore counter doc.** The plan's §5.19 "rolling 24h window across distinct partners" is a *query shape*, not stored state: count by `uid` over any window; distinct `pair_id` values per `uid` give the "distinct partners" reading. Storing a rolling counter server-side would add writes on the money path for a signal whose consumer is a human reading a dashboard.
4. **Stale fact fixed en route:** the dashboard text tile claimed the budget alert fires at 80% of **$5/mo**; the live budget is **$2/mo** (OQ-5, §3 of the Monitoring doc already said so). Corrected in the same tile update.

**Honest limits.** The client outcome rail is Crashlytics — counts, not rates with confidence; "one issue class" grouping means console-side analysis is per-event-name counting. The 429 alert fires on the *first* throttled request in 5 minutes and auto-closes after 30 — it is a "the cap engaged" signal, not a saturation curve. `failed` deliberately swallows the specific rejection (transport, verification, registration) into one token — the diagnostic detail stays in logcat/CF logs and is *not* duplicated into a metric label (bullet 6's contract).

**Verification:** `PairingCeremonyTelemetryTest` (JVM) pins all six wire strings exactly. Full JVM suite **1,248 tests / 153 classes, 0 failures** (the new test class). Functions `node --check` + **71/71** unit and **23/23** emulator suites re-run clean after the index.js edits; `registerPairing` redeployed ("Successful update operation"). GCP artifacts verified by list-and-read after creation: 5 log metrics, 4 alert policies (all wired to the notification channel — the new policy's channel was attached in a follow-up PATCH and verified), dashboard at **9 tiles** with positions and titles re-read.

## #102 — The Pairing Adversarial Matrix: One Boundary Defect Fixed, One Latent Store Hazard Recorded (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Task #48 (user ask 2026-09-27: *"stress test, try breaking, think of edge cases as many as possible and scenarios"*), following the #47 ledger precedent: one adversarial class whose rows are the scenario-matrix cells the existing suites do *not* already pin (`PairingFlowTest` 21, `PairingCodeTest` 13, `PairingTokenTest`, the §12.2 emulator suite) — dedupe verified row by row before writing.

**What shipped.**
- **`PairingAdversarialTest` (10 rows, Robolectric, JVM).** (1) expiry boundary — denied exactly at the deadline on every entry point, nothing consumed; (2) a stale await from an earlier session is refused by the session-id `require`, with a before/after relay-state diff proving zero writes, and the real ceremony the await belongs to still completes; (3) same-account echo partner (user scans own QR) → `SELF_PAIRING`, no pair, no records; (4) a replayed initiator submit is idempotent — identical `Paired`, exactly one enrollment record; (5) a second partner on the same device leaves the first pair's record and server proofs byte-identical; (6) unpair + re-pair replaces only that relationship — sibling pair's record and proofs untouched; (7) the partner half degrades to Rejected ("initiator did not confirm") when the initiator never confirms — never a half-pair, token left to the TTL cleanup; (8) the initiator's poll after cancel returns null quietly; (9) client-clock-lag respond denial reads as `AlreadyUsed` (pinned documented limit, see ruling 2); (10) a seeded 24-iteration ceremony stream over a random uid matrix — durable pair id order-independent, `Paired` ⟺ server-active ∧ mirrored records ∧ equal SAS, no half-pair lingers.
- **Shared harness.** `PairingTestHarness.kt` — the relay/function/DAO/secrets fakes extracted from `PairingFlowTest` (same package, `internal`), so the matrix and the main suite exercise byte-identical fake semantics; `PairingFlowTest` keeps three thin delegates. Two copies of a fake relay that could drift would have made the matrix's evidence weaker than it looks.
- **Boundary fix (production code).** `PairingToken.isExpired`: strict `now > expiresAt` → `now >= expiresAt`.

**Rulings and findings (recorded, not silent).**
1. **The boundary defect: client and deployed rules disagreed by one millisecond.** firestore.rules gate pairing-token reads and response writes on `resource.data.expiresAt > request.time` — deny AT the deadline. The client treated `now == expiresAt` as still live. Consequences differed per path: the read path recovered honestly (rules denial → `TokenExpired`), but the respond path maps any denial to `AlreadyUsed` (shipped mapping: without a read-back a denied write is indistinguishable from a consumed one) — the wrong copy at the boundary. Fixed by aligning the predicate with the rules; at the deadline instant the client now refuses before any network call. `PairingTokenTest`'s pin updated to the new semantics (`expiryDeniesAtTheDeadline_notAfterIt`), and the shared `FakeExchange`'s "rules expiry gate" model is now exact rather than one millisecond lenient.
2. **Respond-denial → `AlreadyUsed` under clock skew is a pinned limit, not a defect.** With the predicate aligned, the remaining reachable case is genuine skew (client behind server). Distinguishing expiry from reuse would need a read-back after a denial on an already-terminal path; rejected as a refinement — both outcomes are terminal with the same action (request a fresh code), the copy is the only delta. Row 9 pins the mapping so a future change is conscious.
3. **Finding (recorded, not changed — outside #48's scope): the secrets vault is write-only in production today.** `SecretsRepository.pairingKey` and `.escapeCode` have zero production readers (grep across `app/src/main` and `app/src/debug`, 2026-09-28): the mailbox CEK is re-derived on the fly per pair (`EventCreator` → `CekDerivation.deriveCek(pairId, …)`), so `finalizePairing`'s `storePairingKey` call is a plan-mandated artifact ahead of its consumers (ADR #22 built the vault ahead of its flows; plan line 530 still lists the write). Latent hazard attached: the store is single-valued, so a second pairing overwrites the first pair's key — harmless while nothing reads it, a real defect the day a reader lands. Recommendation: either wire the reads (escape-code surface / re-pair recovery) or mark the store stage-1 in the plan — the plan and ADR #22 own that decision, so no code changed here.
4. **Row 3's honest frame:** the deployed rules already stop a *different* account from writing another account's token response, so the echo scenario's reachable form is "the same user's own second device" — exactly what `SELF_PAIRING` rejects. The row pins the client-side consequence (both halves Rejected, nothing local persisted).

**Honest limits.** JVM-only: the boundary fix is millisecond-exact and not device-reproducible, so its evidence is the unit pin plus the matrix rows; ceremony device parity remains #39's leg (blocked on the biometric tap). Rows 5/6 model the unpair transition at the client + relay-state layer — the function-side unpair legs stay the §12.2 emulator suite's. The fakes mirror the deployed shapes (rules were read; the emulator suite executes the real rules), they are not the rules themselves.

**Verification:** full JVM suite **1,258 tests / 154 classes / 0 failures** (new class + extracted harness; `PairingFlowTest`'s 21 tests unchanged and green). Enrollment package re-run green after each edit. Client-only change — no functions, rules, or GCP artifacts touched.
