## #103 — The #13 Undercount: Three Repair Defects Behind "3h42m Against a 2h Limit", Fixed and Measured Against the Platform's Own Record (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner bug report #13 (2026-09-27, repeat report — a fix had been claimed once before, so proof was the bar): *"I set a daily usage of 2 hours for Instagram… the block did not activate… Digital Wellbeing [shows] 3 hours 42 minutes and [that was] when it [gave] my 10 minutes warning. I hope the website usage doesn't also have that issue."* Owner ruling the same day: Digital Wellbeing is the source of truth for app usage, and the fix may not be re-claimed without a DW-delta measurement on a real session. The complaint day itself is not journal-reconstructable (the ledger's oldest row is 2026-09-28 01:21 — installed the following night — and Decision 20's rollover-in-place had already overwritten the counter; platform record for 2026-09-27: Instagram 6 h 25 m). The investigation therefore audited what the shipped repair could and could not repair — and found three defects, two of them plan-fidelity gaps in the D3 build itself.

**What shipped (three fixes).**
- **The repair cap is gone (F1).** ADR #73 decision 4 shipped "cap 6 h (beyond it, assume a stale record)"; v3 §5 D3 says verbatim: *"No cap. A cap would re-introduce under-counting, which is the defect class this delta exists to remove. The floor and the clip are the only bounds."* The cap refused exactly the windows that needed repair — OEM freezers keep the process dead for most of a day — and a refusal is permanent (§8.7 advances the baseline past an unrepairable span). `UsageGapRepair.repairWindow` now bounds with the floor and the midnight clip only. ADR #73 decision 4 carries an inline revision marker.
- **The window starts at the last observation, not the death (F4).** ADR #73 decision 1 anchored the gap at the platform's exit timestamp — but OEM freezers SIGSTOP the process minutes before the watchdog kills it, and during the frozen prefix nothing observed anything, so the journal has no row and no live path can credit it. Measured on this device: last journal row 06:00:17, watchdog ANR kill 06:02:41 — the ~156 s frozen prefix was permanently uncounted (the next maintenance pass measures from a baseline the repair already moved). v3 §5 D3's span is `(last_credited_wall_ms, now]`, and the ledger made that readable: the service now passes the newest journal row's wall (`UsageDiagnosticsRecorder.lastObservedWallClock`, boot-scoped, from `latestAccountingEvidence`) as `observedUntilEpochMillis`; the window start is `min(death, last observation)`, never clipped by it. The §8.9 overlap check is what keeps the wider window safe — it subtracts every interval the journal already credited, so the prefix only fills where nothing else has.
- **The user-stop detector was over-broad (F3).** `UserStoppedDetector` counted every `REASON_USER_REQUESTED` exit after the watermark as "the user stopped protection". The X6532's own exit-info (read 2026-09-28, records still present) shows `am instrument` force-stops recorded as reason=10 subreason=21 with descriptions "stop … due to start instr" / "… finished inst", and APK installs as reason=16: every instrumented test run was being read as a user stop, setting `reenable_required=true` / degradation APP_STOPPED and the false "stopped from the Task Manager" card (live-captured 03:54 that morning), and the same misread feeds the repair path's enforcement-loss report. Fix: `NON_USER_MARKERS` names out exactly the AOSP 14 AMS non-user class — instrumentation force-stops ("start instr", "finished inst") and `killBackgroundProcesses` ("kill background", "kill all background"); every unrecognized description stays counted. A false positive costs the user an explicit re-enable prompt (the degraded surfaces); a false negative silently drops the one report that enforcement was stopped by hand — so the list is deliberately evidence-backed and short. `getSubReason()`/`SUBREASON_*` would be the natural discriminator and are absent from the compileSdk 37 stubs (javap-verified — ADR #17's adaptation pattern), so the description string is the only signal.

**Rulings and findings (recorded, not silent).**
1. **The cap removal is a plan-fidelity restoration, not a new deviation.** Where ADR #73's decisions and v3 §5 D3 disagreed, this record follows the plan text (the code's KDoc now quotes it verbatim) and marks #73 as revised — the file's own change-control rule.
2. **The repair cannot measure backwards.** The day's standing diff at close of the legs (journal 3,288,534 / platform 3,466,978 / diff 178,444 ms) is the morning's pre-fix freeze hole — a span whose baseline has advanced, which no path may re-measure — plus pre-fix-era slop. It is permanent for 2026-09-28 only; the fix governs forward. §8.12's correction rail (`ledger-correct`) exists to net a wrong row on request and was proven end-to-end on device.
3. **The wider window's safety is the overlap check, not the anchor.** Widening the start from the death to the last observation could double-credit a span the live path already booked — except that the credit is scaled to `uncoveredMs` (§8.9), which subtracts every ACCOUNTING/RECONCILIATION interval the journal owns. On production baselines the uncovered span ≈ the window (ratio ≈ 1, full credit); a planted/aged baseline shows the scale-down doing its job.
4. **A ~2.8 s tick-boot seam after every restart remains, honestly.** The restart's first tick books from process start and the sub-second lead-in before it is not credited — far below the 20 s floor, where ordinary jitter is meant to stay. `UsageGapRepair.MIN_GAP_MS`'s floor now measures the *extended* span, so a short death window becomes worth repairing exactly when its observed prefix pushes the span past the floor.

**Honest limits.** The exit-path repair remains single-boot by design (§8.5: cross-boot spans are the reboot-recovery path's business, ADR #94's `rebootRecoveryRequest`). A freeze shorter than 20 s before a death stays uncredited (floor, accepted). The DW comparison direction: sojourns under ~1.5–2 s are invisible to the platform's own event record, so both sides of the comparison under-count them (ADR #73's recorded caveat). The domain path measures bytes-through-the-VPN activity, not foreground dwell — its honest comparator is the browser's platform-foreground interval over the same window, not DW's per-app number.

**Verification.** Full JVM suite **1,269 tests / 154 classes / 0 failures** (UsageGapRepairTest 24 — four new F4 pins: a newer observation never lets the window start later than the death, a yesterday observation still clips at midnight, the floor measures the extended span, plus the no-cap far-beyond-6-h case; UserStoppedDetectorTest 11 new rows pinning the AOSP description strings, incl. an instrumentation row beside a real user stop still detecting the real stop). Device legs, X6532 physical (build installed 06:39:11), all via the debug seams:
- **PRE-fix defect signatures.** The kill leg credited 20,226 ms over a 20,225 ms window (`id=4645`) when the death was reachable — the corpus was exact where the shipped window could see it; the aged-baseline leg produced GAP rows (spans 5,942,979 + 22,896,038, total 28.84M ms) with **no** reconciliation credit — the cap refusal; the instrumented run had pinned APP_STOPPED with the false Task Manager card (re-enabled via the Home CTA before the fix shipped).
- **F4 leg, post-fix.** SIGSTOP 06:41:51 (pid 9430) → XOS watchdog ANR kill 06:49:19.603 (reason=6, "Timed out while trying to bind") → sticky restart 06:49:22 (pid 12105). Log: `gap repair: credited 452909ms to com.instagram.android over a 452908ms unobserved window`; ledger `id=4817 RECONCILIATION credited=452909ms span=452909ms`. Zero-double-credit by construction, visible in the chain: the last pre-repair row (id=4815) ends at elapsed 105,470,205 and the credit starts at 105,470,207 (the ledger's end-to-start +2 ms convention). Pre-fix this same window was a sub-floor silent no-op — 452.9 s permanently lost.
- **F1 leg, post-fix.** Planted an 8 h aged baseline (`ledger-age-evidence --es hours 8`) → the maintenance pass recorded `measurement gap 28825144ms` and credited `id=4830 RECONCILIATION credited=3006191ms span=24742164ms` — a 6.87 h span, exactly what the old cap refused.
- **§8.12 reversal.** `ledger-correct` on that row's eventId → `outcome=Written(closingDayKey=2026-09-28, closingDayCreditedMs=-3006191) projectionRewritten=1`; `CORRECTION` row id=4832 credited −3,006,191 ms; totals netted exactly (journal 3,288,534 / platform 3,466,978 / diff 178,444 / `blocked=false`).
- **DW delta, app path (the owner's acceptance bar).** Instagram live session: journal 598,935 ms vs platform 602,418 ms = **99.4%**.
- **DW delta, domain path (the second half of the ask).** A 503 s Chrome session streaming `stream.radioparadise.com`: the `radioparadise.com` rule metered 501,506 ms at 07:04:08 against Chrome's platform foreground 06:55:45→07:04:08 = 503,052 ms = **99.69%**; cross-checked against wall (probe-to-probe metered 414,119 ms vs 413,629 ms = +0.12%); the negative-control rule for a never-visited domain stayed at 0 ms throughout; after the browser was stopped the meter flushed its 15 s idle tail and froze (identical reads across 23 s). Test rules and the VPN were removed/stopped and device state restored in the same session.

## #104 — The Owed Day-7 Milestone: Crossings Inside Reboot Windows Are Delivered by Catch-Up (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner report #14 (2026-09-27, verbatim): *"wait i hit a milestone i expected a notification saying the milestone and the track but it didnt"* — and it never would have. The owner's own day-7 crossing was projected 2026-09-26 22:23:15.596 (back-projected from the still-pending day-30/day-90 alarms), and the device booted ≈22:23:57 (bootCount 126 / rBoot 131,260 ms at the 22:26:08 REBOOT_WALL_DELTA anomaly) — the crossing fell inside the reboot window. Alarms do not survive reboot (plan, unconditional platform behavior); after boot the receiver reconciled only, and reconciliation deliberately never schedules a milestone whose projected time already passed (§8: rescheduling would immediate-fire; the splash card carries it). The crossing *was* detected — `achieved_at` = 1790454331647 (2026-09-26 22:25:31.647 EAT, stamped by an evaluation after boot) — but **evaluations never posted; only the alarm receiver did**, and day-7's alarm had died with the previous boot. The row sat `achieved`-and-unclaimed with no path that would ever post it. Baseline device evidence (new `streak-read` harness): `day=7 achieved=1790454331647 sent=- acked=1790512758691` — the splash card had been dismissed; the notification never existed.

**What shipped.**
- **`MilestoneAlerter.catchUpAfterBoot()`** — for every active habit: run the on-read evaluation (Decision 9 lists `BOOT_COMPLETED` as an evaluation trigger; §8's boot step had only ever described reconciliation — this closes the gap) then deliver any achieved-but-unclaimed milestone.
- **`MilestoneAlerter.deliverUnclaimed(habitId)`** — the same delivery at the app-foreground evaluation boundary, so an alarm the platform suppressed or dropped is delivered at next open instead of never.
- Both funnel into one shared `claimAndPost` — the same atomic claim as the alarm path (`claimNotification`, UPDATE … WHERE `notification_sent_at IS NULL`; 1 row = winner). Exactly-once is a property of the claim, not of the trigger. Permission denial and missing rows are checked *before* the claim, preserving §9: a denied post never consumes the claim, and a later grant still delivers.
- `MilestoneBootReceiver` runs `scheduler.reconcileAll(); alerter.catchUpAfterBoot()` under its existing `withTimeout`; `StreaksViewModel.refresh()` (ViewModel init + `MainActivity.onResume`) runs `deliverUnclaimed` after `refreshActive()`.
- Debug seam `StreaksTestReceiver` (`streak-read`, `streak-catchup`) — `BOOT_COMPLETED` is a protected broadcast the host shell cannot send; `streak-catchup` invokes the boot receiver's exact call.

**Rulings and deviations (recorded, not silent).**
1. **No staleness cutoff.** A late celebration is still a celebration — the splash card has the same property (it waits for the user). A cutoff would rebuild the class of silent loss this fix removes.
2. **Per-milestone notifications stay individual** in catch-up (mirrors the alarm path); the plan's §16 open question concerns the splash card's stacking only, and the card's behavior is unchanged.
3. **Relapsed streaks are never announced retroactively** — catch-up reads the active streak only, consistent with the card's visibility (the alarm path was equally incapable of a post after a relapse).
4. **Option Z (reconcile schedules immediate alarms for passed-unachieved milestones) rejected** — it contradicts §8's explicit "not yet passed" rule, churns alarms, and buys nothing the evaluation-trigger catch-up does not already deliver; Decision 9 is the plan's own shape for this.
5. **Boot reconciliation stays** (plan-named); catch-up is additive in the same receiver call.

**Honest limits.** The real `BOOT_COMPLETED` leg was not device-fired (protected broadcast); the harness invokes the receiver's exact call, and the boot wiring itself shipped 2026-09-14 (device-verified then). Catch-up remains best-effort per REQ-12 — the guarantee is still the splash card; an OEM freeze can delay the next-open catch-up, it cannot drop it. The no-cutoff ruling is deliberate.

**Verification.** JVM suite **1,276 tests / 0 failures** (`MilestoneAlerterTest` 12 — 7 new: device-off crossing stamps+posts at boot catch-up, second habit covered, relapsed streak untouched, foreground `deliverUnclaimed`, nothing-when-nothing-crossed, no second post after catch-up, denied permission keeps the claim). Device legs, X6532 physical (`install -r`, no uninstall):
- **Baseline — the fingerprint:** `streak-read` → `day=7 achieved=1790454331647 sent=-` (also `acked=1790512758691`).
- **Real foreground path:** MainActivity opened from the launcher at 07:28:38 (ATMS, uid 10174 XOSLauncher) → onResume → `deliverUnclaimed` → anomaly `2026-09-28T04:28:39.556Z type=MILESTONE_NOTIFICATION_POSTED milestoneDay=7`; live `NotificationRecord` on `channel=streak_milestones`, title `Day 7 free from qqqq…` (the owner's own habit label), deep-linking Home; `sent=1790569719556`. The owed notification had been waiting ~33 h and reached the shade in production.
- **Exactly-once:** the `streak-catchup` broadcast after the claim posted nothing — still exactly 1 anomaly line and 1 live record. The claim also survived a process death + restart mid-leg (the launcher open collided with the cold-start kill; AMS `Process com.example.myexodus (pid 18150) has died: fg TOP` at 07:28:40.728, restart as pid 18937) — no second post, by construction.
- **Alarms untouched:** exactly the two pending entries — `origWhen=2026-10-19 22:23:15.596` (day-30) and `origWhen=2026-12-18 22:23:15.596` (day-90), tag `MILESTONE_ALARM`, +1 h window.

## #105 — "Interrupted 7 Times": Four of Those Were the Instrument's Own Fingerprints — Purged, With an Honest Recipe (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner report (verbatim): *"well i got a popup sayig usage was interupted 7times well instead of that oh i odnt know how about finding and testing a way to resolve this hech use the research playbook skill to research online"*. The popup is D1's outage report (`AccountingOutageSource.current()` → GAP_DETECTED rows over the trailing 24 h — ADR #94 item 5 / plan §8.7). The `ledger-outage` read on the X6532: `count=7 largestWindowMs=24742164` — and the row dump showed four of the seven were **fabricated by this project's own debug legs** (the #13 repair-cap legs pin a GAP by construction and had no cleanup path: two rows from the pre-fix leg at 06:21:36, two from the F1 leg at 06:52:22 — spans 22,896 s + 5,943 s and 24,742 s + 4,083 s). Two more were truthful records of test-caused interruptions (instrumentation force-stops at 03:57 and 06:26, spans ≈155 s / 167 s — kept, they age out of the 24 h window); one was real (01:14:21, 564 s, PATH_A). So the user-visible count was 7/7 true by the ledger's own contract but only 3/7 informative — 4/7 were the instrument measuring itself.

**What shipped.**
- **`ledger-purge-window` debug seam** (`DeviceVerificationReceiver`, debug-only): `--es kind <KIND> --es fromMs <wall> --es toMs <wall>` deletes journal rows a debug leg fabricated. Evidence kinds only — **crediting kinds (ACCOUNTING/MIGRATION_CARRYOVER/RECONCILIATION/CORRECTION) are refused with an error**, because removing a crediting row after its in-transaction projection update would silently desync the tracker (that flow has `ledger-correct` instead). The production DAO keeps its append-only contract (no `@Delete`); the retention sweep remains the only deleter of real rows.
- **Self-cleaning leg recipes.** `ledger-age-evidence` now captures the wall that goes *into* the draft (not a recomputed one — see defect 2) and prints its own purge command; the `ledger-read` action gained a row tail (`ledger tail HEARTBEAT/GAP_DETECTED wall= elapsed= src=`, top 5) so cleanup is verifiable against actual rows, and the leg KDoc states the contract: **every use ends with the purge**.

**Device-caught defects in the cleanup path itself (all three fixed, recorded not silent).**
1. **A lying instrument.** The purge's first live run reported `deleted=0` while the state showed count 7→3 — `SELECT changes()` after `usePrepared` on Room 3's pooled writer connection returns 0. Replaced with a same-transaction `count(*)` under the identical predicate *before* the DELETE; re-verified on device (`deleted=1`, `heartbeats24h` 622→621, no collateral).
2. **A recipe that misses its own row.** The first recipe recomputed `System.currentTimeMillis()` *after* the append; on a cold receiver process the store open (SQLCipher key derivation) shifted the plant wall outside even a ±60 s window, so the purge missed the plant. Fixed by capturing the wall before the append and printing `±5 s` around exactly that value.
3. **A window that swept real rows.** An intermediate ±60 s recipe deleted the plant *plus one adjacent real heartbeat*. Behaviorally harmless (the §8.7 baseline is the *latest* heartbeat row, and the census is diagnostic only) but wrong in principle: the recipe must delete the fabrication and nothing else. Tightened to ±5 s; both casualties (one real heartbeat at the 07:59 leg, one at the 07:56 window) are recorded here rather than silently replaced.

**Verified end state (X6532, physical).** Purges: 4 fabricated GAP rows (two windows) + the lingering 08:01 plant heartbeat (`deleted=1` exactly). Final reads: `ledger outage count=3 largestWindowMs=564471 promptRoute=null`; gaps = the 01:14 real row + the two truthful test-caused rows; `ledger-read` tails show only real heartbeats. The popup's 7 was the instrument's own noise.

**Persistence posture — the "resolve the interruptions" half, evidence-backed, nothing invented.**
- **App-side is at the platform maximum for the levers that exist.** FGS `specialUse` + `START_STICKY` with the per-start foreground re-arm (ADR #103 F4: sticky restart observed ≈3 s); battery exemption granted on the device (`dumpsys deviceidle whitelist` → `user,com.example.myexodus,10411`) and its reactive request path is D1's `DIRECT_REQUEST` (plan Decision 17 — deliberate, not onboarding); both boot receivers (`BOOT_COMPLETED` + directBootAware `LOCKED_BOOT_COMPLETED`); no `stopWithTask` (the FGS survives Recents-swipe teardown).
- **Rejected levers stay rejected (scar tissue, not re-litigated).** Always-on VPN: the manifest deliberately carries `SUPPORTS_ALWAYS_ON=false` — plan §8.4 ruled "block connections without VPN" can brick connectivity. VPN `START_NOT_STICKY`: §8.4 forbids rebind loops. No AlarmManager/WorkManager watchdog was added: neither survives a force-stop (the case that matters), and both would recreate the watchdog-loop smell the plan already rejected — sticky restart already covers the kill case. **[Amended 2026-10-15 (#152): the final clause is device-falsified. XOS kills the FGS-hosting process ("prcp FGS" cgroup kill, three device-proven occurrences) and ignores START_STICKY, so "sticky restart covers the kill case" held only in the sense that the restart never came; the re-arm sites (app-open/toggle/boot/update) left enforcement down until the next app open. The watchdog rejection itself stands — no new alarm loop was added; #152 hooks the already-running WorkManager cadence (which this bullet's force-stop caveat correctly excludes from the *force-stop* case — that remains unwatchable) to re-arm the FGS on every process start.]**
- **The remaining lever is XOS device settings, and no API or component exposes them.** Research (below) confirms Transsion/XOS has no public autostart deep-link component; the required device-side settings are manual (autostart/App Management, battery "no restrictions" — granted here, memory-cleaner exceptions, Recents lock). The app's job is discovery + honesty: the Protection screen's OEM row falls back to App-info (the existing all-OEMs ruling: OEM-keyed table, generic fallback, no OEM-specific copy) and the battery row carries D1's education. The 01:14 real interruption predates all current settings; with exemption now granted, the diagnostics-and-repair path (gap rows + `UsageStatsManager` reconciliation crediting recoverable usage, ADR #103) is the honest safety net, not a claim of zero interruptions.

**Research sources (secondary; no official Transsion developer documentation exists — presented with links per the standing rule).**
- OEM autostart/battery deep links and manual paths: <https://www.codewithkarani.com/blog/oem-autostart-battery-optimisation-android-deep-links>, <https://thecybernanny.com/en/background-settings-by-phone-brand>
- Infinix/Tecno/itel kill behavior + user mitigations (autostart, battery, memory cleaner, Recents lock): <https://tiktask.ai/it/blog/keep-automation-alive-xiaomi-infinix-tecno-checklist>, <https://audiomack.zendesk.com/hc/en-us/articles/360054835431-Why-is-my-Infinix-phone-experiencing-issues-with-music-or-the-app-stopping>
- Developer-side checklist corroboration: <https://github.com/scaik/scam-max-disabler/issues/15>, <https://inalambrik.freshdesk.com/support/solutions/articles/5000899052/>
- Android platform guidance (FGS types, Doze/battery exemptions, restart contracts) as already implemented per ADR #59/#65/#103.

**Honest limits.** The purge seam is debug-only and device-verified, not unit-tested (the receiver has no test harness; the device legs are the stronger evidence here and are logged). The 3 remaining rows are real records — one genuine pre-fix interruption and two test-caused ones — and the count is a 24 h rolling window, so it decays to zero on its own. No accounting row was touched (verified: journal-vs-platform pair deltas unchanged across the purge). The persistence conclusion is bounded by what XOS exposes: no queryable whitelist state exists, so "whitelisted" is verified only for the battery exemption (`dumpsys deviceidle`) and inferred nowhere else.

## #106 — The Unpair That Killed Every Other Pair: Agreement Keys Go Per-Pair (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner ruling 2026-09-27: a partner supports N users at once (§19.5). Device report: after unpairing one user of several, the remaining pairings went crypto-dead — and a second unpair threw `IllegalStateException: agreement key missing` (logcat `MyExodusPartnerTab: unpair initiation threw`). Root cause: the ECDH agreement key was **device-level**. `BilateralDeletion.shredPair` called `destroyAgreementKey()` on that single key during teardown, so unpairing pair A destroyed the key pair B's mailbox envelope encryption depends on. Second unpair then found nothing to destroy and threw.

**What shipped.** Per-pair agreement keys (§19.5 item 4's "keys per pairId", realized as ADR #106).
- **Alias scheme.** `AccountabilityKeyStore.PAIR_AGREEMENT_ALIAS_PREFIX` + `sessionId`; `pairAgreementAliasOf(sessionId)` is the only constructor. `destroyPairAgreementKey` enforces the prefix (`requirePairAlias`) so a stray device-level destroy cannot ride the same path.
- **Lifecycle.** Generated at `PairingFlow.initiate()` (initiator) and inside `verifyInitiatorMaterial` just before Ready (joiner) — never after, so an aborted attempt leaves nothing; `discardAttemptKey` (best-effort destroy) runs on every abort path: registerToken failure, await-partner rejections, non-Paired submit/cancel/finishJoin outcomes. The alias is recorded on `EnrollmentRecord.myAgreementKeyAlias` (**schema v12**, nullable — pre-v12 rows read back null and resolve through `agreementPrivateKeyFor(alias)`'s device-level fallback).
- **Teardown.** `shredPair` destroys only that pair's alias. The device-level key dies only when `enrollmentDao.all().isEmpty()` after the wipe — preserving the AD-22 wipe canary (a wiped device keeps no key material) and keeping pre-v12 shared-key pairs functional. Unpair-always-erases: the erase envelope is best-effort (`runCatching` around `creator.create`), so a crypto-dead or watermark-gapped pair still erases locally; "not enrolled" now early-returns `Failed` + journal delete; an empty requestEnvelope delivers nothing and honestly lands LOCAL_ONLY_TERMINATED after the 7-day timeout rather than claiming partner-side erasure.
- **Latent defect fixed en route.** `finalizePairing` derived the vault pairing-key CEK from `sessionId`; every other consumer uses `durablePairId`. Now consistent.

**Verified.**
- Unit (JVM, full suite green): `BilateralDeletionTest` +3 — unpair-one-of-N destroys only that pair's alias and the sibling keeps its enrollment, chain, and sends (`assertEquals(listOf(pairAlias), destroyedPairAliases)`, `assertFalse(destroyedDeviceKey)`); device key dies only with the last enrollment (drainQueue Sent(1)/Sent(0)×2); a crypto-dead pair (`agreementKeyMissing = true`) still erases locally and journals `AWAITING_CONFIRMATION` without claiming the partner. `PairingFlowTest` +3 — one pair key per side with the alias on both enrollments, cancel destroys the attempt key, a non-Paired `finishJoin` destroys the attempt key, and the corrupt-partner-payload abort destroys its attempt key. `AccountabilityDatabaseMigrationTest` +1 (`migration11to12_addsThePairAgreementAlias_preservingTheEnrollment`, pinned V11_DDL, enrollment + event + outbox rows survive, alias writable, `rejectionCountSince` intact) plus `MIGRATION_11_12` appended to every per-test migration list; `EnrollmentDaoTest` covers the same step. Full JVM suite after the change: **1,285 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped** (the prior full run, 1,284 with 9 failures, was the per-test migration lists stopping at `MIGRATION_10_11`).
- Device (X6532, physical): DEVICE LEGS.

**Research sources.** None needed beyond the codebase — the defect was reproduced in the existing test harness; no external claim was relied on.

**Honest limits.** The decisive on-device leg for unpair-one-of-N through the production UI requires a warm-window biometric tap (the identity key is `setUserAuthenticationRequired(true)`; no debug unpair action exists in `DeviceVerificationReceiver`), so the production-UI half of §12.3 item 5 is verified at the harness + migration level here and awaits the owner's tap for the UI half. Pre-v12 enrollments keep resolving through the device-level fallback until they are re-paired, which is the intended compatibility path, not a gap.

## #107 — "Interrupted 7 Times", Resolved at the Root: Foreign-Close Confirmation, Completed by its Own Device Leg (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner report: the outage popup plus "Instagram usage: 07:44 in Digital Wellbeing" against the app's own record, with the 10-minute warning firing late — the ask was the real fix, not better reporting. ADR #105 cleaned fabricated rows and classified the persistence posture; this entry fixes the remaining **real** under-accrual mechanism. The ledger day audit pinned the gap to one continuous platform interval (07:34:35–08:09:29) with **6 session gaps of 7–34 s**: Path A closed the usage session on window-state changes emitted by transient surfaces (notification shade, heads-up) while Digital Wellbeing never left the tracked app — the session then stayed closed until the app's own next WSC, so the covered span stopped accruing.

**What shipped.**
- **Path B as liveness oracle.** `EnforcementForegroundService.platformForegroundPackage` / `platformForegroundObservedAtElapsedMs` (`@Volatile`, companion): re-stamped on every 1.5 s tick from the carried poll observation (`notePlatformForeground` in `feedUsage`), cleared to `null` on destroy. A dead or wedged FGS freezes its timestamp — stale reads are treated as no evidence.
- **Foreign-close confirmation (Path A).** `syncUsageSession` consults `shouldDeferForeignClose(activePackage, incomingPackage, oraclePackage, oracleAgeMs)` — defer only when the oracle *still names the tracked app* within `FOREIGN_CLOSE_ORACLE_FRESH_MS = 5_000` — and schedules `scheduleForeignCloseRecheck` at 2 s: the re-check closes (final tick + hand-off to the incoming package) iff `platformForegroundConfirms` fails by then. Over-count bounded by the re-check delay (~2 s), inside the plan's accepted sub-poll sojourn tolerance.

**Device-caught completion (same day, X6532 — the emulator could not have found this).** The defer initially never fired on the device while all three emulator legs passed. Root cause: XOS reports the notification shade as a **`TYPE_SYSTEM` window of `com.android.systemui`** (`event … type=32 winType=3`), which is caught by the **emergency-exclusion branch** — a path that ran `closeUsageSession()` unconditionally, *before* the deferred decision path ever gets the event. AOSP's shade reports a non-system window type, so the emulator only ever exercised the deferring path. Fixed: the emergency branch now runs the identical confirmation (`shouldDeferForeignClose` + `scheduleForeignCloseRecheck`, `scheduleBlocked = false` — the incoming surface is never a tracked app) and closes only when the oracle disagrees. **Skin-neutral by construction**: no OEM check exists anywhere in the rule — only the poll oracle — so every Android skin gets the same behavior.
- The old behavior's fingerprint, from the pre-fix build on device: the session died at exactly the shade WSC with **no defer log** (the emergency branch logged nothing); the defer logs exist now. Emulator legs verified the decision path (shade over YouTube: `foreign transition … oracle=com.google.android.youtube ageMs=833` → deferred ×3 → session held; real switch to Settings: `oracle=com.android.settings ageMs=468` → immediate close; HOME: carried oracle 1261 ms).

**Verified (X6532, physical, new build).** Shade expanded over a tracked Calculator session (seeded rule, cleaned after — see below): `foreign close deferred active=com.transsion.calculator incoming=com.android.systemui` **from the emergency branch** (two consecutive WSCs, no preceding transition log — the branch's signature) plus `foreign transition … oracle=com.transsion.calculator ageMs=222` → `foreign close deferred` from the decision path; the session then credited **continuously across the shade** — tracker reads 396277 → 406282 → 416287 ms, zero gap (pre-fix, the identical event closed the session at the WSC instant). JVM: `ForeignCloseConfirmationTest` (7 cases, incl. stale oracle, null oracle, negative age); blocking-package suite green; docs/plan notes in enforcement plan v2 §7 Step 4.

**Debug seam added for seeded legs.** `usage-clear … --ez withRule true` now also deletes the seeded rule (the historical tracker-only contract left a live 7.2 M block rule behind on the device; both are absent after cleanup, verified by read).

**Honest limits.** The 5 s freshness window bounds how long a genuinely-dead FGS keeps a session alive mid-shade — by design (stale oracle → close as before), and the FGS's death is separately surfaced (degradation states + outages). The re-check introduces ≤2 s of over-count in the rare "real departure raced a shade" case — accepted per plan tolerance, bounded, never an under-count. The mid-shade sessions in this leg were bounded by the FGS being alive: after `install -r` the FGS is down until re-armed (see #108 — that gap is now closed too). The device leg used a seeded Calculator rule because Instagram was correctly walled; cleanup verified (`usage rule … ABSENT`).

## #108 — Direct-APK Updates Left Protection Down: `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` Re-Arms the FGS (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Caught while running the #107 device legs: after `install -r` — the only distribution path this product has (ADR #15: no Play) — the app process is killed, the accessibility service rebinds by itself, and **nothing restarted the FGS**. `dumpsys activity services` showed only `BlockerAccessibilityService`; `EnforcementForegroundService` stayed absent until MainActivity was next opened. Every update therefore left protection silently degraded until the owner happened to open the app — and the outage diagnostics honestly counted the span. The only `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` receiver in the manifest belonged to the widget (refresh only).

**What shipped.** `BootReceiver` now also accepts `android.intent.action.MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` (manifest intent-filter + action check) and runs the same path it runs at boot: fast-path snapshot load, async authoritative rebuild, and `EnforcementForegroundService.start(context)` when Enhanced Mode is enabled. No new component, no new receiver — the boot receiver's contract extended by one action.

**Verified (X6532, physical).** After `install -r` of the fixed build: `MyExodusBoot: android.intent.action.MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED received` in logcat and the FGS live again with a fresh poll stamp (`poll foreground=com.transsion.XOSLauncher carried=com.transsion.XOSLauncher`) ~0.3 s later, with no app launch in between. `dumpsys activity services` shows the service foreground.

**Honest limits.** `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` is sent only on replace, never on first install — fresh-install onboarding behavior is unchanged (the FGS is started from the foreground onboarding context, per plan §8 Step 5). This does not address OEM-initiated kills with no broadcast (the FGS's sticky-restart + degradation surfaces remain that path's answer, ADR #105 posture). A reboot-free update is the only leg run here; a widget-only update path is unaffected (the widget already had its own receiver).

## #109 — The Back Button Exits From Inside Spokes: Every Subpage Owner Handles It, Guarded by Test (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner report: pressing the phone's system back button **exited the app** from inside spoke screens instead of returning to the owning hub. Root cause of the class: every hub hosts subpages by switching on an internal saveable state (`view`, `pane`, `route`, wizard `index`) rather than navigating — the platform had nothing to pop, so back fell through to the activity and finished the app. Compose's back contract is "most recently composed **enabled** handler wins"; nothing owned the subpage state, so no handler existed to win.

**What shipped.** One `BackHandler` per subpage-owning host, each disabled at depth zero so the platform default (Home → exit) survives:
- `AppShell` — `BackHandler(enabled = contentTab != 0) { tab = 0 }`: any non-Home tab returns to Home first; Home keeps the standard exit.
- `PartnerScreen` — `view != null → view = null` (dashboard subpages); `PairingCeremonyScreen` — `BackHandler { exitCeremony() }` (abandon the ceremony cleanly, same path as the cancel affordance).
- `RecoveryScreen` — spoke → hub; `RulesScreen` — `route != ROUTE_LIST → ROUTE_LIST`; `SettingsScreen` — `pane != null → pane = null`; `SetupScreen` — `index > 0 → index - 1` (wizard rewinds, never exits mid-onboarding); `StreaksScreen` — `view != null || addingHabit → view = null`.

Composition order keeps the precedence correct: `AppShell`'s handler is composed first, host handlers later, so a spoke's handler (composed most recently) wins while it is enabled; at depth zero it disables and the tab-level handler takes over.

**Stock M3 surfaces already own back** (no code added): `ModalBottomSheet` (the "Log an urge" sheet) and `AlertDialog` (`AppPickerDialog`, confirmations) handle back themselves — dismissal, not exit.

**Guard test.** `PaneBackAffordanceTest.every screen owning subpage state handles the system back button` (`app/src/test/…/ui/PaneBackAffordanceTest.kt`): scans `ui/` sources for files declaring `private const val (VIEW_|PANE_|ROUTE_)` state and fails any whose file lacks `BackHandler(`. New subpage hosts cannot land without a back owner, and the failure message names the file.

**Verified (X6532, physical, full walk).** Rules→BACK→Home; in-editor (Chrome rule)→BACK→Rules list; Recovery→History spoke (biometric gate prompt shown; BACK dismissed the prompt staying on "Your history is locked.", BACK again → hub); Streaks "What to expect"→BACK→hub; Settings→Protection pane→BACK→Settings index→BACK→Home; Recovery→"Log an urge" sheet→BACK→dismissed to hub; Home→BACK→previous app (the one intended exit). `BlockerActivity` (the block overlay) swallows back via its committed `OnBackPressedCallback(true)` — back cannot dismiss the wall.

**Honest limits.** The guard is per-file, not per-state: a file with two independent subpage states gets a single `BackHandler` reference of unknown completeness (the convention all current hosts follow is one state per host, so no current file exploits this). The ceremony's back path is statically covered plus exercised by its cancel test, not a dedicated device leg. The walk covered every host on the X6532 but not every spoke — spokes with no internal state delegate upward by construction.

## #110 — The Review Pass: Tokens, A11y and Adaptive Type Re-Measured on the Swept Surfaces; the Instrument's Own Failures Named (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Session-plan task #9 (review pass over every swept surface: tokens, states, a11y, adaptive sizing) and #10 (final adaptive-type verification), with one explicit open leg carried from the block-wall pass: **EXPANDED (≥840dp) had never been device-verified** (ADR #86 closed with "EXPANDED remains unverified — task #10").

**Tokens — dark and light, pixel-measured.** All five capturable tabs captured (Recovery returns 0 bytes to `screencap` by design — `FLAG_SECURE`, structurally verified per #29) and scanned for off-token hue (HSV 255–300°, s>0.18, v>0.20 — the historical defect class is undefined M3 slots leaking baseline purple). **Zero off-token pixels in either theme.** Every flagged pixel was the blocked app's own launcher icon in a Rules row (Instagram's gradient at two scroll positions in dark, Instagram again in the EXPANDED light pass) — the icon is the mandated real brand mark, not UI chrome.

**A11y — all six tabs, a11y-tree scan.** Every clickable node checked for an accessible name (own, or in its subtree) and for a ≥48dp touch target. **Zero unnamed clickables, zero undersized targets.** Two initial flags were ruled out as uiautomator artifacts of clipped rows under the floating nav bar: the Settings "unnamed clickable" was the partially-visible Risk windows row whose text descendants the dump prunes when offscreen (same row fully named and present after a scroll), and the Rules "40dp switch" was the same pruning at the list's clipped edge — at full visibility every switch measures 52×48dp exactly.

**Adaptive type — all three window classes now device-verified.** `WindowWidth.typeScaleFactor` (Adaptive.kt: COMPACT 1.0 / MEDIUM 1.125 / EXPANDED 1.25) is applied centrally through the theme's scaled `Typography`. COMPACT is the X6532's native 360dp. MEDIUM was verified in the block-wall pass (×1.125 exact on the warning banner, ADR #86). **EXPANDED verified now** via `wm density 137` (720px → 840.9dp): the Settings headline measured 28.5dp → 35.0dp (×1.23 measured against the ×1.25 expectation — 1px integer rounding at 0.86 px/dp), and the shell switched to its **navigation rail** at the ≥840dp breakpoint (`EXPANDED_RAIL_BREAKPOINT_DP`) — rail labels 21.0dp scaled. Home + Settings captured at EXPANDED: no clipping, no overlap, cards reflow cleanly. Density, night mode and the app's foreground state restored in the same command chain; `wm density` re-reads 320.

**The instrument's own failure modes, named so they are not re-run as findings.** (a) uiautomator prunes the text descendants of rows clipped by a scroll viewport while still emitting the row's clickable node — this produces phantom "unnamed clickable" and "undersized target" reports at list edges; re-dump with the row fully visible before believing either. (b) Reading a rendered numeral from a downscaled screenshot misread the Blocks tile's "8" as "0" at EXPANDED's small scale; the a11y text dump and a correctly-scaled crop both read 8 with the rules list in agreement (tile and list share one `rules` source — `blocks = rules?.size`, HomeScreen.kt:207 — so disagreement is structurally impossible). The reviewing instrument needs the same skepticism as the code.

**Cognitive-load probe re-run (all six tabs).** Home 2 / Rules 14 / Recovery 6 / Streaks 3 / Settings 6 in-page choices — at or within ±1 of the pinned baselines; the probe's raw count includes 5 nav items. Partner measures **13** against the 4 baseline, fully explained by device data rather than UI: the "People you support" list now renders **7 enrollments** — the owner's real pair ("Mama", Sep 19) plus **six code-named test enrollments** (Sep 16/18) left by the multi-user pairing device legs. This is test residue on the owner's device, flagged for cleanup through the production unpair UI (which doubles as #66's pending production verification); the UI itself renders the list correctly (`Rename`-named pencils, selection ring, dates).

**Suite.** Full JVM run on the current tree: **1295 tests, 0 failures, 0 skipped, 155 classes** — all guard tests green (`PaneBackAffordanceTest`, `NoLiteralTypeSizeTest`, `ScaledTypographyTest`, `SecureSurfacesTest`, `LiteralCopyGuardTest`, `NamedSwitchTest`, `ForeignCloseConfirmationTest`, …).

**Honest limits.** "Every page scales" rests on three legs: the scaling is central (theme Typography), literal type sizes are guarded out by `NoLiteralTypeSizeTest`, and device spot-checks cover Home + Settings at EXPANDED plus the block walls at MEDIUM — it is not a per-page per-class device matrix. The token scan samples every 3rd pixel (dense sampling of solid UI colors; a 1–2px antialias fringe could hide ≤ tens of pixels). The Partner residue count depends on the owner's cleanup decision; the six test pairs also feed #52's multi-user scope.

## #111 — One Activity, Every Pair: Own Activity Fans Out to All N Enrollments (schema v12) (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Task #52, recorded in #99 and #110: with N enrollments (§19.5 — one partner supports N users), the user's **own** activity — enforced blocks, overrides, finished recovery sessions — mirrored to only **one** of the N pairs. Root cause: `attempt_log.event_sequence` / `recovery_session.event_sequence` were single-valued columns, the pending queries selected `WHERE event_sequence IS NULL`, and `PartnerSyncCoordinator.syncOnce` runs the per-pair reconcile over every enrollment — the **first** pair's pass linked every pending row (setting the column) and every later pair found nothing pending. The recorded semantics ruling (shipped-pending-veto, same posture as the `PartnerLabel` decision): own activity is device-level and every enrolled partner watches the same user, so the correct behavior is **fan-out — one emitted copy per pair, each into that pair's own chain** (own sequence, own per-pair CEK ciphertext the pair can decrypt).

**What shipped.**
- **Two per-pair link tables (schema v11→v12).** `attempt_mirror_link(attempt_id, pair_id, sequence)` and `recovery_mirror_link(session_id, pair_id, sequence)`, composite PKs, no indices (the PK covers both lookup shapes) — native key types rather than one kind-column table, mirroring the two source tables.
- **Migration `MIGRATION_11_12`** — additive `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, DDL verbatim against the exported `12.json`; plus a rollback companion `MIGRATION_12_11` (DROP both) matching the `MIGRATION_10_9` precedent. Re-upgrade after a downgrade rebuilds the tables empty, so rows delivered while on v11 re-mirror as duplicates on the next warm sync — never data loss, bounded to the downgrade window.
- **No backfill, deliberately.** The pre-v12 record does not say *which* pair received a delivery, so a backfill would either duplicate it to every pair or silently mark undelivered rows as delivered. Instead `event_sequence` stays as the **pre-v12 legacy marker** and the pending queries exclude legacy-linked rows for **every** pair — the old delivery is never duplicated; the historical N−1 gap is accepted and recorded.
- **DAO contract.** `pendingEventCreation(pairId, limit)` = `event_sequence IS NULL AND NOT EXISTS(link for :pairId) ORDER BY occurred ASC LIMIT`; `linkEvent(id, pairId, sequence)` = `INSERT OR IGNORE` — re-running a pair's pass is a no-op that keeps the first delivery's sequence (per-pair exactly-once); `linkSequence(id, pairId)` reads the link back. `RecoverySessionDao` mirrors this on `session_id`, keeping its terminal-state + technique-bearing filters; `RecoverySessionRepository` wraps the pair-aware pair and the coordinator (both reconcile paths) passes the `pairId` and links per pair.

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** Focused 28 tests / 0 failures (`AttemptLogTest` 4, `RecoverySessionRepositoryTest` 7, `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest` 14, `AppRulesStoreMigrationTest` 3); full suite after the change: **1,298 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped, 155 classes**.
- **The decisive test, `multiPair_oneActivityRow_mirrorsToEveryPair_exactlyOnce`:** two enrollments (second with its own EC keypairs), one blocked attempt + one recovery session → `report.eventsLinked == 4`; each pair's chain holds both events at its own sequences; nothing stays pending for either pair; the **second pair decrypts the same activity text through its own derived CEK**; a second sync links nothing (per-pair exactly-once). `linkEvent_recordsPerPairSequence_withoutHidingTheRowFromOtherPairs` (pair-a linked → pair-b still sees the row; relink is a no-op), `legacyLinkedRow_isExcludedForEveryPair`, and the recovery twin ride alongside; the 2→12 migration chain test runs through Room's own post-migration schema validation, and the instrumented suite covers 11→12 up, rollback, and re-upgrade.
- **Device (X6532, physical).** After `install -r` over the live v11 install, `mirror-read` on the real SQLCipher database: `user_version=12` (the store open ran the real v11→v12 migration — a failure would have thrown and logged instead), `attempt_mirror_link rows=0` / `recovery_mirror_link rows=0` (both tables exist and are queryable; empty by design — no backfill), and the source census `attempts=33 attemptsLegacyLinked=33 sessions=7 sessionsLegacyLinked=7` — every pre-v12 row carries the legacy marker, so none of them will be re-delivered to a second pair (never duplicated; the N−1 historical gap stands exactly as recorded). No crash buffer entries after the migration, app process healthy, and the FGS foreground again post-update (ADR #108's re-arm).
- **Debug seam.** `mirror-read` on `DeviceVerificationReceiver` prints `PRAGMA user_version` (the store open runs the real migration first — v12 only if it executed on this device's SQLCipher DB), both link tables' counts and per-pair group-by (the fan-out census), and the legacy `event_sequence` census on both source tables.

**Honest limits.** Fan-out is N×: with N pairs and K pending rows the sync writes N×K link rows and N×K chain events — accepted; §19.5's N is a small roster. Pre-v12 deliveries to one pair never reach the other N−1 (chosen over duplication; recorded). `event_sequence` is now dead weight on new writes — retained only as the legacy marker; dropping it is deferred until no pre-v12 installs remain. The emission half of the device leg (a real attempt fanned out from a real device with N real enrollments) needs the warm-window biometric tap (same combined owner ask as #39/#66) — the JVM test proves the semantics end-to-end with real crypto, so the device half is confirmation, not the only evidence.


## #112 — Signing Falls Back to the Screen Lock: Owner-Ruled Device-Credential Path (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner ruling, 2026-09-28: *"a user can fallback to whatever they have like typing their password"* (the same ruling answered the ask as "Allow PIN/pattern fallback"). It **supersedes plan §5.20's biometric-only rule** (line 279: "show an explicit message rather than silently degrading to … device credential") and the §12.3 test built on it (line 425) — both are annotated in place; owners-over-plan deviations are recorded, not rewritten. Grounding facts, all verified before implementing (research-playbook discipline):

- **Keystore params are immutable after generation** — approving/unpairing keys already on disk cannot gain `AUTH_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL` later. Rotation is not a migration path: `IdentityMigration.migrate` is rotation-**as-revocation** (destroys every enrollment; partners re-enroll). So the ruling can only apply to keys generated from now on; pre-ruling keys keep the biometric path and refuse the fallback with an explicit reason.
- **Legacy API mapping (Tier 1, android-36 sources `KeyGenParameterSpec.java:1511`):** `setUserAuthenticationValidityDurationSeconds(>0)` delegates to `setUserAuthenticationParameters(seconds, AUTH_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL | AUTH_BIOMETRIC_STRONG)` — so API<30 devices' keys already accept device credential (that path is untouched; it was always this shape).
- **Time-bound keys resolve via the lock screen (Tier 1, same javadoc, lines 1483-1491):** operations throw `UserNotAuthenticatedException` when the window is cold; "can be resolved by the user unlocking the secure lock screen … or by going through the confirm credential flow initiated by `KeyguardManager.createConfirmDeviceCredentialIntent`". That intent is therefore not a workaround — it is the documented resolver for exactly this key type, and it works on every API level ≥21 (one mechanism, no per-level branching; `androidx.biometric`'s DEVICE_CREDENTIAL support is API-29+ framework-only and forbids a CryptoObject with device credential, so it was deliberately **not** used for this path).
- **No public capability-query API:** `android.security.keystore.KeyCharacteristics` is not in the platform jar (javap: class not found, android-33/36); the only query surface is the deprecated single-value `KeyInfo.getUserAuthenticationType()` with unverified multi-type semantics. Hence the fallback is **attempt-based**: offer, confirm, and let `initSign` be the real gate (it is the gate regardless — keystore enforces auth types).
- **Server unaffected:** `functions/attestation.js` asserts origin `GENERATED`, purpose `SIGN`, securityLevel ≥ TEE, and challenge equality only — no auth-type assertion — so broadening client auth types needs no functions/rules redeploy.

**What shipped.**
- **Key generation** (`AccountabilityKeyStore.generateSigningKey`, API ≥30 branch): `setUserAuthenticationParameters(AUTH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS, AUTH_BIOMETRIC_STRONG or AUTH_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL)` — the 900 s time window (NFR4) is unchanged; device credential joins as an accepted authenticator.
- **`SigningPath.kt`** (new, `accountability/crypto/`): the pure decision matrix — `chooseSigningPath(biometricsAvailable, biometricsNoneEnrolled, deviceCredentialPossible)` → Biometric / DeviceCredential / Blocked(reason); plus the `NoSignatureReason` taxonomy (`NOT_ENROLLED`, `UNAVAILABLE`, `SCREEN_LOCK_REJECTED`) shared by every "no signature" dialog.
- **`MainActivity.signWithAuthPrompt`** (renamed from `signWithBiometric` — it no longer only prompts biometrics): a working strong-biometric prompt is used whenever it can run (unchanged UX, unchanged two-path CryptoObject contract); otherwise the screen-lock confirm flow signs. A biometric prompt ending in `ERROR_LOCKOUT`/`ERROR_LOCKOUT_PERMANENT` now falls through to the screen lock instead of reading as a cancel; a plain cancel stays a cancel.
- **`signWithDeviceCredential`**: registers `ActivityResultContracts.StartActivityForResult` at construction (ComponentActivity rejects post-STARTED registration), raises `createConfirmDeviceCredentialIntent`, and gates on `initSign` after `RESULT_OK`. Keystore work stays off the main thread; the launch is on it.
- **Dialogs/strings**: the explicit message now appears only when nothing can sign — no biometric + no secure lock (NOT_ENROLLED / UNAVAILABLE, copy updated to name the screen lock), or a pre-ruling key refusing the confirm (SCREEN_LOCK_REJECTED, with the enroll route as the fix).
- **Harness leg**: `PairingTestActivity` gains "Sign via screen lock (ADR #112 fallback)" — confirm-credential → sign a probe → verify against the device's own public key → `SCREEN-LOCK SIGN OK` / `SCREEN-LOCK REJECTED` in the harness log (the honest answer for a pre-ruling key).

**Verified.** JVM: `SigningPathTest` (4 cases: biometrics-win, both fallback shapes, both blocked shapes) + full suite (1,302 tests, 0 failures — see the close-out run). Device: the harness leg on the X6532 (screen-lock confirm → verified signature); five runs, recorded with the batched owner tap ask.

**Honest limits.** Pre-ruling keys (generated before this ADR on API ≥30) are biometric-only and immutable: their signatures require a biometric-typed auth token, so a screen-lock confirm alone cannot authorize them once the warm window is cold (`UserNotAuthenticatedException` → SCREEN_LOCK_REJECTED dialog); enabling the fallback on such a device requires rotating the key, which by protocol means re-enrolling every partner — deliberately not automated, owner-decided per device. There is no capability pre-check (see above), so a pre-ruling key costs the user one screen-lock confirm before the refusal message. The fallback is reached only when biometrics cannot run — a working biometric prompt is never bypassed in favour of the screen lock. **[Amended 2026-10-01 — revision below: the screen lock is also user-reachable from the prompt itself, without a lockout.]**

**Revision 2026-09-28 (task #67 device evidence — corrects the pre-claim).** The X6532 harness ran five times: four fingerprint-confirmed runs (R1–R4; the sensor IRQ is in the HAL log) and one pattern-confirmed run (R5 — the owner reported the "Use pattern" option on the confirm prompt; his lock is a pattern). R1 logged `SCREEN-LOCK SIGN INVALID` from a **harness bug** — the probe signed with raw `signature.sign()` and verified via `AccountabilitySigner.verify`, which rejects high-S (§10): raw keystore output is high-S ~50% of the time; the harness now normalizes with `finishSign` and the production path was never affected. R2–R5 all logged `SCREEN-LOCK SIGN OK — verified against the device public key`. **The pre-ruling-key refusal was NOT reproduced on the owner's device** — the earlier "refusal shape" claim in `Verified` is withdrawn: R1–R4 were authorized by their own fresh biometric auth (the expected path for a biometric-only key), and R5's pattern confirm ran ~45 s after R4, inside the 900 s window a successful unlock deposits (NFR4's own timeout) — a still-valid biometric-typed token can authorize the key, so R5 is not evidence that a device-credential token does. Device-credential isolation needs a ≥900 s no-biometric window — impractical on an actively used phone. The emulator (its PIN pad is the only auth used during the run) is the isolating rig; its isolation legs for both the pre-ruling refusal and the post-ruling acceptance are carried in task #67, stated blocked-explicitly until run.

**Revision 2026-09-28 (late, task #67 Leg A — cold-window refusal REPRODUCED on the X6532, supersedes the emulator plan).** The blocking precondition above (a genuinely cold ≥900 s window) was met on the owner's own phone, so the isolation legs ran on the physical X6532 — no emulator needed (Leg A: pre-ruling key; Leg B: post-ruling key). Cold-window arithmetic, verified from the logs before the run: last biometric auth event anywhere in the system was the WhatsApp fingerprint at 19:50:23.877 (HAL `onAuthenticated(true)`); +900 s ⇒ cold at 20:05:23.868; logcat cleared; no newer `onAuthenticated` before the run. Button tapped at ~20:06:20 → confirm bouncer focused (`dumpsys window`: `com.android.systemui` keyguard confirm) → owner drew his **pattern** at **20:06:52.533** → `I MyExodusPairing: SCREEN-LOCK REJECTED — key refused the signature (pre-ADR #112 biometric-only key; immutable — enroll a biometric)`. This is the first run on the owner's device where the signature attempt could not draw on any still-valid token: the pattern confirm did not deposit a token the biometric-only key accepts (`UserNotAuthenticatedException` at `initSign`), which is exactly the R5 ambiguity resolved — device-credential auth does not satisfy a `BIOMETRIC_STRONG`-only key, empirically, in a verified-cold window. The `Verified` claim above ("screen-lock confirm → verified signature") stands only for a warm biometric window; the honest shape on a pre-ruling key is: biometric prompt works, screen-lock fallback refuses with the explicit message, as the dialog taxonomy documents.

**Revision 2026-09-28 (same evening, task #67 Leg B — post-ruling acceptance, device-credential isolated, PROVEN).** The harness gained a Leg B button ("ADR #112 Leg B: new post-ruling key → screen lock"): it generates a throwaway key under `rig_post112_probe_key` mirroring `AccountabilityKeyStore.generateSigningKey`'s post-ruling spec exactly (`secp256r1`, DIGEST_SHA256, `setUserAuthenticationRequired(true)`, `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(false)`, API ≥30: `setUserAuthenticationParameters(900, AUTH_BIOMETRIC_STRONG or AUTH_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL)` — `AUTH_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` referenced from the production object, not duplicated), raises the same confirm-credential intent, signs through `finishSign` and verifies against the probe's own public key, deleting the probe on every terminal path. Two runs, both on the X6532:
- **Run 1 (20:22:30) — attribution corrected, superseded.** With the window cold beforehand (no successful auth since the Leg A pattern at 20:06:52; cold point 20:21:52), the owner authenticated the bouncer with his **fingerprint** rather than the pattern (self-reported immediately after; HAL `onAuthenticated` 20:22:29.482, requestId 113, owner `com.android.settings`) → sign OK. That run proves the post-ruling key accepts a fresh *biometric* auth delivered through the confirm-credential flow; it does not isolate device-credential acceptance — a fingerprint deposits a `BIOMETRIC_STRONG` token that would authorize any key allowing that class regardless of the pattern. Recorded here as corrected, not erased.
- **Run 2 (20:38:53) — the isolating acceptance leg, clean.** Window verified cold for every auth class: last biometric success 20:22:29.482 (cold at 20:37:29.5); logcat shows the phone **never re-locked** after the bouncer (no screen-off, `isKeyguardShowing:false` at every activity transition, so no unlock deposited a token either) and zero `onAuthenticated` lines in between. At the run, the fingerprint client was armed by the bouncer at 20:38:45.108 and then **cancelled without authenticating** (`cancelAuthentication` 20:38:49.973, `success: false`, requestId 114 — log proof the biometric path was not used), the owner drew his **pattern**, and → `20:38:53.294 POST-112 KEY: screen-lock confirm accepted by the key` / `20:38:53.310 POST-112 KEY: SCREEN-LOCK SIGN OK — verified against the probe public key`. **No token of any class existed in any window; the device-credential confirm alone authorized the signature.** This is exactly the scenario production ships for a device with no enrolled biometric, and it works on XOS.

Together with Leg A (a pattern confirm does not authorize a `BIOMETRIC_STRONG`-only key), the biometric/device-credential question is closed empirically in both directions on the owner's device. The emulator isolation plan is superseded — both legs ran on the physical X6532.

**Revision 2026-10-01 (owner ruling during the first real two-device ceremony — the screen lock becomes user-reachable, not lockout-triggered).** On the X6532C (device B of the two-phone rig), the pairing ceremony's approval prompt offered fingerprint only; the screen-lock route existed solely as a lockout fallback, so an owner whose fingerprint won't read had no way forward. Owner ruling, verbatim: *"we need a fallback if we cant use fingerprint we use password typing"* — the screen lock must be reachable **whenever the user chooses**, not only after `ERROR_LOCKOUT`. The shipped change:

- **`biometricSign` prompt** (`MainActivity`): the secondary button is now **"Use screen lock"** (`partner_signing_use_screen_lock`) instead of "Cancel". `ERROR_NEGATIVE_BUTTON` maps to a new `BiometricOutcome.UseDeviceCredential`, which `signWithAuthPrompt` routes to the existing `signWithDeviceCredential` confirm flow — the Leg-B-proven mechanism, no new auth machinery. Lockout routing is unchanged; a dismissal (back / outside where the OEM allows it) stays a plain cancel → the action reads as nothing having happened and the screen stays put.
- **Why the negative-button handoff and not an in-dialog "Use PIN"**: androidx `BiometricPrompt` throws `IllegalArgumentException` if a `CryptoObject` is passed with `DEVICE_CREDENTIAL` allowed, and `PromptInfo` forbids a negative button when `DEVICE_CREDENTIAL` is allowed. The warm-path CryptoObject contract (per-op auth binding, kept deliberately) cannot coexist with the combined prompt, so the in-dialog credential affordance was impossible without weakening the warm path. One label + one outcome routes **both** prompt shapes (warming and cold) through the proven confirm screen.
- **Reach**: the change sits in the shared `signWithAuthPrompt` — ceremony approval, request approval, and unpair all acquire the escape hatch at once (the same function was already shared; only the prompt config changed). No keystore, rules, or functions change.
- **Verification**: compile-gated (`:app:assembleDebug`) + live on the two-phone rig (ceremony approval via the "Use screen lock" button — device evidence recorded in the pairing plan's §12.3 run notes).

The amendment does not shrink ADR #112's scope: the key params, the decision matrix (`chooseSigningPath`), and the explicit-message taxonomy are untouched. It changes only *when* the user may choose the screen lock.

**Revision 2026-10-01 (late, task #17 — the combined sheet supersedes the negative-button handoff above).** Owner feedback on the handoff: tapping "Use screen lock" dismissed the biometric sheet and opened a SECOND system dialog before any credential entry — the single-dialog goal was not met. The rejection above rested on a claim true only below API 30: androidx.biometric **1.1.0 sources verified in the gradle cache** (`BiometricPrompt.authenticate(PromptInfo, CryptoObject)`) throw "Crypto-based authentication is not supported for device credential prior to API 30" **only when `SDK_INT < 30` and DEVICE_CREDENTIAL is allowed**; on API 30+ the combined crypto + credential prompt is legal and forwarded to the platform (Android 11's device-credential crypto support; the signing key already carries `AUTH_BIOMETRIC_STRONG or AUTH_DEVICE_CREDENTIAL`, ADR #112 key spec). The signing surfaces cannot run below API 31 (in-Keystore ECDH floor), so the pre-30 restriction is unreachable — the warm-path CryptoObject contract is preserved, not weakened. **What shipped:** `biometricSign`'s `PromptInfo` now allows `BIOMETRIC_STRONG or DEVICE_CREDENTIAL` with no negative button (forbidden with credential — `build()` throws), so the sheet carries the "Use PIN/pattern" affordance inline and credential entry happens in the same dialog. `ERROR_NEGATIVE_BUTTON` mapping and `BiometricOutcome.UseDeviceCredential` deleted; the `LockedOut` → `signWithDeviceCredential` fallback stays for OEM sheets that still raise lockout. `SigningPath.DeviceCredential` and `signWithDeviceCredential` stay — the no-biometrics route was already a single dialog. `partner_signing_use_screen_lock` string removed. **Verification:** compile-gated (`:app:compileDebugKotlin`); device leg pending — the two-device journey's approval and unpair prompts exercise the new sheet.

## #113 — Enrollment-Change Invalidation: The Poll and the Sign-Time Seams, Wired (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Task #68 (plan-fidelity reconciliation). The accountability protocol plan's §8.2/§9 require the app to detect biometric-enrollment changes at startup and on resume and "trigger Identity Migration flow immediately", and its §9 KPE row says the same for `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException`. The machinery for both (`BiometricEnrollmentMonitor`, `IdentityMigration`, `rotateKeys()`, `IdentityResetDialog`) had shipped with **zero production callers**, and `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException` was imported-but-unused — the docs described behavior the code never ran. Grounding facts (Tier 1, android-36 `KeyGenParameterSpec.java` javadoc, verified before implementing): `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment` "applies only to keys which require user authentication … and if no positive validity duration has been set" — our signing key carries a 900 s positive duration, so that flag is a **no-op for it** (the v2 pairing plan's `(true)` at line 277 was inert); and enrollment-change invalidation is never raised for such a key. KPE *is* real for it when the secure lock screen is removed/reset, and it surfaces **at sign time**, not at generation or generation-time checks. Conclusion: the resume poll is the only mechanism for enrollment changes, and a sign-time seam is mandatory for lock-screen removal — both were missing.

**What shipped.**
- **Monitor contract, split read/write** (`BiometricEnrollmentMonitor`, plain `SharedPreferences` — deliberately not `EncryptedSharedPreferences`; the cached value is baseline state, not a secret): `enrollmentStateChanged()` is **read-only** and the first run establishes the baseline — never a change, so an app upgrade cannot rotate a healthy identity; detection never writes. `commitEnrollmentState()` is called **only after a successful migration**, so a failed rotation retries on the next resume instead of being silently swallowed. `BiometricManager.from()` adapted to `getSystemService(BIOMETRIC_SERVICE)` (ADR #17's stub-removal list).
- **Sign-time classification** (`AccountabilitySigner.Invalidation` helpers): `isUserNotAuthenticated(e)` / `isPermanentlyInvalidated(e)` — **exact-type checks**, so an unrelated exception can never be misread as invalidation and trigger a destructive migration.
- **`MainActivity` wiring.** Poll: `onResume → checkIdentityEnrollment()` (MainActivity.kt:240, :745) — change detected → migration; success → `commitEnrollmentState()`; `reEnrollmentRequired > 0` → `IdentityResetDialog` (MainActivity.kt:505). Sign-time: `biometricSign` pre-prompt/post-auth and `signWithDeviceCredential` onFailure route KPE to `onKeyPermanentlyInvalidated()` (MainActivity.kt:577, :726, :763), which runs the **same single-flight migration** (`@Volatile identityMigrationInFlight`, MainActivity.kt:774) — poll and sign time cannot double-rotate. Migration = `IdentityMigration.migrate`: rotate keys → epoch bump → enrollments purged → `Migrated(epoch, reEnrollmentRequired = pairs.size)`; `Failed(reason)` on rotation throw (nothing committed).
- **Orphaned pair keys shredded (found while wiring).** `IdentityMigration` deletes every enrollment row but never touched each pair's `accountability_pair_agreement_key_*` keystore entry — rotation was leaving private keys orphaned in the AndroidKeyStore, unreferenced and undeletable by any later path. Since **rotation IS revocation** (§9), `destroyAllKeys()` — the irreversible full-wipe path also used by uninstall/reset — now enumerates `keyStore.aliases()` and deletes every pair-agreement alias (collected to a list first; deleting while enumerating is not safe). Single choke point, one caller (`rotateKeys()`).

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `BiometricEnrollmentMonitorTest` (3): first-run-is-baseline-never-a-change, same-state-after-commit-is-not-a-change, differs-until-committed. `AccountabilitySignerInvalidationTest` (2): `UserNotAuthenticatedException` and `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException` each classify only for their own type. Full suite on the final code: **1,307 tests, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped (158 classes)**.
- **Compile-gated only:** the pair-key shred — Robolectric does not exercise the real AndroidKeyStore, so no JVM test is possible; it is one loop over a documented API, compile- and suite-validated.
- **Device-exercised (X6532, 2026-10-01, owner-authorized enrollment change): the poll's full transition pair.** Fingerprint removed (HAL `dumpsys fingerprint` `count: 0`; `canAuthenticate` 0→11) → HOME + `am start` → resume poll logged `enrollment change detected — running identity migration` then `identity migration done: epoch=1 reEnrollment=0`; prefs committed `0|true → 11|true`; `identity_epoch` row 0→1 (DB pulled and queried). Fingerprint re-added (`count: 1`) → same resume sequence → `identity migration done: epoch=2`, prefs committed back `11|true → 0|true`, `identity_epoch` 1→2. The "migration done" line is only reachable when `rotateKeys()` returns, so both rotations completed without throwing. No `IdentityResetDialog` in either leg — correct: the device was unpaired, so `reEnrollmentRequired = pairs.size = 0` (no connections were cut). **Still unverified by this run:** the pair-key shred inside `rotateKeys()` had no pair-agreement keys on this unpaired device (rides on the compile-gated note above), and the `reEnrollmentRequired > 0` dialog path remains JVM-covered only — a paired re-run would close both.
- **Not device-exercised:** the KPE seam (lock-screen removal → sign → migration) — it needs the owner's lock screen removed with a live sign; the seam's logic is JVM-tested, the wiring is exercised by every resume in normal use.
- **Baseline receipt (device).** On the X6532 the monitor's prefs hold `enrollment_state=0|true` (`BIOMETRIC_SUCCESS | isDeviceSecure=true`), written after this wiring shipped (prefs mtime 2026-09-28 18:47) and unchanged across subsequent resumes and `install -r` — the first-run-is-baseline path ran in production, and no spurious rotation fired (every enrollment intact). Detecting a *change* still requires an enrollment mutation, per the limit below. **[Superseded 2026-10-01: the deliberate enrollment mutation above flipped the committed value `0|true → 11|true → 0|true`, exactly as this bullet predicted.]**

**Honest limits.** Detection is coarse — the poll fires on the next app resume after an enrollment change, not instantly; that is the platform ceiling (the OS cannot raise the event for a time-bound key). The KPE response is a **full Identity Migration — every pair must re-pair — deliberately heavier than the v2 pairing plan §8.4's in-place signing-key refresh** (annotated in that plan); the protocol plan §9's "trigger Identity Migration flow immediately" is the governing requirement, and revocation-by-rotation is the stronger guarantee. §14's `biometric_enrollment_change_detected` telemetry is deliberately not shipped (annotated in the protocol plan) — no consumer exists in the shipped telemetry surface. The v2 plan's `setInvalidatedByBiometricEnrollment(true)` (line 277) is annotated as the verified no-op it is; removing the call itself was not done (it costs nothing and is correct on any future key shape without the duration).

## #114 — Unpair When the Partner Is Unresolvable: Local-Only Shred, Never a Trap (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Task #70 (#66c). On the X6532, the production Partner hub listed residue test pairs whose `/pairings/{pairId}` documents the tester's account can no longer read (participant removed / doc deleted → Firestore `PERMISSION_DENIED`). `endPartnership` resolved the partner identities first and **aborted on null** — the user could not unpair those pairs at all, and successive unpair taps from earlier sessions threw (#66's second failure mode). This contradicted the v2 protocol plan §9 line 460 verbatim: "Partner unreachable during unpair | AD-13b path: local shred proceeds unconditionally; remote state accurately reported as unconfirmed, then `LOCAL_ONLY_TERMINATED` after 7 days." The plan already specified the behavior; the code demanded a partner uid the plan says is not required.

**What shipped.**
- **`BilateralDeletion.initiate(pairId, authorUid: String?, partnerUid: String?, signature)`** — nullable uids. Null = §9's "partner unreachable" row: no message id, no event created, empty envelope journaled, no drain; the local shred still runs unconditionally (AD-13b). Re-entry on such a row neither extends the 7-day window nor loses the row.
- **`reachable` gating** — event creation and `drainAllPending` run only when both uids exist; a creation failure (chain gap/fork, crypto-dead key) still leaves the journal honest (empty envelope → no delivery → timeout, never a claim).
- **Unified continuation early-return** — a stored row that already has an envelope, is a responder row, or whose enrollment is gone continues (`shredIfPending` + resume by role) instead of restarting or deleting; the row is the only report of the remote leg.
- **Superseded-row guard** — a shredded row with a *live* enrollment for the same pairId (re-pair reuses the pairId, AD-07) is dropped so the tap acts on the live pair: without it, `initiate` would "resume" the old request and terminate server state while the live pair stayed enrolled and unshredded. JVM-pinned by `rePairedPair_tapActsOnTheLivePair_notTheShreddedRow`.
- **`MainActivity.endPartnership`** — resolve → null logs `pair X: partner identity unresolved — local-only unpair` and calls `initiate` with null uids; the consent prompt (biometric / screen-lock, ADR #112) is unchanged for both paths.
- **`resumeRequester` tolerates a null partner uid** — no poll and no delivery attempt (none is possible); the 7-day timeout still fires → `terminate` rides along (a denied terminate for a no-longer-member is a documented no-op) → `LOCAL_ONLY_TERMINATED`, never a claim.

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `BilateralDeletionTest` 16/16 — `unresolvablePartner_localShredRuns_neverClaimingThePartner` (shred runs, no drain/delivery, re-entry neither extends the window nor loses the row, timeout → LOCAL_ONLY_TERMINATED with the terminate attempt) and `rePairedPair_tapActsOnTheLivePair_notTheShreddedRow` (superseded-row guard) among them. Full suite on the final code: **1,309 tests, 158 classes, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**. The installed APK (19:09 build) is dex-verified to carry the `local-only unpair` log string.
- **Device (X6532, production UI, residue test pairs).** Log chain per pair: `W/Firestore … PERMISSION_DENIED` → `MyExodusPairUidResolver: pair X: pairing document missing or has no members field` → `MyExodus: pair X: partner identity unresolved — local-only unpair` → BiometricPrompt (`Biometrics/AuthenticationClient: onAuthenticated(true), Owner: com.example.myexodus`) → shred. Journal ground truth (pulled `myexodus_accountability.db`): six REQUESTER rows with `partner_uid` NULL, `message_id ''`, empty `request_envelope`, `delivered=0`, `local_shredded=1` (19:25:18.144, 19:27:29.968, 19:28:37.621, 19:38:06, 19:40:44, 19:42:14 — all six residue pairs). Roster 7→1: only the owner's real pair remains; its pre-v12 enrollment carries a NULL pair-key alias, so the legacy device-level key was never a shred target and survives (AD-22 canary intact). **Successive unpairs no longer throw** — #66's regression case re-verified in this same build (fingerprint-confirmed each time; `onAuthenticated(true)` requestIDs 106–109).
- **Note on the earlier "failed" retries:** two re-taps logged no resolve because the End button was below the fold after the roster shrank — the taps missed the button, not the protocol. Ground-truth DB rows + the fingerprint HAL log (auth at 19:28:36.670 for the third pair) confirmed all attempts landed.

**Honest limits.** A null-uid row can *never* confirm the partner's erase — delivery was impossible by construction, so it always lapses to `LOCAL_ONLY_TERMINATED` after 7 days; that is the honest report (§17 line 655 already forbids presenting it as erasure), not a degraded one. `PairUidResolver.resolve` maps every resolution failure to null — including a transient offline `UnavailableException`, not just `PERMISSION_DENIED` — so an offline unpair of a healthy pair also takes the local-only path: the request is never delivered later, because the uid is never persisted for it. That is consistent with §9's never-trap intent (the alternative is blocking the user on connectivity), and the terminal state reports unconfirmed. The unsuperseded-guard case where a live enrollment exists *and* the row is unshredded is treated as a crash mid-initiate (shred owed), never as supersession — supersession requires `localShredded` on the row.

## #115 — The Partner-Visible Activity Layer: Five Event Types, Journal-First Emission, One Projection Per Pair (schema v13) (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Owner-endorsed next feature (`project_next_feature` memory; plan `app/plans/partner-visible-activity-layer-implementation-plan.md`, 2,179 lines) — the largest V1 fan-out win: retire Req 12's partner leg, Req 14's partner leg, Req 20's coaching-steps gap, Req 15 entirely, close Req 6's offline-aggregation edge and open product decision #12, all on the shipped ADR #111 rails. The owner's directive scoped it as a **delta**: extend the existing deterministic canonical-CBOR `EventPayload`, emit journal-first from the existing write paths, fan out unchanged through the per-pair mirror link tables and the shipped mailbox transport, render a receiver-side summary, one aggregated notification. **Zero new dependencies; no Cloud Functions or Firestore rules change** — mailbox payloads are opaque to the relay.

**What shipped.**
- **Five V1 event types on the existing codec (§A.2).** `streak_updated`, `relapse_recorded`, `milestone_reached`, `coaching_step_updated`, `motivational_message` — each `pv=1`/`sv=1` with `stateAfter {daysFree, relapseCount, milestonesReached[], coachingProgress, eventOccurredAt}`. The encoder writes absent-when-unset fields and definite-length deterministic CBOR, so every pre-existing payload type keeps its exact hash-chain bytes (byte-stability is pinned by `EventPayloadTest`); `motivational_message` reuses the shipped `tx` field for the body (bounded ≤280 NFC) rather than adding a key.
- **Journal-first emission (§A.3).** `PartnerActivityJournal.emitForProfile` writes `partner_activity_outbox` in the *same transaction* as the domain write — inside `HabitRepository.evaluateOnce`/`relapseOnce`/`stampAchievedMilestones` and `RecoveryPlanRepository.add/update/delete` — so a crash can never produce a state change without its partner event. The envelope + signature are created later at reconcile time: **required**, not an optimization, because signing is biometric-gated (the 900 s warm window) and sequence is per-pair; the journal row is the durable record that makes the split-brain failure impossible.
