Consent gates at emission: `shareMilestones` gates `STREAK_UPDATED` (day-crossing only, to bound volume) + `MILESTONE_REACHED`; `shareRelapses` gates `RELAPSE_RECORDED`; coaching and motivational are ungated (no personal-content exposure). `moveItem` deliberately does not emit — reorder is invisible in a count.
- **Fan-out unchanged (§8.2/§19.5).** One third pending queue in `reconcileEvents` alongside attempts and sessions: `partner_activity_link(event_id, pair_id, sequence)` per pair, `INSERT OR IGNORE`, so own activity reaches **every** enrollment exactly once, each pair's chain carrying its own sequence and its own per-pair CEK ciphertext. `RECONCILE_BATCH`-bounded, and `Emit.Stop` (cold window) leaves rows pending for the next pass — never dropped, never double-delivered.
- **Receive projection + monotonic guard (§8.10).** `PartnerActivityReceiver.applyAppended` is the APPENDED hook: a Valid activity payload applies its `stateAfter` **wholesale** into `partner_activity_projection`, guarded by `:sequence > last_sequence` (a duplicate delivery re-applies an identical snapshot; a replay can never roll a card backwards). A Malformed or future-schema body is declined (`classifyActivity`, FR-17) while the chain event remains — the chain is the source of truth, the projection is a view. The motivational body is bound-checked **again at the receiver** (`PartnerActivityJournal.normalizeMessage`, one shared choke point for composer and receiver) because the composer's bound is not a receiver-side guarantee; an oversized or blank message is refused while its snapshot still applies — the message is the optional part, never the state.
- **Schema v13.** `partner_activity_outbox`, `partner_activity_link`, `partner_activity_projection` (pair_id PK, `last_sequence` with 0 sentinel, pinned text + timestamp), `MIGRATION_12_13` + rollback `MIGRATION_13_12`, exported `13.json`; forward-only, additive.
- **Partner UI (§7 Step 11).** PartnerScreen's hub gains a "How they're doing" summary card (dominant days-free stat, relapses, milestones, "Last shared …") and the Activity subpage leads with the Req 15 composer (three suggestions + free text, char counter, error states). **Honesty rules are structural, not copy:** a null aggregate renders "Nothing shared yet" instead of zeros; activity recency ("Last shared") and sync recency ("Last synced") are separate lines (§8.11 — a quiet partner is never reported as a broken sync); a non-HEALTHY chain appends "Some history is missing, so these numbers may be behind." The source is always the Room projection — never Firestore on paint.
- **Home arrival surface (Req 15).** The newest pinned message across pairs renders as a card near the top of Home ("Your partner sent you this", message verbatim, send time), read from a Room **Flow** (`observeLatestPinned`) so a message delivered by a background sync reaches a card already on screen; absent entirely when nothing has arrived. `relativeTimeText` moved from PartnerScreen's privates into `ui/ScheduleFormat.kt` (the shared formatter module) as its second consumer appeared. **[Amended 2026-10-01 (#12): `observeLatestPinned` (single newest across pairs) was superseded by `observePinnedMessages` (all pairs' newest, newest-first with a pair-id tiebreak) — the card now names the sender (roster read, generic title fallback) and pages across senders with prev/next arrows when several partners have sent one.]**
- **Notification delta (§8.12/8.13).** The shipped generic notification gained `VISIBILITY_PRIVATE` + an explicit public version (lockscreen learns nothing beyond the generic copy) and `FLAG_ONLY_ALERT_ONCE` for the activity alert (a burst through one sync pass alerts once); the approval notification deliberately does not set it (each request is its own actionable item).
- **Retention + orphan sweep (§8.17 delta, §A.3).** `sweepActivity` runs at the top of `syncOnce` **before** the empty-enrollment early return: bounded batches drop journal rows past the 30-day mailbox-TTL bound, and projections/links for pair ids no longer enrolled are pruned — self-healing after an unpair that never touched this store, with no `BilateralDeletion` coupling.

**Verified.**
- **JVM (this ADR's delta classes).** `PartnerActivityReceiverTest` 10/10 (wholesale apply, duplicate re-apply, lower-sequence ignore, malformed declined without touching the projection, future schema declined, non-activity untouched, message pinned in one transaction with its snapshot, oversized/blank refused while the snapshot applies, NFC-normalize+trim, newest-across-pairs), `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest` 20/20 (per-pair fan-out from the journal with each chain's own sequence and CEK, cold window leaves pending, unpaired stays local, retention/orphan sweep including on an unpaired device, motivational body on the wire), `PartnerNotificationsTest` 6/6 (privacy + public version + only-alert-once on the activity alert, not on the approval), `PartnerActivityJournalTest` 8/8, `EventPayloadTest` 18/18 + `CanonicalCborTest` 13/13 + `EnvelopeTest` 8/8 (byte-stability of the extension), `AppRulesStoreMigrationTest` 3/3 (v13 forward + rollback), `HabitRepositoryTest` 20/20 (emission + consent gates), `RecoveryPlanRepositoryTest` 9/9. Full-suite regression on the final code: **1,351 tests, 160 classes, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped** (delta added 42 tests across 2 new classes to the 1,309/158 baseline).
- **Two failure rounds during the build (recorded because they were real):** (1) three coordinator tests failed with zero linked events — root cause was the *test helper's* fixture timestamps (Nov 2023), which the new 30-day sweep correctly deleted before reconcile could emit; defaults now use "just written" timestamps with a comment stating why. (2) The remaining failure was a test-harness key bug — the second pair's CEK derived with the first pair's agreement key (BAD_DECRYPT); the test now mints the second pair's own keypairs, as its sibling test already did.
- **Compile-gated only:** the notification delta's tray rendering (Robolectric asserts flags, not pixels) and the Compose surfaces (no Compose JVM harness in this repo).

**Honest limits.** The device leg for the whole layer — real pairing, real emission, real fan-out, real render on the X6532 via the ADR #46 virtual-partner harness — **ran green on 2026-09-29** (`PartnerActivityLayerInstrumentedTest`, 1 test / 80.8 s; logcat `activityLayer=PASS … daysFree=30 relapses=1 milestones=[7,30] coaching=3 pinned="You're doing great — keep going" bIngestedA=1`), so the layer now has device evidence on top of the JVM evidence. Per-event sender state machines (Req 14's PENDING→SENT→ACK prose) are not the shipped shape: the durable local journal is the sender's record and the mailbox ACK is the delivery signal; no partner-viewing ACK exists. The receiver applies `stateAfter` wholesale, so a *stale* event delivered late cannot regress the card but also cannot be merged — supersession is by sequence only. Req 7's "server-side authority prevails" is not the shipped shape: the rails are end-to-end encrypted per pair, the server stores opaque envelopes, and authority is the signed hash-chained sender stream instead. The sweep's orphan prune builds one `IN (…)` list from every projected/linked pair id — beyond SQLite's ~999-variable ceiling the sweep would throw; the call is `runCatching`-guarded and logged, so the cost is a skipped sweep, not a failed pass (accepted: a personal install does not accumulate 999 pairs). §14's monitoring asks are partly shipped (outbox-age/notification-denied/T1 telemetry on the Crashlytics rail; durable chain state driving the in-app chain-status diagnostics) and partly deliberately not built (per-failure metric counters and the §14.2–14.4 server dashboards, which presuppose an ops team this direct-APK product does not have — ADR #15) — recorded as a scoped deviation in the plan's §A.1 table.

## #116 — Connected Instrumented Runs Are Destructive Fixtures: Emulator-Only, Enforced Twice (2026-09-28)

**Context.** Three `./gradlew :app:connectedDebugAndroidTest` runs were aimed at the physical X6532 during the activity-layer session (21:54–23:18 on 2026-09-28). AGP's UTP **uninstalls the app and the test APK when a run finishes** — logcat shows `deletePackageX` for both packages, then `pkg removed` — so the next install created a **fresh app UID** (10411 → 10412 → 10414) with an **empty data directory**. Forensics: `dumpsys package com.example.myexodus | grep firstInstallTime` → `2026-09-28 23:18:55` (a normal `install -r` keeps the old firstInstallTime and only advances lastUpdateTime); every file in the recreated data dir carries one identical 23:19 mtime; two `generateKek` lines in logcat mark the KEK regenerations. Destroyed and unrecoverable on the device: the AppRulesStore (rules, streaks, attempt history, milestones, coaching steps, usage ledger), the AndroidKeyStore KEK + wrapped DEK, the Firebase anonymous identity (a fresh UID mints a new anon user, so the partner mailbox and every pair document are unreachable — the owner's Mama pair orphaned a second time), the accessibility grant, POST_NOTIFICATIONS, usage-stats, the overlay appop and VPN consent.

**Why the existing ruling did not cover it.** The standing "physical device ONLY" ruling governs **verification legs** — `adb install -r` + `adb shell am instrument`, which never uninstall. A Gradle *connected* run is a different animal: it is a **destructive fixture**. UTP tears the install down afterwards, and the six `data/db` suites additionally call `RecoveryManager.resetAllData()` against production paths (the instrumentation process shares the app's UID and data directory).

**Decision — two independent guards, because either one alone can be bypassed.**
1. **Task-level (the primary guard):** `app/build.gradle.kts` — every `connected*AndroidTest` task fails fast in `doFirst` unless `ANDROID_SERIAL` names an emulator. Deliberately fail-fast rather than warn: a connected run that "mostly works" is the failure mode being eliminated.
2. **Suite-level (the belt):** `app/src/androidTest/.../data/db/DeviceSafety.kt` exposes `assumeEmulatorOnly()`; the six destructive suites (`AppRulesStore`, `ActivityLogStore`, `DatabaseMigration`, `MilestoneSchema`, `RulesStoreConversion`, `RulesStoreKeyManager`) call it as their first `@Before` statement, so they **skip** on non-emulator hardware even if invoked directly via `am instrument`. `AppRulesStoreMigrationInstrumentedTest` is deliberately **exempt** — it operates on its own throwaway `migration-helper-test.db` file and is the safe, valuable device-side migration leg. **Corrected by ADR #122 (2026-09-29): the assumption covers the test body only — JUnit still runs `@After`, so every destructive call now routes through `resetAllDataEmulatorOnly()`, which no-ops on a handset. See #122 for the incident this cost and the guard that now enforces it.**

**Verified.** The task guard was exercised with no `ANDROID_SERIAL` set: `:app:connectedDebugAndroidTest` failed in `doFirst` with *"Refusing to run connectedDebugAndroidTest: connected instrumented runs uninstall the app afterwards …"* before any device interaction (the run report shows the task FAILED, every build step up-to-date). The suite guard's allow-path was exercised on `emulator-5554` via `am instrument -w -e class …AppRulesStoreInstrumentedTest`: **`OK (5 tests)`, five passing markers — the guard admits the emulator, it does not silently skip the suites there.** A full-package run (`-e package com.example.myexodus.data.db`) was attempted first and aborted with `INSTRUMENTATION_ABORTED: System has crashed` — the guest's `system_server` died mid-run under host CPU contention (the documented emulator failure mode, not a test or guard failure); the guest respawned system_server on its own and the surgical single-suite run above then passed on the recovered guest. The skip-path (hardware) is not directly exercised here: on the X6532 the guarded suites are reached only by an explicit `am instrument -e class …`, and skipping is the safe direction by construction.

**Honest limits.** The destroyed on-device data is **unrecoverable** — no backup exists for the rules store or the wrapped key material, and an anonymous Firebase identity cannot be restored once replaced (no refresh token survives a sign-out). The owner must recreate rules/streaks; Mama must be re-paired (#71). Restored after the incident (same night): a11y re-bound via the dummy→real cycle, `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` granted, `GET_USAGE_STATS` + `SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW` appops set, FGS re-foregrounded. Device legs remain `install -r` + `am instrument` only; the debug seams (`DeviceVerificationReceiver`) are the supported device-side verification surface, and the `mirror-read` seam gained the v13 census (journal/unlinked/projections/pinned + store counts) as the incident's diagnostic counterpart.

## #117 — The FCM State Matrix, Proven: Foreground, Background and Doze on the X6532 (2026-09-29)

**Context.** The partner pillar plan's §18 carried one autonomous-capable open line: *"FCM delivery works on a GMS Android device across foreground, background, and Doze states."* The wire path was proven (ADR #46's X6532 round trip), and ADR #58 proved the approval flow — but that was a *flow* leg whose last hop is a foreground in-process pull, so the deliberate three-state delivery matrix itself did not exist.

**The seam.** A host-side `messages:send` needs the install's FCM registration token, which the app deliberately never persists (AD-18c — the token is returned by `FirebaseMessaging` on demand and only the `/users/{uid}/push_installations/{androidId}` document holds it server-side). `DeviceVerificationReceiver` gained `-a push-read` (debug source set only): it awaits `FirebaseMessaging.getInstance().token` (the receiver already runs on `Dispatchers.IO`; failure logs `push fcm token unavailable` and continues) and logs `push fcm token=… installationId=…` where the installation id is `PushInstallationRegistrar.androidIdOf` (AD-18a). The token value is **not** recorded here — it is a delivery credential for the install; the seam exists so the host send can target it, nothing more.

**Verified — one session, 2026-09-29, X6532 (GMS), three legs, same install (`install -r` only), one stable token (142 chars, value not recorded — see above):**
- **Foreground.** `MainActivity` verified `topResumedActivity` via `dumpsys` immediately before the send; high-priority data-only wake `fcm-matrix-fg-1` accepted by FCM v1 → `MyExodusPush: wake for fcm-matrix-fg-1` at 03:49:39.229 → `push installation registered (c87c39f6465f02f1)` + `PartnerSyncWorker` SUCCESS.
- **Background.** Launcher resumed (`topResumedActivity=…XOSLauncher`), app process alive behind its FGS; wake `fcm-matrix-bg-1` → `MyExodusPush: wake for fcm-matrix-bg-1` at 03:48:58.665 → registration refresh + `PartnerSyncWorker` SUCCESS.
- **Doze.** `dumpsys deviceidle force-idle` → `mState=IDLE`/`mForceIdle=true` verified, then wake `fcm-matrix-doze-1` → `MyExodusPush: wake for fcm-matrix-doze-1` at 03:50:35.465, sync SUCCESS; `dumpsys deviceidle unforce` **in the same command chain** → `mForceIdle=false`/`mState=ACTIVE` verified. All three wakes arrived ~1 s after the FCM API accepted the send.

**Honest limits.** "Background" is the app's designed steady state on this device — process alive behind the always-on enforcement FGS, which is also the state whose kill behaviour the standing XOS constraints forbid poking (`am force-stop` clears `enabled_accessibility_services`; the a11y rebind dance is not a test fixture). A force-stopped-process delivery case and a process-kill case are therefore **not** exercised. The Doze leg uses debug `force-idle` (deep idle), which exercises the same high-priority FCM exemption the real idle state uses. One device, one OEM skin: the line asks for "a GMS Android device" and the X6532 is the project's GMS reference device; a second GMS skin is not covered by this leg.

## #118 — Home's Milestone Section Is Permanent: Four Explicit States and the `loadFailed` Signal That Made Error Reachable (2026-09-29)

**Context.** Owner product call (2026-09-29, verbatim): *"on the home page we had a section for milestones add it back, when the page loads before it used to wait for all info to load then the milestone section appears but now i want it there and also having its states empty, loading, success, error."* The section was gated on `streakDays != null`, so it entered the tree only after the streak read resolved — a late pop-in that read as a missing section — and it had **no error state at all**: `HabitRepository.refreshActive()` returned silently when the store refused to open, leaving `resolved` false forever (an eternal skeleton), and `StreaksViewModel.refresh()` had no exception path (a read that threw killed the coroutine and changed nothing on screen). Three of the four states existed implicitly; error did not exist as a signal.

**What shipped.**
- **`ui/home/MilestoneSectionCard.kt`** — the section extracted into its own `internal` composable (`MilestoneSectionCard(habit, streakResolved, loadFailed, onRetry, onOpenStreaks)`) with one shared card chrome (`MilestoneCardShell`, `surfaceContainerLow` / `shapes.large` / 16dp / 10dp spacing) across all four states so the section never re-skins. Precedence is explicit and commented: `loadFailed` (error) outranks `!streakResolved` (loading) outranks `habit == null` (empty) outranks success — a failed read never resolves, so without error-outranks-loading the card would sit in loading forever with no way to act on it. The grid (`MilestoneGrid`), its 30-column constants and the four-state body moved here from `HomeScreen.kt` (which shed ~170 lines).
- **The `loadFailed` signal, both layers.** `HabitRepository` gains `loadFailed: StateFlow<Boolean>`, set when `storeFactory.open()` returns null in `refreshActive()` and cleared by every `publishState` (every definite read proves the store opened); `StreaksViewModel.refresh()` mirrors it and additionally catches thrown exceptions — `CancellationException` rethrown, anything else sets `loadFailed` — so a corrupt row that throws mid-read is the same recoverable state as an unopenable store, not a silent coroutine death. `resolved` now copies `repository.resolved.value` instead of being set unconditionally, which is what lets "still loading" survive a failed attempt.
- **Loading keeps the title in the first frame.** The loading card renders the real "Milestones" title plus skeletons in the exact value slots (day chip, full-width grid band, legend line), so resolving swaps values in place instead of reflowing the card — the section reads as present-and-filling, which is the owner's actual ask. (First build rendered only skeletons; the Compose test caught that the title was absent — the failure was a real UX finding, fixed rather than asserted around.)
- **Error is actionable.** The error card carries `home_grid_error_body` ("Your data is safe") and a "Try again" button wired to `streaks::refresh` (`onRetryStreak`), through `AppShell`'s new `streakLoadFailed` collection. The empty card carries the ladder drawn un-filled with its rungs still ringed plus the `home_streak_cta` ("Set up a habit") path to the Streaks tab.
- **HomeScreen** now renders the section as a permanent `item(key = "milestones")` — no condition around it — with three new string resources (`home_grid_empty_body`, `home_grid_error_body`, `home_grid_retry`).

**Reachability, honestly.** When the store can't be opened at process start, `MainActivity`'s gate (`usable=false`) replaces `AppShell` with `DatabaseHealthScreen` — Home is never composed, so the *startup* failure is that screen's job, not this card's. The error state's real trigger is therefore the **mid-session refresh failure**: a store that opened once and now refuses (`open()` null while health is Ready) or a read that throws on corrupted data. That path previously rendered an eternal skeleton indistinguishable from loading; it is now the explicit error card with a retry. Stated in the composable's KDoc so the state is never mistaken for the startup path's duplicate.

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `HabitRepositoryTest.refreshActive_storeUnavailable_reportsLoadFailed_whichTheNextResolvedReadClears` (real Room store under Robolectric): after seeding a habit, the store files are replaced by a *directory* at the DB path so the open fails with cant-open (not corruption → factory returns null); the blocked repository reports `refreshActive() == null`, `loadFailed == true`, `resolved == false`; healing the path and re-reading clears the flag and resolves. Suite on the final code: **1,352 tests, 160 classes, 0 failures, 0 errors, 0 skipped**.
- **Device (X6532, `install -r` only).** `MilestoneSectionCardTest` — the real composable rendered through `createAndroidComposeRule<ComponentActivity>`, the seams being plain inputs so every state is reachable including error: **`OK (5 tests)`** — loading (title, no state copy), empty (explanation + CTA), success (day chip + legend, no stale copy), error (failure + retry, no stale copy), and error-over-a-held-habit (a failed refresh over a previously loaded habit is still the error state — the in-memory grid is stale). Each state asserts what it must **not** show, so no two states can silently collapse into one.
- **Live screenshots.** Loading captured on emulator-5554 (title + skeleton chip + skeleton grid band on the first frame while the store opens); empty captured on the X6532 (un-filled ladder with the 7/30/90 rings, explanation copy, CTA fully reachable by scroll).

**Honest limits.** The success state was not live-screenshotted on either device — both devices currently carry no habit (the X6532 store is post-incident, ADR #116) — its on-device evidence is the instrumented render above, which is the stronger form anyway since it asserts the day chip and legend text. The loading card shows its title before the read resolves, which is deliberate (present-and-filling); if a read hangs indefinitely without failing, the card shows the skeleton until the error path trips — there is no synthetic timeout, matching the repository's no-timeout read contract.

## #119 — Notifications Name Which Partner: Per-Pair Tray Entries, Generic Lockscreen Copy, and a Harness That Pairs Like a Real User (2026-09-29)

**Context.** Owner ruling (2026-09-29, verbatim): *"no it has to mention the partner name and a notification too, so puser know cause user can have multiple partners if they want"* — and, in the same breath, *"also we dont have data yet so add it pair users actually do the steps a user will."* Three defects under one ruling. (1) Every partner-facing post used invariant copy ("New activity from your partner", "Approval request from your partner", "Your partner responded"), so with the §19.5 multi-partner model — a device holds N enrollments by owner ruling — the tray could not say *which* partner an item concerned. (2) Each notification type posted under one fixed id (2001/2002/2003), so a second partner's pending approval request would **silently replace** the first's tray entry. (3) The device harness (`PartnerTestIdentity` + the instrumented tests) never passed pairing labels even though production's `PairingCeremonyScreen` collects one on both sides — so every device test left label-less enrollments and the notification path was never exercised with real-shaped data.

**Decision.**
- **Private copy names the partner; the lockscreen copy stays generic (FR-13).** `PartnerNotifications` posts each type with a `*_title_named` string ("Approval request from %1$s") as the unlocked title, and keeps the plain string as the `setPublicVersion` title. The unlocked tray disambiguates partners; the locked screen still learns nothing. New `namedTitle` parameter in `post()` defaults to null, so the generic path remains the loader.
- **The name is the pairing label, else the key-id short form** — `displayNameOf(enrollment) = partnerLabel ?: shortKeyId(partnerSigningKeyId)`, reusing the #63 convention already on the Partner tab. Never a fabricated name, never a long key.
- **Per-pair notification ids.** `idFor(base, pairId) = base + pairId.hashCode()`. N partners' entries coexist; `onlyAlertOnce` still coalesces one partner's burst to one alert. A hash collision degrades to replace-in-place — never to a lost item for a different pair.
- **Callbacks carry the enrollment, not just an id.** `ApprovalFlow.notifyRequest/notifyDecision` are now `(EnrollmentRecord) -> Unit`; `MailboxMessageHandler.notify` is `(pairId, payloadType)`. The DI lambdas resolve the enrollment per post (the mailbox path does one `enrollmentDao.getByPairId` lookup). Approval types are filtered out of the generic post **at the composition layer** — the approval flows own their notifications, so one event can never double-post. **[Amended 2026-10-01 (ADR #127): the notify hook gained a third parameter — `(pairId, payloadType, messageBody)` — and the motivational message posts with `partner_message_title_named` + the quoted body (`namedText`, private only; the public version stays generic per the FR-13 rule above); all partner-event taps now land on Home via `EXTRA_START_TAB` under this object's own request code.]**
- **String contract.** The three `_named` forms are commented as the private unlocked copy only; the plain forms are the lockscreen-safe public version and the no-label fallback — never removed.
- **Harness walks the real name exchange.** `PartnerApprovalInstrumentedTest` now calls `initiate(uidA, MY_LABEL_A)` / `complete(..., myLabel = PARTNER_LABEL_B)` — the same two production call sites the ceremony screens drive — and asserts the persisted `partnerLabel` on both enrollment rows. It grants POST_NOTIFICATIONS via `uiAutomation.executeShellCommand("pm grant …")` (this module carries no androidx.test:rules, so no `GrantPermissionRule`; a real user grants the same permission in the system dialog), then reads the **real tray**: the named title under `idFor(NOTIF_APPROVAL_REQUEST, pairId)` and the still-generic `publicVersion` title.

**Verified.**
- **JVM (Robolectric).** `PartnerNotificationsTest` — pre-existing assertions (denied-permission silence, channel importance, `onlyAlertOnce` split) updated to the per-pair id form, plus: the unlocked title names the partner while `publicVersion` stays generic; two pairs' approval requests post two distinct ids, each title naming its own partner. `MailboxMessageHandlerTest` — the notify hook is asserted to carry the pairId (`("pair-1" to TYPE_ACTIVITY)`), proving the naming data reaches the composition layer; all ordering/ack assertions unchanged. The full JVM suite ran on the final tree ahead of the device leg: **1,353 tests, 160 classes, 0 failures, 0 errors** (build `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 18m`, both debug APKs, 2026-09-29).
- **Device (X6532).** The end-to-end leg — real pairing with labels, both enrollment rows asserted, the named tray entry and generic public copy read back — runs with the owner's one-tap warm window (task #94); evidence appended here at that run.

**Honest limits.** The device leg is **pending at write time** (the run needs the owner's warm-window tap) and is tracked as task #94, not silently dropped. Requester-side *decision* notifications are Robolectric-pinned only — the harness's requester half posts via the same `post()` helper, but the device leg asserts the request path only. `idFor`'s hash-collision behavior is a named, bounded degradation, not tested for collisions (the two fixed ids used in tests don't collide; a deliberate collision fixture would test an event the code deliberately tolerates). NFR2's checklist wording is relaxed by dated revision notes in the plan and the V1 doc, per the plan-fidelity protocol — the wire-payload contract (AD-11) is untouched.

## #120 — The Request Id Was Half an Id: A Cross-Pair Primary-Key Collision Silently Ate the Second Partner's Approval (2026-09-29)

**Context.** The #94 device leg — the ADR #119 harness run that pairs like a real user and reads the real tray — failed on the X6532 with `java.lang.IllegalStateException: A has no pending RESPONDER row after the pull` (2026-09-29 09:24). The pull itself was healthy: `pulledCount=1`, the event row appended, the chain head advanced, the AD-10b processed marker written, the mailbox doc acked. The handler must therefore have fired `onAppended` for a verified `TYPE_APPROVAL_REQUEST`, yet `ApprovalFlow.onRequestAppended` inserted nothing — and logged nothing on any of its rejection paths. Everything downstream of the append was silent.

**Root cause, pinned by device forensics.** `ApprovalRequest.eventIdOf(senderKeyId, sequence)` derived the canonical request id as `"<senderKeyId>:<sequence>"` — without the pair. Three facts combine into a collision: (1) the signing key is device-level, shared by every enrollment on the device (§19.5 multi-pair — by design); (2) every pair's chain starts at sequence 1; (3) `request_event_id` is the `approval_requests` primary key. The X6532's pulled `myexodus_accountability.db` held the proof: a leftover REQUESTED→APPROVED row from an earlier partnership with id `438bce4a74a6df09253f253b22d1fb621ef310b2ad6ed9620000cbec290507d9:1`, and the new pair's request — same device key, same sequence 1 — derived the byte-identical id. `dao.insertIgnore` hit the PK conflict, returned −1, and the `if (inserted != -1L) notifyRequest(...)` guard treated the collision exactly like a replay: no row, no notification, no log. A re-pair is not required to hit this — any device in two pairs where both partners' chains reach sequence 1 (guaranteed) collides on the first request of the second pair. This is the multi-pair path the #71 re-pair and §19.5 rulings depend on.

**Decision.**
- **The canonical id gains the pair: `eventIdOf(pairId, senderKeyId, sequence) = "<pairId>:<senderKeyId>:<sequence>"`.** Both derivation sites (`ApprovalFlow.requestApproval` line 71, `onRequestAppended` line 157) call the one helper; the id remains derived, never self-reported, and the response payload still carries it verbatim so `applyResponse` resolves the requester's row unchanged. Room needs no migration — the column is a String PK and the id is an opaque key everywhere (no production code parses it; verified by grep).
- **The silent IGNORE path now logs.** `inserted == -1L` is still the legitimate Step 11 replay path (hence `Log.i`, not `W`), but it names the request id — the cheapest guarantee that a future cross-key collision can never hide for a day again.
- **Mixed-version behavior, stated plainly.** A pre-fix in-flight request holds an old-format id; its response payload carries that same id; both sides' lookups are by-value, so a request minted before the update still decides and applies after it. Old and new formats cannot collide with each other (different segment counts). No compat shim: the project is pre-release, direct-APK, single-fleet (ADR #15).
- **The instrumented test derives the id through the same helper** (`ApprovalRequest.eventIdOf(pairId, …)`) instead of hand-building the string — the harness assertion can't drift from production derivation again.

**Verified.**
- **JVM (Robolectric).** New regression `ApprovalFlowTest#onRequestAppended_sameChainPositionInTwoPairs_producesTwoRows` — two enrolled pairs, same sender key, both at sequence 1: two distinct rows, each with its own pairId/message, two notifications (fails on the old derivation at the second `byId(…)!!` — the second insert no-ops there). `ApprovalFlowTest` 19/19 green; the full accountability + partner JVM suites green on the fixed tree (2026-09-29).
- **Device (X6532).** Re-run of `PartnerApprovalInstrumentedTest` on the fixed build with the owner's warm window — evidence appended here at that run (task #94).

**Honest limits.** (a) The collision was found by device forensics, not by any test — the JVM suite had a single-pair fixture, and the bug needs N≥2 pairs sharing a device key; the new regression test is the direct antidote for that class. (b) The fix removes the collision the code can prove (identical derivation inputs); it does not enumerate every possible malformed id, because the id is derived from verified chain positions on both sides and never accepted from the wire. (c) The `Log.i` line makes a returned −1 observable but is not a hard failure — a future silent-ignore class elsewhere in the flow is still possible and would need its own evidence, as this one did.

## #121 — Partner Context Everywhere: the Selected Partner Is Visible and Switchable on Every Partner View, and a Message Is Addressed to Its Recipient (schema v14) (2026-09-29)

**Context.** Owner report (2026-09-29, verbatim): *"also in the partner page we have so many operations and subpages but as we do them and move on even to the smallest thing like write a message, the partner needs to be able to see or change the partner, and it should syc all over in the partners pages and subpages"* — the §19.5 multi-partner model (one device, N supported users) had exactly one surface that named which user was in view: the hub. Every subpage (Requests, Activity, Pairing) and the compose dialog spoke of "your partner" while acting on the selected pair only, so a supporter holding three partners could approve a request, read a relapse, or write encouragement and have no way to tell — on the page itself — which of the three it concerned. Worse, the two writes were not pair-scoped: switching selection inside a subpage was impossible (the only switcher was the hub's roster), and a motivational message journaled with no addressee fanned out to **every** enrolled pair — a privacy defect across partners, not just a legibility one.

**Decision.**
- **The selection is one value, persisted, and every view re-scopes from it.** `PartnerSelection` (prefs-backed `selectedPairId`, §19.5) is the single source of truth; `selectUser(pairId)` persists then reloads, and the reload's newest-pairing fallback persists the resolved id so a cold start never re-decides. **Switching no longer clears the open subpage** (it used to eject to the hub): the reload re-scopes every stream the page renders, so the viewer stays where they were — which is the owner's actual ask.
- **`PartnerContextBar` on every partner subpage.** A `surfaceVariant` strip under the `BackRow` reading "Viewing / <partner>" — the pairing label, else the #63 key-id short form — plus a **Switch** action offered only when the roster holds more than one enrollment (a switcher with nothing to switch to is a control that lies).
- **The switcher is a dialog over the page, not a trip home.** An `AlertDialog` listing the roster as `PartnerUserRow`s (the same radio row the hub's `PartnerUsersCard` renders — extracted so "which partner is selected" looks the same wherever it is asked), with a single **Close** confirm: selecting a row switches and dismisses, so the page underneath is already the newly-scoped one.
- **The composer names its recipient.** `ComposeMessageDialog` renders "To: <partner>" above the field, and `onSendMessage(pairId, text)` captures the pair **at send time** (a null pair fails the send loudly instead of journaling an unaddressed message).
- **Addressed journal rows (schema v14).** `partner_activity_outbox` gains a nullable `target_pair_id`: NULL = fan out to every enrolled pair (the existing *state snapshot* semantics, unchanged), non-null = this row concerns one pair only. `pendingForPair` becomes `… AND (target_pair_id IS NULL OR target_pair_id = :pairId)`. `MIGRATION_13_14` is one additive `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN`; every pre-v14 row reads back untargeted, which is exactly its historical meaning. `MIGRATION_14_13` recreates the table without the column (SQLite ≥ 3.35's `DROP COLUMN` cannot be assumed) copying every row, and recreates the index the drop took with it — Room validates unknown columns **and** declared indices on open, so a downgraded build would otherwise fail to open the store.
- **Hub counts carry the loaded set (#97, folded into the same pass).** "Recent activity (n)", "Requests awaiting your decision (n)", "Your requests (n)" — the reference material's count-aware section headers; and the Pairing page's separate identity card was folded into the sync hero (`SyncCard(showIdentity = true)`), because two cards answering one question ("who is this and is it healthy?") was the page's one real overload.

**Verified.**
- **JVM (Robolectric, real Room store).** `PartnerActivityJournalTest#targetedMessage_pendsOnlyForItsPair_whileSnapshotsStillFanOut` — a message journaled with `targetPairId = "pair-1"` and a snapshot emitted for the same profile: the addressed pair sees the message with its addressee recorded, the fan-out snapshot stays untargeted, and **pair-2 sees only the snapshot** (the privacy assertion). `AppRulesStoreMigrationTest#migration13to14AndBack_dropsTheTargetColumn_andKeepsTheJournalRows` — direct 13→14 (column added, legacy row reads back untargeted) then 14→13 (column physically gone, every row survives the recreate, the index is back). The 2→14 chain test (`migration2to14_addsEveryTableAndRoomAcceptsTheSchema`) additionally exercises the migrated predicate end-to-end on a store Room itself validated. Both classes green on the current tree: `PartnerActivityJournalTest` **9/9**, `AppRulesStoreMigrationTest` **4/4** (BUILD SUCCESSFUL). Full JVM suite after the pass: **classes=160, tests=1356, skipped=0, failures=0, errors=0.**
- **Device (X6532, instrumented, SQLCipher driver stack).** `AppRulesStoreMigrationInstrumentedTest` **16/16 OK** — including the two new legs `migrate13To14_addsTargetPairId_andPreservesJournalRows` and `migrate14To13_dropsOnlyTheTargetColumn_andKeepsJournalRows` (248s, `adb install -r` + `am instrument`, never uninstalled). The device run earned its keep: both new legs **failed on first execution** and every failure was in the assertions, not the migrations — (i) the addressed-predicate expectation asked for 1 row for the pair the message was addressed to, but the untargeted fan-out row *also* pends for that pair, so the correct count is 2 (1 was the count for the *other* pair, which is the discriminating case and is asserted separately); (ii) the index assertion counted every index on `partner_activity_outbox` and got 2, because a TEXT primary key leaves a `sqlite_autoindex_…` row beside the declared index — it now asserts the declared index by name. Both legs also needed the `seedCoachingStep` row that `assertPreExistingDataSurvives` checks (a missing row surfaces as SQLCipher's misleading "step() must be called before reading column values"). The migrations themselves were correct throughout; the JVM suite had not covered this class.

**Honest limits.** (a) A message journaled but *not yet delivered* whose store is downgraded to v13 and re-upgraded loses its addressee (the column is dropped and re-added NULL), so it would fan out to every pair on delivery — bounded to unattempted transport state (no user-entered state is lost), stated here rather than silently accepted. (b) The context bar names the pairing label or its short key-id; a device whose partners were never labeled still shows codes, which #63's rename remains the fix for. (c) The switcher appears on subpages only with N>1 enrollments — with one partner there is nothing to disambiguate and no control is drawn.

## #122 — The Emulator Assumption Never Covered `@After`: Destructive Fixtures Now Route Through One Non-Throwing Emulator Chokepoint (2026-09-29)

**Context.** ADR #116 put the six destructive `data/db` suites behind `assumeEmulatorOnly()`. On 2026-09-29 a full-suite `am instrument` run against the X6532 — an ordinary device leg, the very workflow #116 blesses — skipped every guarded test body and **still wiped the owner's production rules store.** The evidence is unambiguous: logcat recorded **12** `MyExodusDb: Recovery action: user-confirmed reset of all database data` lines from the instrumentation process (6 at 12:38:31, 6 at 12:42:56), the runner output shows the affected classes printed with **zero result dots**, and the wrapped-key prefs (`myexodus_rules_db_keys` 12:42, `myexodus_db_keys` 12:42, `myexodus_activity_log_keys` 12:38) carry the wipe window as their mtime.

**Why the guard failed — the mechanism, not a lapse in care.** JUnit runs `@After` **even when `@Before` aborts on an assumption violation**. Four of the six suites reached `RecoveryManager.resetAllData()` from `tearDown` through a *stateless factory* (`recovery()` / `resetAll()`), so there was nothing to throw and stop the call: the assumption shielded the test body and left the destructive teardown fully armed. The other two (`DatabaseMigration`, `MilestoneSchema`) wipe from `tearDown` behind a `lateinit` field, so on a handset they failed with `UninitializedPropertyAccessException` instead of wiping — accidentally safe, and noisy: 10 of the 18 failures in that run were this error. **A guard that only protects the test body is not a guard for a teardown-driven fixture.**

**Decision — guard the *call*, not the *test*.**
- `RecoveryManager.resetAllDataEmulatorOnly()` (in `DeviceSafety.kt`) is the single destructive chokepoint: it consults the emulator check and **no-ops** on real hardware, so it is safe from `@Before`, from `@After`, and from seeding helpers alike — the failure mode is impossible at every call site at once, rather than being a per-suite discipline that four suites had already got wrong.
- **All six suites route every destructive call through it** (setUp, tearDown, factory helpers). The two `lateinit` teardowns additionally guard the field itself (`if (::recovery.isInitialized)`) so an aborted setUp cannot even reach the helper — that removes the 10 cascading errors and makes a handset run report clean skips.
- The raw call survives in exactly two files, both deliberate: `DeviceSafety.kt` (the helper's own body) and `EnhancedModeDeviceSuite.resetData()` — a genuine physical-device suite that must seed from a clean store.
- **Two enforcement layers keep it from regressing.** A JVM `DestructiveFixtureGuardTest` fails the build if any other androidTest file calls raw `.resetAllData()`; and `app/build.gradle.kts` registers `src/androidTest/java` as an input of the unit-test tasks, because without that the guard is decorative — the first negative control ran against an **UP-TO-DATE** task and never executed.
- **Device-run rule:** run a **class list**, never the whole package, on a handset (`am instrument -e class …`); grep the teardown, not just the assumption, before any instrumented run on the phone.

**Verified.**
- **JVM / enforcement.** The guard's negative control: reintroducing a raw `.resetAllData()` into `MilestoneSchemaInstrumentedTest` → `:app:testDebugUnitTest --tests …DestructiveFixtureGuardTest` **BUILD FAILED** (naming the offender); restoring the helper → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** — which also proves the Gradle input registration works, since an androidTest-only edit re-ran the task instead of leaving it UP-TO-DATE. `:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` green. Full JVM suite after the change: **classes=161, tests=1357, skipped=0, failures=0, errors=0** (up from 160/1356 — the +1 class and +1 test are `DestructiveFixtureGuardTest`).
- **Device (X6532).** `adb install -r` (never uninstalls) + `am instrument` over the six previously-lethal classes: `Tests run: 23, Failures: 10` on the **pre-fix** APK shape (the 10 being the `lateinit` teardown errors; **0 reset markers** — the guarded resets no longer fired), then **`OK (23 tests)`, 0 failures, 0 resets** after the `isInitialized` guard (the six suites skip cleanly on the handset). Reset markers in the run window (`logcat -T`): **0**, against **13** accumulated before it. The owner's `myexodus_accountability.db` (pair enrollments) was untouched by the whole incident — it is not on `resetAllData`'s delete list — while the rules store, its wrapped keys and the activity-log database were destroyed and are unrecoverable.

**Honest limits.** (a) This incident already cost the owner his on-device rules/streaks/limits/ledger for the second time in two days; the fix prevents the next wipe, it does not restore this one. (b) `EnhancedModeDeviceSuite.resetData()` still wipes the live store **on the phone by design** — every enhanced-mode device leg starts from a clean database and seeds its own rules, so each such run costs the owner his real rules. That trade-off is now stated instead of being silently implied by the emulator guard; changing it needs a product decision (e.g. a throwaway store for that suite), and is not taken here. (c) The guard covers raw `.resetAllData()` calls only — a future fixture that destroys state by another route (direct file deletes, a different reset API) would need its own rule.

## #123 — From Pairing Onward: a New Partner Gets No Pre-Pairing History, Plus One Pairing-Time State Snapshot (2026-09-29)

**Context.** The activity-layer device leg (`PartnerActivityLayerInstrumentedTest`, task #77) failed on the X6532 with a received chain far longer than the single event the harness had sent. Forensics: the reconcile queues were **unbounded in time**. `AttemptLogDao.pendingEventCreation`, `RecoverySessionDao.pendingMirror` and `PartnerActivityDao.pendingForPair` asked only "no link row for this pair yet?", so the first sync after a pairing linked **every** unlinked row the store had ever accumulated — attempts, recovery sessions and journal rows from before the partner existed — and delivered the owner's entire pre-pairing history to a person who had just been invited in. The instrumented test was the messenger, not the bug: its `head == 1` assertion had been written against a device where nothing else ever queued. Bounding the queues creates the opposite risk — a newcomer's dashboard stuck on "waiting for first update" until the owner's next real transition, which for a day-counter can be a day away.

Owner ruling (2026-09-29): **from pairing onward, plus a state snapshot.** A newcomer sees what has happened since the pair began and one fresh snapshot of the current state; they never see what happened before them.

**Decision.**
- **The bound is the enrollment, inclusively.** `EnrollmentRecord.createdAtEpochMillis` (`created_at`, written at `finalizePairing`) is the authoritative pairing time. The three pending queries gained `AND <time column> >= :sinceMs` (`occurred_at_epoch_millis`, `started_at_epoch_ms`, `created_at_ms`), and `PartnerSyncCoordinator.syncPair` threads `enrollment.createdAtEpochMillis` through `reconcileEvents(pairId, sinceMs)` into all three. `>=` and not `>` so a row stamped at exactly the pairing instant is delivered — a session that started in the same millisecond the enrollment committed is post-pairing activity, and the snapshot below depends on that boundary being inclusive.
- **One snapshot at pair-complete.** `PartnerActivityJournal.emitPairingSnapshot(db, pairId, atMs)` writes a single **targeted** `TYPE_STREAK_UPDATED` row (schema v14's `target_pair_id`, ADR #121), so consent gating (`HabitProgress.shareMilestones`), the `stateAfter` projection and the notification all ride the shipped rails. No new event type, no protocol change, no schema bump.
- **The seam is post-durable, defaulted, and non-fatal.** `PairingFlow` gained `onEnrolled: suspend (pairId: String) -> Unit = {}`, invoked **after** `secretsRepository.storePairingKey(...)` inside `runCatching` — the enrollment and its key are already committed, so a snapshot failure can never fail an already-durable pairing, and the debug harness (which carries no rules store) compiles and behaves unchanged. `AccountabilityModule.providePairingFlow` supplies the production lambda.
- **Consent is not invented.** A profile with no resolvable habit, or one that never granted milestone sharing, emits nothing: an unresolvable habit cannot have consented (Appendix A.3), and fabricating a snapshot would be exactly the share the user withheld.

**Verified.**
- **JVM (Robolectric, real Room store).** `AttemptLogTest#pendingEventCreation_boundsToThePairingMoment` (attempts at 1_000/2_000/3_000 with `sinceMs = 2_000` → the at-moment and after rows, never the earlier one); `RecoverySessionRepositoryTest#pendingMirror_boundsToThePairingMoment`; `PartnerActivityJournalTest#pairingSnapshot_isOneTargetedStreakUpdate_carryingTheCurrentState` (kind/target/daysFree/relapseCount/milestones/`createdAtMs` on the new pair's queue, **nothing visible to an existing pair** — the privacy assertion) and `#pairingSnapshot_isGatedByTheShippedConsentFlag_andNeedsAProfile`; `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest#reconcile_boundsEveryQueueToThePairingMoment` — all three queues seeded with a pre-pairing and a post-pairing row, asserting `eventsLinked == 3`, the pre-pairing rows' link sequences stay NULL, and the excluded journal row is still pending under the legacy `sinceMs = 0` read. The coordinator test's times are `System.currentTimeMillis()`-relative on purpose: a fixed 2023 fixture falls outside `ACTIVITY_RETENTION_MS` (30 days) and is swept before reconcile runs, which would make the test pass for the wrong reason. The 7 touched classes: **79 tests, 0 failures**; whole-tree JVM run on the same tree: **classes=161, tests=1362, failures=0, errors=0, skipped=0** (up from 161/1357 — +5 tests, the five new cases above).
- **Compile.** `:app:compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` and `:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` green — the instrumented leg's replacement assertions type-check against the production seams.

**Honest limits.** (a) The bound is wall-clock: if the device clock moves backwards between the enrollment write and a domain event, that event's row falls below `sinceMs` and never reaches the newcomer. A forward jump only excludes pre-pairing rows, which is the intent; a monotonic-clock column is the upgrade path if the backwards case ever shows up. (b) An owner who never granted milestone sharing pairs with a partner whose dashboard stays on "waiting for first update" until the first consented event — deliberate, since the alternative is a snapshot that ignores consent. (c) The bound is applied on the **emitting** device: the peer's inbound chain is untouched (a remote sender's own pre-pairing rows are bounded by that device's copy of this same rule). (d) The instrumented leg's state-coupled `head == 1` assertion is now state-independent (chain growth equals `pull.appended`, plus A's exact message decrypt-and-found in B's chain) because the app's own `PartnerSyncWorker` runs concurrently with `am instrument` — per-pass counts are not attributable.

## #124 — The Reference Grammar, Ported: One Status Pill, One Stat Scale, and the Requests Queue Losing Its Context Bar (2026-09-29)

**Context.** Owner direction (2026-09-29, verbatim): *"use the component reverse engineering skill to improve the activity page in partner, Requests, and pairing checking in on cognitive overload too, use the folder /Users/musila/Documents/ReferenceInspirationUI … same with the recover page and streaks page, dont foget about cleanup too"* — and the correction that the earlier ADR #121/#97 pass had shipped *declutter only*, not the reference-driven visual port. Reverse-engineering the reference folder (`204def2b…` = a stat row with the label + caption left and the number + baseline unit right; `11.54.23` = big-number stat tiles; `c5335b01…` = segmented code entry) against the app found three grammars the app was rendering three different ways each: a status (grey caption here, prose sentence there, tinted label elsewhere), a count (three different type treatments), and a single-destination row (LinkRow in two places, a hand-rolled copy in a third).

**Decision.**
- **One status atom.** `ui/StatusPill.kt` — `StatusPill(text, tone)` over `PillTone { QUIET, POSITIVE, NEGATIVE }`; the same escalation ladder the partner cards use (a fact / a settled good outcome / the one that needs the eye) at chip scale. Tint **and** the word, never tint alone. Adopted by the partner request rows (trailing edge), the Recovery technique cards (effort), and the withdrawal timeline's live-week marker. `requestStatusTone` is a pure function in `PartnerPresentation.kt` (next to `requestStatusText`'s resource mapping) so word and weight cannot disagree.
- **One stat scale.** `StatColumn` (value over label, `headlineSmall` primary) for peer counts — used by the Activity card and the partner summary's second row; `HeroStatRow` (label + freshness caption left, `displaySmall` value with the unit riding its baseline right) for the card's one dominant metric (days free). Units are part of the number, not a stacked label.
- **Row grammar.** Request rows: message + relative time left, status pill right, `HorizontalDivider` between rows. The Action card: leading primary-tinted mark beside the title, full-width primary CTA. The Recovery crisis card was a hand-rolled `LinkRow` copy *minus its chevron* — the row that navigated nowhere visually; it now uses the shared `LinkRow` (third consumer).
- **Owner ruling (live device review, 2026-09-29): the Requests subpage drops the `PartnerContextBar`.** Verbatim: *"well requests doesnt need viewing section ie viewing mama switch"*. Activity and Pairing keep it; the Requests queue reads as one list, and the hub's roster is one tap back. This narrows ADR #121's "every partner subpage" to "every partner subpage that is not a decision queue".

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `PartnerPresentationTest` **13/13** including the new `requestStatusTone` case (APPROVED→POSITIVE, REJECTED→NEGATIVE, REQUESTED/EXPIRED/CONSUMED/unknown→QUIET — an unknown status must never inherit another status's weight); `LiteralCopyGuardTest` and `NoLiteralTypeSizeTest` green; `:app:compileDebugKotlin` clean.
- **Device (X6532, `adb install -r`, never uninstalled).** Relaunch on the new APK: the Activity subpage renders the context bar, the mark + full-width CTA compose card, and the divider + stat-column Activity card; the Requests subpage renders **without** the context bar (BackRow → empty-state card, screenshot `10-requests.png`); the Activity bar still present after the change (semantics dump). Recovery renders the technique pill ("Medium effort", bounds `[470,679][640,704]`) and the crisis `LinkRow` ("Need urgent help?") — pixels cannot be attached **by design**: `RecoveryScreen` is FLAG_SECURE (`screencap` returns 0 bytes, the privacy invariant from ADR #80 working, verified again incidentally here). Streaks renders its setup state on this device (no habit configured).
- **Cleanup.** The removed `partner_summary_milestones` string has no remaining references; `StatBlock` deleted with no references; import hygiene checked in every touched file (compile-clean, no orphaned symbols).

**Honest limits.** (a) The hero stat row's **populated** state was not re-captured this session: every harness pair on the X6532 shows the empty summary (the 2026-09-28/29 wipes took the seeded data), and seeding requires the owner tap-gated harness — the empty state and the identical `StatColumn` scale in the Activity card were seen rendering, and `PartnerDashboardInstrumentedTest` covers the underlying read model. (b) The Streaks hero and timeline could not be visually re-verified without creating a habit — that is the owner's real data and was not touched; the ADR #85 hero stands unchanged and the one Streaks delta (the timeline's live-week marker becoming a pill) is code-level only. (c) Removing the Requests bar means a supporter with N>1 partners no longer sees whose queue they are clearing *on that page*; this is the owner's explicit ruling, stated here so a future reader does not "fix" it back.

## #125 — The Orphaned Pairing Now Diagnoses Itself: One Denial Signal, Three Guards, and the Same Re-Pair Card (2026-09-29)

**Context.** Open Product Decision #30, found live on 2026-09-28: the anonymous Firebase uid **is** the account (§6), so a sign-out, an app-data clear or a reinstall mints a new uid that is a member of no pairing document. Every `/pairings/{pairId}` read is then denied by the rules (`request.auth.uid in resource.data.members`), sync freezes silently at the last watermark, and nothing on screen says why or what to do. The owner's real pair was (and remains) in exactly this state — only a re-pair, which needs the partner's phone, fixes it. Option (b) of the OPD was chosen: detect and surface, as the same observation class as the AD-22 wipe detector (a live enrollment plus evidence that its pairing is gone) — and deliberately *not* option (c) (identity backup/export), which would be a new mechanism on the auth model.

**Decision.**
- **One write site, the read choke point.** `PairUidResolver.resolve` is where every pair-document read in the app starts, so the observation lives there rather than in each caller (the sync coordinator, the ceremony, the unpair flow, `MainActivity`'s identity path all route through it). A denial is recorded; a server answer clears.
- **`PERMISSION_DENIED` is the only qualifier.** The rules gate the read on membership, so a denial is an answer about *this uid*; everything a missing connection can produce — `UNAVAILABLE`, `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`, a null code — must never raise the card. The guard is structural (`isDefinitiveDenial`), not time-based, because the SDK only reports `PERMISSION_DENIED` off a rules evaluation, which requires a server response.
- **Two more guards, both earned by how Firestore actually reports failures.** (i) **Attestation** — when App Check rejects a request the client sees *the same* `PERMISSION_DENIED` a rules rejection produces (researched before shipping, not assumed: Google developer forums and the firebase-android-sdk issue tracker both document enforcement surfacing as PERMISSION_DENIED; the official docs describe enforcement without naming the client-visible code). So the flag is only written when `AppCheckInstaller.canAttest()` can still mint a token: a build-attestation problem is not an identity loss and re-pairing could not clear it. Failures answer "no" and the next pass re-checks. (ii) **Cache** — the write is unconditional (only the server can produce a denial), but the *clear* requires `!snapshot.metadata.isFromCache`: an offline pass served from cache must not disprove a real denial and blink the card off and on.
- **State lives outside the database, deliberately.** `PairingReachabilityStore` (prefs, same shape as `ProtectionStatusStore` / `BootReconciliationStore`) — first denial wins ("since" must not move), any server success clears it, `syncOnce` prunes pairs no longer enrolled, and `PairingFlow`'s `onEnrolled` clears it for the pair being created. That last one is load-bearing: AD-02 pair ids are *deterministic* (`min(uidA,uidB) + "_" + max(uidA,uidB)`), so re-pairing reuses the id and a stale flag would otherwise outlive the fix and accuse a brand-new, working pairing.
- **The surface reuses the shipped grammar, no new variant.** `Paired.pairingUnreachable` drives the existing `PairingGoneCard` (shared with the unpaired and wipe-detected states) on the hub — verdict before content, because a pairing that cannot sync makes every number below it a stale claim — and on the Pairing subpage, above the sync card. Two honesty rules ride along: the STALE line ("Sync is taking longer than usual — the phone may be offline…") is suppressed while unreachable, since the network is the wrong suspect and the real cause is named in the card above it; and `syncNeedsAttention` excludes the unreachable state so the hub does not render two cards about one fact. The copy names the honest causes (reinstall/app-data clear, or the pairing ended from the other side) and the one route forward (pair again with a new code) — the same route the wipe card names, for the same reason: this device cannot restore a pairing unilaterally.

**Verified.** Whole-tree JVM suite after the change: **162 classes / 1,375 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped** (+2 over the pre-change tree), with the three touched suites green in the same run.

- **JVM.** `PairingReachabilityStoreTest` **8/8** — the six store-contract cases (first-denial-wins timestamp, per-pair clear, prune, prefs durability across store instances, denial qualifier) plus two new truth tables for the extracted composites: `shouldRecordDenial` (the exact denial qualifies; `canAttest=false` excludes it; `UNAVAILABLE`/null/non-Firestore throwables never do) and `shouldClearDenial` (a server answer clears; a cached answer and an absent snapshot do not). `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest` **23/23** including `reachabilityPrune_seesExactlyTheEnrolledPairIds`, `reachabilityPrune_stillRunsWithNoEnrollmentLeft` (the prune runs before the empty-enrollment early return), and `multiPair_anUnresolvablePairIsSkipped_neverBlockingTheRest`. `PartnerDashboardSourceTest` **24/24** including `deniedPairingDocument_readsAsUnreachable` and `reachablePairing_isNotFlagged_andAFailedFlagReadReadsAsReachable` (a flag-store read failure must read as reachable — never an accusation off a broken read).
- **Device (X6532, `adb install -r`, never uninstalled).** The production sync pass recorded **four genuine denials** — logcat `MyExodusPairUidResolver: … PERMISSION_DENIED: Missing or insufficient permissions.` alongside `SyncReport(enrolled=true, uidsResolved=false, …)` — and `shared_prefs/pairing_reachability.xml` held exactly the four pairIds embedding the device's *previous* anonymous uid (the rotation signature), while the same pass synced the other nine enrollments unflagged — the attestation half answered "yes" in practice, so the flag was not blanket-suppressed. Prune verified live: all four flagged pairIds are still enrolled (13-row enrollment table read from the accountability DB), so the flags correctly survived. Hub: selecting a denied pair rendered "Can't reach your partner" + body + the re-pair CTAs; the nine healthy pairs render no card. Pairing subpage: the card sits above the sync card, with the STALE verdict line suppressed while the raw "Last synced 9h ago" timestamp line remains. A deliberately induced server-side document deletion that the client served from its cache produced **no** flag (the read succeeded from cache — an offline pass cannot manufacture a denial). Device state restored after the pass (selection back to the owner's working pair); the four flags persist by design.
- **Cleanup.** The now-unused `FirebaseFirestoreException` import removed from `PartnerSyncCoordinator` (grep-verified single-site); all touched files compile clean.

**Honest limits.** (a) A partner-side ending and this device's own identity rotation are indistinguishable to the client (both are "the document denies me"), so the copy presents them as a set of possible causes rather than a diagnosis — the fix is the same, and the partner can say which it was. (b) The attestation guard is exact in one direction only: a build that cannot **mint** a token is correctly excluded (`canAttest` false), but a build whose *valid* token is **rejected server-side** is indistinguishable from a rules denial — the client sees one `PERMISSION_DENIED` either way. That build would raise the card, and re-pairing would not clear it. It is a live risk only where App Check enforcement is on and the calling certificate is unregistered (this project's known instance: a release build before its SHA-1 is added to the App Check provider). The upgrade path if it ever bites is a canary read the denied device *should* be allowed (its own idempotency record), denials on both meaning "the rules are not the problem" — deliberately not built, since it doubles the round-trip on every denial and the app has no attestation-health surface to hang it on. (c) The signal is per-pair and lives in the same install as the enrollment, so a full app-data clear takes the flag with the enrollment and reads as `WipeDetected` (AD-22) instead — correct, and the copy for both states routes to the same action. (d) The card's CTA is the ceremony, which does not exist on the debug harness path — the diagnosis still renders there, only the way out is absent, unchanged from the wipe card's behavior. (e) Option (c) of OPD #30 (identity backup/export) is not taken: every mechanism that would let the app restore an anonymous identity edge-ward is also a mechanism that could restore it for someone who cleared the app on purpose, and the v2 crypto already requires a fresh SAS/QR ceremony for any new pairing. (f) The re-pair clear (`PairingFlow.onEnrolled` → `markReachable`, `AccountabilityModule`) is verified in halves — the store's clear behavior and the AD-02 deterministic-id reuse (`PairingFlowTest`) — but the composition itself has no test: the ceremony cannot be driven from the debug harness, so a live clear-on-re-pair is owner-tap gated and unverified end-to-end.

## #126 — The 2026-10-01 Honesty Batch: Eight Owner-Feedback Items, One Signing Sheet (#10–#17, #22) (2026-10-01)

**Context.** Owner feedback collected during the first real two-device test day (2026-10-01) and the preceding UX review, filed as tasks #10–#17 plus #22. Common thread: every surface that *acts* must say what happened, and every surface that *claims* must be able to prove it — same standard the rest of the app holds.

**What shipped (per item).**
- **#10 — SAS buttons re-worded by phase.** The joiner (whose phone shows the digits first) gets `Confirm` / `Stop`; the initiator (who *heard* them read out) gets `They match` / `They don't match`, with body copy ("If anything is different, stop — don't continue."). A single shared pair of buttons was the confusion: "They match" asked the wrong person to compare. `PairingCeremonyScreen.kt:762–793`.
- **#22 — Pairing failures name their cause.** `pairingFailureCopy(reason)` maps the server's contract codes (`PAIRING_ACTIVE`, `TRANSCRIPT_MISMATCH`, `SAME_KEY`, `SELF_PAIRING`, `RATE_LIMITED`, `PROTOCOL_VERSION_UNSUPPORTED`, attestation family, local sentinels) to specific, non-retryable copy; only a **null** reason (a true connectivity failure) gets the "check both phones are online" message. Retry advice for a server rejection can never succeed — that was the defect.
- **#11 — Notifications name the sender; a message alert shows the message.** `notifyPartnerEvent` gained a `messageBody` parameter: a motivational message renders as `"%1$s sent you a message"` + the quoted body (`partner_message_quoted`); other events keep `"New activity from %1$s"`. Both are unlocked-tray copy only — the FR-13 public lockscreen version stays generic.
- **#12 — Home carries the partner's pinned message.** Each partner's newest pinned message, newest-first, named for its sender, paged with next/back arrows when several senders exist; the age line shows the message's own send time, so a stale message cannot read as fresh. `HomeScreen.kt:140, 229–244, 733–782`.
- **#13 — The interruption report can be dismissed; a fired fix confirms itself.** New pure precedence `OutageStage.stage()` over (newest measured outage, `dismissedAtMs`, last fired fix, battery exemption, freshness): a dismissal silences only the evidence it named — evidence newer than the dismissal re-raises the report — and the resolved confirmation appears only when the fired fix is actually in effect (exempt + fix fired ≥ newest evidence + within freshness + not dismissed). `AccountingDiagnosticsState.dismissedAtMs`/`markDismissed`; `AccountingOutageSource.current()` reads `measuredOutagesSince` (rows carry `wallClockTsMs`); the Home banner gained a Dismiss action (report variant) and a resolved variant ("Background tracking can run again…"). The notification and Settings offer clear automatically because both read `current() → null`.
- **#14 — Manual checks demoted to recovery.** `MainActivity.onResume` now fires the production sync pass in-process (best-effort, logged, online-gated) so the 15-min periodic/idle cases stop being the user's problem; the Partner subpage's "Check now" renders only when `checkFailed || syncNeedsAttention(state)`; the EndedCard's check demoted to a text button; ProtectionScreen's integrity "Recheck" hidden while VERIFIED. Cold-auth behavior was verified first: the pass never prompts (cold window → honest stop), so foreground triggering cannot ambush the user with a biometric sheet.
- **#15 — Action feedback triad at the rename dialog.** Saving state (label "Saving…", field disabled), failure kept inline ("Couldn't save the name — the change wasn't written. Try again."), dialog closes only on success. Audit result: the partner request surfaces and RuleEditor already carried in-progress/terminal feedback; the remaining log-only sites (ceremony label save, cancel paths, post-save reconcile) are acceptable and noted here.
- **#16 — Ending a partnership tells the other phone.** The responder of a delivered deletion request now gets a named HIGH-importance notification on `partner_alerts`: `"<label> ended the partnership"` / `"Ended <time>. The shared data was erased on both phones."` Mechanics: the mailbox document's server timestamp is threaded through a 3-argument `onDeletionRequest` hook (arrival time would lag days on an offline peer); the label is read **before** `handleIncoming` — the shred destroys the enrollment row the name lives on; the post is wrapped so a notification failure can never flip the shred result (which would leave the request unacked); a no-time string variant covers a document without a server timestamp rather than fabricating one. New per-pair id `NOTIF_PARTNERSHIP_ENDED = 2004`, generic public lockscreen copy per FR-13. The handler itself still never notifies — the composition (DI lambda) does, keeping the handler's §8 Step 13 contract intact.
- **#17 — One biometric dialog.** Documented as the late-2026-10-01 revision of **ADR #112** (above): the combined `BIOMETRIC_STRONG or DEVICE_CREDENTIAL` sheet supersedes the negative-button→confirm-credential chain; `UseDeviceCredential`/`ERROR_NEGATIVE_BUTTON` deleted, `LockedOut` fallback kept for OEM sheets.

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `OutageStageTest` **11/11** (the full precedence truth table: dismissal silences only named evidence, newer evidence re-raises, fix-after-evidence confirms once, stale fix silent, no-fix-never-confirms). `MailboxMessageHandlerTest` green with the timestamp-threading assertion (`Triple("pair-1","m1",1_700_000_000_000L)`). Full-tree run on the final code: **164 classes / 1,394 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped** (up from 162/1,375 at ADR #125).
- **Compile.** `:app:compileDebugKotlin` + `:app:compileDebugUnitTestKotlin` green after every item.
- **Device.** Ran on the owner-directed two-device journey (2026-10-01, both physical devices; ADR #127's device record has the room-level detail): #10's phase-split SAS buttons + #11/#12's sender-named notifications and Home pinned card were exercised in the live ceremony and messaging pass; **#16 verified end-to-end** (B, the responder, got the named "ended the partnership" notification with the erasure line); #17's combined biometric sheet handled the real unpair prompt (one sheet, owner's fingerprint, unpair proceeded); #14's foreground pass ran on both devices' resumes (cold-auth honest stop, never a prompt). #13 (outage dismiss/resolved) and #15 (rename triad) were not specifically re-driven that day — JVM-pinned only. (#22's failure copy was not re-triggered — the day's pairing succeeded.)

**Honest limits.** (a) #14's foreground pass runs on every resume with no throttle — flagged for owner review; if battery telemetry ever shows it, a minimum-interval gate is the upgrade. (b) Hiding "Check now" unless attention is needed trades discoverability for calm; the owner chose "automatic processes, manual = recovery only". (c) #16's post happens after the shred inside the same handler call — a crash in that window loses the announcement (the journal row still exists; no re-notify path). Accepted; no poison-pill re-post by design. (d) #15's remaining log-only sites are listed above rather than silently fixed. (e) The #17 sheet is compile-verified only until the device journey runs it.

## #127 — Messaging on the User's Terms: Send-Kick, Honest Outcomes, Live Repaint (2026-10-01)

**Context.** The 2026-10-01 owner-directed two-device journey (wipe → pair A↔B → real-user passthrough) surfaced five UX/reliability items (#29–#33) and one live bug the owner watched happen: after the partnership ended, the responder's Partner page kept rendering the erased data. Common thread: a message must leave when the user acts, say what actually happened, and every surface showing partnership state must track it in real time.

**What shipped (per item).**
- **#29 — Kick on Send + honest delivery feedback.** `MessageDelivery.kt` derives the composer outcome in the user's terms: `SENT` only when the send-kick's pass actually cleared the pair's outbox; `QUEUED` when offline, when the auth window is cold (a declined/absent biometric prompt is not a failure — the journal-first emit rides a later warmed pass), or when anything for the pair is still pending; `FAILED` only when nothing was journaled. The kick runs the production pass in-process, so delivery no longer waits for the 15-min cadence. Device-journey finding that shaped it: without the authorize step a message sat unlinked indefinitely, because nothing else in a normal session raises the signing prompt.
- **#30 — Live repaint signal.** `PartnerSyncSignals.pulledEvents` (buffer 1, DROP_OLDEST) ticks from `runPartnerSyncPass` — the single seam shared by the periodic worker and all `MainActivity` in-process call sites — and the Partner dashboard re-reads on the tick. Buffer semantics: a tick with no collector is dropped, never queued (a stale replay must not fire a spurious reload on first compose). The Home pinned card needs none of this — it observes Room directly.
- **#31 — The sender sees their own last message.** The Activity view shows the newest message this device sent, so composing isn't blind on the sender's own screen.
- **#32 — Send feedback toast.** SENT/QUEUED surfaces as immediate feedback instead of silent success.
- **#33 — Partner notification tap opens Home.** The alert opens Home (where the pinned-message card is), not the tab it happened to name.

**The live bug and its permanent fix (owner: "real time updates even after partnership end… not staring at deleted data").** Root cause: the repaint tick was gated on `report.pulledCount > 0`, but a deletion/unpair pass reports `pulledCount == 0` **by design** — the FCM deletion-wake path returns before the watermark query — so the responder's page never re-read and kept showing the pre-shred dashboard after the shred had run. Fix, at the contract level rather than the symptom: `BilateralDeletion.resumePending()` now returns whether any journal row advanced (before/after comparison over the mutated columns via a `progressSignature()` string — a data-class `equals` would compare the `requestEnvelope` ByteArray by reference and report every re-read as a change); `PartnerSyncCoordinator.SyncReport` gains `unpairAdvanced`, set from the pull's `deletionRequested` per pair, carried through the unpaired early-return (the one state where the unpair's own tail — redelivery, owed ack, confirmation poll, timeout — is the ONLY network work left), and OR-aggregated across pairs; `runPartnerSyncPass` ticks on `pulledCount > 0 || unpairAdvanced`. Every unpair stage (peer shred pulled, ack confirmed, timeout report, redelivery) therefore repaints an open page.

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `BilateralDeletionTest` 18/18 with two new cases: `resumePending` reports true exactly on real change (confirmation, owed-ack write) and false on a settled row — so the tick cannot spin on no-op passes. `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest` 25/25 with two new cases: a `deletionRequested` pull reports `unpairAdvanced` at `pulledCount == 0`, and a post-shred device (no enrollment) still runs `resumeUnpairs` before the early return with the change carried into the report. Full-tree JVM suite on the final code (close-out run): **165 classes / 1,406 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped** (ADR #126's 164/1,394 was the pre-repaint-fix tree).
- **Device (both apps reinstalled `-r`, never uninstalled).** The full unpair ceremony ran end-to-end on the two physical devices: A (initiator) → biometric → local shred → await; B (responder) received the request by FCM wake, shredded, acked, terminated, and posted the #16 "ended the partnership" notification; A's next pass polled the ack and flipped its card to "Your partner's device has also erased its data." Both ends' Partner pages settle on the correct terminal card — no stale dashboard — and Done → clean "No partner yet" empty state. The `pairings/{pairId}` `PERMISSION_DENIED` seen during this ceremony is the **designed** idempotent no-op (the rules pin members + active→terminated; a second terminate flip is always denied and deliberately tolerated) — explicitly NOT the ADR #125 identity-orphaning class, whose tell is denial on pair-doc *reads* while owned pairs exist.
- **Cleanup.** Journey close-out wiped app data on both devices (`pm clear com.example.myexodus` only), leaving both at fresh onboarding; the a11y settings ended clean-`null` on both (no stale pre-listed component), so first-run setup binds normally.

**Honest limits.** (a) The repaint mechanics are JVM-pinned and the end states are device-verified; a *live* deletion pull with the page open was not re-run on hardware after the fix (it would need a fresh pair + unpair — the exact scenario the unit tests model at both seams and the code path `pulled?.deletionRequested == true` covers with one flag at one call site). (b) `resumePending`'s signature comparison lists the mutated columns by hand; a future column that unpair progress writes must join `progressSignature()` or its advance won't tick. (c) The send-kick raises the signing prompt on a cold window at Send time — agreed owner behavior, but it means Send can interrupt with a biometric sheet; the message is journaled before the prompt, so a cancel loses nothing.

## #128 — Streak Display: Day 1, Never Day 0 (2026-10-03)

**Context.** Owner device-testing feedback (2026-10-03): a streak started while the calendar was already mid-run still counted from 0 — the engine's accumulator is whole completed days (0 until the first full 24 h), and every surface rendered that raw. Owner ruling, verbatim: *"lets never tell the user 0 we show 1 then we proceed from there this is ux"*. The constraint on any fix: the engine's anti-minting invariant (§6.2 — only elapsed time credits days) must survive untouched.

**Decision.**
- **One ordinal display function.** `daysFree(elapsedMs) = habitDays(elapsedMs) + 1` (`ui/streaks/StreaksPresentation.kt`) — "the day of the run the user is in". Every user-facing count routes through it: Home hero + StatTrio, MilestoneSectionCard ("Day 1 of 90" on a fresh streak), Streaks tab summary + RelapseCard, StreaksViewModel's relapse note, `HabitState.currentDay`, and `PartnerActivityJournal.daysFree` (a fresh streak's shared rows read Day 1; a profile with no streak at all still reports 0 — there is nothing to floor). Milestones still fire at their 24 h thresholds — only the rendering moved.
- **Engine untouched.** The accumulator stays whole completed days; `daysUntil` stays completed-days (`milestoneDay − completed`, i.e. `milestoneDay − daysFree + 1` — the truthful countdown). Backdating keeps its shipped semantics (§7.5/§8 Step 3: the date is a label, elapsed credits nothing), so a backdated streak reads Day 1 — the owner's report fixed exactly at the display boundary.
