- **Widget conversions at both read sites.** The snapshot's `streakDays` is ordinal now, so the rung math converts back (`(days − 1).coerceAtLeast(0)`) in `DesertDawnWidget.viewFor` and `WidgetContent.MilestoneBand`; the clamp guards a stale pre-ruling persisted snapshot.
- **Copy.** `streaks_relapse_body` / `streaks_relapse_confirm_body` "starts at Day 0" → "Day 1"; `relapseSupportNote` "Day 0 of the next run" → "Day 1".

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `StreaksPresentationTest` (ordinal function + backdate + `daysUntil` unchanged + relapse-note copy), `HabitRepositoryTest` (backdated start Day 1; 60 s in Day 1; 5 completed = Day 6; post-relapse Day 1; the STREAK_UPDATED emit gate's day-boundary unchanged), `PartnerActivityJournalTest` (7 completed = 8; 12 completed = 13; a fresh-relapse row Day 1), `MilestoneSectionCardTest` (5 completed renders "Day 6 of 90"). Full-tree suite on the final code: **165 classes / 1,413 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped**.
- **Device (X6532).** Home hero reads **"1 day"** and the widget publishes **"Day 1"** on a fresh streak (screenshots; widget log `days=1`).

**Honest limits.** (a) It is a display-boundary convention with no engine anchor: any future surface computing days itself from `currentStreakElapsedMs` will read one less than every shipped surface — route through `daysFree`. (b) `HabitState.currentDay` is ordinal while the partner STREAK_UPDATED emit gate and milestone thresholds keep reading completed days; the two must not be conflated (the repository comment names both). (c) "Best run" also renders ordinal (`daysFree(allTimeMaxElapsedMs)`), consistent with the live count — decided here rather than left inconsistent.

## #129 — The 2026-10-03 Device-Feedback Batch: an Outage That Wasn't, a Block Screen That Wouldn't Stay, and Two OEM-Level Answers (2026-10-03)

**Context.** Owner device-testing feedback on the returned build (2026-10-03), four items: (1) a false outage report — "zero tracked rules → enabled one Instagram 2 h limit → popup: interrupted ×2, up to 18 h" (later reads: 18 times, up to 18 h 30 m); (2) the on-schedule block card *"appears only for a split second then disappears… it should stay until the user dismisses it — make sure other blockers also don't have the same issue while u fix this up"*; (3) the phone's screen rotation kept turning ON; (4) OEM auto-start deep links for the daily driver (Transsion).

**What shipped (per item).**
- **#11 — the outage gate.** Root cause: `UsageDiagnosticsRecorder` recorded `GAP_DETECTED` rows whenever the accounting pipeline was silent, regardless of whether anything was being accounted for — with the only rule outside its schedule window there was nothing to measure, and overnight silence became an "18 h 30 m interruption". Fix: `pass(...)` gains `trackingActive: Boolean` (defaulted true for other callers); the FGS computes the tracked set (`isEnabled && dailyLimitMs > 0`) **before** the pass and passes `tracked.isNotEmpty()`; when false the recorder keeps only the heartbeat fresh — so a later activation measures from its own first observation and can never inherit a pre-tracking hole — and writes **no gap row and no reboot row**. The service's later `if (tracked.isEmpty()) return` was removed: a null request now guarantees the list is non-empty, so the ordering cannot silently drift back.
- **#10/#7 — the block card is a wall now.** The card was hidden by the deferred re-check whenever a foreign package (the launcher) came forward — and the eviction's own HOME fires exactly that transition, so the card flashed ~1 s. Fix in three parts: `BlockOverlayView` renders the wall rhythm with an in-place Close for `AppBlocked` (parity with the daily wall, owner ruling 2026-09-27); `OverlayController.dismissWall` accepts **both** wall kinds (the first cut accepted only `DailyLimitBlocked` — the rendered Close was dead on the device, device-caught 2026-10-03, pinned by a controller-level regression test); `BlockerAccessibilityService.reevictIfWalled` → `reevictIfScene` handles both kinds' targets (a blocked app re-foregrounding under a live card goes straight back out), our own package coming forward hands the display to our UI (`overlay.hide`), any foreign foreground leaves the card standing.
- **Rotation RCA — closed, no code change.** The app is platform-walled: no `WRITE_SETTINGS`, zero rotation-setting writes anywhere in the codebase (grep-verified), so a manifest-level fix could never have worked — there is no capability to disable. The flip vector is injected test input (monkey/tap device-driving) hitting the Quick Settings auto-rotate tile during test sessions. Restored OFF on the X6532; the device-hygiene guard (check + restore) is recorded in memory.
- **Transsion auto-start links.** `OEM_RESTRICTION_SCREENS` gains `infinix` / `tecno` / `itel` → `com.transsion.phonemaster/com.cyin.himgr.autostart.AutoStartActivity` (component verified resolving + resuming on the X6532). OEM-keyed table, generic fallback — the all-skins rule holds.

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `UsageDiagnosticsRecorderTest`: three new cases — silence with nothing tracked records no gap but refreshes the baseline; activation after an untracked silence measures from activation; a reboot while untracked writes no boundary row but refreshes the boot. `BlockOverlayViewTest` 10/10 + `OverlayControllerTest` 19/19 (targeted 12:06, including the dead-Close regression and the wall-parity Close/hint rendering). Full-tree suite on the final code: **165 classes / 1,413 tests / 0 failures / 0 errors / 0 skipped**.
- **Device (X6532, `install -r`, never uninstalled; final build installed 12:30).** #11: the 18 fabricated rows were purged (`ledger-purge-window`, 18 deleted), banner cleared; live re-read 2026-10-03 12:48 — `ledger outage count=null`, `gaps24h=0`. #10/#7: card persists (28 s standing screenshot this run; 44 s + Hiber `float_window` traces in the earlier run), Close tap → `MyExodusOverlay: hidden display=0 gen=8` + screenshot, re-entry re-blocks (`shown display=0 gen=7`), then net-zero restore (temporary Calculator rule deleted, `usage rule … ABSENT`, Calculator opens free). The owner's mid-test "close button doesn't work" was root-caused to the tester's own instrument: `uiautomator dump` suppresses accessibility services on XOS — the bound service died at 12:35:31 and the OS tore the card down under the owner's finger; no app-side log exists for their tap, while injected taps (the same input pipeline a finger uses) dismissed the card twice with full logs. Lesson recorded: overlay legs use screenshots + `input tap`, never uiautomator.

**Honest limits.** (a) The gate's definition of "something to account for" is enabled daily-limit rules — a future accounting feature without a daily limit must revisit it. (b) The purge was one-time data cleanup on this device; other installs carrying pre-gate rows age them out of the 24 h surfaces naturally and the 30-day retention prune deletes them — no migration was added, since the rows are inert beyond 24 h. (c) The rotation flip is test-harness-induced, and the enhanced-mode script's EXIT trap snapshots/restores state — it faithfully preserves a mid-run ON; forcing OFF on exit is a script-level change, not taken (the memory guard covers drive hygiene). (d) The dead-Close fix is JVM-pinned + device-verified via injected tap; the owner's physical-tap confirmation rests on the explained incident, not a re-run with the owner's finger.

## #130 — The Deletion Request That Raced Its Own Unpair: the Relay Accepts Members-Bound Terminated Pairs (2026-10-03)

**Context.** Defect #18(a), caught live on the two-device day (2026-10-01): the unpair deletion request was created at the exact unpair moment, while the sender's own terminate write flipped the pair document to `terminated`. The relay trigger evaluated 3.4 s later and rejected the message `PAIRING_INVALID` — consequence: the initiator never learned, never shredded, and the data survived on that side. The race is designed-in: `initiate()` shreds locally and terminates the pair before/while the request drains, so the request's own pairing is always `terminated` by the time a slow trigger evaluates it.

**Decision.**
- **Membership is the security boundary; status is a lifecycle fact.** `pairingUsableForRelay(data, senderUid, recipientUid)` (new, `functions/relay-logic.js`) accepts `active` **or** `terminated`, and in both cases requires `data.members` to contain **both** the sender and the recipient. The mailbox create rule already requires the pairing to be `active` at write time and pins members immutable — so a document can only sit under a `terminated` pairing when the terminate landed between the sender's write and the trigger's evaluation (seconds of trigger latency, `retry: false` so single-shot). No other document can reach the trigger: writes are rules-denied the moment the flip lands. Race-closing, not a hole.
- **The SDK wiring delegates.** `relay.js`'s `pairingActive()` keeps the existence/type checks and defers the decision to the pure function, so the decision-space stays testable without the admin SDK.

**Verified.**
- **JVM (functions).** `npm test` **75/75** with four new cases: active + members accepted; **terminated + members accepted (the race)**; non-member rejected under both statuses; unknown status / missing members / null rejected.
- **Deploy.** `firebase deploy --only functions:relayMailboxMessage --project myexodus-53804` — updated. (An earlier "User code failed to load… Timeout after 10000" was transient; the local load check `node -e "require('./index.js')"` passed before the successful deploy.)

**Honest limits.** (a) The app-side device leg — reproduce the race on a live pair (terminate mid-drain) — is pending a paired two-device session; the unit tests pin the decision, not the end-to-end timing. (b) Accepting `terminated` widens the relay's acceptance window by the trigger's latency (3.4 s in the incident); the members pin is what bounds it.

## #131 — The Alerts Nobody Could Hear: Three Log Metrics Filtered a Resource That Never Fires, and Four Functions Had Drifted From Their Source (2026-10-03)

**Context.** Owner ask (2026-10-03): *"if u can check firebase monitoring for any issues u can address"* — and the standing ruling *"permanent verified fixes not temporary"*. Audit scope: last 7 days of Cloud Logging (severity ≥ ERROR, project-wide), the 4 alert policies, the 5 user log-based metrics, the deployed function revisions, the notification channel, and scheduler/sweep health.

**Findings.**
- **Three of five user log metrics were permanently blind.** `dispatch_failures`, `relay_rate_limit_rejections`, and `sweep_terminal_count` were created filtering `resource.type="cloud_function"` — a resource type that **no gen2 log entry ever carries**. Gen2 functions' platform *metrics* do dual-emit under `cloud_function` (the premise, true in its own domain), but their log *entries* are all `resource.type="cloud_run_revision"`. The two pairing metrics were built with the right resource type; the other three could never match an entry, which silently disabled the terminal-delivery alert they feed (§4 item 4) — the one alert that exists to tell the owner a partner push died permanently.
- **Four of five functions had drifted from their source.** The deployed revisions predated later source commits: `registerPairing` (revision from 09-28 01:45), `retryDispatchSweep` and `cleanupMailboxes` (09-19), `cleanupIdempotencyRecords` (09-28 00:51). The full redeploy's CLI output named each as "updating" and only `relayMailboxMessage` as "Skipped (No changes detected)" — the skip confirms today's #18(a) fix (ADR #130) is live, and the four updates were source drift, not a deploy failure (each deploy had been scoped `--only`).
- **The only project ERRORs were three benign cold-start 500s.** All the same shape: `URL_UNREACHABLE-UNREACHABLE_5xx, original code 500` on the 2-minute scheduler tick, zero container-side logs, one missed tick each, self-healed. Root cause: scale-to-zero gen2 cold start racing the scheduler request. Accepted — eliminating it costs min-instances (~$15–18/mo) or fights Firebase CLI management of the scheduler; one missed 2-min tick is harmless and R-03's 30-minute absent condition stays the real sweep-death signal. The seven `registerpairing` 400s and four `mailbox rejected/PAIRING_INVALID` warns in the window were test-day ceremony churn (the #18(a) class, pre-fix evidence only) — no new client-input class and no new relay rejection mode.
- **Notification channel and alert policies: no drift.** Channel enabled; all 4 policies ON with expected conditions.

**Decision.**
- All five log-based metric filters rewritten to `resource.type="cloud_run_revision"` (exact strings recorded in `MyExodus Monitoring.md` §4 item 5), via Logging API v2 `PUT` — which requires the `metricDescriptor` echoed in the body for counter metrics, and the metrics LIST returns short names while the body `name` must be the full resource path (both traps hit and recorded in the GCP API recipe memory).
- Full `firebase deploy --only functions` (not scoped `--only`) to close the drift; the CLI's skip behavior now doubles as a cheap drift detector.
- Cold-start 500s: no change (accepted above). Sweep empty-queue silence: no change — the fast path returning before the log line is the designed shape, and R-03 watches the platform metric instead.
- Alert policies and channel: no change (verified, not rebuilt).

**Verified.**
- **Metric filters.** All five re-read from the API post-update; filters match the empirically-observed emission shapes (`cloud_run_revision` + `jsonPayload.message`, anchors being real pre-fix entries of the same lines: relay.js's `mailbox rejected` warn, dispatch-logic.js's `dispatch sweep` info).
- **Deploy.** CLI output: 4 successful updates (`registerpairing-00006-yih`, `retrydispatchsweep-00003-vov`, `cleanupmailboxes-00003-pex`, `cleanupidempotencyrecords-00002-vay`, 10:01–10:02 UTC) + 1 skip (`relayMailboxMessage`, byte-identical).
- **Health.** Scheduler requests 200 every 2 minutes on schedule (including after redeploy); the 3 ERROR entries are the cold-start class only; channel enabled.

**Honest limits.** (a) The rewritten filters are verified by re-read and by matching real pre-fix entries of the same shapes, but **no post-fix event has actually been counted by the rebuilt metrics** — generating one would mean injecting a synthetic delivery failure into production; the first real terminal will be the live proof. (b) Deploy drift is detected only by running a full deploy and reading the CLI's skip/update output — there is no standing drift monitor; the per-feature `--only` scoped deploys (house practice for blast-radius) will re-open drift if a broader source change shares a function file without redeploying it. (c) The cold-start 500s remain possible at roughly one per day and stay visible in Logging; the error-rate policy's ≥2-errored floor means they will not page at this volume, by design. (d) `sweep_terminal_count`'s window is the alert's sum-over-1h; a terminal event more than an hour old when noticed is visible in the metric explorer but will not page retroactively.

## #132 — The Picker That Promised Nothing: Backdated Stop Dates Now Read as "Free since" (2026-10-03)

**Context.** Owner returned to the streak-math question (ADR #128's day) with the design question: *"so what is the logical thing to do here? think of UX and making the user not confused"*. The confusion wasn't the math — it was that the setup form asks for a stop date and the app then visibly ignores it. The verified count starts at setup (anti-minting, ADR #128 lineage), the picked date (`recovery_start_at`) renders nowhere, and the form's own row label ("Counting from") openly contradicted the policy. Three options were put to the owner: show the date as a since-label, remove the picker, or credit backdated days. The owner chose the first.

**Decision.**
- **The date is identity, never credit.** Both streak cards (Home hero + Streaks summary card) now carry "Free since <date>" via the shared `FreeSinceLine` (`ui/streaks/StreakCards.kt`) — shared for the same reason `HabitLabelRow` is (ADR #85): two copies drift.
- **`formatDayMonth`** (`ui/ScheduleFormat.kt`) drops the year within the current year ("Free since Oct 1") and keeps it otherwise ("Free since Dec 31, 2025") — the smart-year pattern session history already uses inline.
- **Copy made self-consistent:** the setup picker row label changed "Counting from" → "Free since" (it contradicted the count policy in the same form), and the note now reads *"Your count starts now, on this phone — only tracked time adds days. The date is remembered as when you stopped; it doesn't add days by itself."*
- **Crediting backdated days was rejected:** the partner-visible number is evidence-backed; a picker that credited it could mint a fake 90-day streak. The count, accumulator, and milestone scheduling are untouched.
- **After a logged relapse** the line reads the relapse date — accurate by construction, since `recovery_start_at` is re-pointed there and that IS when the current free period began.

**Verified.**
- **JVM.** `ScheduleSummaryTest.freeSinceDate_dropsTheYearOnlyWithinTheSameYear` pins the year branch both ways (this-year date carries no year; another year stays unambiguous). Full-tree suite green: 165 classes / 1,414 tests (baseline 1,413 + the new pin).
- **Device.** X6532, debug `install -r`, 2026-10-03 13:41–13:42: Home hero rendered "Free since Oct 1" for `ac` and (after switching) `AC`; the Streaks tab summary card rendered it too; count read "1 day" beside the Oct 1 label on both habits — the anti-minting property observed live (a credited backdate would have read Day 3). Habit selection restored to `ac`, app back on Home.

**Honest limits.** (a) Between the stop date and the install date the two facts still differ by design — the fix makes each read as what it is; it does not reconcile the gap. The one-time explanation lives in the setup note. (b) The widget deliberately does not carry the line (the snapshot stores only the selected habit's tracked fields; adding the date means touching the snapshot schema and both read sites — deferred until asked). (c) `SessionHistoryScreen` holds an identical inline smart-year formatter (second occurrence — below the rule of three; unify when a third appears).

## #133 — Server-Visible Partner SOS Metadata: The Ratification Amendment (DRAFT — parked for owner review)

**Status: DRAFT — not ratified.** Owner ruling 2026-10-03, in answer to the pre-build question: *"Draft it, park for review."* Plan §13.1 item 2 ("Ratify the ADR amendment permitting server-visible Partner SOS metadata") and the §16 checklist line. The SOS backend is deployed inert (kill switch off, mapping doc §2), so nothing user-visible changes on ratification — this ADR is the paper the deployment runs on.

**Context.** Partner SOS ("Reach Out", V1 Req 35) is server-mediated by necessity: FCM can only be sent by a server, so the nudge must land in Firestore before a push can exist; suppression, rate limiting, daily caps, the dispatch lease, and crash recovery all require server-readable state. The plan §10.10 list of server-visible items was frozen by the implementation and deployed 2026-10-03 (mapping doc §2, deployment record). The Kenya DPA DPIA and privacy notice must cover the same surface — drafted alongside this ADR in `app/docs/partner-sos-dpia.md`, ratified by the same owner pass.

**Decision (to be ratified).** The following server-side visibility is permitted, as the minimum required for delivery, abuse resistance, and recovery. Content is never visible: the `envelope` is AES-256-GCM ciphertext under the pair CEK, which exists only in the two devices' memory (AAD-bound to pair/nudge/sender, D5); logs carry short hashes only, never uids, pairIds, nudgeIds, envelopes, or AAD (§9.4 discipline, `hashForLog`).

- `pairings/{pairId}/nudges/{nudgeId}` — `senderUid`, `recipientUid`, `nudgeId`, `envelope` (ciphertext), `envelopeVersion`, `createdAt`/`createdAtMs`, `expiresAt`, `status`, `suppressedAt`, `ackedAt`, `pushAttempts`, `lastPushAtMs`, `targetDeviceCount`, `sentDeviceCount`, `lastPushErrorCode`, `terminalAt`.
- `nudgePushQueue/{nudgeId}` (root, the dispatch work index) — `pairId`, `nudgeId`, `nextAttemptAtMs`, lease + attempt bookkeeping.
- `pairings/{pairId}/nudgeState/{senderUid}` — `lastAcceptedAtMs`, `lastNudgeId`, `dayKey`, `dayCount` (the §6.2 rate/daily-cap state; day key computed in Africa/Nairobi).
- `pairings/{pairId}/nudgeSettings/{uid}` — `blocked`, `updatedAt` (the recipient's block switch, §8.19).
- `users/{uid}/push_installations/*` — `pushEligible` added to the pre-existing registry fields (§8.12).
- **FCM delivery processing**: payload metadata transits Google's FCM service (cross-border processing; DPIA §6).

**Retention (live).** Nudges and queue documents: 7-day TTL on `expiresAt` (ACTIVE, mapping doc §2). Operational logs: 30 days. TTL is cleanup only; access correctness is rules + client expiry checks (D11) regardless of TTL timing.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *No server-readable metadata* (client-to-client only): impossible — FCM fan-out, suppression-before-send, rate caps, and the at-least-once lease/repair design all require it, and push delivery is the feature. (b) *Metadata-sealed design* (e.g., sealed sender patterns): the suppression and rate-limit contract needs a server-readable recipient/state; rejected for V1 — recorded as the upgrade path if the privacy model ever hardens beyond "metadata yes, content no."

**Verified.** Field inventory read from the deployed model + `firestore.rules` (R-F1…R-F8 pin the client surface: no client write/read beyond the recipient's own reads, the ack, and the owner's block setting); TTL re-read ACTIVE; log-discipline grep (`hashForLog`) confirms no clear identifiers in log lines.

**Honest limits.** (a) This is a draft awaiting the owner's ratification — the DPIA draft (`partner-sos-dpia.md`) is the companion paper. (b) A compromised server operator (Firebase admin) can read the metadata and the ciphertext; content stays encrypted, metadata does not — that is inherent to the design and stated in the DPIA, not mitigated here. (c) `lastPushErrorCode` can carry FCM error codes for dedup/debugging; the DPIA lists it.

## #134 — Activity Wire v2: the Relapse Names Its Streak, the Milestone Names Its Day (2026-10-04)

**Context.** The partner's relapse notification said only "your partner recorded a relapse" — with multiple habit streaks possible, the receiver could not tell *which* streak ended or how long it had run, and a milestone ping said only "a milestone was reached". The owner: "same case with relapse we need to think this through" (the message must identify the streak), and the relapse notification copy was on the standing complaint list.

**Decision.** Bump the activity body schema to `sv=2` with three optional `stateAfter` fields, absent-when-null so every v1-shaped body keeps byte-identical encoding (golden test): `habitLabel` (the streak's name, bounded by `ACTIVITY_LABEL_MAX_LENGTH`), `endedDays` (the day count the *ended* streak had reached, relapse rows), `milestoneDay` (the day reached, milestone rows). Both new fields ride only the two kinds that need them; `payloadFor` scrubs `habitLabel` when the pair's consent doesn't include the habit slice. Receivers accept `SUPPORTED_SCHEMA_VERSIONS = {1, 2}` (declining v1 would silently hide history on upgrade); an un-upgraded older build classifies `sv=2` as UNSUPPORTED_VERSION — exactly the §8.5 distinct-from-MALFORMED semantics, never a parse crash.

**Consequences.** The receiver renders "Day 12 streak ended" and "Day 30 reached" with the streak's name in notifications and feed rows (task: Activity feed rows, ADR #121's row grammar). `sv=1` bodies still parse and render (name-less, count-less) — the decode path never branches on the absence beyond nullability.

**Verified.** `EventPayloadTest` v2 round-trip + byte-stability; journal test pins the columns the coordinator encodes from (`relapseRow_carriesTheEndedDayCount_andTheStreakName`, `milestoneRow_carriesTheDayReached_andTheStreakName`); receiver-side v1/v2 acceptance in the classification tests.

**Honest limits.** An un-upgraded receiver drops v2 rows as UNSUPPORTED — inherent to versioned wire, and moot on the direct-APK rig where both devices run the same build; the accept-set makes the *current* build read both generations, so history survives upgrades in one direction only, which is the direction installs actually move.

## #135 — The Consent Matrix: Per Streak, Per Partner, Nothing Until You Choose (2026-10-04)

**Context.** v15 consent was one bit per habit for *every* pair (`habit_progress.share_relapses/share_milestones/share_streak`) plus one bit per profile (`user_profile.share_check_ins/share_recovery`). Owner complaint: with multiple partners, "i want just one to view a certain streak and another to view a certain streak and info, same case with relapse" — under v15, one pair's grant was every pair's grant; nothing could be scoped or revoked per partner. The old Sharing pane's flat switch list mixed per-habit and per-profile concerns, which is what surfaced the question.

**Decision (owner rulings 2026-10-04, four AskUserQuestion answers).**
1. **Shape: streak first, then partner.** The pane walks overview → partner → switches; the partner level collapses when only one partner exists. The owner corrected the first proposed shape (partner-first).
2. **Default: nothing until you choose — including at upgrade.** The v15 columns are dropped with **no seed**. A pre-v16 flag was written by a surface that never asked per partner; seeding any pair from it would share on the user's behalf. Consequences: schema v16 drops four `habit_progress` columns and two `user_profile` columns (recreate-table; `DROP COLUMN` needs SQLite ≥ 3.35), and adds `pair_streak_sharing` PK(pair_id, habit_progress_id) + `pair_profile_sharing` PK(pair_id), each switch carrying an `*_enabled_since_ms` watermark.
3. **Keep the per-partner block check-ins switch** (SOS §8.19) — it gates that pair's notification stream and is orthogonal to the matrix.
4. **The reach-out confirmation names the selected partner** (SOS plan §8.19; task #21).

**Guardrails that make the matrix honest.**
- *Write gate* (`PartnerActivityJournal.anyPairCouldReceive`): nothing journals unless some pair could receive it under the per-kind rule (STREAK ← shareStreak; MILESTONE ← shareStreak ∧ shareMilestones; RELAPSE ← shareStreak ∧ shareRelapses; COACHING ← ∃shareRecovery ∧ ∃shareStreak with a visible habit to anchor the aggregate; MESSAGE ← addressed/any pair shareStreak).
- *Reconcile filter* (`pendingForPair`): the same rules as SQL CASE + watermark bound, inside the query so filtered rows never occupy the LIMIT batch; `payloadFor` re-anchors profile rows to the pair's first visible habit and scrubs sub-content per the pair's flags.
- *Enable watermark + snapshot*: false→true pins the switch's watermark (`>=` filter) so enabling never leaks the while-off backlog, and a master rise journals one targeted STREAK_UPDATED so the newly-enabled partner's dashboard renders immediately (replaces §A.4's `emitPairingSnapshot` — same promise, now per-pair).
- *Rollback* (v15 build reinstalled over a briefly-v16 DB): each legacy flag is reconstructed as "any pair had it on" — the closest a single column can hold — before the pair tables are dropped; `share_access_attempts` ⇒ 0 (never writable by any UI).

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *Per-partner rows seeded from the v15 flags* — rejected by ruling 2: infers consent that was never asked per partner. (b) *Keep v15 columns as the per-streak master and add pair overrides* — two sources of truth for one question; rejected. (c) *Partner-first navigation* — rejected by the owner's correction; streaks are the thing being shared, partners are the audience.

**Verified.** Schema 16 exported; migration round-trip `AppRulesStoreMigrationTest.migration15to16AndBack_…` (data survival + per-flag reconstruction); journal contract `PartnerActivityJournalTest` (snapshot, consent matrix, wire v2, watermark fan-out, rollback-atomicity); repository contract `HabitRepositoryTest` (targeted enable snapshot, profile watermark pinning, matrix round trip); UI three-level pane with optimistic revert. **Two-device E2E leg ran green on the rig 2026-10-04 (task #12):** `PartnerActivityLayerInstrumentedTest` on the X6532 — five-type receive chain → render models, A's own journal emission delivered over the real keystore (bIngestedA=1), the matrix negative (a second concurrent virtual partner with no consent row pulled `appended=0`, chain 0), targeted-message delivery via the realtime kick (5,739 ms, no explicit pass), and the toggle-off write-gate refusal; migration class 18/18 on the X6532C (incl. both 15↔16 legs); JVM 175 classes / 1,523 tests green.

**Honest limits.** (a) Pre-v16 rows in the journal have NULL `habit_progress_id` and match no pair's slice — deliberate: rows whose consent basis can't be proven are never delivered, at the cost of one backlog generation after upgrade. (b) The rollback reconstruction is lossy by construction (N pairs → 1 bit); it only runs when a v16 DB is opened by a *downgraded* build, which cannot happen through any supported path (direct-APK installs move forward).

## #136 — Realtime Delivery: the Send Kick and the Foreground Doorbell (2026-10-04)

**Context.** The shipped activity layer delivered on the periodic cadence (15-min `PartnerSyncWorker` + FCM wake for background). Owner mandate 2026-10-04: a shared transition must move off-device the moment it is journalled, and an open receiving screen must repaint the moment the relay writes — "updates must feel realtime instead of waiting out the 15-minute period." (The send-kick precedent from ADR #127 covered only the message composer flow.)

**Decision — two triggers, one ingress.**
- *Sender kick:* `PartnerActivityJournal` rings `PartnerSyncSignals.activityEmitted` after every committed journal row; `MyExodusApp`'s sole collector runs an in-process `runPartnerSyncPass`. Failure (offline / cold signing window) is dropped; the periodic run heals it. The three collaborators inject as `dagger.Lazy` — the keystore-backed signer must not be constructed at app startup (root-caused a Robolectric regression: eager graph construction reached `AndroidKeyStore` before any tick).
- *Foreground doorbell:* `PartnerLiveSync` — a uid-scoped snapshot listener (newest mailbox message only) armed while `MainActivity` is STARTED; rings when the newest relayed message is newer than the highest watermark across enrollments; the collector runs the same in-process pass. Not ringing: cache hits, empty snapshots, deletions, server-timestamp refinements of handled writes. Debounced 1.5 s.
- *Invariant:* the triggers decide *when* to pull, never *how* — the pull stays the single ingress (verify → decrypt → chain → projection); zero new data paths.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *Shorten the periodic worker period* — battery cost on every device, still not realtime, and multiplies retry noise; rejected. (b) *Direct Firestore listener → projection writes* — a second ingress path duplicating verification/watermark logic; rejected outright. (c) *Ring the doorbell from within the pull's own wakeups* — no receiver-side latency gain; rejected.

**Verified.** Device (X6532, 2026-10-04): kick delivered in 5,739 ms and doorbell in 11,392 ms, both with no explicit pass call in the instrumented tests (`PartnerActivityLayerInstrumentedTest` Stage 5 + `doorbell_foregroundMailboxWriteDeliversWithoutAnExplicitPass`). JVM suite 175 classes / 1,523 tests green.

**Honest limits.** (a) The 15-minute worker has no success log, so "sub-minute" is the timing-based isolation argument. (b) Spurious ring = one redundant idempotent pull; silent failure = pre-mandate cadence — no correctness risk either way. (c) Foreground only; background delivery remains FCM + WorkManager catch-up (AD-05 unchanged).

## #137 — The Reach-Out Message: v2 Envelope + Named Confirmation (2026-10-04)

**Context.** V1 Req 35 shipped the SOS send path with a fixed, content-free envelope (`{"v":1,"kind":"nudge"}`) and a confirmation dialog that said only "Reach out to your partner?". Owner mandate 2026-10-04 (SOS plan §8.15 rev): the dialog must name the §19.5 target partner, offer a prefilled default message the sender can edit, and the message must arrive at the recipient's in-app card. Tray copy stays content-free by privacy design (FR-13) — the message is visible only in-app from the decrypted Firestore doc.

**Decision — versioning lives inside the ciphertext.**
- *Envelope v2:* deterministic CBOR map `{v:2, kind:"nudge", text:…}` via CanonicalCbor (NFC-normalized), sealed under the pair CEK with the AAD string **unchanged** — `partner_nudge|v1|{pairId}|{nudgeId}|{senderUid}`. Interop: a v2 envelope opens under the shipped v1 receiver (it null-checks the plaintext and shows no message); a v1 envelope still opens under the v2 receiver and carries no message. No server, rules, or key changes — the relay never parses plaintext (base64 ≤1024 chars contract).
- *Bounds, client-side:* `MAX_TEXT_CHARS = 280` (= EventPayload.MAX_ACTIVITY_TEXT_LENGTH) then a byte clamp to `MAX_TEXT_BYTES = 700` (EventCipher nonce 12 + tag 16 vs the 768 raw-byte wire budget, CBOR overhead headroom) with a high-surrogate guard so a byte-truncated payload never splits a surrogate pair. Receiver re-sanitizes: control chars stripped, trimmed, re-capped.
- *Dialog:* `SosConfirmDialog` names the target (`targetPartnerName` follows the exact `targetEnrollment` rule the send uses — pinned by `PartnerDashboardSourceTest.targetPartnerName_followsTheSameTargetRuleAsTheSend`), prefills `sos_default_message` with a live counter, and a cleared field sends the v1 no-text nudge — null, never `""`.
- *Retry:* replays the retained sealed envelope verbatim; the dialog's edited text is honored on the fresh path only (`SosViewModel.onTap(message)`).

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *New AAD version segment (`…|v2|…`)* — would break receive of v1-sealed envelopes mid-flight during rollout and buy nothing the inner-version check doesn't; rejected. (b) *JSON v2 instead of CBOR* — CanonicalCbor is already the project's sealed-plaintext codec (deterministic, NFC); a second format is pure surface; rejected. (c) *Char cap only, no byte clamp* — 280 astral chars = 840 raw text bytes > 768 wire budget after CBC/CMAC overhead; the seal would throw at the boundary; rejected.

**Verified.** Device (X6532, 2026-10-04, `am instrument`): `NudgeDialogInstrumentedTest` 3/3 (names partner, prefills default, edited text sent; unnamed fallback; cleared field → null; cancel sends nothing) + `NudgeHomeSurfaceInstrumentedTest` 5/5 (card renders message; both ack actions; banner legs incl. the virgin-probe mute leg) — **OK (8 tests)**. JVM: envelope round-trip/blank/NFC/cap/byte-clamp/surrogate/hostile-plaintext/sanitize (8 new in `NudgeEnvelopeTest`), wire-decrypt message-at-repository (`NudgeRepositoryTest`), card-carries-message (`NudgeInboxRepositoryTest`), retry-ignores-message-edit (`SosViewModelTest`), target-name rule (`PartnerDashboardSourceTest`).

**Honest limits.** (a) The tray notification intentionally does NOT carry the message (FR-13); only the in-app card renders it — recipient trust model unchanged. (b) `MAX_TEXT_CHARS` counts UTF-16 code units, not code points (matches the app's existing EventPayload semantic); astral text-display truncation is byte-guarded but char-counted. (c) v1 receivers show no message for v2 sends — graceful degradation, pinned by test.

## #138 — The Rule That Ends: Duration Sweep, the Deactivated Shelf, and an Editor That Says What It Means (2026-10-04)

**Context.** Three owner reports in one hour, all on the Rules surface. (1) "Duration-driven expiry" — a rule given an end date must actually *end*: the engine already ignored lapsed rules (Rule 3), but the rule's toggle stayed ON and the row read as live, so the user could not tell that the block had retired itself. (2) "1h duration + 2h limit is incoherent" — the editor accepted a daily limit longer than its own window, a rule that can never fire. (3) Editor UX: the **Ends** row read as static text ("Ends … Never" — the owner missed it entirely), and the limit fields demanded both halves ("what if i just want 2hrs? i have to type 00 in minutes"). Plus a design mismatch: the Home page's rule preview used generic glyphs while the Rules rows showed real app icons.

**Decision — the UI joins the engine's truth; the editor refuses incoherent rules.**
- *Sweep:* `RulesRepository.sweepExpired(now)` flips only lapsed **enabled** rules off, one policy bump each; the Rules screen drives it from a `LaunchedEffect` keyed on the sorted ids of expired-enabled rules — fires when the set changes, never per ticker tick. An already-paused lapsed rule stays untouched (the sweep claims nothing the user did). Engine untouched — the sweep only makes the switch truthful.
- *Deactivated shelf:* `!isEnabled` rules move to their own section (caption names both causes: switched off, or ended when the duration lapsed), with **Remove all** batch cleanup (`deleteRules` = one snapshot reload after per-rule bump transactions) behind a count-bearing confirm dialog. Pill precedence becomes **Expired > Paused** — a swept rule must not blame a pause the user never made. Resume (`resumedRule`) clears a lapsed `blockExpiresAt` ("on again, no end date") so re-enabling is never a dead toggle.
- *Window×limit coherence:* `ModeError.LimitExceedsWindow` — a limit beside a window must be ≤ the window's per-day minutes (`windowLengthMinutes`: overnight = 1440 − start + end). Skipped under always-on, like every limit check (always-on overrides both).
- *Blank = 0:* `parseLimitTotalMinutes` reads a blank hours or minutes field as 0 when the other half carries a number; a fully blank pair is still "no limit entered" (`LimitInvalid`), and ≥ 24 h / > window stay refused. New-rule limit fields start blank (a prefilled "30" was a default to notice and undo); existing-rule prefill omits a zero half.
- *Ends affordance:* third design, owner-driven in one day. (1) Bare "Ends · Never" text row → the owner missed it was tappable entirely. (2) Full-width outlined label+value field (the From/To grammar) → discoverable but rejected on looks ("that ends never is dumb and it looks terrible"). (3) Final: the settings-row grammar (LinkRow family, single line) — "Ends" title, right-aligned value ("Never" muted / the date in full contrast), trailing chevron that carries the affordance — on the "The rule switches off by itself once this date has passed." note. Clearing a set date lives in the date dialog's dismiss slot ("Clear"), so the row keeps one calm line and a 48 dp target.
- *Home parity:* the Home block list now renders `AppMark` (the real launcher icon, same composable the Rules rows and the picker use) at the Rules size; websites keep the globe fallback.
- *Repeated failed saves re-announce themselves (device-caught while running this matrix):* the banner's scroll-to-top `LaunchedEffect` is keyed on `(error, saveAttempt)` — a second save that produces an identical `EditorError` value (data-class equality skips recomposition) previously left the banner off-screen with zero feedback, which read as an inert Save button during testing. One attempt counter, incremented per save(), three lines. The Remove-all dialog body also became a `<plurals>` resource after the device dialog read "1 rules will be deleted".

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *Have the sweep write on a periodic worker* — the flip only matters when the user can see it, and the Rules screen already reads live; a worker adds battery + a second writer for zero visible gain; rejected. (b) *Hide expired rules from the list (engine-consistent, UI-minimal)* — deletes the user's ability to re-arm or clean them and makes rules vanish with no trace; the shelf + toggle is the honest shape; rejected. (c) *Auto-clear lapsed `blockExpiresAt` at save instead of resume-clear* — the expiry is provenance (see the "until" suffix); erasing it early loses why the rule went quiet; rejected. (d) *Silently coerce a too-long limit down to the window on save* — a save must store what the user sees; refusing with the reason is honest; rejected. (e) *Minutes field auto-fills "0" when hours typed* — mutating the user's field mid-typing is worse than accepting blank-as-zero; rejected. (f) *Unify the two identical private `StatePill` copies into one atom* — 2 occurrences, below the 3+ DRY threshold, and exact copies don't drift by themselves; deferred until a third caller appears.

**Verified.** Device (X6532, 2026-10-04): `rules-expire` debug seam planted a lapsed expiry under `example.com` (raw UPDATE — no policy bump; the flip under test is the app's own) → tab-away/return ran the app's sweep → reader log `enabled=1 → enabled=0` and the row moved to Deactivated with the Expired pill and switch off (screenshots ed10/ed11 series); toggle-on resumed to enabled=1 and the row returned to Websites; a re-plant re-lapsed it and the sweep re-flipped it (idempotent cycle). `--es read 1` (read-only mode) carried the resume leg without the plant side effect. Editor input-domain matrix, all five cases on the final Ends-design build (X6532, 2026-10-04, fresh "New website block" + vimeo.com test target created and deleted the same run): blank/blank → LimitInvalid banner; "0" + blank minutes → the same LimitInvalid banner (reached by scrolling up — see the save-attempt finding below); 30 h → LimitTooLarge banner; 10 h beside the 9 h overnight window → LimitExceedsWindow banner; "2" hours with blank minutes → live caption "= blocked after 2h a day" mid-typing, then saves as a "10:00 PM–7:00 AM (overnight) · 2h daily limit" row — the owner's journey in one step, no 00. Ends row cycle screenshotted end-to-end: Never → tap → dialog (past dates unselectable, OK disabled until a pick) → Oct 5, 2026 value; reopened dialog shows Clear only when a date is set; Clear → Never. Remove-all dialog renders with the count (and its "1 rules" plural was caught and fixed here). Both fixes then verified on the reinstalled build the same evening: a second identical failed save auto-scrolls back to the banner every attempt, and the Remove-all dialog reads "1 rule will be deleted permanently." Home real-icon rows screenshotted earlier the same day. App-type rule editors share `validateBlockMode`/`parseLimitTotalMinutes` (the website-only extra is the S9.1 target chain), covered by JVM pins plus the earlier daily-limit device runs. JVM: `PolicyDomainTest.sweepExpired_flipsOnlyLapsedEnabledRules`, `deleteRules_removesTheBatch_andReloadsTheSnapshotOnce`; `RuleModeTest` — `validate_limitLongerThanItsWindow_isRejected`, `windowLengthMinutes_countsOvernightWindowsToMidnightAndOn`, `resumedRule_clearsALapsedExpiry_butKeepsAFutureOne`, updated `parseLimitTotalMinutes` blank pins; `ScheduleSummaryTest.rowState_lapsedDurationOutranksPause_becauseTheUserNeverPausedIt`.

**Honest limits.** (a) The sweep runs when the Rules screen is visited (or the expired set changes) — between the lapse instant and the next visit, the engine already ignores the rule (Rule 3), so what is stale is only the *toggle's* display, never enforcement. (b) "Remove all" deletes every deactivated rule — the confirm dialog carries the count, but there is no per-cause filtering (by design: one shelf, one action). (c) Blank-as-zero is field-order-agnostic: blank hours + "30" reads 30 m, which is the literal reading but silent about intent; the live "= blocked after …" caption remains the feedback loop. (d) The Home rows now match Rules in mark and grammar; the Home list still intentionally drops the Switch (taps route to the editor, finding #19).

## #139 — The Mailbox Read Under Every Pair's Keys: One Pair Gate for Foreign Documents (2026-10-04)

**Context.** Defect #18(c), observed on the 2026-10-01 two-device day: `SIGNATURE_INVALID` on the same two messages under *every* enrolled pair, plus a pull-skip (a deletion request attempted under older pairs' iterations but never listed in its own — and, pre-ADR #130, never delivered in verifiable form). Investigation 2026-10-04 read `FirestoreMailboxReceiver.pull` against the §19.5 topology — one mailbox (`/mailboxes/{uid}/messages`) backs every pair a device holds. The push-wake path filtered a fetched document by `pairId`; the bulk watermark query handed every listed document to `MailboxMessageHandler` **unfiltered**. Under another pair's keys verification can only fail, so every foreign document logged SIGNATURE_INVALID into that pair's `invalid` counter — and when a repair was in flight, `flagTamperIfRepairing` classified it TAMPER_SUSPECTED: a false tamper signal produced by ordinary multi-pair traffic.

**Decision.**
- **The pair gate moves into `MailboxMessageHandler.handle`** — the single choke point both pull paths already route through: `doc.pairId != pairId` → `Kind.MISSING` (skipped), before any verification. Null pairId (hand-written / pre-schema) is foreign — the wake path always treated it so; now every path does. The wake path's `takeIf` was removed (now redundant); its comment records why non-owning pairs still see the wake id (the gate turns it into MISSING before verification).
- **The bulk loop's watermark still advances across foreign documents** — each is gate-skipped, so moving past them is pure quota: the next pass stops re-reading the shared mailbox's foreign tail. Comment states this at the loop.
- **Pull-skip verdict (the second half of #18(c)):** the 2026-10-01 shape — the deletion request never listed under its own pair — was the pre-#130 relay rejection: a relay-rejected document gets **no push at all**, so the wake path never fetched it, and the bulk watermark had already advanced past its server timestamp. Re-delivery writes the same document id (ALREADY_EXISTS path, same timestamp), so a rejected doc can never re-surface for its own pair; ADR #130 closed the deletion-request class by accepting terminated+members at the relay. Residual generic hole (any message whose push is lost *and* whose commit visibility lagged a watermark-advancing pass) is documented as an honest limit — the wake path plus `onDeletedMessages()` full reconciliation cover the normal cases.
- **#18(b) severity (poison replay), settled without a new mechanism:** an unverifiable document is never acked and never marked processed (by design — "stays subject to re-delivery"), so it lives to the 30-day mailbox TTL; its re-attempts are bounded per pair by the watermark and, before this fix, multiplied by every enrolled pair. After the gate, only its own pair ever attempts it. Severity: monitoring/telemetry noise only — no chain, data, or security effect, and planting one requires a member sender (rules gate mailbox writes to the pair's members). No further change; owner reviews the severity statement.

**Verified.** JVM: `MailboxMessageHandlerTest` — new `foreignPairDocument_isSkipped_beforeVerification` (foreign pairId → MISSING, no append/ack/notify, `tamperChecks == 0`; null pairId → MISSING too) on top of the existing pipeline-order suite, all fixture docs now carrying their pairId. Targeted sync suite (`MailboxMessageHandlerTest`, `PartnerSyncCoordinatorTest`, `MailboxOutboxDrainerTest`) green. Device observation rode the same-day live-pair legs (this ADR's session): post-fix pulls log no SIGNATURE_INVALID for foreign docs.

**Honest limits.** (a) The residual pull-skip hole (lost push + commit-visibility lag) is bounded and rare but not closed — closing it needs a second reconciliation path (pull-all or a pair-scoped mailbox), which the quota model deliberately avoids. (b) The gate is JVM-pinned; the foreign-doc noise itself was a live observation, so the fix's on-device proof is the absence of the old log lines during the same-day runs, not a constructed reproduction.

## #140 — The Website Tunnel Turns Itself On: Production Activation and Lifecycle (2026-10-04)

**Context.** The website engine (S9 UI + the metering plan's S0–S11) is the app-owned `DnsSinkholeVpnService`: one tunnel serving both website-rule kinds — static block rules answered NXDOMAIN by `DomainBlocklist` (mode-independent) and usage-state rules shaped/metered by `DomainUsageEngine` (mode-gated, §13 rollout). Through S11 it only ever started from debug harnesses; `MyExodus V1.md` carried the gap verbatim ("inactive in release builds: no production-side start call site exists yet"). Task #26: the production path.

**Decision — one predicate, one policy object, three boundaries.**
- *Activation predicate:* at least one live website rule in the published snapshot (`urlRules.isNotEmpty()`) **and** the one-time system consent on file. Both rule kinds count — the tunnel is what blocks static rules at DNS *and* what meters/enforces limited ones. No live website rule ⇒ no tunnel (battery).
- *Policy:* `VpnLifecycle` owns the whole decision as a pure function `decide(hasRules, consented, state)` → `START`/`STOP`/`NONE`, with `ensure(context, hasRules)` executing it against live state. START only from `STOPPED`/`FAILED`/`REVOKED`; a live tunnel (`STARTING`/`ACTIVE`/`DEGRADED`) is never restarted (DEGRADED is a live tunnel — the in-app banner owns it). STOP whenever no live rules remain and the tunnel is up — including `STARTING` (the last rule can vanish mid-establish) and including with consent revoked (stopping never needs consent).
- *Call sites (all idempotent, all through `ensure`):* `MainActivity.onResume` (fast-path + DB load, then ensure — the same boundary the other refresh passes use); `RulesScreen`'s `LaunchedEffect` keyed on the rules list + engine status (the moment rules are created/deleted or the screen returns from the consent dialog); `BootReceiver` on boot **and** `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` (both kill the process — without this the tunnel would stay down until the app was next opened; nobody opens the app after an update).
- *Consent:* always the platform's own dialog (`VpnService.prepare`), never launched automatically — the only entry is the explicit "turn on" row in Rules, shown by the engine-off card when live website rules exist but the tunnel isn't up. A non-null `prepare()` result means no consent on file; `ensure` then starts nothing. Revocation clears the platform's consent, so the same row naturally resurfaces after a revoke.
- *UI truth:* the engine-off card distinguishes not-consented (turn-on row), FAILED (retry row), and DEGRADED (banner-owned); copy never claims the tunnel is on when it isn't.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *Start the tunnel at app start unconditionally* — battery + a standing VPN notification with zero rules to enforce; rejected. (b) *Auto-launch the consent dialog from `onResume`* — a system dialog on top of whatever the user was doing, outside any explicit ask; consent stays row-driven; rejected. (c) *Trigger only from the Rules screen* — misses boot and updates (rules armed, tunnel down until the app is opened); the boot/install receiver is the boundary that closes it; rejected without it. (d) *A periodic reconciler worker* — every real boundary already calls `ensure`; a worker adds a moving part and battery for nothing; rejected.

**Verified.** Device (X6532C, debug `ENFORCE` build, 2026-10-04): create `example.com` via the real UI → snapshot bump → `policy blocklist: 1 host rules` → `vpn-state ACTIVE` + `tun0` up; `ping example.com` → unknown host with `nxdomain example.com v4 rule=policy written=true`; an unrelated domain resolves. Delete via the real UI (row → Edit block → Delete → confirm) → snapshot bump → `policy blocklist: 0 host rules` → `MyExodusVpnLifecycle: stopping website tunnel (no live website rules left)` → `vpn-state STOPPED` → `tun0` gone, `example.com` resolves + ICMP replies. Receiver path: `install -r` → `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` → cold DB load → `starting website tunnel (live website rules present)` → ACTIVE. Engine-off card copy + consent-dialog launch path verified visually (the system's `ConfirmDialog` activity starts; see #142 for the XOS appop-ignored state). JVM: `VpnLifecycleTest` (6 tests) pins the full decide() matrix — START from STOPPED/FAILED/REVOKED, NONE on live states, no autonomous start without consent in any state, STOP symmetry incl. mid-establish and revoked-consent.

**Honest limits.** (a) Boot/install path: the tunnel comes up after the cold DB load (10–16 s device-measured) — during that window a freshly started process has no tunnel; nothing else is metered/enforced yet either, so the gap is real but closed by the same boundary that creates it. (b) The consent dialog is the platform's; in the XOS "Forget VPN" state (appop `ACTIVATE_VPN=ignore`) the platform's own dialog self-destroys ~100 ms after launch and the re-grant path is outside the app's control — full account in #142. (c) Flavor note (read-verified, not device-ran): the start site is not flavor-gated, so a release-flavor build (mode `OFF` until the §13 rollout) starts the tunnel for website rules and its static block rules enforce at DNS while limit-mode rules mint no enforcement — the OPD #27 release-honesty gap unchanged in nature, now with this extra daylight; debug builds are the rig's (`ENFORCE`). OPD #27 remains the owner's call.

## #141 — One Active Profile, One Rule Set: the Engine Mirrors the UI's Pick (2026-10-04)

**Context.** During the #140 device legs the receiver-path snapshot read `0 app, 3 url rules` while the Rules UI showed nothing. Rig residue: the VPN spike harness had seeded its rules under a second `is_active=1` profile (`spike-harness-profile`), and `RestrictionRuleDao.getActiveRules()` was a JOIN over `is_active = 1` — a union across *every* active profile — while the rules UI (and `UserProfileDao.observeActive`) uses `ORDER BY user_id LIMIT 1`. Net: a stray second active row could make the engines enforce rules the UI could never show, and the harness's own seeded run was reading the wrong profile's rule set.

**Decision.**
- `getActiveRules()` is now a subquery that mirrors the `observeActive` pick exactly (`user_id = (SELECT user_id FROM user_profile WHERE is_active = 1 ORDER BY user_id LIMIT 1)`) — one authority for "the active profile", so engine truth and UI truth cannot diverge even under stray residue. `UserProfileDao.getActive()` gained the same explicit `ORDER BY user_id` so all three readers agree deterministically.
- `SchemaConstraints`' comment records the state of the single-active invariant: partial unique index rejected (Room cannot model partial indexes — a DB carrying one fails TableInfo validation on every open, caught 2026-08-29); single-active remains profile-management work; the mirror guarantees engine == UI meanwhile.
- Harness (`VpnSpikeActivity`): seeds under the **active** profile (the policy-seeded run now exercises the real path, including `rule=policy` NXDOMAIN), sweeps any old `spike-harness-profile` rows, and deletes its own rules after the gates run — the rig no longer leaves residue that outlives its session.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *Partial unique index on `is_active=1`* — already rejected in the v1→v2 era (Room/TableInfo validation failure) and it would turn a stray row into a hard open-failure instead of a resolved pick; rejected. (b) *Keep the union, filter later* — still enforces rules from a profile the user cannot see; rejected. (c) *Deactivate-on-activate enforcement in the profile flow now* — the actual invariant fix, but it is profile-management scope (recorded in SchemaConstraints for the V1 UI workstream); the mirror is the correctness fix the engines need regardless; deferred.

**Verified.** JVM: `PolicyDomainTest.activeProfileQueries_agreeUnder_aStraySecondActiveRow` — with a stray second active profile and a rule under each, `getActive()`, `getActiveRules()`, and the published snapshot all agree on the lowest `user_id` pick and exactly its rule. Device (X6532C, 2026-10-04): receiver-path snapshot pre-fix `0 app, 3 url rules` → post-fix `0 app, 0 url rules`; harness run green end-to-end (policy-seeded NXDOMAIN proof, `success=true`, self-clean left 0 app / 0 url).

**Honest limits.** (a) The mirror resolves the stray-row *read*; the second active row itself remains possible (profile management/manual edits) — the invariant blocks enforcement divergence, not the residue. (b) The tiebreak is `user_id` lexicographic order — deterministic and mirrored everywhere, but not "most recently activated"; a future profile-management flow that activates a different profile must rely on deactivate-on-activate (same deferred item).

## #142 — The Revoke→Re-Consent Dead End: REVOKED Is Startable, and START Means Start (2026-10-04)

**Context.** Device-found on 2026-10-04 running the #140 legs. Reproduced shape: "Forget VPN" in system settings sets appop `ACTIVATE_VPN` to `ignore`; `onRevoke` fires and the engine state goes sticky `REVOKED`; from then on the system's own consent dialog (`com.android.vpndialogs/ConfirmDialog`) launches then self-destroys ~100 ms into `onResume` (3× reproduced, transition `aborted=true`), and `prepare()` keeps returning non-null. After the owner re-granted (the appop bit was restored), the Rules engine-off card vanished — consent was on file — and **nothing ever restarted the tunnel**: `decide()` excluded `REVOKED` from START, so every ensure call was a no-op while the state stayed sticky. Second latent defect in the same class, found reading the service: `onStartCommand` accepted `ACTION_START` only when the state was `STOPPED` — an explicit start from **FAILED** (the engine-off card's retry row walks `VpnLifecycle.start`) and from **REVOKED** was silently dropped.

**Decision.**
- `VpnLifecycle.decide`: `REVOKED` joins `STOPPED`/`FAILED` as a startable state **given consent**. Revoke clears the platform's consent, so nothing starts without a fresh grant — but once the user re-grants, this is exactly what brings the tunnel back. Without it the cycle dead-ends: the row that walks the user to the dialog disappears the moment consent is on file again.
- `DnsSinkholeVpnService.onStartCommand`: `ACTION_START` is accepted from any non-live state (`STOPPED`, `FAILED`, `REVOKED`); `STARTING`/`ACTIVE`/`DEGRADED` ignore it (a second establish would only churn the tunnel). STOP unchanged.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *Clear the REVOKED state when consent returns* — there is no event to hook; consent return is invisible (`prepare()` simply returns null next call). (b) *Collapse REVOKED into STOPPED at the start sites* — erases the tamper state that the engine-status UI exists to show; rejected. (c) *Only widen the service, keep decide() strict* — nothing would ever send the START; the pair of fixes is the mechanism.

**Verified.** JVM: `VpnLifecycleTest.revokedTunnel_restartsOnlyOnceConsentIsBack` (START with consent, NONE without) plus the updated matrix (`liveRulesWithConsent_startFromIdleAndFailed` covers FAILED). Device (X6532C, fixed build, 2026-10-04): device sat in sticky `REVOKED`; `install -r` → `MY_PACKAGE_REPLACED` → receiver-path cold load → `starting website tunnel (live website rules present)` → `policy blocklist: 1 host rules` → `vpn-state ACTIVE` — the full recovery path that previously dead-ended. Subsequent UI delete → `stopping website tunnel` → `STOPPED` (stop symmetry intact).

**Honest limits.** (a) In the XOS appop-`ignore` state the platform auto-declines its own dialog — outside the app's control; the app-side path is correct (the system activity verifiably starts), and the rig restored the appop via `cmd appops set … ACTIVATE_VPN allow`. A user-facing re-grant path from that exact state was not established in this session; every other revoke shape (another VPN taking over, disable in settings) surfaces the row and walks the normal dialog. (b) The `REVOKED`-recovery proof exercised the receiver path; the onResume/RulesScreen call sites share the same `ensure`+`decide` core (JVM-pinned), so the device proof covers the policy end-to-end, not each call site separately.

## #143 — The Signing Prompt Is the Sheet: an App-Rendered Picker Was Tried and Owner-Rejected; the Light-Mode XOS Text Bug Is System-Side (2026-10-04)

**Context.** ADR #112's decision put the credential affordance *inside* the system sheet (combined `BIOMETRIC_STRONG or DEVICE_CREDENTIAL`, one dialog, no chaining). Owner-reported observation on the light-mode rig phone (X6532C): the sheet's bottom-left credential option is clickable but its text **paints invisible**; the same option renders fine on the dark-mode phone (X6532). A fix attempt this session added an app-rendered picker dialog in front of the sheet ("Use fingerprint" / "Use PIN or pattern"), routing the choice via `chooseSigningPath`.

**Decision — reverted by owner ruling the same day.** "I hate that stupid modal… the feature with biometrics already has on the bottom left use pattern or pin… just showing the popup with biometrics" — the sheet's own affordance is the single prompt; an app-rendered picker in front duplicates a control the platform already provides and adds a decision step the owner explicitly did not want. The picker (`SigningChoiceDialog`, `signingChoice` state, `requestSigningChoice`/`resolveSigningChoice`, three strings) was fully reverted; the pre-picker routing stands unchanged (`chooseSigningPath` → sheet; lockout → device-credential fallback per #112; KPE → migration). The invisible-text observation is recorded here as a **system-side defect, parked**: the sheet is SystemUI-rendered and not app-restylable, so no in-app workaround is warranted (and an in-app workaround is exactly what was rejected).

**Evidence for the finding (hierarchy-level, because captures are impossible).** A live sheet on the light-mode phone dumped: node `text="Use Numeric Password"`, class `Button`, `clickable=true`, bounds `[16,1408][306,1504]` (bottom-left) — the text exists in the accessibility tree while painting invisibly. Screenshots of the sheet are impossible on this build: `screencap` returns 0 bytes and `screenrecord` yields a **black frame** (secure surface) — both verified this session. Dark-mode phone renders the same node visibly. Working conclusion (not yet source-verified): a SystemUI theming defect in the light-mode credential-button text color, OEM/SystemUI-side.

**Verified.** Revert compiles + rebuilt `app-debug.apk` installed `-r` on both rig phones; the signing flows (pairing ceremony sign on both phones, this same session's re-pair) ran through the plain sheet with no picker present.

**Honest limits.** (a) The system-side root cause is inferred from device behavior — we have no SystemUI source for this XOS build; a genuine fix would be platform-side. (b) The parked research question (whether any app-side theme attribute could influence the sheet's button text on this OEM) was not investigated beyond establishing that the sheet is not app-rendered; owner left it "problem for another day".

## #144 — The Identity Wipe Day: Re-Pair After the Deterministic-PairId Block, and What the Paired #5 Re-Run Actually Captured (2026-10-04)

**Context.** Post-#139 verification day needed a healthy pair, but the rig's pair was mid-collapse from a real owner action: fingerprint removal on the X6532 (19:50) with a pair ENROLLED. The enrollment-change poll committed migrations epoch 3 (19:50, `reEnrollment=12`) and epoch 4 (19:52, `reEnrollment=0`) — `runIdentityMigration` rotates + `destroyAllKeys` shreds every pair key including the live pair's. `LocalWipeDetector` then classified the device by its own definition — prior-pairing signal (agreement key present) + empty enrollments = wipe — and surfaced "Pairing data was lost" (no auto-recovery, AD-22). On the other phone (X6532C) the pair still existed; a naive re-pair was **blocked by the backend guard**: `registerPairing` compares `proofs[uid].transcriptHash` for the still-`active` deterministic `pairIdOf(ownUid, partnerUid)` → `PAIRING_ACTIVE` ("unpair the existing partnership first").

**Decision — the sequence that actually works, now device-proven.**
1. *Unpair from the surviving side first* — the surviving phone (C) ran its own `BilateralDeletion` initiate (owner sign): `delivered=1`, `local_shredded=1`, ack path live. Only a `terminated` pair record unlocks re-pairing on the same uid pair (backend then bumps `pairingEpoch`; the fresh ceremony landed at **pairing epoch 9** on both sides).
2. *Same deterministic pairId is reused* — no orphan row may remain on either side, or the roster shows duplicate cards (C still holds two stale enrollment rows from earlier same-day ceremonies; cleanup queued as an owner-window unpair each).
3. *Ceremony direction that satisfies the #5 goal*: fresh invite (10-min TTL) minted from the wiped phone; joiner entered the code; SAS **73780** rendered identical on both; both signed via the plain system sheet; activation both sides, partner labels cross-set correctly ("X6532C" ↔ "X6532"), hub card + sync card live.

**#5 paired re-run — what was captured, honestly.** The re-run's two targets: (a) *paired migration* — the 19:50/19:52 migrations ran **with a pair enrolled** (`reEnrollment=12` at epoch 3): keys shredded while paired, wipe card shown after, no crash, poll-driven. Confirmed. (b) *KPE live sign* — the designed scenario (lock removed → key KPE-seeded → next sign's pre-prompt probe raises `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException` → migration → dialog → retry). **It did not fire**: by the time the first post-wipe sign ran (this ceremony), the enrollment poll had already committed the migrations and rotated to a fresh key, so the probe found a **valid** key — no KPE line in the log, identity epoch stayed 4, the sign succeeded first attempt. The KPE branch remains JVM-pinned and **device-unexercised**; its live repro recipe is the poll-blind shape: remove and re-add the lock **within one poll interval** so the state nets back to `"0|true"` and the poll commits nothing — then the next sign carries a stale key and the probe fires. Recorded as available-on-demand, not a blocker.

**Verified.** Device, both phones: pre-ceremony DB forensics (A: empty enrollments + wipe card; C: stale rows + unpair row `delivered=1 local_shredded=1`); ceremony end-state DBs — identical `SvBQFOk…` pairId, `pairing_epoch=9` both sides, labels swapped correctly; hub cards + "Synced 10m ago" on A. Logs: `enrollment change detected — running identity migration` / `identity migration done: epoch=3/4` pairs; no `permanently invalidated` line during the ceremony sign. All work on the reverted build from #143 installed `-r` on both phones.

**Honest limits.** (a) KPE branch untouched on device (above) — the recipe exists but was not exercised; exercising it needs another lock remove+re-add window on the X6532 (owner's phone, owner's lock). (b) The two stale C rows stay until an owner-unpart each; the roster will show three "X6532" cards until then. (c) The wipe→re-pair flow required the dual-sided unpair; there is no single-sided recovery path for a wiped identity by design (the surviving side's keys can never verify the wiped side's new ones — the pairing record must terminate first).

## #145 — The Widget Staleness Week: the Platform's Periodic Update Is the Backstop a Dead Process Needs (2026-10-14)

**Context.** Owner report: the Home widget showed a milestone frozen for about a week; it only moved when the app was opened. Diagnosis: the provider declared `updatePeriodMillis="0"` (original spec choice — "the app drives every refresh itself", with the platform periodic update dismissed as "wakeups that render an unchanged snapshot"). That rationale silently assumed a live process. On this device XOS kills the FGS-hosting process as *cached idle & background restricted* (recorded since 2026-09-19), and a killed/frozen process runs no app-side writer — so a week with no app launch meant a week of no updates, exactly the window the widget exists for. Compounding but by design: `needsStreakRefresh` (ADR #87) re-reads the streak only across day boundaries or reboots, so the writer itself was never the churn source the original `0` guarded against.

**Decision.** `updatePeriodMillis` 0 → **1800000** (the platform minimum; smaller values are clamped). The platform's periodic widget update is a second, independent driver: it cold-starts a dead process, and (per the XOS `Hiber/importantScene: widget` log observed this session) widget-hosting packages are treated as an important scene the freezer avoids transitioning. App-side writers remain the primary driver (15-minute `HealthCheckWorker`, event-driven snapshot publishes, resize callback) — unchanged, ADR #40/#87. The hard limit stands: a force-stopped (or background-restricted) app receives no broadcasts — no widget mechanism can reach it; the heal is a user launch.

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *Spec Decision 14's `onEnabled`/`onDisabled` scheduling* — never adopted (ADR #40); it would not have helped either, since those callbacks themselves need a live process. (b) *Shorter interval* — clamped by the platform to 30 minutes; unavailable. (c) *WorkManager-only* — already present and genuinely the primary path; but OEM background-restriction suppresses JobScheduler too, while widget updates are a surface the OS prioritizes — two independent platform mechanisms beat one.

**Verified.** Device (X6532, 2026-10-14), dead-process leg with the app never opened — `kill -9` at 19:58:03 (`pidof` empty at 20:04); AMS at 20:06:20 `startProcess name=com.example.myexodus knownToBeDead=true` → `Start proc 9404 … for service …SystemJobService`; 20:06:23 `MyExodusWidget: widget publish: … days=11 … write=true` (the writer re-stamped); 20:06:25 `widget view: status=PROTECTED days=11 checkedAgo=null` (fresh render, no staleness caption); the four size compositions rendered and the launcher applied (`TranGriffin/AppWidget: hook widget[7]`); screencap confirmed the fresh "Day 11" face. Provider record (dumpsys appwidget) reads `updatePeriodMillis=1800000` with widget id=7 bound to the receiver. JVM: the ADR #87 suite (day-boundary `needsStreakRefresh`, restamp gating) green.

**Honest limits.** (a) The confirmed cold-start trigger in this leg was the **JobScheduler** path (HealthCheckWorker at its 15-minute window), not an appwidget tick — the platform broadcast is dumpsys-verified as configured and documented Android behavior, but its own cold-start delivery was not separately isolated tonight (the job revived the process 8 minutes after the kill, before any 30-minute tick). (b) 30 minutes is coarse: worst-case display staleness without any app-side writer is ~30 min, plus doze deferral. (c) The day count only changes at day boundaries anyway; the freshness caption is the real liveness signal, and it ages into UNVERIFIED on a dead process by design. (d) A force-stopped app is unreachable by every mechanism above (platform limit).

## #146 — The Alert That Kept Coming Back: Persisted Watermarks, an Unconditional Re-Assert, a Reactive Banner, and a Cause-Agnostic Copy (2026-10-14)

**Context.** Owner report: "MyExodus was stopped from the task manager" kept re-appearing "even when I reenable" — days after the last real stop, on a phone whose owner never opens the Task Manager ("how can an active user access taskmanager"). Root-cause chain, all four legs device-proven on the X6532:
1. **Re-fire.** The FGS start path wrote the exit-scan watermark with async `apply()`; XOS SIGKILLs this process routinely, and a lost flush left the watermark stale — the 10-12 force-stop was re-detected at the 10-13 and 10-14 starts. Worse, `reenable()` cleared the flag but never advanced the watermark, so even a successful tap could not retire old exit records.
2. **Self-silencing alert.** `ProtectionStatusMonitor.publish()` was change-gated end-to-end (store write *and* the degraded notification, ADR #121(3)). Once the state persisted and the notification was cancelled out-of-band — a package update (`install -r` is this app's only distribution channel) or a swipe — nothing could ever re-post it while the state stayed unchanged.
3. **Late banner.** The Home banner read the store once at composition + on resume, but the FGS's stop detection runs *after* the resume read — a freshly detected stop showed no banner until the next resume/relaunch (device-caught: stop → open app → no banner; close/reopen → banner).
4. **Wrong copy.** The alert named the Task Manager as the cause, but the detector counts every explicit stop shape (plan v2 §8.2 "do not conflate") and the device's own exit history shows system_server-initiated stops — the named cause was wrong for every real detection on this device.

**Decision.**
1. `requiresExplicitReEnable` and `lastKnownGoodExitCheckEpochMillis` writes are `commit()` — the flag+watermark gate whether an old exit can re-fire, and an async flush lost to an OEM SIGKILL is a days-old stop resurrected.
2. `reenable()` advances the watermark with the clear (`maxOf(existing, now)` — a backward clock correction must not regress it). The tap asserts "from here on"; there is no masking window, because a tap can only happen while this process is running.
3. `publish()` keeps the store write and the transition log change-gated, but re-asserts the degraded notification **unconditionally** on every refresh; `setOnlyAlertOnce(true)` keeps re-asserts silent. This supersedes #121(3)'s fully-gated reconciliation: a gate that can never observe "the alert is gone but the state persists" cannot restore it.
4. Degradation is exposed as a `StateFlow` (seeded from the store so a cold start renders a persisted banner immediately), collected by MainActivity — a detection landing after the UI's resume read reaches the banner in-session.
5. Copy is cause-agnostic: "Protection is paused / Something on your phone closed MyExodus. Time limits stay paused until you re-enable." Move to string resources with it (both surfaces share the strings).

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *`reenable()` nulls `degradation`* — already rejected in audit finding #14 (pre-empts the derivation and freezes the stale notification); reconfirmed here. (b) *Change-gate the notification but re-post on resume only* — resume-only is exactly the banner bug's shape: it makes visibility a function of the user opening the app, which is the state this whole ADR removes. (c) *Distinguish "real" Task-Manager stops from system stops and keep the specific copy* — not determinable: every explicit stop arrives as reason=10 with a system_server pid in the description, and Task Manager stops are themselves system_server-initiated; naming any single cause cannot be made honest. (d) *A repost timer* — unnecessary; refresh cycles already exist (FGS 30 s, 15-minute worker, accessibility callbacks) and an unconditional publish rides them for free.

**Verified.** Device (X6532, 2026-10-14): tap Re-enable → flag=false + watermark advanced + `protection APP_STOPPED -> OK` logged, notification 1002 cleared; `kill -9` → relaunch → flag stays false, no re-detection line, watermark advanced (the Oct-12 offender sits permanently below it); inverse control — `am force-stop` of a *running* app → detection fired (banner + notification), so the fix silences the re-fire only, not the alarm; `install -r` (out-of-band cancel) → launch → 1002 re-appeared with the new copy; force-stop of the running app → relaunch → banner present in-session (uiautomator-verified) without a HOME cycle; "Times blocked" label fits its two-line slot at 720 px. JVM: `ProtectionStatusMonitorPublishTest` (4 — repost-after-cancel, healthy-posts-nothing, flow-tracks-publish-and-clear, only-alert-once), `ProtectionStatusStoreTest` (6 — reenable advances watermark, never moves it backward, keeps degradation, persists across instances, nulling, defaults), `UserStoppedDetectorTest` +6 adversarial (strict `>` boundary, ±1 ms, embedded kill-background markers, case-sensitivity fail-safe, row-level gating, newer-row keying).

**Honest limits.** (a) A swipe-away of the paused alert is re-asserted and re-alerts once per dismissal — deliberate: the state persists until it is acted on. (b) Users who *did* stop the app by hand now see the generic copy too — deliberate, cause cannot be named truthfully. (c) `commit()` is a main-thread disk write; it runs only at FGS start and on tap — bounded, and correctness outranks the microseconds here. (d) The banner's clear on re-enable rides `refresh()`; both call sites invoke it immediately after `reenable()` (unchanged, finding #14).

## #147 — The Drain That Could Still Throw: the Outbox's Local-DB Half Is Now Total (2026-10-14)

**Context.** The 2026-10-14 stress campaign's A4 slice (crash window between the Firestore commit and the local SENT mark) found that `MailboxOutboxDrainer.drain()` — a function whose entire design is "always returns a `DrainResult`" — could still throw. The four local-bookkeeping calls (`markSent` on the happy path and on the ALREADY_EXISTS replay path, `recordAttempt` on the PERMISSION_DENIED and generic-failure paths) sat bare inside `drain()`: a Room/SQLCipher hiccup (disk full, locked DB — Tier B's disk-fill scenario, not a theoretical one) escaped the function. The consequences are contract violations, not just a lost log line: `PartnerSyncCoordinator.syncPair` calls `drainOutbox` at line 170 **unwrapped** — every sibling step in the same function is `runCatching`-wrapped (146/154/158/164/180), precisely because drain was trusted to be total — so the throw skipped `pullMailbox` for that pair and aborted the remaining pairs' whole slice, breaking both documented invariants ("a drain failure never skips the pull", KDoc line 39; "one pair's failure never skips another's", line 101).

**Decision.** `drain()` is total by construction: a private `bookkeeping(what, messageId, block)` helper wraps all four local-DB call sites in try/catch → `Log.w(… "self-heals on the next pass")`, never rethrows. The network-outcome path (`Sent` / `PermissionDenied` / `Failed`) is untouched, ALREADY_EXISTS replay semantics are untouched, and §9's leave-pending-on-failure rule is untouched — a lost SENT mark simply self-heals on the next pass (doc id = message id → replay reads ALREADY_EXISTS → marked then). No `runCatching` was added at the coordinator call site: the class contract is now honest, and the bare call site stays as the design pin that keeps it so (`BilateralDeletion.kt:361` already wrapped; left as-is).

**Alternatives considered.** (a) *`runCatching` at the coordinator call site* — guards one of two callers, leaves the class's own KDoc false, and the next caller re-inherits the bug; the stateful stress fake drives the class directly, where a call-site guard would never be visible. Root fix at the shared function, per the "one guard where all callers route" rule. (b) *Surface the bookkeeping failure in `DrainResult`* — the result describes the network outcome; a mark failure is already observable as the row staying PENDING plus the warning log, and a new variant would ripple through coordinator and BilateralDeletion `when`s for observability alone. (c) *Retry the mark within the pass* — no fixed in-pass action exists while the DB is full/locked; the 15-minute cadence is the retry, unchanged.
